`plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #180` (this entry); `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md` (new "Post-create room changes" subsection + the "header dates are immutable" contract + the actual-range UI on the row + drawer + email + receipt); `plan/docs/BACKEND.md` (the new `actualDateRange` field on `reservations/{id}` + the new `POST /api/bookings/add-room` route); `plan/docs/API-ROUTES.md` (the new endpoint signature + the request/response shape). **(5) Test coverage** — 6 pure-helper tests (shared) + 5 + 11 source-text endpoint tests (guest-app) + 2 + 1 admin tests (admin-app) + 12 UI source-text tests (admin-app) = **37 new tests** across 5 test files. **(6) Follow-ups (not blocking)** — the `firebase/tests/mrb-14-add-room.emulator.test.ts` round-trip is deferred to the local environment that has the Java emulator (mirrors the MRB-11 + CRL-09 precedent); a future MRB-15 item may add a server-side `applyReservationDiscount` that mirrors the add-room atomicity (the MRB-12 loop works but a transactional sibling is the natural endpoint once add-room exists). **(7) Rejected** — letting the header's `checkIn` / `checkOut` mutate on reschedule (the driving bug — silently leaks the rescheduled child's range into email subject, receipt PDF, dashboard date filter, checkin reminder cron); allowing staff to add a room at NEW dates (the spec says "using its current dates" — a different-dates add would silently re-anchor the header's "shared" semantics); a separate "header dates updated" email (the existing `booking-rescheduled` action with the reservation-scope view already covers it); per-child `voucher` / `corporate` re-application on add-room (the spec keeps voucher per-child + corporate per-reservation; re-applying would be a silent usage-cap violation); guest self-service add-room (out of scope for MRB v1 — the existing guest flow ends at create, and the desk is the only entity that can guarantee availability + bed-inventory + corporate-cap arithmetic on the fly); rendering the per-child dates in the per-row `Booking` child rows (the child row's "Dates" column already shows the child's own dates — the divergence only matters at the reservation row + drawer + email + receipt level); a separate `divergentDateRanges[]` array (one `actualDateRange` is enough — the children are the source of truth, the header only tracks the MIN/MAX + the flag). **(8) Gates** — MRB-04 (the reservation header + aggregate counters — the new field slots into the existing header shape); MRB-07 (the multi-room `WalkinRoomStay` pattern + per-action scope labels — the new "Add room to reservation" button carries the `renderActionScope("reservation")` chip from MRB-07); MRB-08 (corporate + voucher `usageCount` increment-by-N — the new handler follows the same `+= 1` per room added rule, not `+= assignedRooms.length` for create); MRB-09 (the reservation-scope email view — the new `actualDateRange` field is a property of the view, not a separate parameter); MRB-10 (the reservation-scope guest lookup — the `actualDateRange` field is read by the lookup projection so the guest sees the per-child dates on `/my-booking`); MRB-11 (the stored `revenueAllocation` + the `aggregateRevenueAllocation` — the add-room handler re-stamps the aggregate in the same transaction); MRB-12 (the AdminContext reservations listener + the drawer reservation strip — the same listener the strip's money pills read; the new strip pill is a sibling of the MRB-12 Total/Paid/Balance pills); MRB-13 (the cancel-scope picker routes through the same per-child iteration — the add-room handler reuses the per-child + reservation-scope status matrix); MRB-15 (the report-reconstruction property tests pin the date-divergence accounting — the Java-gated emulator test is the MRB-15 follow-up). **(9) Driving bug** — pre-MRB-14, `handleRescheduleBooking` did `transaction.update(reservationDocRef, { checkIn, checkOut, numNights, ... })` to the new child's dates. When the desk extended one room by 2 nights, the header's "Jan 1 → Jan 5" became the new child's "Jan 1 → Jan 7" — and every other room in the reservation, the email subject, the receipt PDF, the dashboard's date filter, and the checkin reminder cron all suddenly showed "Jan 1 → Jan 7" even though the other 2 rooms were still "Jan 1 → Jan 5." The fix is to **freeze the header's `checkIn` / `checkOut` at create time** (they become the original shared dates) and add a new `actualDateRange` field for the children's spread. Once children diverge, every UI surface + email + receipt renders per-child dates. The dashboard's date filter is a MRB-15 follow-up (the per-room dates are surfaced on the row + drawer; the dashboard's date-range filter still reads the header's shared range by design — a divergent reservation is rare enough that the staff filters the children directly via the row's tooltip + the per-room `Dates` column on the expanded child rows). |
| 181 | **MRB-15 Remaining Tests + MD Sync (shipped 2026-08-03, implementation record):** eight audit sub-items closed out (v0.249.0 → v0.255.0) on `feature/phase-12-mrb-15-01-lifecycle-invariants` through `feature/phase-12-mrb-15-08-legacy-fallback` (8 feature branches + 8 merge commits into `dev`). **Driving theme** — the MRB-01 → MRB-14 work introduced a new reservation-scope layer (the `Reservation` header + per-child `bookings/{id}.reservationId` foreign key + the `kind: "reservation"` email/lookup shapes); the MRB-15 audits guard the cross-cutting invariants the new layer depends on, in source-text form (cheap, deterministic, <5s per file per `plan/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md §Testing`). The behavioural emulator tests (the `firebase/tests/mrb-15-*.emulator.test.ts` round-trips) are deferred to the local environment that has the Java emulator (mirrors the MRB-11 + CRL-09 + MRB-14 precedent). **(1) MRB-15-01 — Lifecycle invariants (v0.249.0, 14 tests in `mrb-15-01-lifecycle-invariants.test.ts`)** — across the full create → cancel lifecycle, exactly ONE `roomCount` increment at create, exactly ONE `activeRoomCount` increment at create, exactly ONE `loyalty` earn entry (`earn-${bookingId}` construction) on the eventual check-out (the cancel-side clawback at MRB-05 already guards the negative side), exactly ONE per-action email template render (no `booking-cancelled` AND `booking-cancelled-reservation` firing for the same destroy), exactly ONE `cancelledRoomCount` increment per cancelled child, exactly ONE `clawback-${bookingId}` pointsHistory entry per cancelled child. The map-based `pointsHistory` invariant `rewardsPoints == sum(pointsHistory.points)` is preserved end-to-end. **(2) MRB-15-02 — Payment-vs-room state (v0.249.0, embedded in 15-01)** — the `paymentStatus` aggregate reads from the child `childStatuses` array (NOT a hardcoded `["checked-in"]` / `["checked-out"]` literal — that was the MRB-15-03 fix below). **(3) MRB-15-03 — Transactional summary counters (v0.250.0, 13 tests in `mrb-15-03-transactional-counters.test.ts` + 4 updated MRB-05 tests)** — **REAL BUG FIX**: pre-MRB-15-03, `handleCheckinBooking` and `handleCheckoutBooking` updated the booking's own `status` but NEVER recomputed the header's `checkedInRoomCount` / `checkedOutRoomCount` — the counters were silently stuck at `0` forever. The fix reads the children via `adminDb.collection("bookings").where("reservationId", "==", bookingReservationId)` in the SAME `runTransaction` and recomputes the aggregate `paymentStatus` from the actual `childStatuses` (not the hardcoded `["checked-in"]` / `["checked-out"]` literal — correct for N>1 mixed states). The `paymentStatus` aggregate is now a true `computeReservationAggregatePaymentStatus(postStatuses)` derivation. Counter ownership contract is newly documented: `roomCount`→create+add-room, `activeRoomCount`→create+add-room+cancel, `cancelledRoomCount`→cancel only, `checkedInRoomCount`→check-in+check-out (read+write), `checkedOutRoomCount`→check-out only. **(4) MRB-15-04 — N=1 + legacy null-`reservationId` byte-equivalence (v0.251.0, 16 tests in `mrb-15-04-single-room-header-path.test.ts`)** — for an N=1 reservation, the handler's output is byte-equivalent to the pre-MRB-01 single-booking path. For a legacy pre-MRB-01 booking (no `reservationId`), the handler's output is byte-equivalent to the pre-MRB-01 single-booking path. The N=1 path carries `kind: "single"` (not `kind: "reservation"`) on the lookup response — falls through to the per-child view. **(5) MRB-15-05 — Canonical/copy consistency (v0.252.0, 16 tests in `mrb-15-05-canonical-copy.test.ts`)** — no real drift found; codebase was already consistent. The audit pins the canonical copy: British "Cancelled" (not American "Canceled"); title-case StatusBadge labels; subject pattern `[${brandName}] <verb>: ${ref} (N room[s])`; preheaders end with period. **(6) MRB-15-06 — PEX (hold-expires) fan-out (v0.253.0, 15 tests in `mrb-15-06-pex-fan-out.test.ts`)** — no real drift found; codebase was already consistent. The audit pins the `holdExpiresAt` fan-out contract: `computeHoldExpiresAt` is the single stamping helper; add-room INHERITS `reservation.holdExpiresAt ?? null` (no fresh stamp); reschedule PRESERVES (Firestore field-level merge); walkin stamps `null`; reject-payment stamps a fresh value. Every `holdExpiresAt`-touching path is covered (PEX-01..06). **(7) MRB-15-07 — Checkout + loyalty earn audit (v0.254.0, 13 tests in `mrb-15-07-checkout-loyalty-earn.test.ts`)** — the loyalty earn path (positive side that pairs with MRB-15-01's clawback). The audit pins: `earn-${bookingId}` paired with `clawback-${bookingId}`; exactly 2 actual constructions of `earn-${bookingId}` (checkout's `awardNow` + post-settlement); exactly 1 `clawback-${bookingId}` construction (in `handleCancelBooking`). The 3rd reference is a JSDoc link, not a construction. **(8) MRB-15-08 — Legacy null-`reservationId` fallback audit (v0.255.0, 19 tests in `mrb-15-08-legacy-fallback.test.ts`)** — every MRB path's null-`reservationId` branch falls through to the legacy single-booking path. `handleCreateBooking` / `handleCreateWalkin` ALWAYS mint a fresh `reservationId` (server auto-mints when the body omits it; client may preallocate for idempotency). Legacy null-`reservationId` bookings can only exist from pre-MRB-01 docs already in the database — the only way to have one in post-MRB-01 code is a pre-existing doc. The audit pins: cancel falls through to per-child (the reservation-scope branch is gated on BOTH `scope === "reservation"` AND `lookedUpReservationId.length > 0`); check-in / check-out / verify-payment / mark-payment-confirmed skip the header mirror (gated on `bookingReservationId.length > 0`); add-payment / add-refund use the dual-source pattern (legacy = `bookings/{id}/payments/`, new = `reservations/{id}/payments/` + `/refunds/`); `readTransactionalFolioSnapshot` early-returns; `loadReservationEmailView` returns `null`; `handleLookupBooking` returns `kind: "single"`; `handleRecordCancellationException` / `handleGetCancellationLiability` use the dual-source read. CRL-07 cancellation liability snapshot lives on the booking doc for legacy (the booking IS the reservation) and on the header for new reservation-scope cancels. **`handleAddRefund` and `handleMarkPaymentConfirmed` do NOT mirror the header** (the mirror is reserved for `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` which fires from the payment-uploaded transition — the mark-payment-confirmed handler is a thin status flipper; the add-refund handler is a money path that only appends a refund record). **Test coverage** — 14 + 13 + 16 + 16 + 15 + 13 + 19 = **106 source-text tests** across 7 new test files (plus 4 updated MRB-05 tests + 1 MRB-12 rewrite = **111 net new tests** in the MRB-15 umbrella). **Rejected** — full behavioural emulator tests (the `firebase/tests/mrb-15-*.emulator.test.ts` round-trips) at this stage (deferred to the Java-emulator env; the source-text guards are the higher-signal lower-cost coverage); fixing any of the 7 no-drift audits (the audit goal was to pin the existing contract, not to refactor); expanding the MRB-15-08 audit to cover the per-action email `kind` shapes (the email + lookup shapes are MRB-09 + MRB-10 territory, already tested); rewriting the 8 test files to share a common base (each audit has a distinct contract — sharing a base would couple them and obscure the per-audit contract). **Gates** — MRB-01 (the `reservationId` field + the `Reservation` header shape), MRB-04 (the reservation-scope aggregate + the new `actualDateRange`), MRB-05 (the header mirror for payment status + the loyalty earn/clawback), MRB-09 (the reservation-scope email view), MRB-10 (the reservation-scope guest lookup), MRB-11 (the per-child `revenueAllocation` + the `aggregateRevenueAllocation`), MRB-13 (the cancel-scope picker), MRB-14 (the immutable header dates + the add-room endpoint). **Follow-ups (not blocking)** — `firebase/tests/mrb-15-*.emulator.test.ts` round-trips deferred to the Java-emulator env; a future MRB-15.x item may add the per-action `kind` shape to the email + lookup response (the current audit pins the existing per-action split). **Driving bug** — MRB-15-03 was the only audit that found a real bug: pre-MRB-15-03, `handleCheckinBooking` and `handleCheckoutBooking` did `transaction.update(reservationRef, { checkedInRoomCount: ???, checkedOutRoomCount: ??? })` but the source of those values was missing — the counters were never recomputed, so a 2-room reservation that fully checked in still showed `checkedInRoomCount: 0` on the header. The aggregate `paymentStatus` was a hardcoded `["checked-in"]` / `["checked-out"]` array literal that was only correct for N=1. |

| 182 | **MRB-15-09 Admin Reservations Listener Token Refresh (proposed 2026-08-03):** the `subscribeToReservations` listener in `AdminContext` force-refreshes the current ID token BEFORE attaching `onSnapshot` to the `reservations` collection. **The driving bug** was an operator-reported console error on the staging admin app: `Error listening to reservations collection: FirebaseError: Missing or insufficient permissions.` despite the user being signed in as a valid staff user (the auth gate at `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:809-822` accepted the role claim). **Root cause** — the `isStaff()` rule on `/reservations/{id}` (`firebase/firestore.rules:140-141`) requires the ID token presented on the listener handshake to carry the `role` custom claim. The auth-state callback calls `getIdTokenResult(firebaseUser, true)` which forces a token refresh and validates the claim before setting `currentUser`. The `currentUser`-gated listener effect then attaches `onSnapshot`, but the **Firestore SDK uses its OWN cached ID token** for the listener handshake — if the SDK's cache is one refresh behind the React state (e.g. the staff claim was minted server-side moments ago, the user's session was idle long enough for the cached token to lose the claim, or the operator just signed in for the first time in a new tab), the listener attaches with the stale token and the server replies `Missing or insufficient permissions`. The current user's role is correct (the auth gate already passed), but the SDK hasn't re-fetched the token yet. **Fix** — inside the `useEffect`, BEFORE calling `onSnapshot`, call `auth.currentUser?.getIdToken(true)` and await the returned promise. The `true` argument is the SDK's force-refresh flag — `getIdToken()` (no flag) would honour the cached token and defeat the fix. The await makes the listener attach strictly after the refresh resolves; a `cancelled` flag protects the unmount-mid-refresh race (a stale refresh resolving after cleanup must NOT re-attach the listener — that would leak a duplicate listener and stall the UI on the wrong user's data). A `.catch` logs the refresh failure (network down, refresh token revoked) without attaching the listener — the UI stays on its last known state until the next auth cycle. **Implementation** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:1529-1654` (the reservations `useEffect`, replacing the MRB-12 inline `onSnapshot` call). **Tests** — 8 new source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/mrb-15-09-admin-reservations-token-refresh.test.ts` (effect is present, `getIdToken(true)` precedes `onSnapshot`, targets `auth.currentUser`, awaits via `.then`, has the `cancelled` race guard, still returns an unsubscribe, preserves the error-logging contract on `onSnapshot`, logs the refresh failure in `.catch`). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #182` (this decision), `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (new "Always force-refresh the ID token before attaching a `onSnapshot` to a staff-gated collection" entry). **Rejected** — extending the force-refresh to every other staff-gated listener in `AdminContext` (bookings / rooms / roomPrivate / roomBlocks / notifications / members / etc.) at the same time: the same race exists in theory, but the operator's bug report + the 5-day-old MRB-12 `subscribeToReservations` listener are the only observed failure in the field; widening the patch now would mean reviewing every listener for the same pattern and bumping the test count to N×8. The fix is small + isolated, the source-text test pins the contract for THIS listener, and the same pattern is now documented in `GOTCHAS.md` so the next listener added to the file naturally follows it. If a second operator hit surfaces, a follow-up MRB-15-09b can wrap the remaining listeners in a `useFreshStaffToken` hook; the per-listener regex tests already cover the inline pattern. **Gates** — MRB-12 (the listener this fix patches), MRB-15 (the audit umbrella this fix is filed under; mirrors the MRB-15-03 "real bug fix" precedent).

| 183 | **MRB-15-10 Room Types Hydration Preserves CHD-03 / EXB-01 Cap Fields (proposed 2026-08-03):** the AdminContext `roomTypes` hydration `useEffect` (which syncs local state from the `settings/hotelConfig.roomTypes` snapshot) preserves EVERY field the `RoomTypeEntry` contract guarantees — `maxChildren` (CHD-03), `maxExtraBeds` (EXB-01), and `extraBedRate` (EXB-01). **The driving bug** was an operator report on the staging admin app: "it is still not saving the children and max bed updates in the admin settings in the room types." **Root cause** — the pre-MRB-15-10 hydration mapping at `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:4815-4834` (the `useEffect([hotelConfig.roomTypes])` that runs on every snapshot echo) silently DROPPED the three CHD-03 / EXB-01 fields when mapping the snapshot to local state. The mapping preserved `value / label / shortLabel / imageUrls / bedDefinition / description / amenities / maxCapacity / pricePerNight / weekendRate / corporateRate` (11 fields) but omitted `maxChildren / maxExtraBeds / extraBedRate`. Two visible symptoms: **(1) Display bug** — the Room Types table's `{type.maxChildren ?? 0}` and `{type.maxExtraBeds ?? 0}` columns always rendered `0` for every type, even when Firestore held a non-zero value, because the local state had the fields dropped (the `?? 0` fallback fired). The Edit form's `defaultValue={editType.maxChildren ?? 0}` and `defaultValue={editType.maxExtraBeds ?? 0}` also rendered `0` in the input even when the stored value was non-zero. **(2) Save bug** — if the operator opened Edit, saw `0` in the input (the hydration drop), and saved WITHOUT changing the field, the form submitted the displayed `0` and the existing stored value was overwritten with `0`. Operators reasonably read this as "the save isn't working." The save write path itself is correct (the form reads the typed value and `updateRoomType` spreads it through to `saveRoomTypes`), but the round-trip on the next snapshot silently strips the values back out, so the user always sees 0. **Fix** — add the three fields to the mapping with the same normalization the `addRoomType` + `updateRoomType` writers use (`maxChildren: Math.max(0, Math.floor(Number(t.maxChildren) || 0))` for the integer cap; `maxExtraBeds: Math.max(0, Math.floor(Number(t.maxExtraBeds) || 0))` for the integer cap; `extraBedRate: Math.max(0, Number(t.extraBedRate) || 0)` for the rate — no `Math.floor` because rates can be fractional pesos). The same `Math.max(0, ...)` floor mirrors the `addRoomType` writer at `AdminContext.tsx:4866-4913` so the hydration and the writer agree on what the persisted shape looks like. **Implementation** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:4815-4843` (the `useEffect` body, three new mapping lines). **Tests** — 6 new source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/mrb-15-10-room-types-hydration-preserve-cap-fields.test.ts` (the hydration `useEffect` is locatable; `maxChildren` is preserved; `maxExtraBeds` is preserved; `extraBedRate` is preserved; the three fields are all present together — a one-shot tripwire that fires if a "I'll just copy-paste the existing mapping" refactor drops all three at once; every other `RoomTypeEntry` field on the contract is also present — catches a future extraction to a `pickRoomTypeFields` helper that omits a field by accident). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #183` (this decision), `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (extend the existing "always force-refresh the ID token" entry's sibling gotcha: "the Firestore snapshot hydration for any list-shaped collection must preserve EVERY field the contract guarantees — a missing field on the mapping silently resets that field to the type's default for every subsequent read"). **Rejected** — extracting the hydration mapping to a `normalizeRoomTypeConfig` helper (the pattern payment methods and booking sources use for their own hydrations — `normalizePaymentMethodConfig` at `AdminContext.tsx:4355-4363` and the `normalizeBookingSourceConfig` sibling). The contract is 14 fields today and growing; a bespoke inline mapping is the right shape for now. A future MRB-15-10b can promote it to a helper when the field count crosses ~20 or when a second consumer needs the same shape (the existing `RoomTypeEntry` type is already the single source of truth — the helper would just be a typed mirror). The same shape applies to ANY future snapshot-driven list-shaped state in `AdminContext` (and other React contexts) — a new `useSyncCollectionState<T>(snapshot, mapper)` hook would be the right place to encode the "every field is preserved" contract once. **Gates** — MRB-15 (the audit umbrella this fix is filed under; mirrors the MRB-15-03 / MRB-15-09 "real bug fix" precedents), CHD-03 (the `maxChildren` field this fix preserves), EXB-01 (the `maxExtraBeds` + `extraBedRate` fields this fix preserves). The pre-MRB-15-10 mapping was actually the second time this exact pattern bit the codebase — the booking-intercom audit 2026-07-06 found a similar field-drop in the BI-08 voucher / corporate code read paths (resolved in a separate fix); this MRB-15-10 entry documents the same pattern in the room-types hydration and locks the contract at the source level.

| 184 | **CHD-11 Soft-Constraint Children Picker (proposed 2026-08-03):** on `/book`, the children count picker is unbounded (within a sane soft cap of 10), the room-type card carries a live per-type **Fits / Tight / Doesn't fit** capacity indicator, and the Step 1 → Step 2 submit gate is the only hard validation surface. The constraint moves from the picker to the right layer — the constraint is real, the picker is the wrong enforcement surface. **Driving motivation** — the operator-facing UX bug report on 2026-08-03 ("in the /book page, I should be able to add N number of children and not to be bound by the room type since I am still choosing room type and/or rooms at this stage"). The current picker greys out the + button at the per-type cap (`numChildren >= selectedMaxSelectableChildren || numChildren >= Math.max(0, guests - 1)` at `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx:2055-2058`) and renders "You have reached this room type's limit for the current group" as a dead-end tail. Three reasons the picker is the wrong surface: **(1) dead-end at the exploration stage** — the guest is still choosing room type and/or quantity; greyed-out + with no path forward reads as "no", not "try a different room"; **(2) hides the multi-room escape hatch** — two adjacent Single Rooms can sleep the same three children the picker blocks; the cap is computed against one room, not the cart; **(3) treats the picker as if the cart were final** — the cart is a draft until Step 3; the cap belongs at the commit surface, not at the draft surface. **The pattern: exploration-first, validation-on-commit.** Three layers, with the constraint surfacing at the right one for each. **(1) Picker** — drop the `disabled` on the + button; soft-cap at `MIN(10, guests - 1)` (the existing `Math.max(0, guests - 1)` invariant that requires at least one adult); drop the "You have reached this room type's limit" tail; replace with a forward-looking "Pick a room type that fits your group, or add a second room." Keep the "Up to N can fit when extra beds cover the overflow" hint — it's a real and useful pointer to the extra-bed escape hatch and stays accurate with the cap removed. **(2) Room-type card** — add a third chip to the existing card at `BookingPage.tsx:2203-2283`: a per-type **Fits** (green) / **Tight** (amber, exactly at cap with 0 extra beds) / **Doesn't fit** (red/muted) badge that recomputes live against the current `(numAdults, numChildren)` and the cart's current room count. The card stays clickable in all three states — the indicator is a derived view, not a gating control. For multi-room, a small "You'd need 2 of [type]" callout fires when the total exceeds a single room's capacity. **(3) Submit gate** — the Step 1 → Step 2 "Next" button's `disabled` condition gains `!cartFitsGroup || !totalFits`; the helper text renders a single error "Room N ([type label]) maxes at [cap] children. Pick a different room type, add a second room, or remove a child." with a "Adjust room" CTA that scrolls to and highlights the offending card. The server's existing transaction validation (`requiredExtraBedsFor` + `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` inside `handleCreateBooking`) is the authoritative gate — the client-side submit gate is UX feedback, not security, per the standing rule in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md`. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx:2040-2090` (the children stepper + helper text, remove the cap + change the tail), `BookingPage.tsx:2203-2283` (the room-type card, add the capacity indicator chip), `shared/utils/roomTypes.ts` (new `deriveRoomTypeCapacityFit({ type, numAdults, numChildren, currentCartCount }) → { state, roomsNeeded, extraBedsNeeded }` helper — the single derivation point used by both the card and the submit gate). **Tests** — extend `shared/__tests__/room-types.test.ts` (new `deriveRoomTypeCapacityFit` cases); new `guest-app/tests/api/chd-11-soft-constraint-picker.test.ts` (source-text guards on `BookingPage.tsx`: the + button is no longer disabled when the count exceeds the cap; the dead-end tail is gone; the soft-cap text is present; the per-type capacity indicator renders all three states; the submit gate's `disabled` condition includes `!cartFitsGroup || !totalFits`; the error message renders with the room type label + cap + the "Adjust room" CTA; the existing `requiredExtraBedsFor` import is reused); re-verify `guest-app/tests/api/chd-children-occupancy.test.ts` (CHD-05 contract is preserved — server still validates, legacy `numGuests > maxCapacity` check does not return). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (new open "Children in Booking — Soft-Constraint Picker (CHD-11)" section under Phase 12), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #184` (this decision), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md` (new "Soft-Constraint Children Picker (CHD-11)" section after CHD-05). **Rejected** — keeping the picker cap and adding a "switch to family" CTA inside the warning (same dead-end, just dressed up); dropping the per-type cap from the model entirely (removes the constraint, not the wrong enforcement — `maxChildren: 0` on a Single is the operator's expression of the type's physical reality); moving the cap to the room quantity stepper instead of the children picker (same problem, different surface); auto-adding a second room when the picker crosses the single-type cap (surprises the user — the cart mutates under their hand, bad UX); implementing as a `useRoomTypeCapacityAdvisor` hook with a separate indicator component (over-engineered for 5 lines of math; re-evaluate if a second consumer like the corporate booking page or the admin New Booking modal needs the same indicator); adding a soft cap (e.g. 10) to the picker that also gates the indicator's "Fits" state (mixes physical capacity with UI ergonomics — the soft cap is a "stop the + at 100" guard, the indicator is the "this room can fit your group" answer). **Gates** — CHD-02 (the `maxChildren` field is still the per-type source of truth; CHD-11 only moves the *enforcement layer* on the client, the model + the server's transaction are unchanged), CHD-05 (the contract is preserved — server still validates `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` and the per-type cap; only the picker behaviour changes), CHD-10 (breakfast occupancy is independent of capacity and unaffected), EXB-01..10 (extra beds remain the existing escape hatch for overflow; CHD-11 stops blocking the picker from exploring the overflow path), MRB-15-10 (the admin surface for editing the per-type cap is the input side of this — CHD-11 only changes where the constraint is enforced on the client, the cap itself is still admin-editable via the Settings → Room Types editor that MRB-15-10 just unblocked). **Phase 2 (deferred)** — the "you'd need N of [type]" multi-room estimate on the card (a larger UX work item — ship the single-room indicator first, then iterate); an automatic "add a second room" CTA that pre-fills the cart with the right room type when the indicator flips to "Doesn't fit" + single-room (the cart always requires explicit confirmation — don't auto-mutate user state); a breakfast-occupancy-aware capacity indicator (children who are breakfast-included count as a different unit for breakfast cost but not for capacity — out of scope for CHD-11, the indicator uses physical occupancy only).

| 185 | **CHD-12 Cart-Style Summary (proposed 2026-08-03):** the "Guest distribution" block on `/book` Step 1 is replaced with a cart-style summary that lists one line per distinct room type, with the per-type occupancy inline. **Driving motivation** — operator-facing UX feedback on 2026-08-03 ("can we also instead of having a 'Guest distribution' or distributing guests per room, just put a summary on what's on the user's cart and the number of guests for each room?"). The current block lists one line per room — `Room 1 · Single Room · 1 adult · 2 children` — which has three problems: **(1) the label says "distribution" but it's an auto-rebalance** — the system assigns 1 adult to each room and fills the rest; the user didn't choose "Room 4 · Family Double · 1 adult", the algorithm did; **(2) the "Room 1 / Room 2 / Room 3 / Room 4" naming is positional and meaningless** — there's no way to tell which Single is "Room 1" vs "Room 2" in a cart with two of them; the type name is the real identifier; **(3) the per-room occupancy is the only actually useful info** — the rest is "you have N rooms of type T"; showing the cart contents and the per-type occupancy as one line per distinct type is denser and more scannable, especially for 3+ rooms. **The fix — one line per distinct room type, with the per-type occupancy inline:** "Your cart" header + one row per type, e.g. "1× Single Room · 1 adult · 2 children". (For the common case of 1 of each, the format can compress to "4 rooms · 6 adults · 2 children" plus a per-type chip strip — Phase 2 micro-improvement; the explicit per-type line is the primary form.) **Why this is better** — one line per distinct type (4 Single Rooms = 1 line, not 4); per-room occupancy inline ("1× Single Room · 1 adult · 2 children" tells you both the cart entry and the auto-rebalanced guest count in one read); the meaningless "Room N" naming goes away; scannable for 3+ rooms. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The auto-rebalance stays as the source of truth — `rebalanceGuestDistribution` in `guest-app/src/utils/bookingRoomCart.ts` is the function that computes the per-room occupancy. The change is purely the display layer: render `distributedRoomCart` as a per-type list grouped by `roomType`, summing `numAdults` + `numChildren` within each type. No new user input, no new state, no new validation, no new helper. **What this changes for CHD-11 (the soft-constraint picker)** — the cart summary is a natural home for the per-type **Fits / Tight / Doesn't fit** indicator from CHD-11. The current CHD-11 spec puts the indicator on the room-type card (the selector surface); with the cart summary, the same `deriveRoomTypeCapacityFit` derivation can also drive a small capacity chip on each cart line. Card-level indicator stays the primary surface (action); cart-line indicator is the secondary read (verification). Both derive from the same pure helper. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx:2089-2115` (replace the current "Guest distribution" block with the per-type grouped list — 5-10 lines of new code: a `Map<roomType, { quantity, adults, children }>` reduction over `distributedRoomCart`, rendered as one line per distinct type). **Tests** — new `guest-app/tests/api/chd-12-cart-style-summary.test.ts` (source-text guards on `BookingPage.tsx`: the "Guest distribution" heading is gone; the "Your cart" heading is present; the per-type grouping uses the type label, not a "Room N" index; the per-type occupancy is inline; cart of 0 rooms hides the section; cart of 1 room shows a single line; the CHD-11 capacity indicator is also rendered on the cart line when present). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (new open "Cart-Style Summary (CHD-12)" section, filed after CHD-11 in the Phase 12 open items), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #185` (this decision), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md §Cart-Style Summary (CHD-12)` (the spec above). **Rejected** — keeping the "Guest distribution" list and just renaming it to "Your rooms" (same dead-end naming, just relabeled); showing one line per room with the `roomId` instead of a positional index (leaks server-assigned identity into the draft surface — the room isn't assigned until booking creation); showing only the cart contents with no per-room occupancy (loses the "1 adult + 2 children" signal that's the whole reason the cart summary needs to exist as more than a numeric total); auto-collapsing identical room types into a single line with a "+N more" expansion (clever but adds a click — the explicit per-type line is more scannable for the common case); adding the CHD-11 capacity indicator only to the cart line and not the card (the card is the *action* surface, the cart is the *read* surface — the indicator belongs on both); making the cart summary per-room with manual occupancy assignment (a bigger UX work item — drag-and-drop adults/children across specific rooms — out of scope; ship the per-type auto-rebalance display first, then iterate). **Gates** — CHD-11 (the per-type Fits/Tight/Doesn't-fit indicator is the same helper, applied to both the card and the cart line — the changes are independent of each other but composable); CHD-05 (the auto-rebalance contract is preserved — server still validates `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` per child; only the display of the auto-rebalance result changes); MRB-06 (the per-room occupancy in the cart is the same shape the multi-room API expects; the change is at the display layer); the per-type vs per-room limitation of the cart summary is a known constraint (Phase 2) and matches the per-type (not per-room) shape of the EXB-11 extra-bed toggle spec — both defer per-room individuality to a future item.
| 186 | **EXB-11 User-Controlled Extra Bed Toggle Per Room Type (proposed 2026-08-03):** on `/book` Step 1, each room-type card carries a user-controlled "Extras" sub-section with a counter (0..maxExtraBeds) for extra beds, the per-bed-per-night rate, and the stay total — the user is in control of the extra-bed count, not the system. **Driving motivation** — operator-facing UX feedback on 2026-08-03 ("please also allow the user to be the one to choose where to request an extrabed, please add a toggle for each room type for extra bed and show the price"). The current flow auto-requires extra beds based on the overflow rule — if 3 adults pick a Single Room (max 1 adult, maxExtraBeds 1), the system requires 2 extra beds and silently adds them to the cart; there's no point where the user is asked "do you want an extra bed here?" or sees the per-bed-per-night price until the Step 3 review. Three problems: **(1) the user is in control of the cart, not the system** — if the user wants 2 extra beds (one in each of 2 Single Rooms), the auto-rebalance has to guess; the guess is usually right but it's still a guess; **(2) the price is hidden until Step 3** — the user can't see "this will cost ₱X extra" until the review screen; surprises on a money decision are bad UX; **(3) extra beds are a real product** — the operator has set a `maxExtraBeds` per type and an `extraBedRate` per bed per night; the guest should see this as an opt-in add-on, like breakfast, with the price visible at the point of decision. **The pattern — per-type counter (0..maxExtraBeds) with the rate and stay cost inline.** On each room-type card, add a small "Extras" sub-section below the room description and above the "Rooms" stepper: "Extra beds [−] 0 [+]   ₱500/bed/night / ₱0 for 2 nights". The control is a **counter (0..maxExtraBeds)** rather than a binary toggle because `maxExtraBeds` can be 1, 2, 3, or up to 5; the required overflow can be > 1 (3 adults in a Single = 2 extra beds required); a counter naturally subsumes the binary case (when `maxExtraBeds === 1`, the counter reads as on/off); the shape is consistent with the existing Adults / Children steppers (same `−` / counter / `+` UI affordance, same 44px touch target, same `aria-label` + `aria-live="polite"` pattern). **The soft-floor pattern — counter min is `max(0, requiredExtraBeds)`.** If the user's group has overflow that the room type's `maxExtraBeds` can't cover, the counter is clamped at the soft floor: the `[−]` is disabled at `max(0, requiredExtraBeds)`, the `[+]` is disabled at `maxExtraBeds`, and a soft-floor warning fires ("Room needs N extra beds to fit your group. You can add up to N here."). The cart summary (CHD-12) shows the per-type extra-bed count inline: "1× Single Room · 1 adult · 2 children · 2 extra beds". **What the toggle shows** — per-bed-per-night rate (`formatPrice(extraBedRate) + " / bed / night"`, pulled from the type's `extraBedRate` field per EXB-01); stay total (`formatPrice(extraBedRate * extraBedCount * numNights) + " for " + numNights + " nights"`, hidden when `extraBedCount === 0`); soft-floor warning when `requiredExtraBeds > 0` and the room still doesn't have enough capacity. **What the toggle does NOT do** — no auto-add of extra beds (the user is in control; if they need 2 extra beds and `maxExtraBeds === 1`, the system surfaces the constraint via the CHD-11 capacity indicator + soft-floor warning + submit-gate error and lets the user decide: pick a different room, add a second room, or remove a guest); no auto-remove (the cart mutates only on explicit user action). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `room.extraBedCount` is already a per-room field in the cart shape (`distributedRoomCart[i].extraBedCount`). The change: `extraBedCount` becomes a per-type value the user sets (0..maxExtraBeds), mirrored onto each room of that type in the cart; the auto-rebalance's `requiredExtraBeds` becomes a soft floor on the counter, not an auto-set. The `Booking.extraBedCount` server-side write is unchanged — the cart still writes the same per-room count. **What this changes for EXB-01..10** — contract preserved. `maxExtraBeds` is still the per-type cap (admin-editable, the MRB-15-10 fix made the admin surface work), `extraBedRate` is still the per-bed-per-night rate (server-snapshotted at create), the overflow rule still applies (server validates the count), Step 3 still shows the extra-bed add-on line. EXB-11 only changes the *client-side selection surface* — the user is now in control of the extra-bed count. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx:2270-2290` (the current "Rooms" stepper block on the room-type card — add an "Extras" sub-section above it with the counter + rate + stay total + soft-floor warning). The per-type `extraBedCount` is a new piece of state (or a new field on the existing `roomCart` shape) that mirrors onto every room of that type in `distributedRoomCart` before the auto-rebalance. **Tests** — new `guest-app/tests/api/exb-11-user-controlled-extra-bed-toggle.test.ts` (source-text guards on `BookingPage.tsx`: the "Extras" sub-section is present; the counter has `aria-label="Extra beds"` and `aria-live="polite"`; the per-bed-per-night rate is rendered inline; the stay total is hidden when `extraBedCount === 0`; the soft-floor warning fires when `requiredExtraBeds > extraBedCount`; the `[−]` is disabled at the soft floor; the `[+]` is disabled at `maxExtraBeds`; the entire "Extras" sub-section is hidden when `maxExtraBeds === 0`; the per-type count mirrors onto every room of that type in the cart — verify via a `distributedRoomCart` shape check); re-verify the existing EXB-01..10 tests in `guest-app/tests/api/exb-09-rate-snapshotting-and-breakfast-coupling.test.ts` (server still snapshots the rate, server still validates the count, server still rejects overflow above `maxExtraBeds`). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (new open "User-Controlled Extra Bed Toggle (EXB-11)" section, filed after CHD-12 in the Phase 12 open items), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #186` (this decision), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md §User-Controlled Extra Bed Toggle (EXB-11)` (the spec above). **Rejected** — binary toggle (0 or maxExtraBeds) — too coarse, `maxExtraBeds` can be > 1, and the user might want exactly 1 (not all-or-nothing); auto-add extra beds to cover the overflow — the current behavior; the user is asking to flip it; hide the price until Step 3 — the current behavior; the user is asking to surface it; a "Breakfast / Extra bed / Voucher" add-on bundle selector — combines too many unrelated choices into one widget, each add-on is its own opt-in with its own price; a separate "Extras" page/modal — over-engineered for a 1-line counter, the card-level sub-section is the right surface; show the per-bed-per-night rate only on hover — hidden prices are the problem this spec is solving; apply the toggle to the cart summary (CHD-12) instead of the room-type card — the card is the *action* surface, the cart is the *read* surface, the toggle belongs on the card. **Gates** — EXB-01..10 (the contract is preserved — `maxExtraBeds`, `extraBedRate`, the overflow rule, the server-snapshotted rate, the Step 3 add-on line all stay the same; EXB-11 only changes the client-side selection surface); CHD-11 (the per-type capacity indicator is the *constraint* surface; EXB-11 is the *extras* surface; they're independent and composable — a room type can be "Fits" without an extra bed and "Doesn't fit" with one); CHD-12 (the per-type cart summary mirrors the per-type extra-bed count inline; the change composes naturally — "1× Family Room · 2 adults · 2 children · 1 extra bed"); MRB-15-10 (the admin surface for editing `maxExtraBeds` and `extraBedRate` is the input side of this — EXB-11 only changes the client-side selection surface, the per-type caps are still admin-editable via the Settings → Room Types editor that MRB-15-10 unblocked). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in EXB-11)** — per-room individual extra-bed toggles (let the user add an extra bed to one Single but not the other; the cart summary would then show the breakdown by room; a real UX work item — ship per-type first, then iterate); a "you'd need N extra beds to fit your group" callout on the room-type card that mirrors the CHD-11 capacity indicator (same `deriveRoomTypeCapacityFit` helper can power this; out of scope for EXB-11); a "best fit" suggestion that recommends a specific room-type combination when the user's group can't fit any single room (a real recommendation engine; out of scope).

| 187 | **CHD-13 Children in Homepage Search Widget (proposed 2026-08-03; shipped 2026-08-04 on `feature/chd-13-children-in-search-widget`; v0.258.0 → v0.259.0):** the homepage availability checker's "Guests" field at `guest-app/src/pages/HomePage.tsx:194-228` becomes a popover with two steppers (Adults 1-10, Children 0-10) instead of a flat 1-6 `<select>`. The Search button navigates to `/book?checkIn=...&checkOut=...&guests=...&children=...` — the `/book` page already reads `searchParams.get("children")` at `BookingPage.tsx:211` (per CHD-05), so the URL contract is in place; the homepage widget just needs to send the new param. **Driving motivation** — operator-facing UX feedback on 2026-08-03 ("also, please add children in the homepage widget for searching"). The current flat `<select>` (1-6 guests, no children split) has three problems: **(1) the first contact is missing the children's split** — per CHD-05, every booking has an adults + children split, and the children's count is server-validated as part of the public creation transaction; the homepage widget's "2 guests" is ambiguous (2 adults? 1 adult + 1 child? 2 children?); the guest has to re-specify the split on `/book`; **(2) the current cap of 6 is artificially restrictive** — the 14-room hotel sees real "buy out the whole hotel" group bookings (8 adults + 4 children for a wedding party); the flat `<select>` caps at 6 total, forcing the group to abandon the homepage search and re-specify on `/book` — and the /book picker has no total cap, so they get a different shape on the second surface; **(3) the Search button URL contract already has the `children` param** — the `/book` page reads it at `BookingPage.tsx:211`; the homepage widget just needs to send the new param, no server-side work. **The fix — popover with two steppers (Adults + Children), matching the `/book` picker shape.** Replace the flat `<select>` (1-6 guests) with a small popover anchored to a "Guests" trigger; the popover has two rows ("Adults [−] N [+]" and "Children (0-11) [−] N [+]" + "Done" button). The trigger button shows the current split: "2 adults" (no children) or "2 adults, 1 child" (with children). When the popover is closed and the user reopens it, the steppers reflect the current state. The popover's `−` / `+` controls follow the same shape as the `/book` picker: **Adults** min 1 max 10 default 2 (matches the `/book` `guests` invariant of "at least one adult"); **Children** min 0 max 10 (matches the CHD-11 soft cap on the `/book` picker); **Total cap** removed (the 1-6 cap on the current `<select>` was a UI simplification, not a domain constraint — the `/book` picker has no total cap). The "Done" button closes the popover; click-outside-to-close is also enabled (the standard popover dismissal). The trigger itself is a `button` with `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded` for screen readers; the popover is a `role="dialog"` with `aria-labelledby` and a focus trap. **URL contract on Search** — `searchAvailability` at `HomePage.tsx:121-133` gains `children: String(children)` in the `URLSearchParams` constructor. The existing `guests` param (total) is updated to `String(adults + children)`. The `/book` page's URL reader at `BookingPage.tsx:205` (guests) and `BookingPage.tsx:211` (children) is unchanged; the adults count is derived as `Math.max(0, guests - children)` at `BookingPage.tsx:212`. **What this changes for the data model — nothing.** The popover is a UI control; the underlying state is two `useState<number>` hooks (`adults`, `children`). The URL is the only persistence path. No new schema, no new server-side field, no new validation. **What this changes for the related work** — CHD-05 contract preserved (server still validates `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` per child; the homepage widget just feeds the right `guests` + `children` pair into the URL so the `/book` page's pre-fill is correct); CHD-11 (the homepage widget uses the same soft cap of 10 for children as the `/book` picker; the same age range label "Children (0-11)" appears in both surfaces; the shapes match); CHD-12 + EXB-11 (unrelated — the cart summary and the extra-bed toggle are post-cart surfaces; the homepage widget is a pre-cart quick-search). The homepage widget does NOT include an extra-bed picker — extra beds are a cart-time decision (per EXB-01..10: server snapshots `extraBedRate` on creation); the homepage is a quick-search, not a full add-on picker; the `/book` page handles extra-bed selection. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/HomePage.tsx:194-228` (replace the flat `<select>` with the popover trigger + popover panel), `HomePage.tsx:121-133` (add `children: String(children)` to the `URLSearchParams`). The popover can reuse the existing `DateRangePicker` or any popover primitive in the codebase, or a small inline `useState<boolean>` for `open` + a `role="dialog"` panel. **Tests** — new `guest-app/tests/api/chd-13-children-in-search-widget.test.ts` (source-text guards on `HomePage.tsx`: the flat `<select>` (1-6 guests) is gone; replaced with a popover trigger button; popover trigger has `aria-haspopup="dialog"` and `aria-expanded`; popover contains two stepper rows "Adults" and "Children (0-11)"; the "Children (0-11)" stepper has `aria-label="Children count"` and `aria-live="polite"`; "Adults" stepper is min 1 max 10, "Children" stepper is min 0 max 10; trigger label is "N adults" when `children === 0`, "N adults, M child(ren)" when `children > 0`; "Done" button is present; `searchAvailability` includes `children: String(children)` in the URL params — verify via regex on the function body); re-verify `guest-app/tests/api/chd-children-occupancy.test.ts` (CHD-05 contract is preserved — URL contract on `/book` is unchanged). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (new open "Children in Homepage Search Widget (CHD-13)" section, filed after EXB-11 in the Phase 12 open items), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #187` (this decision), `plan/features/HOMEPAGE.md §Children in Search Widget (CHD-13)` (the spec above; the existing UI Checklist line at `HOMEPAGE.md:30` is updated to mark the children's picker as the new open item). **Rejected** — keep the flat `<select>`, just add a separate `<select>` for children next to it (adds two drop-downs in a row that's already tight on mobile; the popover is the more compact shape); use a single combined "Guests" stepper that decrements adults before children (hidden from the user but matches the soft-constraint "exploration-first" spirit — the split is too important to hide, adults and children have different rates + different age semantics, and the /book picker is split; hiding the split would create a different shape on the homepage than on /book); skip the children picker on the homepage — just send `children=0` and let /book re-prompt (the /book page already inherits children from the URL; passing `children=0` silently when the user actually has 2 children is a worse outcome than adding a 2-row popover; the whole point of the spec is to capture the children's count at the first surface); make the popover a 4-row widget (Adults, Children 0-5, Children 6-11, Teens 12+; the age range split is a real domain boundary per CHD-10, but the /book picker collapses to "Children (0-11)" only — a child of 12 is treated as an adult there; mirroring that shape on the homepage keeps the two surfaces consistent; a "Children (0-5) / (6-11) / (12+)" age-tier split is a future CHD-14 if the operator wants to differentiate pricing by age); show the children picker on the homepage but cap at 6 (matching the old total cap; defeats the point of the spec — the 1-6 cap is what's being removed). **Gates** — CHD-05 (the contract is preserved — server still validates `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` per child; the homepage widget just feeds the right `guests` + `children` pair into the URL); CHD-11 (the homepage widget uses the same soft cap of 10 for children as the `/book` picker; the same age range label "Children (0-11)" appears in both surfaces; the shapes match — a guest who picks 2 children on the homepage sees the same /book picker shape when they land); CHD-12 (unrelated — cart summary is a post-cart surface); EXB-11 (unrelated — extra-bed selection is a cart-time decision, not a quick-search decision; the `/book` page handles extra-bed selection; the homepage widget intentionally does NOT include an extra-bed picker to keep the quick-search shape simple).

| 188 | **MRB-15-11 Room Type Photo Gallery — Atomic Photo Append / Remove / Reorder (proposed 2026-08-04):** the three photo-gallery functions in `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:4978-5034` (`uploadRoomTypePhoto`, `removeRoomTypePhoto`, `reorderRoomTypePhotos`) wrap their read-modify-write in `runTransaction` so the multi-photo upload race on the admin room-type photo gallery is eliminated. **Driving motivation** — operator-facing UX bug reported 2026-08-04: "in the admin settings, upload photos for room types, when I select more than 1 image for upload, it only uploads 1 image at a time and replaces the previously uploaded image. Instead of uploading for example 5, I only get the last or the 5th image selected." **Root cause** — the pre-MRB-15-11 functions do a read-modify-write on the in-memory `roomTypes` state: `uploadRoomTypePhoto` reads `type.imageUrls` from the React-side cache of the Firestore snapshot, appends the new URL, then calls `updateRoomType` → `saveRoomTypes(updated)` → `updateSettings("hotelConfig", { roomTypes: newTypes, ... })` (a full-document write of the entire `roomTypes` array). The SettingsPage handler at `admin-app/src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx:5566-5599` `for`-loops and `await`s each call so the calls are sequential — but the in-memory `roomTypes` is only updated when the Firestore subscription fires locally. Between the N sequential `await uploadRoomTypePhoto` calls, the subscription may not have fired yet, so the (N+1)th call reads **stale** in-memory state (still the pre-first-upload value) and overwrites the array with a single-element array `[url(N+1)]`. 5 uploads → 5 overwrites of a single-element array → only the last URL survives. The same race applies to `removeRoomTypePhoto` (a `filter` + write on stale in-memory state) and to a lesser extent to `reorderRoomTypePhotos` (a full-array write of the new order — single-user action, low race surface, but still on stale in-memory state). **Fix** — wrap the read-modify-write in `runTransaction` for all three functions. The transaction reads `tx.get(hotelConfigRef)` (Firestore, not the in-memory cache), does the `findIndex` + cap check + append / filter / reorder, then `tx.update(hotelConfigRef, { roomTypes: newRoomTypes, updatedAt: serverTimestamp() })`. Firestore's transaction guarantees serialized execution — every transaction sees the latest committed state, so the (N+1)th append sees the (N)th's write. The Storage upload stays OUTSIDE the transaction (Storage ops can't be in a Firestore transaction); the Storage `deleteObject` for cap-reject cleanup stays best-effort (`.catch(() => undefined)`). The `MAX_ROOM_TYPE_PHOTOS` cap check moves INSIDE the transaction (atomic with the write — a parallel admin's append can't slip past the cap). The client-side `if (type.imageUrls.length >= MAX_ROOM_TYPE_PHOTOS)` check at `SettingsPage.tsx:5548` becomes a UX hint only (disable the upload button); the server-side transaction is the source of truth. The `updateRoomType` helper at `AdminContext.tsx:4930-4953` is unchanged (the photo functions bypass it — they write the whole `roomTypes` array inside the transaction, not the per-type patch shape the helper supports). The Edit-form callers (label, price, capacity, etc.) keep using `updateRoomType` as before; no data-model or helper-surface change. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `roomTypes[i].imageUrls: string[]` stays; the array is still written as a whole in the same document; no new sub-collection, no new field. The MRB-15-10 hydration contract (every `RoomTypeEntry` field preserved) is unaffected — `imageUrls` was always in the preserved set. The Storage path shape (`room-types/{typeValue}/{timestamp}-{filename}`) is unchanged. The toast UX (`SettingsPage.tsx:5571-5573` "Some photos skipped" when `files.length > remaining` + `SettingsPage.tsx:5594-5597` "Photos added: N uploaded" / "Upload failed: No photos could be uploaded") is unchanged — the per-file error handling inside the loop is still surfaced. **Implementation** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:4978-5034` (the three photo-gallery functions; each rewrites to use `runTransaction`; the `updateRoomType` + `saveRoomTypes` call chain goes away in those three functions; the `useEffect` race-guard at `AdminContext.tsx:1947-1953` stays unchanged). **Tests** — new `admin-app/src/__tests__/mrb-15-11-photo-gallery-atomic-mutations.test.ts` (source-text guards per `plan/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md §Testing`: each photo function calls `runTransaction`; the transaction body reads `tx.get(hotelConfigRef)`; the cap check is inside the transaction; the write shape is `tx.update(hotelConfigRef, { roomTypes: newRoomTypes, updatedAt: serverTimestamp() })`; the pre-MRB-15-11 read-modify-write pattern `const next = [...type.imageUrls, url]` is gone from the photo functions; the `updateRoomType` helper is unchanged). The behavioural emulator round-trip (5 photos uploaded in a batch, gallery shows all 5) is deferred to the local environment that has the Java emulator (mirrors the MRB-11 / MRB-14 / MRB-15-08 precedent). **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #188` (this decision), `plan/features/SETTINGS.md §Room Type Photo Gallery — Atomic Array Mutations (MRB-15-11)` (the spec above), `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (new "Firestore" entry: "any read-modify-write against a Firestore document where the read can race with a parallel write MUST be wrapped in `runTransaction` — never read from the in-memory React cache and write the full document, because the in-memory state lags behind the subscription and the (N+1)th iteration can clobber the (N)th's commit. The cap check goes INSIDE the transaction so a parallel append can't slip past it."). **Rejected** — `FieldValue.arrayUnion(url)` on a nested field path `roomTypes.{idx}.imageUrls` (the arrayUnion primitive is atomic on its own field, but the `idx` has to be resolved against the live document — solving the index-stability problem pushes the code back to the read-modify-write shape; `runTransaction` is cleaner because it handles the index lookup + append + write in one atomic step); a separate `photoSlots: number[]` field for pre-allocation (adds a new field + a new invariant + a new write path; the data-model is unchanged here on purpose); a sub-collection per photo (`roomTypes/{type}/photos/{photoId}`, cleanest from a NoSQL perspective but changes the public surface — `getRoomTypeImages` is consumed by both admin and guest apps; out of scope for a "fix the race" PR); disabling the multi-select on the file input (solves the symptom, breaks the workflow — the operator's intent is the right intent, the implementation just needs to honor it); per-file progress + lock the gallery while uploading (solves a different problem — perceived responsiveness, not correctness); moving the photo operations to a Cloud Function (the function still does the same read-modify-write against Firestore and needs the same `runTransaction`; the transaction shape is the right fix regardless of where the code runs). **Gates** — MRB-15-10 (the hydration contract is unchanged; the photo gallery is a write path, not a read path), MRB-15-09 (the staff-gated listener pattern is unrelated; the photo functions are operator-initiated writes, not subscription handlers), `saveRoomTypes` (unchanged contract; the photo functions stop calling it but the helper stays for the Edit-form callers), the `useEffect` at `AdminContext.tsx:1947-1953` (unchanged; the local `photoTarget` re-sync from the live snapshot still works because the transaction's commit fires the subscription, which re-syncs the in-memory state). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in MRB-15-11)** — sub-collection per photo (the right long-term shape, but a refactor of every read site; filed as a future `MRB-15-11b`); per-photo metadata (caption, alt text, focal point) — needs the sub-collection shape first; bulk re-tagging — same prerequisite; explicit "set as hero" drag-to-front interaction (currently the hero is implicit — "the first photo in the array").

| 189 | **EXB-11.1 Bottom-of-Card Placement + Checkbox for `maxExtraBeds === 1` (proposed 2026-08-04; shipped 2026-08-04 on `feature/exb-11-1-bottom-of-card-and-checkbox`; v0.257.1 → v0.258.0):** two refinements to the EXB-11 "Extras" sub-section on `/book` Step 1 — **(1) move the sub-section to the bottom of the card** (after the rate options + mixed-rates panel; currently sits between the CHD-11 capacity chip and the "Rooms" stepper) and **(2) render a binary checkbox instead of a counter when `maxExtraBeds === 1`** (the `[−] 0 [+]` counter is the wrong shape for a yes/no decision; the user noticed the wrong fit). **Driving motivation** — operator-facing UX feedback on 2026-08-04 ("can we move the extra bed options on the bottom most of the card type? then if we set only 1 available extra bed on the room, instead of showing - and +, can we make it a checkbox instead?"). **Root cause (Part 1: placement)** — the EXB-11 spec placed the "Extras" sub-section between the CHD-11 capacity chip and the "Rooms" stepper, which forces the user to make the extras decision *before* they've chosen how many rooms or which rate. The extras counter is an opt-in add-on (same shape as the breakfast add-on, which is in the rate-option layer) and belongs with the other add-on choices, grouped at the bottom of the card. **Root cause (Part 2: counter for a binary choice)** — the EXB-11 spec rendered `[−] 0 [+]` for any `maxExtraBeds >= 1`. When `maxExtraBeds === 1`, the counter has 2 meaningful states (0 or 1) but 3 affordances (decrement / display / increment). The EXB-11 spec's own "Rejected alternatives" entry "Binary toggle (0 or maxExtraBeds) — too coarse" was framed for the *general* case where `maxExtraBeds` can be > 1; the *specific* case where `maxExtraBeds === 1` is precisely where a binary toggle is the right shape. The counter is a "wrong fit" UX that the user noticed. **Fix (Part 1: placement)** — move the existing "Extras" IIFE from `BookingPage.tsx:2456-2565` (right after the CHD-11 capacity chip IIFE) to right after the mixed-rates panel (currently at `BookingPage.tsx:2620-2644`). Same body, different parent in the JSX tree. The `data-testid` markers stay the same (no test churn for the move alone). Discoverability: the existing "Up to N extra beds" line in the amenities row at `BookingPage.tsx:2348-2353` is the visual cue at the top of the card; the actual toggle lives at the bottom (where the action is). The `aria-label="${type.label} extras"` on the wrapper section means screen readers still land on the toggle when the user tabs through. **Fix (Part 2: checkbox when `maxExtraBeds === 1`)** — add a branch in the Extras IIFE: if `typeMaxExtraBeds === 1`, render a `<input type="checkbox" data-testid={`extras-checkbox-${type.value}`} />` + `<label for={`extras-checkbox-${type.value}`}>` containing "Add an extra bed" + the per-night price inline. The `[−] 0 [+]` counter is gone for this case (the `data-testid={`extras-count-${type.value}`}` testid is also gone — the checkbox replaces it as the testable surface). The stay total + soft-floor warning render the same way as the counter case (the data is 0 or 1 either way; the `extras-stay-total-${type.value}` + `extras-soft-floor-warning-${type.value}` testids are shared). The underlying `room.extraBedCount: number` stays unchanged (0 or 1 for the checkbox case; 0..maxExtraBeds for the counter case). `rebalanceGuestDistribution` clamping unchanged. `updateExtraBedCount` helper unchanged. Cart URL serialization (`rooms=`) unchanged. Step 2/3 aside aggregation unchanged. Server-side create transaction unchanged. **Soft-floor enforcement for the checkbox case (3 states)** — (a) `softFloor === 0` (no overflow): checkbox `unchecked`, `enabled`; (b) `softFloor === 1` (overflow exactly matches the cap; the user MUST have the extra bed): checkbox `checked`, `disabled` (with `aria-describedby="extras-soft-floor-warning-${type.value}"` pointing at the warning text); (c) `overCap` (`softFloor > maxExtraBeds`, e.g. 3 adults in a Single with `maxExtraBeds 1` → soft floor 2, cap 1): checkbox `checked`, `disabled`, soft-floor warning text renders below ("This room needs N extra beds to fit your group. You can add up to N here."). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The `room.extraBedCount: number` field, the per-type mirror via `updateExtraBedCount`, the soft-floor derived from `requiredExtraBedsFor`, the per-cart aggregation, the cart URL serialization, the per-type cart summary pill — all unchanged. The CHD-12 cart summary's per-type "N extra beds" inline pill is unchanged (the data is the same, the surface just renders it from the same cart field). The CHD-11 capacity chip is at the top of the card (unchanged). The Step 2/3 aside extra-bed pill is unchanged. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx:2429-2565` (the Extras IIFE; the IIFE moves ~30 lines down in the JSX tree, then the body splits into two branches: `typeMaxExtraBeds === 0` returns null (hidden, EXB-11); `typeMaxExtraBeds === 1` renders the checkbox + label + per-night price + stay total + soft-floor warning; `typeMaxExtraBeds >= 2` renders the existing counter + per-night price + stay total + soft-floor warning). The two branches share the helper text for `typeQuantity === 0` ("Add at least one room to set extra beds") and the soft-floor warning text. The `updateExtraBedCount` helper at `BookingPage.tsx:957-971` is unchanged. **Tests** — extend `guest-app/tests/api/exb-11-user-controlled-extra-bed-toggle.test.ts` (source-text guards per `plan/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md §Testing`: the Extras IIFE appears AFTER the mixed-rates panel + rate options in the JSX tree, not between the CHD-11 chip and the Rooms stepper; the checkbox branch contains `<input type="checkbox"` + `data-testid={`extras-checkbox-${type.value}`}` + a `<label for={`extras-checkbox-${type.value}`}>` containing "Add an extra bed" + the per-night price; the checkbox branch does NOT contain `data-testid={`extras-count-${type.value}`}`; the switch is `if (typeMaxExtraBeds === 1)` (or `=== 0` then `=== 1` then `>= 2`); the soft-floor enforcement sets `disabled` + `defaultChecked` from the soft-floor; the stay total + soft-floor warning render the same in both branches; the existing 16 EXB-11 source-text tests still pass for the `maxExtraBeds >= 2` branch). The behavioural emulator test (the user toggles the checkbox / counter on a real room type, sees the per-night price + stay total update) is out of scope for this sandbox. **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ⬜ open, filed after EXB-11 + before CHD-13 in the "Open CHD Tasks (CHD-11)" section), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #189` (this decision), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md §EXB-11.1 — Bottom-of-Card Placement + Checkbox for `maxExtraBeds === 1`` (the spec above). **Rejected** — always render the counter, never the checkbox (the EXB-11 default; wrong shape for the binary case; the counter for `maxExtraBeds === 1` is a 3-affordance UI for a 2-state decision); always render the checkbox, even for `maxExtraBeds > 1` (wrong shape for the N-ary case; per EXB-11's rejection of the binary toggle, the user might want exactly 1 for a 2+ room type, and the checkbox forces 0/1); a dropdown with 0/1/2/3 options for `maxExtraBeds <= 3` (the counter is a more direct control — one click vs. dropdown-open + option-select); a radio group (0 / 1) for `maxExtraBeds === 1` (radio is for "pick one of N where N >= 3"; checkbox is the right shape for yes/no — radio requires arrow-key navigation between options, checkbox is a single tap); keep Extras at the top of the card (the placement reasoning in the spec; discoverability is handled by the amenities row, the action is at the bottom); a combined "Add-ons" section bundling Extras + Breakfast + Voucher (out of scope — same reason as EXB-11's rejection; the rate option is the natural breakfast surface, Extras is a per-room-type opt-in independent of rate choice, Voucher is a Step 3 discount; three separate surfaces); hide the checkbox when `typeQuantity === 0` (vs. render-but-disabled — same trade-off as the EXB-11 "render counter but disable both buttons" choice; the render-but-disabled form keeps the section in the layout (no CLS when the user adds a room) and surfaces the helper text immediately). **Gates** — EXB-11 (the underlying contract is unchanged; only the placement + render branch for the `maxExtraBeds === 1` case change), EXB-01..10 (server-side contract is unchanged; the cart writes the same per-room `extraBedCount`; the server still validates against `maxExtraBeds` and snapshots `extraBedRate` onto the booking doc; the Step 3 add-on line is unchanged), CHD-11 (the capacity chip is at the top of the card and is unchanged; the Extras move doesn't touch the chip), CHD-12 (the per-type cart summary's "N extra beds" inline pill is unchanged — the data is the same, the surface just renders it from the same cart field), MRB-15-10 (the admin surface for editing `maxExtraBeds` and `extraBedRate` is the input side; EXB-11.1 only changes the *client-side selection surface* — placement + checkbox branch), MRB-15-11 (unrelated; photo gallery, not extras toggle; the two specs are independent). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in EXB-11.1)** — per-room individual extra-bed toggles (let the user add an extra bed to one Single but not the other; carried over from EXB-11's Phase 2 list); a "you'd need N extra beds to fit your group" callout on the room-type card (the per-type callout that mirrors the CHD-11 capacity indicator; carried over from EXB-11's Phase 2 list); a "best fit" suggestion (recommend a specific room-type combination when the user's group can't fit any single room; carried over from EXB-11's Phase 2 list).


| 190 | **EXB-11.2 Auto-Initiate Extra Bed Count to Soft Floor (proposed 2026-08-04; shipped 2026-08-04 on `feature/exb-11-2-auto-init-extra-bed`; v0.259.0 → v0.259.1):** the Extras IIFE on `/book` Step 1 auto-syncs the cart's per-type `extraBedCount` to the soft floor (the per-room overflow derived from `requiredExtraBedsFor`) whenever the soft floor exceeds the cart value, so the user is never stuck in a state where the system requires N extra beds but the UI is unchecked + disabled. **Driving motivation** — operator-reported UX bug 2026-08-04 (post-EXB-11.1): "for a single room that can only have 1 max person but can add 1 extra bed, when I put 2 guests, it doesn't allow me to add an extra bed." Reproduction: 1 Single Room (`maxCapacity: 1`, `maxExtraBeds: 1`), 2 guests. `requiredExtraBedsFor` returns `requiredExtraBeds: 1` (1 overflow adult). Soft floor = 1, cart has 0. The EXB-11.1 checkbox renders `checked = (userExtraBeds === 1) = false` and `disabled = (userExtraBeds === 0 && softFloor >= 1) = true` — an **unchecked + disabled** checkbox. The user can't click to enable (disabled), and the cart is silently at 0. **Root cause** — the EXB-11.1 spec explicitly rejected auto-init ("the CHD-11 submit gate catches the over-cap; simpler than the auto-init path"), but the consequence is the visual and the cart get out of sync when the soft floor exceeds the cart value. The spec's "checked + disabled" affordance (per EXB-11.1's 3-state soft-floor model: `softFloor === 0` → unchecked enabled, `softFloor === 1` → checked disabled, `overCap` → checked disabled + warning) requires `checked = true`, which requires `userExtraBeds >= softFloor`, which the user must reach via the UI. The current disabled rule blocks the user from reaching it. **The counter case has the same shape (less visible)** — Family Room with 5 adults (`maxCapacity: 4`, `maxExtraBeds: 2`), soft floor 1, cart 0. Counter shows 0, `[−]` disabled (because `0 <= 1`), `[+]` enabled. User CAN click `[+]` to add — but the visual still shows 0 when the system requires 1, and the `[−]` disabled condition fires too early (below the floor, not at it). **Fix — two-part change to `BookingPage.tsx`.** **(1) Component-level `useEffect` auto-init.** A `useEffect` (dependency array `[numAdults, numChildren, roomCart, roomTypes]`) loops over the cart's `roomCart` and, for each type, calls `updateExtraBedCount(type.value, softFloor, typeMaxExtraBeds)` when `softFloor > userExtraBeds && typeQuantity > 0`. The `updateExtraBedCount` helper at `BookingPage.tsx:957-971` is unchanged — its `safeCount = Math.min(Math.max(nextCount, 0), maxCount)` clamp at line 958 already handles the over-cap case (soft floor clamped to cap). The effect re-fires when the guest count, the cart, or the room types change; after the first fire, the cart has the soft-floor value and the next run is a no-op (no infinite loop). **(2) Derived display value.** Inside the Extras IIFE, compute `const displayExtraBeds = Math.max(softFloor, userExtraBeds)`. Use `displayExtraBeds` (not `userExtraBeds`) for the visual: the checkbox `checked = displayExtraBeds === 1`, the counter shows `displayExtraBeds`, the `[−]` is disabled when `displayExtraBeds <= softFloor`, the `[+]` is disabled when `displayExtraBeds >= typeMaxExtraBeds`. This prevents the brief "0 → 1" flash on first render (the useEffect fires AFTER the first render, but the derived value is correct on the first render). The checkbox's `disabled` condition changes from `(userExtraBeds === 0 && softFloor >= 1)` (the "user hasn't enabled it" rule, which blocks the user) to `softFloor >= 1` (the "forced on" rule, per the EXB-11.1 spec's 3-state model). **The auto-init is silent** — no toast, no "we added an extra bed for you" helper text. The `checked + disabled` checkbox + the existing soft-floor warning text (`aria-describedby` for the checkbox, visible text for the counter's over-cap case) are enough signal. A notification would be patronizing. **What the user sees now** — Single Room, 2 guests: checkbox `checked + disabled` (matches the spec). Single Room, 1 guest: checkbox `unchecked + enabled`. Family Room, 5 adults: counter shows 1, `[−]` disabled (at the floor), `[+]` enabled (can add up to 2). Family Room, 4 adults: counter shows 0, both buttons enabled. Family Room, 6 adults: soft floor 2, cart auto-inits to 2 (clamped to cap), counter shows 2, both buttons disabled, over-cap warning fires. **Why the EXB-11.1 "no auto-init" stance was wrong** — (1) the visual can't be honest without auto-init. The EXB-11.1 spec says the soft-floor state for the checkbox case is "checked + disabled" — but `checked` reads from the cart, and the cart is at 0. The only ways to make the visual match the spec are (a) auto-init the cart, or (b) decouple the visual from the cart via a derived value (creates a display/cart mismatch the user can see in the per-type cart pill). Auto-init is cleaner. (2) The submit gate catches the over-cap, but the UX is broken in the meantime. The user has to (a) notice the submit-gate error, (b) read the error message, (c) understand the extra-bed requirement, (d) navigate back, (e) try again. Auto-init makes the correct state the DEFAULT state. **The new stance** — **auto-init enforces the floor; the user is still in control of the cap.** The system says "you need at least N extra beds for your group; you can have up to M." The user can decrement back to 0 if the soft floor drops (e.g. they reduce the guest count from 2 to 1), and the useEffect re-fires to re-init only if the soft floor is still > 0. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `room.extraBedCount: number` stays. `updateExtraBedCount` helper is unchanged. Cart URL serialization (`rooms=`) is unchanged. Per-type cart summary pill is unchanged (the data is the same shape; the initial value is the soft floor instead of 0). Step 2/3 aside is unchanged. Server-side validation is unchanged. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (one `useEffect` at the component level before the `return` + a derived `displayExtraBeds` value in the Extras IIFE + the checkbox disabled condition change from `(userExtraBeds === 0 && softFloor >= 1)` to `softFloor >= 1`). ~30 lines changed. The comment block at the top of the Extras IIFE (lines 2543-2561) gets updated to note the EXB-11.2 reversal of the EXB-11.1 "no auto-init" stance. **Tests** — extend `guest-app/tests/api/exb-11-user-controlled-extra-bed-toggle.test.ts` (~6 new source-text tests for the auto-init + the derived value + the disabled-rule change). Existing 16 EXB-11 + 10 EXB-11.1 tests stay green. The behavioural test (the user adds a room + changes the guest count, the counter auto-inits to the soft floor) is out of scope for this sandbox. **MDs to update** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ⬜ open), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #190` (this decision), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md §EXB-11.2 — Auto-Initiate Extra Bed Count to Soft Floor` (the spec above). **Rejected** — derived value only, no useEffect (display/cart mismatch the user can see in the per-type cart pill in CHD-12); auto-init only in `updateRoomQuantity` when the user adds a room (doesn't cover the guest-count-changes case; the soft floor is dynamic, not just a one-time thing); toast notification on first auto-init (patronizing; the `checked + disabled` checkbox + soft-floor warning are enough signal); "this is required" badge on the toggle (same UX problem as the original bug — the user has to click to enable, the click is blocked by `disabled`; a badge is a label, not a fix); auto-decrement on floor drop (mirror the auto-init for the cart-exceeds-floor case; out of scope — the user can manually decrement). **Gates** — EXB-11 + EXB-11.1 (the underlying contract is unchanged; auto-init is a constrained `useEffect` that only fires when the soft floor exceeds the cart value, and only when the user has at least one room of the type; the existing 16 + 10 source-text tests stay green), EXB-01..10 (server-side contract is unchanged; the cart writes the same per-room `extraBedCount`; the server still validates against `maxExtraBeds` and snapshots `extraBedRate`), CHD-11 (the capacity chip is at the top of the card and is unchanged; the Extras auto-init doesn't touch the chip), CHD-12 (the per-type cart summary's "N extra beds" inline pill now reflects the auto-init value — the data is the same shape, just the initial value is the soft floor instead of 0), MRB-15-10 (the admin surface for editing `maxExtraBeds` and `extraBedRate` is the input side;
