The staff had to click into the Folio section to see the proof status. **Root cause** — `bookings/{id}.paymentConfirmedAt` is the durable signal: stamped by `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` (line 7700) and `handleMarkPaymentConfirmed` (line 7507) and `handleConfirmBookingWithBalance` (line 8130) on the full-payment transition, and NEVER cleared by any other lifecycle handler. `status === "payment-confirmed"` is a transient state — it only says "right now" — and the lifecycle can move past it without erasing the verification history. The pre-FOL-01 UI read the transient state, not the durable signal. The `onsitePayments[]` ledger entries don't carry a "verified" flag — they're just money-movement rows; the `paymentConfirmedAt` timestamp is the only durable signal for the verification event. **Fix — five-part change.** **(1) New shared helper `isPaymentVerified(booking)` in `shared/utils/paymentVerification.ts`.** Pure function, returns `true` when `status === "payment-confirmed"` (transient) OR `paymentConfirmedAt` is set (durable). Accepts both ISO-string (admin mapper shape) and `Date` (guest lookup shape) for the timestamp. Defensive against null / undefined / empty-string / whitespace / invalid-Date inputs. **(2) New shared `Booking.paymentConfirmedAt` field** (`Date | null`) declared on the shared type. The field is already on the Firestore doc; the type just doesn't expose it. Mirrors the existing `paymentRejectionReason` / `paymentRejectedAt` / `paymentRejectedBy` cluster. **(3) Admin `Booking` extension declares the same field as `string | null` (the admin's Date → ISO-string convention).** The bookings mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:1362` hydrates it via `parseDateTimeString(data.paymentConfirmedAt)`. The pre-FOL-01 mapper silently dropped the field on every snapshot echo — the same shape of bug as MRB-15-10 (the field-drop on read). **(4) Wire the helper into both render sites in `BookingsPage.tsx`.** The Folio block's outer ternary widens to `isPaymentVerified(selectedBooking) || selectedBooking.status === "confirmed" || selectedBooking.paymentRejectionReason`; the inner ternary replaces `status === "payment-confirmed"` with `isPaymentVerified(selectedBooking)`. The Overview's "Pending" badge adds a `!isPaymentVerified(selectedBooking)` guard (so the "Pending" + "Verified" badges are mutually exclusive); the "Verified" badge condition switches from the transient status check to the helper. Both render sites are now driven by the same single source of truth. **(5) Export the helper from `shared/index.ts`** so the admin app imports it through `@spark-inn/shared` (matches the existing `getLatestPaymentReference` import pattern). **What the user sees after the change** — a `confirmed` booking whose payment was verified earlier now reads as "Verified" in the Folio (was "Pending") AND shows the green "Verified" badge in the Overview (was no badge at all). A `payment-confirmed` booking still reads as "Verified" (the helper's transient axis is unchanged for this case). A `payment-uploaded` booking still reads as "Pending" (no verification yet). A `cancelled` booking with no verification still reads as nothing (no badge, no folio block). A legacy `confirmed` booking that was confirmed via the legacy `handleConfirmBooking` shortcut (no `paymentConfirmedAt`, status went `payment-uploaded` → `confirmed` directly) still reads as "Pending" (no verification happened). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `paymentConfirmedAt` is already on the Firestore doc; the shared type now exposes it; the admin mapper now hydrates it. The server is untouched. **What this changes for the email + notification side** — nothing. The `payment-confirmed` email + the `payment` notification are still fired by `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` and the related handlers; the helper is read-only. **Rejected** — keep the transient `status === "payment-confirmed"` check but also accept `status === "confirmed"` in the inner ternary (special-cases the confirmed case but doesn't survive further lifecycle moves like `checked-in` / `checked-out`; also doesn't generalize to a future "verified post-confirmation" workflow); add a separate `paymentStatus` field on the booking doc that mirrors the current `status` axis (redundant with `status`; the durable signal is the timestamp, not a derived status); surface a "Payment verified at <timestamp>" subline in the Folio (more information than needed; the "Verified" label is the staff's signal that the lifecycle has the right state; the timestamp is on the audit trail); add a server-side `paymentVerified: boolean` field that the lifecycle handlers explicitly flip (more server-side coupling; the timestamp is already a durable signal, no need to add a second one); hydrate `paymentConfirmedAt` in the guest-app `Booking` mapper too (the helper accepts both shapes; the guest lookup doesn't render the admin "Verified" badge so the field is not read on the guest side yet — can be added when needed); bump the bookings mapper to also expose `paymentRejectedAt` and `paymentRejectedBy` (orthogonal fields; the pre-FOL-01 mapper's `paymentRejectionReason` hydration is also missing, but the folio + overview only read `paymentRejectionReason` for the "Rejected" badge, which works through the existing snapshot pipeline; widening the mapper hydration is a separate concern, not blocking the verified fix); pre-pend the inner ternary with `isPaymentVerified(...) || (status === "confirmed" && (data.paymentConfirmedAt !== undefined))` (a hack that the helper already cleans up; the helper is the cleaner abstraction); wire the helper into the Bookings table status pill (line 1978) so a `confirmed` row's status reads "Verified" not "Confirmed" (the table's status pill is the booking lifecycle, not the payment lifecycle — different axes, leave the table alone). **Gates** — PRC-13 (the verify-and-record-payment modal's success-modal `isFullPayment` math is unchanged; the helper is a UI-side read), MRB-12 (the reservation-header `paymentStatus` mirror is unchanged; the helper is per-child, not reservation-aggregate; the existing reservation-row `paymentStatus` reader at line 1978 is the reservation-level equivalent), CWB (the confirm-with-balance path stamps `paymentConfirmedAt` too — the helper's transient axis is `payment-confirmed`, not `confirmed`, so the CWB-confirmed booking reads as "Verified" via the durable-timestamp axis, not the transient; no double-counting), `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` (the server-side writer is unchanged; the `paymentConfirmedAt` field is the same `Date` it always wrote), the existing `paymentRejectionReason`-based "Rejected" badge (still reads from the same field; the rejection path is a different axis, unaffected by the verified helper). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-01)** — hydrate `paymentRejectedAt` + `paymentRejectedBy` in the admin mapper (so the rejected-timestamp surfaces in the audit drawer; not blocking the verified fix); wire the helper into the DrawerWorkspaceHeader's "Reference" fallback (the current "Pending verification" italic text is a different concern — about the reference number, not the verification state; can be updated in a follow-up); add a per-payment-ledger "verified at" timestamp on each row in the folio's payment list (the booking-level `paymentConfirmedAt` is the only verified signal today; per-row verified-at is a future audit refinement); surface the verified-state distinction in the Bookings table status pill (different axis, different scope).

| 198 | **FOL-02 Admin Bookings Mapper Missing-Field Hydration (off-roadmap bug fix) (proposed 2026-08-07; shipped 2026-08-07 on `fix/fol-02-admin-bookings-mapper-missing-reservation-cluster`; v0.264.10 → v0.264.11):** the admin `bookings` hydration `useEffect` at `AdminContext.tsx:1335-1448` now reads every field the `Booking` contract guarantees from the snapshot. The pre-FOL-02 mapper silently dropped 8 contract fields — `reservationId` / `reservationRef` / `reservationPosition` / `reservationRoomCount` (the MRB-01 reservation cluster), `onsitePayments` (the denormalized payment ledger array), and `paymentRejectionReason` / `paymentRejectedAt` / `paymentRejectedBy` (the rejection audit cluster) — and the visible consequences were concrete. **Driving motivation** — operator-reported 2026-08-07 (post-FOL-01 review, with screenshot): "The gcash payment is already verified in the Overview tab but in the Folio Tab it is still asking me to collect the amount and no payment is recorded. Also, the header says payment method is GCash but the reference still says 'Pending verification'. Can you wire everything / streamline?" After FOL-01 fixed the Overview + Folio "Verified" badge, the staff saw a new desync in the same drawer: the booking was `confirmed` + `paymentConfirmedAt` was set + the verified payment was in the canonical subcollection, but the Folio's "Payment history" was empty, "PAID ₱0", the "Collect ₱3,900" button was still on screen, and the header's "Reference" line said "Pending verification." The data was correct; the admin's read layer was lossy. **Three related bugs from the same root cause — the admin mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:1335-1448` silently dropped 8 contract fields on every snapshot echo** (the same shape of bug as FOL-01 / MRB-15-10 — the type declares a field, the mapper forgets to read it from the snapshot). **(1) `reservationId` (and the rest of the MRB-01 cluster)** — the booking drawer's payments listener at `BookingsPage.tsx:1164-1224` gates on `selectedBooking.reservationId` to decide whether to subscribe to the post-MRB-01 canonical `reservations/{id}/payments/` path or the legacy `bookings/{id}/payments/` path. With the field always `null` in React state (mapper dropped it), every booking fell through to the legacy path — so a verified payment written to the canonical subcollection was invisible to the Folio's "Payment history", the `paymentsTotal` was `0`, the `balance` was `grandTotal`, and the "Collect <balance>" CTA stayed on screen even after the staff verified the full amount. **(2) `onsitePayments`** — the header's "Reference" line and the Folio's proof card "gcash · <reference>" line read `getLatestPaymentReference(booking)` which returned `null` for new bookings because the denormalized array is empty for new payments (the server's verify / add-payment handlers write to the subcollection, not the array — the array is a pre-MRB-04 relic that was never wired to the new write path). The staff saw "Pending verification" forever. The Bookings table's "PAID" pill at `BookingsPage.tsx:2074` reduced over the empty array and rendered ₱0 paid for every row; the advanced filter's "Reference" search at line 1759 matched against the empty array and returned no results. **(3) `paymentRejectionReason` / `paymentRejectedAt` / `paymentRejectedBy`** — the dashboard's reject-payment card read `paymentRejectionReason` from the unhydrated field and silently lost the rejection reason on every snapshot echo. **Root cause** — same shape of bug as FOL-01 (just shipped) and MRB-15-10 (the room-types hydration dropped `maxChildren` / `maxExtraBeds` / `extraBedRate`). The admin `Booking` extension declared the fields but the mapper never read them from the snapshot. The pre-MRB-01 to pre-MRB-04 / pre-MRB-15-10 era the mapper grew organically and the contract changed under it; FOL-02 is the cleanup that pins the contract at the source level. **Fix — three-part change.** **(1) Hydrate the 5 missing fields in the mapper at `AdminContext.tsx`.** The MRB-01 reservation cluster (`reservationId` / `reservationRef` / `reservationPosition` / `reservationRoomCount`) with the same `String().trim()` / `Number.isFinite()` normalization the writers use. The `onsitePayments` array with per-entry normalization (`id` is `String()`, `amount` is `Number()`, `recordedAt` handles Date + Firestore Timestamp + ISO string). The default for `onsitePayments` is `[]` (never `undefined`) so the read is always safe. **(2) Hydrate the 3 rejection fields** (`paymentRejectionReason` / `paymentRejectedAt` / `paymentRejectedBy`) — same `parseDateTimeString` convention as `paymentConfirmedAt` from FOL-01. Ships as a single bundle so the next mirror-bug (a future dashboard card reading a different field that the mapper also drops) doesn't bite tomorrow. **(3) Wire the live reference lookup.** A new `selectedBookingLatestReference` `useMemo` in `BookingsPage.tsx` computes the reference from the live subcollection listener state (preferred) + the booking's denormalized `onsitePayments[]` array (fallback). Pattern matches the `getBookingFolio` helper's live-vs-persisted disambiguation. The new `latestPaymentReference` prop on `BookingDrawerWorkspaceHeader` consumes the memo; the Folio's proof card consumes the same memo. The pre-FOL-02 inline `getLatestPaymentReference(selectedBooking)` call is gone from the Folio's render block. **What the user sees after the change** — a `confirmed` booking with a verified payment now shows: Folio "Payment history" populated from the subcollection, "PAID ₱3,900" (was "PAID ₱0"), "BALANCE ₱0" (was "₱3,900"), no "Collect" button (was "Collect ₱3,900"), header "Reference: <ref>" (was "Pending verification"), proof card "gcash · <ref>" (was "gcash · No reference"). The Bookings table's "PAID" pill now reads the actual paid amount; the advanced filter's "Reference" search now matches. The dashboard's reject-payment card now shows the rejection reason after a staff reject. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. All 8 fields are already on the Firestore doc; the mapper just wasn't reading them. The server is untouched. **What this changes for the email + notification side** — nothing. The mapper is a read path. **Implementation** — ~80 lines changed across 3 modified files (`AdminContext.tsx` mapper + `BookingDrawerWorkspace.tsx` header + `BookingsPage.tsx` memo + JSX) + 2 new test files (~24 tests total). **Tests** — 14 source-text guards in `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-admin-bookings-mapper-missing-fields.test.ts` + 10 source-text guards in `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-live-reference-wiring.test.ts`. All other admin + shared tests pass (10 pre-existing failures in `bookings-staff-email-dedup.test.ts` / `booking-room-cart.test.ts` / `api-base-url.test.ts` / `dashboard-overdue-checkouts.test.ts` are unrelated to FOL-02, also failing on the dev baseline). **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §FOL-02`, this decision entry, `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped 2026-08-07), pattern documented in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` as an extension of the FOL-01 "transient vs durable signal" gotcha (the "field-drop on read" pattern is the same shape as MRB-15-10). **Rejected** — hydrate only `reservationId` and ship the rest as a follow-up (half-fix; the user would discover the same bug pattern tomorrow on `onsitePayments` + the next day on the rejection cluster; the single-bundle pattern matches MRB-15-10's "pin every contract field" fix); denormalize the payments ledger into the booking's `onsitePayments[]` array server-side as well (broader scope than needed; the read layer can prefer the live state + fall back to the array; the server's `bookings/{id}/payments` vs `reservations/{id}/payments` split is the canonical design per MRB-04); modify the shared `getLatestPaymentReference` helper to accept a second `livePayments` parameter (cleaner shared API but couples the helper to the live-vs-persisted disambiguation, which is an admin concern; the local memo + prop pattern keeps the helper pure); add a server-side sync from the canonical subcollection to the booking's `onsitePayments[]` array on every verify / add-payment (more server-side coupling for a problem the read layer can solve); hydrate every other missing field on the admin `Booking` extension (`rateBreakdown` / `memberDiscountPct` / `pointsAwarded` / `confirmedWithBalance` / `unpaidCheckoutReason` / `rescheduleHistory` / `isTestData` / `earlyCheckIn` / etc.) — out of scope for the visible bug; can ship as FOL-03 in a future pass if any of these bite. **Gates** — MRB-01 (the contract is preserved end-to-end; the new bookings + the admin surface + the deep-link resolution all read the same four fields from the snapshot; legacy null-`reservationId` bookings keep today's self-contained behavior), MRB-04 (the canonical vs legacy subcollection split is preserved; the mapper hydration makes the listener correctly subscribe to the canonical path for new bookings; the legacy path stays correct for legacy bookings), MRB-12 (the reservation-header `paymentStatus` mirror is unchanged; the helper is per-child, not reservation-aggregate; the existing reservation-row `paymentStatus` reader at line 1978 is the reservation-level equivalent), PRC-13 (the verify-and-record-payment modal's success-modal `isFullPayment` math is unchanged), the live reference lookup is consistent with the existing `getBookingFolio` helper's live-vs-persisted disambiguation. **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-02)** — hydrate the rest of the missing fields on the admin `Booking` extension (`rateBreakdown` / `memberDiscountPct` / `pointsAwarded` / `pendingLoyaltyPoints` / `loyaltyAwardStatus` / `pointsAwardedAt` / `checkedOutWithBalance` / `checkedOutFolioTotal` / `checkedOutCollectedTotal` / `earlyCheckoutOriginalCheckOut` / `confirmedWithBalance` / `confirmedWithBalanceReason` / `confirmedWithBalanceAt` / `confirmedWithBalanceBy` / `unpaidCheckoutReason` / `unpaidCheckoutApprovedBy` / `rescheduleHistory` / `isTestData` / `testRunId` / `earlyCheckIn`) — out of scope for the visible bug; can ship as FOL-03 in a future pass if any of these bite; denormalize the payments ledger into the booking's `onsitePayments[]` array server-side on every verify / add-payment (broader scope; would need a Firestore trigger or a write-path migration; the read layer fix is enough for the visible bug); build a "pin every field" contract test for ALL fields on the admin `Booking` extension (the FOL-02 test pins the 8 newly-hydrated fields + the FOL-01 field; a future FOL-03 could expand the contract enumeration).
| 199 | **FOL-03 Check-in / Check-out `runTransaction` Reads-Before-Writes Ordering (off-roadmap bug fix) (proposed 2026-08-07; shipped 2026-08-07 on `fix/fol-03-checkin-checkout-transaction-read-order`; v0.264.11 → v0.264.12):** the `handleCheckinBooking` and `handleCheckoutBooking` server handlers in `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts` are reordered so every `await transaction.get(...)` completes BEFORE any `transaction.update(...)` / `transaction.set(...)` call — the structural requirement Firestore's `runTransaction` API enforces. The pre-FOL-03 surface read the children-for-count snapshot AFTER the writes that mutate the child statuses, which is exactly the reads-after-writes shape the SDK forbids. The visible consequence was a 500 on `POST /api/bookings/checkin` for a confirmed + payment-confirmed booking: `Error: Firestore transactions require all reads to be executed before all writes.` **Driving motivation** — operator-reported 2026-08-07 (post-FOL-02 review): "I tried to check-in on a confirmed payment and confirmed booking but when I check-in, it says [Check-in readiness: Booking status must be confirmed or payment-confirmed]. index-DDEDt6Xi.js:4233  POST https://stg.sparkinnbohol.com/api/bookings/checkin 500 (Internal Server Error) ... Error updating booking status: Error: Firestore transactions require all reads to be executed before all writes." The readiness check (a precondition gate) passed; the check-in API itself died in the transactional write block before the booking + room + reservation-header mirror could flip. **Root cause** — the pre-FOL-03 `handleCheckinBooking` and `handleCheckoutBooking` both read the booking doc, the room doc, and the "other check-ins in this reservation" doc first, then started a write (`transaction.update(bookingRef, ...)`), then read the "all children of this reservation" doc for the `checkedInRoomCount` + `paymentStatus` mirror. The read needed to compute the post-update mirror was placed AFTER the writes that mutate the child statuses (the very thing the mirror needs to count), which is structurally impossible to satisfy with a single transaction. The Firestore SDK throws the canonical error and aborts the transaction. The same shape of bug lived in `handleCheckoutBooking` and would have fired the moment a guest tried to check out. **Fix — three-part change to `bookings.ts`.** **(1) Pre-read the children-for-count snapshot BEFORE any write** — the read is hoisted out of the post-write block into the pre-write block in both handlers. The read's result is stored in a new `childrenForCount` local. **(2) Compute the post-update child statuses in a separate `postUpdateChildStatuses` array** — built from the pre-read's docs with a per-doc ternary that replaces the target booking's status with its post-update value (`"checked-in"` for checkin, `"checked-out"` for checkout) while leaving the other children's pre-update statuses untouched. The SDK doesn't let us observe the post-update state via a real read (we'd be back to reads-after-writes), so we have to construct it. **(3) Apply all writes in a single contiguous write block AFTER the reads** — the booking + room (and, for checkout, the intercom thread + the reservation header) updates are placed after the pre-reads; the reservation header's `checkedInRoomCount` / `paymentStatus` mirror write uses the pre-computed `postUpdateChildStatuses` so the transaction remains valid. **Why this is the right shape** — (1) atomicity is preserved (the whole point of MRB-15-03 — the booking + room + reservation-header mirror still flip together or not at all), (2) the SDK's "all reads first" requirement is now satisfied structurally (no human reviewer can accidentally re-introduce a read-after-write), (3) the post-update state is computed deterministically from the pre-read + the per-doc ternary (no double-read, no stale cache), (4) the variable name `postUpdateChildStatuses` makes the "pre-read + post-update replacement" semantic explicit. **What the user sees after the change** — check-in on a confirmed + payment-confirmed booking returns 200, the booking flips to `checked-in`, the room flips to `occupied`, the reservation header's `checkedInRoomCount` increments correctly, and `paymentStatus` is recomputed. Check-out is the same shape with `"checked-out"`. The error toast that fired on every pre-FOL-03 check-in / check-out click is gone. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The writes are the same; only the order changed. The reads are the same; only their position relative to the writes changed. The mirror math is the same; only its source changed (from a now-impossible read to a pre-computed array). **What this changes for the test surface** — the FOL-03 source-text test pins the new ordering; the 2 affected MRB test files (`mrb-15-03-transactional-counters.test.ts` + `mrb-05-aggregate-and-handler-mirror.test.ts`) are updated to reference `postUpdateChildStatuses` instead of the old `childStatuses` name in 7 places total. **Implementation** — ~30 lines changed in `bookings.ts` (the pre-read hoist + the per-doc ternary + the new var name in the mirror write) + 1 new test file (9 source-text guards) + 2 modified test files (7 var-name references). **Tests** — 9 source-text guards in `guest-app/tests/api/fol-03-checkin-checkout-transaction-read-order.test.ts` (the new pre-read appears before the first write in `handleCheckinBooking`; the new pre-read appears before the first write in `handleCheckoutBooking`; `postUpdateChildStatuses` is constructed via the per-doc ternary that replaces the target booking's status; the mirror write in `handleCheckinBooking` reads from `postUpdateChildStatuses`; the mirror write in `handleCheckoutBooking` reads from `postUpdateChildStatuses` for BOTH `newCheckedInCount` AND `newCheckedOutCount`; the pre-FOL-03 read-after-write pattern is gone from both handlers; the test slice boundaries correctly bound the `handleCheckinBooking` body to the `handleCheckoutBooking` declaration and the `handleCheckoutBooking` body to the `handleLookupBooking` declaration so the test reads only the relevant code; the 2 MRB test files reference the new var name). The check-in / check-out end-to-end behaviour tests (`bookings-checkin.test.ts` + `bookings-checkout.test.ts` + `bookings-checkout-archive.test.ts`) already cover the round-trip; the FOL-03 source-text pin is the cheap, deterministic guard against future reordering. No new runtime tests added. All other guest + admin + shared tests pass (the 3 pre-existing BF-04 failures in the test discipline retrofit are unrelated to FOL-03, also failing on the dev baseline). **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §FOL-03`, this decision entry, `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped 2026-08-07), pattern documented in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` as an extension of the FOL-01 / FOL-02 entries (the "Firestore `runTransaction` requires all reads before all writes" gotcha). **Rejected** — split the read-then-mirror-write into a second transaction after the booking + room writes commit (loses atomicity — the whole point of MRB-15-03; a crash between the two transactions leaves the booking `checked-in` with a stale `checkedInRoomCount`); use a Firestore `writeBatch` for the writes and a separate query for the mirror read (batches don't support transactional reads; the mirror needs to be atomic with the booking + room writes); reorder the writes to do the reservation header first, then the booking + room (wrong — the count derives from the booking status flip, not the other way around; the order is booking → room → header mirror); add a "post-write re-read" workaround that suppresses the SDK error (the SDK requirement is structural, not a warning; a workaround would be a future regression time-bomb); pre-compute the mirror on the client and pass it in the request body (couples the server to the client's read of the children collection; defeats the server's role as the source of truth; the pre-read-in-transaction is the canonical Firestore pattern); rename `childrenForCount` to `preUpdateChildStatuses` and skip the per-doc ternary (less explicit — the reader has to mentally apply the ternary to every read site; the explicit `postUpdateChildStatuses` is the more readable shape; the trade-off is one extra `const` for one extra `let`-style clarity). **Gates** — MRB-15-03 (the transactional counters are still atomic — the booking + room + reservation header still flip together; only the read order changed), MRB-01 (the reservation header is still the aggregation target; the `checkedInRoomCount` / `paymentStatus` mirror is still written in the same transaction), PRC-13 (unrelated; verify-and-record-payment doesn't touch the checkin/checkout handlers), the FOL-01 / FOL-02 read-side fixes are unaffected (the mapper hydration is independent of the server's transaction shape). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-03)** — add a lint rule that flags any `await transaction.get` after a `transaction.update` in the server handlers (would catch future re-introductions of the same bug at the AST level; out of scope for the FOL-03 fix; can be added in a future tooling pass); refactor the 2 handlers to share a `readAllChildrenInTransaction` helper that returns the `postUpdateChildStatuses` shape directly (DRY win but a separate refactor; FOL-03 is the bug fix, not the refactor); add a runtime test that exercises the check-in happy path against the Firestore emulator (the existing `bookings-checkin.test.ts` already covers the surface; the emulator round-trip is the local-env test pattern from MRB-11 / CRL-09 / MRB-14, deferred to the same environment).

| 200 | **FOL-04 My-booking payment-method label is dynamic (off-roadmap bug fix) (proposed 2026-08-07; shipped 2026-08-07 on `fix/my-booking-payment-method-dynamic-label`):** the payment-method label on the public `/my-booking` single-booking card + the reservation-scope card is now resolved from the admin's `settings/hotelConfig.paymentMethods[].label` (the same source the `/book` page already uses), with a shared legacy map as the last-resort fallback. The pre-FOL-04 my-booking page hardcoded a 4-key `paymentLabels` map (`gcash` / `pay-at-hotel` / `paypal` / `bank`); the reservation-scope card dumped the raw `paymentMethod` key directly. **Two visible symptoms.** **(1) Drift after admin rename** — when the admin renamed a method's `label` in Settings → Payment Methods (e.g. `gcash` → "GCash Only"), the booking page picked up the new label immediately (it reads the array dynamically) but the my-booking page kept showing the legacy "Digital Wallet (GCash/Maya)" — guests saw two different labels for the same booking across the two surfaces. **(2) Ugly raw keys for custom methods** — when the admin added a custom method (`pesonet`, `credit-card`, `crypto`), the my-booking + reservation cards fell through to the raw method key because the hardcoded map had no entry, so the guest saw "pesonet" / "credit-card" as the label. **Driving motivation** — visual diff on the live screenshot 2026-08-07 (Mavis review of the existing my-booking page in conversation with the user). **Root cause** — each guest-facing page owned its own `paymentLabels` / `legacy` object literal. The booking page (`BookingPage.tsx`) reads `pm.label` from the admin config dynamically; the booking confirmation page (`BookingConfirmPage.tsx`) and the my-booking page (`BookingLookupPage.tsx`) carried independent hardcoded maps. The three surfaces could (and did) drift. **Fix — three-part change.** **(1) New shared helper `resolvePaymentMethodLabel(methodKey, paymentMethods)` in `shared/utils/paymentMethodLabel.ts`** — single source of truth for the guest-facing label. Resolution order: (a) match by `method` in the admin-supplied `paymentMethods[]` array → return that entry's `label`; (b) fall through to the `LEGACY_PAYMENT_METHOD_LABELS` map (`gcash` / `bank` / `pay-at-hotel`) — the three keys the booking + corporate + store flows used before the dynamic array existed; (c) fall through to the raw `methodKey` as a last resort (the key is a short kebab-case slug; the guest sees something readable, staff can identify the gap from the lookup's `paymentMethod` field). The helper is defensive about input shape (nullish / non-string / non-array / missing `label` field) so a malformed admin doc never throws. **(2) `BookingLookupPage.tsx` now fetches `settings/hotelConfig` and resolves the label dynamically** — a new `useEffect` fires the `getDoc` on `doc(db, "settings", "hotelConfig")` when a result card becomes visible (single OR reservation-scope), is a no-op when the search form is alone, and stores the filtered `paymentMethods[]` (only entries with `typeof method === "string" && typeof label === "string"`) in local state. The single-booking card + the reservation-scope card both render through the new helper, so the two cards can never drift apart. **(3) `BookingConfirmPage.tsx` refactored to use the same helper** — the previous in-page `legacy` map and `dynamicPaymentMethodLabel` + `fallbackPaymentMethodLabel` ternaries are replaced by a single `dynamicPaymentMethods` state that feeds the same helper. The two pages now read from one shared legacy map; renaming `gcash` to "GCash Only" on the admin side flips the label on the confirmation page + the my-booking page + the my-booking reservation card in one shot. **What the user sees** — the screenshot's "Payment Method: Digital Wallet (GCash/Maya)" stays exactly the same for the current admin config (the legacy map carries it). After the admin renames `gcash` → "GCash Only" in Settings, the my-booking card + the reservation card + the booking confirmation all show "GCash Only" on the next page load. After the admin adds a custom `pesonet` method, the cards show "PESONET Bank Transfer" (or whatever the admin typed) instead of the raw "pesonet". **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The booking doc's `paymentMethod` field is unchanged. The admin's `paymentMethods[]` array is unchanged. Only the render path changed. **What this changes for the server** — nothing. The fix is fully client-side. The shared helper lives in `shared/utils/` so future server-side label rendering (e.g. an admin-side "guest-facing label preview" in Settings) can reuse it; no server change in FOL-04. **Implementation** — `shared/utils/paymentMethodLabel.ts` (new, ~80 lines; the helper + the `LEGACY_PAYMENT_METHOD_LABELS` map + the per-step resolution comments); `shared/index.ts` (1 line — re-export the helper); `shared/__tests__/paymentMethodLabel.test.ts` (new, 9 behavioural unit tests covering the admin-wins / legacy-fallback / raw-key / empty-label / nullish-input / non-array / non-string-method cases); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingLookupPage.tsx` (replaced the hardcoded `paymentLabels` map with a `resolveLabel` closure; added a `useEffect` that fetches `hotelConfig.paymentMethods[]` when a result card becomes visible; the single-booking card at line ~1286 + the reservation-scope card at line ~1507 both render through `resolveLabel`); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingConfirmPage.tsx` (replaced the in-page `legacy` map + the `dynamicPaymentMethodLabel` / `fallbackPaymentMethodLabel` ternaries with a single `dynamicPaymentMethods` state that feeds the same shared helper). ~50 lines changed across 2 page files + 1 new shared util + 1 new test file. **Tests** — 9 behavioural unit tests in `shared/__tests__/paymentMethodLabel.test.ts` (admin label wins over the legacy map for a shared key; admin-configured label is returned for a matching method; falls through to the legacy map when the method is not in `paymentMethods[]`; falls through to the raw key when no admin + no legacy match; ignores admin entries with a missing/empty `label` field so a malformed doc never poisons the fallback; returns empty string for empty / non-string / nullish `methodKey`; does not throw on non-array / malformed `paymentMethods` input; ignores admin entries whose `method` is not a string). All other shared + guest + admin tests pass (`tsc -b` clean for both apps). **MDs** — this decision entry, `plan/features/BOOKING-LOOKUP.md §Data & Logic Checklist` (the `paymentMethod` row now references the dynamic label resolution), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped 2026-08-07), pattern documented in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` as an extension of the existing "hardcoded UI label vs admin-editable config" gotcha (decision #108 / SETTINGS.md §Payment Methods already established `paymentMethods[]` as the canonical source; FOL-04 extends the contract enforcement to the public lookup surface). **Rejected** — fetch the label server-side and include it in the `POST /api/bookings/lookup` response (the lookup endpoint is privacy-sensitive — adding a settings read into it would couple a public-facing credentialed endpoint to the admin's settings path, broadening the read blast-radius for no privacy gain; the public client is allowed to read `settings/hotelConfig` directly today, and the existing `BookingPage` already does the same fetch; the client-side helper is the smaller blast-radius); add a `paymentMethodLabel` field to the booking doc at create time and read it back on lookup (denormalization doubles the storage and creates a second source of truth that can drift from the admin config — a guest who renames a method weeks later would see the old label on every pre-rename booking; the dynamic read is the canonical "what does the admin say NOW" answer); have the my-booking page import `usePublicSiteContent` to read the bundled `hotelConfig` (the hook fetches the whole `websiteContent` + `roomTypes` + `paymentMethods` + `seniorPwdOnlineEnabled` bundle for the marketing site — pulling it in just to read `paymentMethods[]` is a 10× heavier payload and a 10× heavier read path; a focused `getDoc(settings/hotelConfig)` filtered to `paymentMethods[]` is the right read shape for this surface); rename `LEGACY_PAYMENT_METHOD_LABELS` to `FALLBACK_PAYMENT_METHOD_LABELS` for accuracy (the map is the last-resort fallback, not a "legacy" snapshot — but the existing codebase uses "legacy" for the same semantic (e.g. `LegacyPaymentMethodConfigSchema` in `shared/schemas/paymentMethod.ts`); keeping the name consistent with the existing terminology is the lower-friction choice); ship a separate `usePaymentMethodLabel(methodKey)` hook so other future pages can opt in (the helper is one line of `resolvePaymentMethodLabel(methodKey, paymentMethods)` — a hook is overkill for a pure function and would re-introduce the duplication problem if every page owns its own hook instance; the helper is the right granularity; future pages import the same helper + the same `useEffect` shape from `BookingLookupPage` if they need the dynamic fetch); cache the `paymentMethods[]` array across pages via a shared context (broader scope; the cache invalidation story (admin edit + cross-tab) is the public-site cache's responsibility per `usePublicSiteContent`; coupling my-booking to that cache brings the heavyweight bundle back; a per-page focused fetch + the cross-tab invalidation already wired by `usePublicSiteContent` (the `bustPublicSiteContentCache` write) is enough — future per-page migration to a shared cache can be a separate refactor if N pages all need it). **Gates** — #108 / SETTINGS.md §Payment Methods (the contract that `settings/hotelConfig.paymentMethods[]` is the canonical source for guest-facing labels is now enforced on every public surface; no admin-facing surface was touched), MRB-10 (the reservation-scope lookup card's `paymentMethod` render now uses the same helper as the single-booking card; the privacy posture is unchanged — `paymentMethod` was always a wire field, only the displayed label changed), CRL-* (unrelated; the cancellation preview + the destructive cancel + the refund lifecycle are server-side and untouched), the dynamic `paymentMethods` fetch is consistent with the existing `BookingPage.tsx` read of the same doc (same `getDoc` shape, same `typeof method === "string" && typeof label === "string"` filter — the two surfaces are now parallel). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-04)** — extend the shared helper to render a `paymentMethodDescription` (a second field on the admin config) for the receipt PDF + the booking confirmation's expanded details card (the receipt PDF currently shows the same single-line label; a richer multi-line description would be a small admin-config change + a helper extension; out of scope for the FOL-04 label-drift fix); add a cross-page "payment method label" cache that the public-site content cache can invalidate (would avoid the per-page Firestore read when many pages need the same array; the per-page read is cheap today, the cache is only worth it if N > 2 pages grow into the same pattern); add a `paymentMethodIcon` field to the admin config and ship a small icon component so the my-booking card can render the same icon the booking page already shows (UI polish; would unify the visual surface; out of scope for the FOL-04 label-drift fix).

| 201 | **FOL-05 Verify-Payment Reservation-Scope + Dashboard One-Card-Per-Reservation (off-roadmap bug fix) (proposed 2026-08-07; shipped 2026-08-07 on `fix/fol-05-verify-reservation-scope`; v0.264.12 → v0.264.13):** the dashboard's "pending payment alerts" list groups children of a `reservationId` into one card per reservation (not one per child), and the server's three payment handlers (`handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` + `handleAddPayment` + `handleRejectPayment`) gain a sibling-flip pass that mirrors the FOL-03 check-in / check-out pattern — pre-read children BEFORE any writes, compute the per-child post-update status array (`postUpdateChildStatuses`), queue per-sibling `transaction.update` calls for every covered child, update the reservation header's `paymentStatus` via the N>1 aggregate helper. The operator-reported bug was "for reservations with multiple rooms, I see 1 row for verification per room". **Driving motivation** — operator-reported 2026-08-07: "for reservations with multiple rooms, can we verify the payment only once? right now, I am seeing 1 row for verification per room." **Two related issues from the same root cause.** **(1) Dashboard UI** — the pre-FOL-05 list was `bookings.filter(b => b.status === "payment-uploaded")`, a flat per-booking render. A 2-room reservation with both children `payment-uploaded` rendered TWO cards (one per child). The verify amount on each card was the lead room's `totalPrice - onsitePayments.reduce(...)` — not the reservation's. **(2) Server** — the pre-FOL-05 `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` only flipped the TARGET child's `status` to `payment-confirmed`, even when the cumulative reservation payment covered all siblings (the money was already at the reservation level per MRB-04 Phase 2.x, but the booking-doc status updates were per-room). So even if a guest paid the full reservation in one GCash transfer and the staff verified the full amount on the lead, only the lead flipped to `payment-confirmed` — the second room stayed `payment-uploaded` and the dashboard kept showing the second card. The staff had to verify a second time on the second card (with `min="0.01"` blocking a zero-amount verify). The same shape of bug lived in `handleAddPayment` (the staff-new-payment path) and in `handleRejectPayment` (the rejection path). **Root cause** — money lives at the reservation level post-MRB-04 (the verified payment record lives at `reservations/{id}/payments/{paymentId}`), but the booking-doc `status` transitions were per-room. A single verify call's cumulative payment often covered siblings the handler never touched. The flat per-booking dashboard list amplified the visible bug: the user saw N cards where 1 was the right answer. **Fix — three-part change spanning the dashboard, the server, and the shared mirror contract.** **(1) Dashboard groups children of a `reservationId` into a single `PendingPaymentItem`** — a new `useMemo` at `admin-app/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx` builds a `Map<reservationId, Booking[]>`, sorts each group by `reservationPosition` (the FOL-05 lead is the first child = the lowest position), and emits one item per group. The item carries reservation-scope `totalPrice` (from the `Reservation` header, falling back to the children-sum for the cold-start race), `paidAmount` (from the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate, MRB-12's wire, falling back to the in-memory `onsitePayments` sum), and `dueAmount`. The room breakdown lives in `rooms[]` (per-room `{ bookingId, roomNumber, roomType, totalPrice, status }`). The card renders one row per item with the `R-YYYYMMDD-NNNNN` reservation ref + a `{N} rooms` chip + the reservation-scope total/paid/due. Legacy N=1 paths render as a single-row item keyed by the lead's `bookingId` — byte-equivalent to the pre-FOL-05 surface. **(2) Verify modal pre-fills the amount to the reservation-scope `dueAmount` and shows a per-room coverage preview** — a new `verifyScope` state (`PendingPaymentItem | null`, `null` for the N=1 / legacy case) carries the reservation-scope context. The `openVerifyForm(item)` action sets `verifyTarget` to the lead booking (the verify handler still takes a `bookingId`) + sets `verifyScope` to the item + pre-fills `verifyAmount` to `item.dueAmount`. The modal renders a "Coverage preview" panel listing each room with a "Cleared" / `{N} still owed` / "Pending" badge that updates live as the staff edits the amount. The preview is a CLIENT-SIDE approximation; the server is the source of truth (a fresh `runTransaction` reads the live state on submit). **(3) Server's three payment handlers gain the sibling-flip pass** — `(a)` pre-read children for the reservation BEFORE any writes (`adminDb.collection("bookings").where("reservationId", "==", bookingReservationId)`, hoisted into the read phase per FOL-03); `(b)` compute `postUpdateChildStatuses` — for verify / add-payment, the rule is "if the child's current status is `pending` or `payment-uploaded` AND the new cumulative reservation payments cover its `totalPrice`, the post-update status is `payment-confirmed`"; for reject, the rule is "every child currently in `payment-uploaded` flips to `pending`"; `(c)` queue per-sibling `transaction.update` calls — each flipped sibling gets its own `transaction.update(bookings/{id}, { status: "payment-confirmed" / "pending", paymentConfirmedAt: now / paymentRejectedAt: now, handledBy: staffUid, holdExpiresAt: ..., updatedAt: now })`; `(d)` update the reservation header's `paymentStatus` via the N>1 aggregate — `paymentStatus: computeReservationAggregatePaymentStatus(postUpdateChildStatuses)`, not the pre-FOL-05 single-child mapper. The pre-FOL-05 `if (fullyPaid && bookingReservationId.length > 0)` and `if (transitionedToPaymentConfirmed && bookingReservationId.length > 0)` guards on the header mirror are REMOVED — the FOL-05 mirror fires on EVERY verify / add for new reservations (a partial that flips zero siblings still leaves the aggregate unchanged, and a partial that flips N siblings correctly surfaces the new aggregate). The new guard is `if (bookingReservationId.length > 0 && siblingChildBookings.length > 0)`. The status check is RESERVATION-AWARE — the pre-FOL-05 verify handler threw `ALREADY_CONFIRMED` whenever the target was `payment-confirmed` or `confirmed`; the FOL-05 check RELAXES this for N>1: a target already past the money gate + flippable siblings proceeds (the target's own status update is skipped because its post-update status equals its pre-update status), but the sibling-flip pass still runs. N=1 + no flippable siblings throws `ALREADY_CONFIRMED` (preserves the pre-FOL-05 contract for the legacy surface). The same relaxation applies to `handleRejectPayment`. The `siblingFlippedCount` / `siblingRejectedCount` field on the 200 OK response surfaces the flip count to the admin UI (the post-verify success modal renders a "X more rooms cleared — {reservationRef}" breadcrumb when the count is > 0; the bell panel emits a separate `payment` notification with the same breadcrumb). **What the user sees after the change** — the dashboard's pending-payments list shows one card per reservation (a 2-room reservation renders 1 card, not 2). The verify modal pre-fills the amount to the reservation's outstanding balance (e.g. ₱10,000 for a 2-room reservation), and the per-room coverage preview shows "Room 101 → Cleared" / "Room 102 → Cleared" before the staff hits submit. The server flips BOTH rooms in one transaction. The card drops off the dashboard on the next snapshot. For a partial payment (e.g. ₱5,000 of ₱10,000), the preview shows "Room 101 → Cleared" / "Room 102 → ₱5,000 still owed", the server flips the one covered child, the second room stays `payment-uploaded`, and the card stays on the dashboard with the remaining balance. The reject path is symmetric: a reservation-scope reject flips every `payment-uploaded` sibling back to `pending` in one transaction. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The write paths write to the same fields; the new sibling-flip pass adds per-sibling `transaction.update` calls. The reservation header's `paymentStatus` is already a N>1 aggregate field per MRB-05 / MRB-12 — the FOL-05 change just routes the verify / add / reject mirrors through the aggregate helper instead of the single-child mapper. **What this changes for the email + notification side** — the post-verify success modal surfaces a "X more rooms cleared" breadcrumb (the PRC-13 email + the bell `payment` notification are unchanged — the original `payment-confirmed` email already covered the money event when the FIRST room flipped; the sibling flips are metadata for the staff, not a new guest-facing event). The post-reject path adds a symmetric breadcrumb. **What this changes for the dashboard** — the StatsCard's "Pending Payments" count is now the count of grouped items (which equals the count of proofs queued — the new contracts the proof = the reservation's proof, not the per-room proof). The recent-bookings strip is unchanged (per-booking shape, byte-equivalent). **What this changes for the BookingsPage** — nothing. The BookingsPage already groups multi-room reservations into one row (per MRB-07 / MRB-12) and the per-child verify reachable from the drawer's Folio section uses the same handler, which now correctly flips all covered siblings when verified against a single child. The table view's behaviour is unchanged. **Why one click verifies the whole reservation, not a per-room sub-step modal** — the actual money is held at the reservation scope post-MRB-04 (one payment record at `reservations/{id}/payments/{paymentId}`); the per-room status flips are bookkeeping. A per-room sub-step modal would force N staff clicks for the same end state, with no upside (one GCash / bank transfer covers the whole reservation, so the ref + method are the same for every room). The FOL-05 contract is "one click = whole reservation, with a per-room coverage preview in the modal so the staff sees the math". The user's confirmation: "go with option A" (one-click whole-reservation verify, with the per-room coverage preview). **What the operator sees after the change** — the dashboard's pending-payments list for the operator's 2-room test case now shows ONE card with `R-20260807-00012 · 2 rooms · ₱10,000 due`. Clicking "Verify & Record" opens a modal with the ₱10,000 amount pre-filled and a per-room preview showing both rooms as "Cleared". The staff types the GCash ref, hits Verify. The card drops off the dashboard on the next snapshot. The 2-card "verify once per room" UX is gone. **What the test surface covers** — 23 source-text guards in `guest-app/tests/api/fol-05-verify-reservation-scope.test.ts` (per-handler slice boundaries, the FOL-03 reads-before-writes invariant on all 3 handlers, the `postUpdateChildStatuses` array name + the flip rule, the per-sibling `transaction.update` writes, the aggregate mirror write, the reservation-aware status check, the response field surfacing, the shared FOL-05 contract across all 3 handlers) + 15 source-text guards in `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-05-dashboard-pending-payments.test.ts` (the `PendingPaymentItem` type at module scope, the grouped `useMemo` computation, the per-reservationId grouping key, the lead-by-position sort, the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate read, the dashboard card render with the `{N} rooms` chip + reservation-scope labels, the `openVerifyForm(item)` call site, the `verifyScope` state, the per-room coverage preview, the verify modal's reservation-scope math for the success modal, the rejection modal's reservation-scope wording). The behavioural round-trip is the live dashboard; the source-text guards pin the FOL-05 contract at the source level so a future "I'll just revert to per-booking" refactor breaks the test instead of silently regressing. **What this changes for the test surface (collateral updates)** — the 3 pre-existing MRB-04 Phase 3 source-text tests (`mrb-04-phase-2x-refund-verify.test.ts` + `mrb-04-phase-3-reservation-payment-status.test.ts` + `mrb-15-08-legacy-fallback.test.ts`) are updated to match the FOL-05 contract: the helper is now `computeReservationAggregatePaymentStatus(postUpdateChildStatuses)` (the N>1 aggregate), the gate is now `bookingReservationId.length > 0 && siblingChildBookings.length > 0` (FOL-05 legacy skip), the pre-FOL-05 `fullyPaid` / `transitionedToPaymentConfirmed` guards are GONE. All 9 pre-existing test failures (4 in `bookings-staff-email-dedup.test.ts` + the firebase emulator tests + 1 in `convert-inquiry-read-order.test.ts` which is flaky) are unrelated to FOL-05, also failing on the dev baseline. **Implementation** — 1 new test file (fol-05-verify-reservation-scope.test.ts, 23 guards) + 1 new test file (fol-05-dashboard-pending-payments.test.ts, 15 guards) + 3 modified test files (the MRB-04 Phase 3 source-text pin) + 4 modified source files (guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts — the 3 handler bodies + the post-transaction side effects; admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx — the `verifyAndRecordPayment` + `rejectPayment` return types + inline Promise annotations; admin-app/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx — the grouped `useMemo` + the card render + the verify modal with the per-room coverage preview + the verify success modal's reservation-scope math; the `dashboard-payment-actions.test.ts` — the `title` attribute is now a dynamic variable, the test matches the new shape). ~200 lines of new code in `bookings.ts` (the sibling pre-read + the `postUpdateChildStatuses` computation + the per-sibling `transaction.update` writes + the aggregate mirror, applied 3 times) + ~150 lines of new code in `DashboardPage.tsx` (the grouped `useMemo` + the reservation-aware card render + the per-room coverage preview). **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §FOL-05` (this entry), this decision entry, `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped 2026-08-07), pattern documented in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` as an extension of the FOL-03 "Firestore `runTransaction` requires all reads before all writes" gotcha + the MRB-04 "reservation-owned payment subcollection" pattern. **Rejected** — keep the per-room sub-step modal in the verify flow (forces N staff clicks for the same end state; one GCash / bank transfer covers the whole reservation, so the per-room sub-steps add friction without value; the user's explicit "go with option A" preference); add a new `verify-reservation` endpoint that takes a `reservationId` and flips all children (would duplicate the FOL-05 sibling-flip logic on the server side without enabling a different UX; the FOL-05 verify flow is "use the existing `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` endpoint with the lead booking + the server flips all covered siblings" — the same endpoint, the same contract, the same response shape, just enriched with the `siblingFlippedCount` field); compute the per-child flip client-side and send N verify requests (loses atomicity — a staff-verifies-2-rooms-then-network-blips race could leave one room in `payment-uploaded` and one in `payment-confirmed`; the FOL-05 single-transaction approach is atomic); add a denormalized `paidAmount` field on the reservation header that the dashboard reads instead of the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate (denormalization creates a second source of truth that can drift; the aggregate is the canonical "sum every payment" answer; a future "we'll also denormalize the per-room paid amount" pattern doubles the storage + the bug surface; the FOL-05 dashboard uses the existing aggregate wire the BookingsPage reservation row already uses); apply FOL-05 only to the verify path and leave the reject path at the per-room scope (asymmetric UX — the staff would have to click reject once per room; the same FOL-05 pattern closes the same shape of bug for all 3 payment handlers; the cost is ~50 lines per handler for the symmetric behavior); use a Firestore `writeBatch` for the per-sibling updates (batches don't support transactional reads; the FOL-05 mirror needs to be atomic with the sibling flips — a batched write could leave the header stale if a concurrent verify fired between the batch and the mirror read; the `runTransaction` + per-sibling `update` pattern is the correct shape); expose the per-room coverage preview as a separate "preview" endpoint (the preview is a CLIENT-SIDE approximation from the live data the dashboard already has in memory — a server endpoint would add latency for a UX hint that doesn't need to be authoritative; the server is the source of truth on submit, the preview is just a hint). **Gates** — MRB-04 (the canonical vs legacy subcollection split is preserved — the FOL-05 verify / add-payment / reject handlers still read the payments subcollection from the reservation (new reservations) or the booking (legacy); the sibling-flip pass writes per-sibling status fields on the booking doc, which is the same path the pre-FOL-05 single-child update used), MRB-05 (the N>1 aggregate helper is the canonical reservation-scope payment-status reader; FOL-05 routes all 3 mirror writes through the aggregate instead of the single-child mapper, which is the N>1-correct shape), MRB-12 (the admin reservation-scope paid-amount aggregate is the dashboard's data source — same wire the BookingsPage reservation row already reads; the FOL-05 dashboard's `pendingPaymentItems` computation reads the same `reservations` + `reservationPaidAmount` slice MRB-12 added), MRB-13 (the cancellation-scope sibling-flip pattern is the same shape — both batch operations on multiple children of a reservation; MRB-13 is the cancel-scope precedent FOL-05 builds on), MRB-15-03 (the sibling-flip pass is inside the same `runTransaction` as the target update + the reservation header mirror — atomic across all 3 writes; no partial states where the target flipped but the siblings didn't, or the siblings flipped but the header didn't update), FOL-03 (the sibling pre-read sits BEFORE the first write per the FOL-03 "reads before writes" rule; the per-sibling `transaction.update` calls are placed after all reads; the pre-FOL-05 check-in / check-out ordering fix is the template the FOL-05 sibling-flip pass follows), FOL-01 (the `isPaymentVerified(booking)` helper is unchanged — the FOL-05 sibling flips correctly set the `paymentConfirmedAt` timestamp on every covered child, so the helper returns `true` for all of them; the post-FOL-05 UI's "Verified" badge lights up correctly for every covered child), FOL-02 (the admin mapper's MRB-01 cluster hydration is unchanged — the dashboard's grouped `useMemo` reads the same `reservationId` / `reservationPosition` fields the FOL-02 mapper hydrates), PRC-13 (the verify modal's pre-fill math uses the reservation-scope `dueAmount` instead of the per-room `totalPrice - onsitePayments.reduce(...)`; the success modal's `isFullPayment` + `remainingBalance` now reflect the reservation-scope math via `verifyScope.paidAmount`; the FOL-05 contract is "verify the whole reservation in one click, with the per-room coverage preview in the modal"). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-05)** — apply the same FOL-05 sibling-flip pattern to `handleApplyDiscount` (the discount path) so a reservation-scope discount covers N children in one click (parallel scope; the MRB-12 discount modal already has a "This room" / "All N rooms" segmented control that calls the existing per-child apply-discount endpoint N times — the FOL-05 pattern would consolidate that into a single transactional sibling-flip pass; out of scope for the verify-payment bug); expose the per-room coverage preview server-side (a `GET /api/reservations/{id}/coverage-preview?amount=X` endpoint that returns the per-room "Cleared" / `{N} still owed" / "Pending" badges — would be authoritative but adds latency for a UX hint; the client-side approximation is the smaller blast-radius); add a runtime test that exercises the verify happy path against the Firestore emulator for a multi-room reservation (the existing `bookings-payments-multi-room.test.ts` would be the right home; the source-text guards are the cheap, deterministic pin; the runtime round-trip is the local-env test pattern from MRB-11 / CRL-09 / MRB-14, deferred to the same environment); apply FOL-05 to the corporate-inquiry → reservation conversion path (the convert-inquiry handler creates N children; a parallel FOL-05 sibling-flip pass would consolidate the N `transaction.create` calls — out of scope for the verify-payment bug); add a `paymentStatus` per-room preview on the BookingsPage reservation row's tooltip (the BookingsPage already surfaces the reservation-scope `paymentStatus` via the MRB-12-02 pill; a per-room tooltip would be a deeper drilldown — future UI refinement).
| 202 | **BAR-01 Booking Architecture Refactor — Denormalize Rooms into the Booking Doc (proposed 2026-08-08; ⏸ deferred with trigger — see `plan/features/BOOKING-UNIFIED.md` for the full spec; ticket to be filed as MRB-16 at step 0):** the post-MRB-01 `reservations/{id}` wrapper doc has accumulated responsibilities across 16 separate steps (MRB-01..15 + CRL-07/08 + FOL-03/05) and is now a full first-class entity — payments + refunds subcollections, denormalized counter fields (`roomCount` / `activeRoomCount` / `cancelledRoomCount` / `checkedInRoomCount` / `checkedOutRoomCount`), derived `paymentStatus` aggregate mirror, sibling-flip pass on every payment / checkin / checkout handler. The destination shape: **1 booking doc, 1 lifecycle, 1 payment path, N rooms in a `rooms: RoomLine[]` array on the booking.** The `reservations/{id}` wrapper is deleted in step 6 (with a 90-day archive window). **Driving motivation** — the BAR-01 spec captures the cumulative cost of MRB-01..15: every "what does this action do for N>1?" question now needs the same shape of fix (pre-read children, build a per-child post-update status array, queue N `transaction.update` calls, write a header mirror). FOL-03 + FOL-05 are the two surface-level examples; the same shape will need to apply to every future multi-room action (apply discount, partial refund, per-room check-in). The deeper smell: two sources of truth for the same thing. Money lives at the reservation scope (`reservations/{id}/payments/{paymentId}`); per-child `status` flips are per-booking-doc; the header's `paymentStatus` is a derived projection of the children. The refactor collapses these into one. **Why a refactor, not another patch** — FOL-05 closed the verify-payment bug surgically (~200 lines of sibling-flip), but the same shape of work will keep recurring for every future multi-room action. The 16-step accumulation is the signal: a pattern that needs the same fix in every new feature is a shape problem, not a per-feature problem. Patching FOL-06 (apply-discount) and FOL-07 (partial refund) with the same sibling-flip pattern would add ~50-100 lines each + a per-feature coverage preview, and the patches would have to stay byte-equivalent forever. The refactor makes the patches structurally unnecessary. **What it is** — 7-step migration (each step independently shippable + reversible behind a feature flag). **(1) Add `rooms: BookingRoomLine[]` to the Booking type (dual-write, no read change)** — the type is named `BookingRoomLine`, not `RoomLine`, because `WalkinRoomLineInput` + `FingerprintableRoomLine` already exist and mean different things. (2) Backfill existing reservations (one-shot script in top-level `scripts/`). (3) Switch payment reads to prefer `rooms[]` over the children list (behind a `BOOKING_UNIFIED_READ_ROOMS_ENABLED` flag — no feature-flag mechanism exists in the codebase today, so step 3 builds the minimum: a server env var + a `VITE_` mirror). (4) Move payments subcollection from `reservations/{id}/payments/` to `bookings/{id}/payments/`. (5) Move refunds / **charges** / liability / email view — the `reservations/{id}/charges/` folio subcollection moves with them (BookingsPage dual-source reads + void guard, the server transaction, the `collectionGroup("charges")` Sales tab, and the ETR test-run cleanup path list). Steps 4-6 each ship their `firebase/firestore.rules` + `firestore.indexes.json` changes in the same PR — the new booking-parented paths need rules before the first write, and the charge-void guard's `get()` path must follow the charge to its new parent. (6) Delete the FOL-05 sibling-flip pass + the reservation docs (with 90-day archive window) + the MRB-15-03 transactional-counters code (counters are derived on-read). (7) MDs + audit. **What it deletes** — FOL-05 sibling-flip pass (~200 lines), FOL-03 reads-before-writes for payment handlers (still good practice, no longer load-bearing), MRB-15-03 transactional counters (~5 denormalized counter fields + the `computeReservationAggregatePaymentStatus` helper usage), MRB-13 reservation-scope cancel (the `Map<code, count>` dedup + the `cancelledRoomCount` increment + the `paymentStatus` aggregate mirror), MRB-14 add-room (the new endpoint + the separate `RoomLine` derivation), CRL-07 dual-source liability read (always on the booking now), the `reservations` collection (empty, listener + collectionGroup aggregate removed). **Net code change: ~-1,200 lines** (1,500-2,000 lines removed; 300-500 added for the new `rooms[]` array plumbing). **What it enables** — apply-discount whole-reservation (one doc write, no scope picker), partial refund with per-room allocation, per-room check-in, multi-night add-on per room, cancellation policy per room. All are "mutate `rooms[i]`, no N-doc iteration, no header mirror." **Trigger conditions (all 3 must be true to start)** — (1) MRB-15 has fully shipped (current state ✅); (2) the team has bandwidth for a 6-month project (solo-dev shop) / 2-month project (3-engineer team), no P0 work in flight; (3) the next "multi-room" feature (apply-discount, partial refund, per-room check-in, per-room add-on) is on the roadmap — refactor first, then the new feature ships on the new shape. **Why deferred** — the FOL-05 patch is the contract; the BAR-01 refactor is the destination. The patch is the right shape for the current bug + the next 2-3 features; the refactor only pays off when the next "multi-room" feature is on the roadmap AND the team has the bandwidth for a multi-week refactor. **Visual tour** — `plan/project/BAR-01-booking-flow-tour.html` (11-step post-refactor walkthrough with the partial-payment scenario; built 2026-08-08 as a stakeholder alignment aid for the migration). **Open questions (resolve at step 0)** — booking `id` wire ref contract (`bookingRef` vs `reservationRef` post-refactor), counter-derivation cost at N=10+ (current ceiling is 3-4 for a 14-room property), `rooms[]` array size cap (well under 1 MiB at N=14; defer subcollection-vs-array for white-label era when N>20), `roomId` + `roomNumber` + `roomType` denormalization staleness (same drift risk that exists today, not a new problem). **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKING-UNIFIED.md`, this decision entry, `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (new "BAR — Booking Architecture Refactor" row in Phase Status Overview + the BAR-01-00..07 task breakdown in the BAR section). **Pattern to land in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` at step 0** — "Two sources of truth for the same thing (reservation header mirror vs per-child status)" as a new "Data model" entry. **Audit amendment (2026-08-08) — starting BAR-01 in the near term is rejected; do BAR-02 + BAR-03 first.** The audit verified the diagnosis (handler names, helper names, the GOTCHAS reads-before-writes entry, and the FOL-05 38-test count all check out) but found the destination shape trades away the availability path: the double-booking transaction queries `bookings where roomId == X and status in ROOM_OCCUPYING_STATUSES` then filters dates in memory, with 9 `where("roomId", …)` call sites across booking creation, room blocks, and corporate inquiries. Collapsing children into `rooms[]` removes the queryable per-room row — `roomId` needs a denormalized `roomIds: string[]` for `array-contains`, per-room `status` can no longer be filtered server-side, and MRB-14 per-room date divergence can't be indexed — so the system's most safety-critical transaction gets *harder*. The array shape also self-limits (open question 3 concedes a subcollection past N>20), meaning the white-label era would migrate back toward per-room docs. **New trigger condition 4:** BAR-01 is gated on availability moving to room-type inventory (free units per type per night); that is a separate project. **The sibling-flip is the price of queryable per-room state, not an accident** — an N-doc write in one transaction at N=1-3 is not a Firestore problem. **Near-term path instead:** **BAR-02** — delete the five counter fields + the header `paymentStatus` mirror and derive them on read (audit verified **no `where()` / `orderBy()` anywhere references them**, so this is a display-layer change with no migration; it removes the actual two-sources-of-truth risk and the MRB-15-03 write path); **BAR-03** — extract the FOL-05 pass into one `applyReservationScopePaymentTransition` helper called by the three payment handlers, which answers this entry's core complaint (every new multi-room feature re-deriving the same fix) without deleting the per-room documents. Also noted: **"net −1,200 lines" is not a business outcome** and should not on its own justify a 6-month solo project with an irreversible step on a live money system. Revisit BAR-01 on evidence from real multi-room features after BAR-02 + BAR-03 ship. **Rejected (per the BOOKING-UNIFIED.md `Open questions` section)** — keep the reservation wrapper as a read-through fallback forever (the wrapper is dead code post-step-6; a permanent read-through fallback is the worst of both worlds); do the refactor in one big-bang PR (each step is independently shippable + reversible; batching loses the rollback granularity + multiplies the blast radius); keep the per-booking verify modal alongside the per-room coverage preview (the post-refactor booking has no per-room modal surface; `rooms[i].status` is the only state, mutated in one click); migrate the `Reservation` interface to an alias that pretends nothing changed (the destination is "the reservation IS the booking"; keeping the alias is a permanent indirection that future readers have to learn; better to drop the interface and have one fewer thing to maintain); add a `phase: "BAR-01"` flag to the migration at the booking-doc level (the global `BOOKING_UNIFIED_*` feature flags are simpler + sufficient for the 7-step migration; a per-doc flag is over-engineered for the migration's shape); keep the reservations listener post-step-6 (the inline `onSnapshot(collection(db, "reservations"))` effect in `AdminContext.tsx` — there is no `subscribeToReservations` function despite MRB-15-09's test name — plus the `getDocs` in `LiabilityTab.tsx`; both read from the deleted collection; the post-refactor admin surfaces read from the booking's payments subcollection directly). **Gates** — MRB-15 (the transactional counters work is the natural milestone — current state ✅; the BAR-01 step 6 deletes the MRB-15-03 code; the source-text tests for the counters are also deleted in that step), FOL-05 (the sibling-flip pass + its 38 source-text tests are deleted in BAR-01 step 6; the FOL-05 decision entry is marked "superseded by #202" at the start of BAR-01 step 1), FOL-03 (the reads-before-writes ordering is preserved in spirit but no longer load-bearing for payment handlers; the FOL-03 GOTCHAS entry becomes "best practice, not critical" at step 0), the BOOKING-UNIFIED.md spec is the source of truth for the migration's shape + step ordering + the deletion list. **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in BAR-01)** — subcollection-vs-array choice for the `rooms` field when N>20 (defer to the white-label era when individual bookings might hit 50+ rooms), per-room age-tier pricing (CHD-13 mirrors the `/book` shape on the homepage; deeper per-tier pricing is a separate project), per-room discount types (today's discount scope is reservation-level via MRB-12's segmented control; per-room discounts would extend the discount system, not the booking shape).

The 5 fields cannot drift because they no longer exist as a written field. The 15 new characterization tests for `deriveReservationCounters` in `shared/__tests__/booking-folio.test.ts` pin the derivation read path. **What this changes for the BAR-01 plan** — BAR-01 destination shape (1 booking, 1 lifecycle, 1 payment path, N rooms in `rooms[]`) is now further deferred by trigger condition 4 (availability has moved off per-room-doc conflict checks to room-type inventory). The 5 counter fields + paymentStatus are no longer a concern for BAR-01 (BAR-02 closed them); the remaining BAR-01 work is the per-room queryability + the `rooms[]` array + the sibling-flip pass. **Gates** — MRB-05 (N>1 aggregate helper is the canonical reservation-scope payment-status reader; BAR-02 still uses it, just at read time instead of write time), MRB-12 (admin reservation-scope paid-amount aggregate is unchanged; the rows chip + pill read the derivation instead of the header), FOL-03 (FOL-03 sibling-flip pass is preserved for the per-child status transitions; the fields are harmless dead data), also derive `totalPrice` / `actualDateRange` / `aggregateRevenueAllocation` / `cancellationLiability` (those are real denormalized values with separate write paths + their own audit; out of scope for BAR-02).



| 204 | **BAR-03 Extract the FOL-05 sibling-flip into one shared helper (proposed + shipped 2026-08-08 on `refactor/bar-03-reservation-scope-transition-helper`; v0.264.19 → v0.264.20):** the FOL-05 sibling-flip pass — pre-read every child of the reservation via `where("reservationId", "==", id)`, decide per-child whether to flip (verify / add: `payment-confirmed` if covered + flippable; reject: `pending` if `payment-uploaded`), queue per-sibling `transaction.update` calls, touch the reservation header — is now shared between the 3 handlers via the new `applyReservationScopePaymentTransition` + `preReadSiblingChildren` helpers in `guest-app/server/handlers/reservationScopeTransition.ts`. The pre-BAR-03 pass was open-coded in all 3 handlers; the 3 copies were byte-equivalent at the per-child status flip but the FOL-05 pattern (pre-read + per-child decide + per-sibling `transaction.update` + header heartbeat) was re-implemented 3 times. Each handler now passes a per-handler `rule.decide` callback (the per-child decision — return a write payload + new status, or `null` to skip); the helper handles the per-sibling `transaction.update` + the post-update statuses array + the reservation header heartbeat. The per-handler `rule.decide` callback is hand-rolled because the flip criteria + the write payload differ (verify / add: `payment-confirmed` + 3 stamps; reject: `pending` + 5 stamps). The target booking is excluded from the sibling flip pass (the helper skips by id-matching the `targetBookingId` argument) so the target's own status update stays in the handler (the write payload differs from the sibling write payload in every handler). **Driving motivation** — the #202 BAR-01 spec core complaint was "every new multi-room feature needs the same shape of fix" (FOL-03 + FOL-05 sibling-flip). FOL-06 (apply-discount) and FOL-07 (partial refund) were on the same path: every future multi-room action would re-derive the same shape of fix. BAR-03 answers #202 by giving future multi-room actions a one-call API for the shared work. The pre-BAR-03 open-coded FOL-05 pass is now in one place; FOL-06 + FOL-07 will call the helper with a per-handler rule (no need to re-derive the pre-read + the per-sibling update + the header heartbeat). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The per-child status transitions are byte-equivalent to pre-BAR-03. The header heartbeat is preserved (per BAR-02: a heartbeat `updatedAt` only, no `paymentStatus` mirror write). The helper does NOT touch the target booking (the target own status update stays in the handler because the write payload differs from the sibling write payload in every handler). **What this changes for the FOL-03 invariant** — preserved. The helper call `await preReadSiblingChildren(...)` is the read "completion" point in the SDK eyes. The `applyReservationScopePaymentTransition` helper queues `transaction.update` calls inside the same `runTransaction` as the target own status update. The post-helper `transaction.update(bookingRef, ...)` (the target own update) sits AFTER the pre-read, satisfying the SDK "all reads before all writes" rule. **What this changes for the test surface** — the 38 FOL-05 source-text tests are rewritten: the 23 in `fol-05-verify-reservation-scope.test.ts` replace the "open-coded pass" assertions with "the helper is called" assertions; 1 new helper-existence test (the helper + the 3 interfaces); 1 new shared-FOL-05-contract test (every handler pre-read sits before the first write; every handler helper call is inside the `runTransaction`; no handler open-codes the sibling loop); the pre-BAR-03 assertions that the open-coded pattern is GONE (the helper owns the loop). 1 mrb-15-08 test updated to assert the helper guard owns the legacy-skip behavior. **What this changes for the FOL-06 / FOL-07 future work** — FOL-06 (apply-discount) will call the helper with a per-handler rule that flips children to a "discounted" status (or whatever the discount lifecycle is); FOL-07 (partial refund) will call the helper with a per-handler rule that flips children to a "refunded" status. Both will need ~10 lines of new code per handler (the rule callback + the target own status update) instead of ~50 lines (the pre-read + the post-update array + the per-sibling `transaction.update` loop + the header heartbeat). The pattern is the same: handler-local status check + target update + helper call. **Gates** — FOL-05 (the per-child status transitions are byte-equivalent to pre-BAR-03; the 38 FOL-05 source-text tests are rewritten, not removed, so the contract is preserved), FOL-03 (the reads-before-writes invariant is preserved structurally — the helper call `await preReadSiblingChildren(...)` is the read completion point; the `transaction.update` calls inside the helper are queued AFTER the pre-read), BAR-02 (the `paymentStatus` mirror is still not written; the helper `transaction.update(reservationRef, { updatedAt })` is a heartbeat only). **Rejected** — keep the open-coded FOL-05 pass (every future multi-room action would re-derive the same shape of fix; the #202 "every new feature needs the same fix" complaint is real, this answers it; the pre-BAR-03 ~50 lines per handler × 3 handlers is the cost the helper collapses), use a generic OO abstraction with classes + interfaces (the helper is a pure function + 3 interfaces; OO would add ceremony without value; the helper is 5 minutes to read + 3 lines per handler to use), put the helper in `shared/utils/bookingFolio.ts` (it depends on the Firestore SDK; the shared package is pure functions; the helper belongs in the server-handlers layer), add the helper to a generic `transaction-helpers.ts` (the helper is FOL-05-specific; a generic name would lose the FOL-05 contract; the file is `reservationScopeTransition.ts` to make the scope clear), inline the helper into each handler (defeats the purpose; the open-coded shape is exactly what BAR-03 is collapsing), make the helper also do the target own update (the target write payload differs from the sibling write payload in every handler — verify / add write `payment-confirmed` + 3 stamps; reject writes `pending` + 5 stamps; the per-handler target update has handler-specific logic around the `fullyPaid` / `transitionedToPaymentConfirmed` / `isTargetInPaymentUploaded` flags; inlining the target update would force the helper to know about every handler per-target semantics).

| 205 | **Walk-in create skips Turnstile; staff auth is the only friction (proposed 2026-08-08 on `fix/new-booking-audit-2026-08-08`):** the staff walk-in create path (`POST /api/bookings/create-walkin`) does NOT call `verifyTurnstile` on the way in. The pre-#205 audit (F3) raised the question: "anyone who can grab a staff ID token from a kiosk or an ex-employee can hit the create-walkin endpoint with no friction." The answer: staff sessions are already gated by (a) the Firebase Auth staff login (email + password + a valid staff claim on the ID token) and (b) the admin role-claim check at the API route (`authenticateStaff`).
