Both are stronger than the Turnstile bot check on the public create path — Turnstile proves the request came from a browser, but a staff session proves the request came from a human with valid credentials. The walk-in endpoint is a *trusted* surface; a leaked staff token is the same blast radius as a leaked staff account (revocation = the staff-side credential rotation). **Driving motivation** — the audit (2026-08-08) of the booking flow raised the F3 question; the audit's own assessment was "probably acceptable for now, but worth a one-line decision so it doesn't drift." The decision keeps the no-Turnstile shape but records the rationale so a future reviewer doesn't see it as a gap. **What this changes for the bot stack** — nothing. The public + corporate booking flows still run Turnstile + honeypot + 5/min rate limit on create. The walk-in surface inherits the staff-tenant's auth posture. **What this changes for the rate limit** — nothing. The walk-in endpoint shares the `bookings-reschedule:30/min/IP` rate-limit bucket (decision #180 / MRB-14) for `add-room` + `reschedule` + the walk-in create path. Staff under a slow lane can still create one walk-in per ~2 seconds; not enough to hit the cap in normal desk operation. **What this changes for the audit** — the F3 finding is closed. The "no Turnstile on walk-in" line in the audit report becomes a "no, by design" reference to this decision. **Gates** — Auth/Roles (the staff auth + role-claim gate is the only friction on the walk-in create; both are server-side and stronger than Turnstile), AUDIT-2026-08-08 (F3 closed), BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT (the New Booking modal's submit path is unchanged; the staff session is the trust boundary). **Rejected** — add Turnstile to walk-in create (a staff session with a valid ID token is already a stronger signal than Turnstile; adding Turnstile on top would add latency for the desk with no security gain), require a separate "walk-in password" the desk types per booking (the staff-claim on the ID token is the canonical desk signal; a second password is friction without value), restrict the walk-in endpoint to admin role only (front desk needs to create walk-in bookings as part of their normal workflow; the front desk role is the second tier under admin per AUTH-ROLES; demoting the endpoint to admin-only breaks the desk workflow), audit log every walk-in create for retroactive review (a useful future addition but out of scope for this decision; the create transaction already stamps `handledBy: currentUser?.uid` on the booking doc, which is the audit trail).

| 206 | **NBS-2026-08-08 booking-flow audit fixes (F1, F2, F4, F5, F8, F9, F10, F11, F12, F13) shipped 2026-08-08 on `fix/new-booking-audit-2026-08-08`;** the 2026-08-08 booking-flow audit (covering public `/book` + corporate `/corporate/book` + admin New Booking modal + admin Calendar create) closed 10 of 13 findings in one batch: **(F1) admin walk-in now preallocates `bookingId` + `reservationId`** in a `useState` lazy init on the modal; the same pair is reused across retries inside the modal session, so a retry-after-uncertain-response hits the server's idempotency replay path (MRB-02 / decision #164) instead of creating a duplicate booking. Calendar create gets the same preallocation. **(F2) error recovery CTA on guest + corporate pages** — the previous catch only auto-redirected for the "Room no longer available" message; every other 4xx stranded the user on Step 3 with no next step. The fix adds a `submitErrorAction: "back-to-step-1" | "retry" | "none"` discriminator; the sticky footer renders a matching CTA. The MAX_STAY_NIGHTS / MAX_ADVANCE_DAYS / past-checkin / rate-limit cases are mapped to "back-to-step-1" or "retry" so the spec's "every error has a next step" rule is satisfied. **(F4) no fake walk-in emails/phones** — the previous walk-in path wrote `walkin-${Date.now()}@example.invalid` + the literal string `"n/a"` to Firestore when the desk left the email/phone fields blank. The fake email then occupied the match-by-email field for every later Spark Rewards link / /my-booking lookup / contact-inquiry reply. The fix requires both fields at submit time and stores the desk-entered values. **(F5) consent wiring on guest `/book`** — the previous `canConfirm` only read `termsConsent` (Step 3 checkbox), but the server validates `guestDetails.consent` (Step 2). The fix ANDs both checkboxes so the gate matches the server's expectation. The duplicate-checkbox UX is a pre-existing smell; a future refactor should consolidate. **(F8) Calendar create now collects the adult/child split + extra bed count** the same way the New Booking modal does. The pre-F8 path only collected `guestCount` (the total), so a 3-guest booking in a 2-adult room silently defaulted to "all adults, no extra beds" — bypassing the EXB-03 overflow check. **(F9) walk-in reset on success no longer seeds the next stay with `roomType: ""`** — the previous `roomTypes[0]?.value || ""` while the catalog was still hydrating produced a permanent empty dropdown. The fix seeds with `""` and lets the existing effect re-sync to the first loaded type on the next paint. **(F10) corporate checkIn/checkOut URL fallback** — a direct hit to `/corporate/book?checkIn=invalid` previously landed the form with the bad value and a blank date picker. The fix validates the URL value against the `YYYY-MM-DD` shape; an invalid or missing value falls back to today's Manila date. **(F11) auto-redirect timer is held on a `useRef` + cancelled on unmount + cancelled on the manual-nav CTA** — the previous bare `setTimeout` raced a user-initiated nav (clicking the back CTA before 5s elapsed) and clobbered the URL. **(F12) corporate confirm-step URL params** — preserved the `step: "confirm"` write so the confirmation page deep-links correctly. **(F13) walkin `isSubmitting` is reset on success** — the existing `finally` block already handles it; this decision records the contract. **Driving motivation** — the 2026-08-08 audit found the SEV-2 issues listed above plus 3 SEV-3/4 nits; closing them in one batch keeps the booking flow's three surfaces (public + corporate + admin) on a consistent contract. The fixes preserve the existing test contracts (no test changes for handlers; the new source-text tests pin the new client-side contracts). **What this changes for the API contract** — `addWalkinBooking` now accepts optional `preallocatedBookingId` + `preallocatedReservationId` parameters (back-compat: absent = auto-mint, the historical default). **What this changes for the UI contract** — the walk-in modal now requires a real email + phone at submit time (the previous placeholder-write path is gone); the Step 3 sticky footer on guest + corporate now renders a recovery CTA. **What this changes for the test surface** — 6 new source-text test files: `admin-app/src/__tests__/nbs-2026-08-08-walkin-preallocation.test.ts` (the preallocation contract), `admin-app/src/__tests__/nbs-2026-08-08-walkin-required-fields.test.ts` (the no-fake-email guard), `admin-app/src/__tests__/nbs-2026-08-08-walkin-reset-room-type.test.ts` (the F9 reset guard), `admin-app/src/__tests__/nbs-2026-08-08-calendar-adult-child-extrabed.test.ts` (the F8 steppers), `guest-app/tests/api/nbs-2026-08-08-booking-error-recovery.test.ts` (the F2 + F11 + F5 contract), `guest-app/tests/api/nbs-2026-08-08-corporate-error-recovery.test.ts` (the F2 corporate mirror + the F10 URL fallback). **Gates** — MRB-02 (the preallocation contract matches the public path's pattern; the server's `WalkinBookingSchema` already accepts the optional `reservationId` per decision #164), CHD-01 (the calendar's adult/child split goes through the same `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` server validation; the F8 fix sends both fields so the EXB-03 overflow check fires for staff-created bookings too), BOOKING-FLOW (the Step 2 + Step 3 consent wiring matches the server's `guestDetailsSchema.consent: z.boolean()` validation), BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT (the New Booking modal + the Calendar create modal now share the same preallocation + required-fields contract), GOTCHAS (no new GOTCHAS rules introduced; the existing "client must always submit a real email/phone" rule now applies to walk-ins too). **Rejected** — do a single "fix all audit findings" PR (this decision IS that PR; the batching keeps the 10 fixes in one logical commit so the audit verdict is reproducible from the diff), refactor the duplicate Step 2 + Step 3 consent checkboxes (a real smell but out of scope; the F5 fix is the minimum that prevents the silent server 400), move the walk-in modal email/phone validation to the server side (the fix is at the client; the server already accepts any string; the silent placeholder write was the real bug), add a settings flag to opt out of the walk-in email/phone requirement (every hotel will require this; no operator asked for the opt-out; the flag would add maintenance surface without a corresponding benefit).

| 207 | **BFP-01 Registration PDF pre-fills the silog checkbox from `booking.breakfastSelections` (proposed + shipped 2026-08-09 on `feature/bfp-01-registration-pdf-breakfast-prefill`):** the registration PDF's "Breakfast Silog Selections" block at `BookingsPage.tsx:2905-2986` now pre-fills the matching silog checkbox per guest per night when staff pre-selected one in the booking drawer (`selectedBooking.breakfastSelections["${date}-guest-${g+1}"]`). The pre-selected item renders a **filled brand-color box (`pdf.rect(..., "FD")`) + white checkmark drawn as two line segments + bold label in the brand color**; the other silog options stay as hollow checkboxes so the guest can still change their mind at check-in. Helper line updates from "Circle or check your choice for each guest per day." to "Pre-selected choices are marked; circle or check to change." **Driving motivation** — the registration form is a printable the guest signs at check-in, but staff have been capturing the guest's silog choice in the drawer for the kitchen prep view (Dashboard "Today's Breakfast Prep" + Reports "Daily Kitchen Prep" already consume `breakfastSelections`). The PDF was a blank checklist that ignored the captured data — the printed form drifted from the digital record. The drawer pre-check is now the source of truth for the printed form. **Match is by silog `name` (not `id`)** because the drawer's `<select>` stores the item name (per `BookingsPage.tsx:5594`). Editing a silog name in Settings after capture will silently drop the pre-check for that booking — accepted trade-off, matching the drawer's persistence shape. A future "match by id" refactor would require a one-line write change in the drawer's `handleBreakfastSelection` (store `item.id` as the value) + a small follow-up to migrate existing selections by name-lookup against the current `breakfastConfig.silogItems` list. The checkmark is drawn as two `pdf.line()` segments instead of a unicode `✓` glyph because the `setPdfFont` helvetica fallback doesn't ship a reliable tick glyph — the two-line approach is font-agnostic and matches the existing `setDrawColor(255, 255, 255)` pattern used elsewhere in the file. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `breakfastSelections` is already on the booking doc; the PDF just reads it. **What this changes for the test surface** — one new `it()` block in `admin-app/src/__tests__/pdf-generation-repair.test.ts` asserts the helper text update, the `b.breakfastSelections?.[${date}-guest-${g+1}]` read, the `FD` mode rect, the two checkmark line segments, the white draw color, and the bold/normal font flip. The pre-existing breakfast-block test (`renders breakfast silog selections as compact inline guest rows`) still passes — the hollow-rect path is preserved verbatim in the `else` branch. **MDs** — `plan/features/EMAIL-PDF-STORAGE.md` §Breakfast section bullet + the §Checklist gain a "Drawer pre-fill (2026-08-09)" line and a new ticked checklist row. **Gates** — EMAIL-PDF-STORAGE (the registration form's section is now wired to the drawer's persistence; the receipt PDF's "breakfast" line + the booking confirmation email's "breakfast" line are unchanged — those are price-axis surfaces, not the printable form), BSP-01 (the `breakfastSelections` hydration in `AdminContext.tsx` is the read path the PDF now depends on; already shipped, unchanged), the daily-kitchen-prep Reports aggregator (`ReportsPage.tsx:1050-1056`) keeps its own `breakfastSelections` consumer path — no conflict with the new PDF consumer, both are read-only. **Rejected** — render the pre-checked item as a circled-letter "A/B/C" instead of a checkmark (the two-line checkmark is more scannable on a printed page; circled letters are easier to smudge when the guest signs over them with a pen), pre-fill all silog items the guest has tried historically (the drawer stores one selection per `${date}-guest-${g+1}` key — there's no history of "tried" silogs in the data model; would require a new field), show the captured selection as a label-only line (no checkbox) so the guest can't override it (the explicit project decision is "pre-selected choices are marked; circle or check to change" — the guest can always change their mind at check-in; removing that affordance would be a worse UX than the original blank form), show the captured selection as a separate "Already on file" row above the silog options (visual noise; the filled-checkbox + bold-label in-row is denser and matches the existing checklist shape), change the drawer to store `item.id` as the value (a one-line write change but a behavior change for in-flight bookings — existing name-keyed selections would need a name-to-id migration against `breakfastConfig.silogItems`; out of scope for the BFP-01 fix; tracked as a Phase 2 micro-improvement if a name-edit incident ever happens in production), add the test to a new test file (the `pdf-generation-repair.test.ts` file already has a "renders breakfast silog selections as compact inline guest rows" test in the same shape; the new test lives next to its peers). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in BFP-01)** — change the drawer to store `item.id` and migrate existing name-keyed selections (would eliminate the silent-drop-on-rename edge case; one-line write change + a one-time migration in `AdminContext.tsx` snapshot mapper); add a per-cell "Guest changed their mind" stamp on the registration form that lets the desk mark which silog was actually served (the kitchen-prep view already tracks `breakfastServed` per `${date}-guest-${g+1}` key; surfacing it on the form would unify the "what was captured" + "what was served" surfaces; out of scope for the printable-form pre-fill); render a small "(from booking drawer)" tag next to the pre-filled checkbox to make the source of the pre-check explicit (the bold brand-color label + the helper-line copy already signal it; the tag would be visual clutter for staff who already know where the data comes from).

| 208 | **CLS-01 Lifecycle transition confirmation modals (proposed + shipped 2026-08-09 on `feature/cls-01-lifecycle-transition-confirmation-modals`):** the three lifecycle transition buttons in the drawer's primary action footer (`Confirm booking` / `Verify guest ID & check in` / `Review folio & check out`) now open a `ConfirmStatusModal` shell instead of firing `handleStatusTransition` directly. The shell wraps the existing `<Modal>` (focus trap, ESC, backdrop, framer animations, mobile bottom-sheet for free) and accepts `confirmTone` (`primary` / `warning` / `danger`) so the CTA inside the modal matches the button the desk just clicked. Each call site composes the transition-specific context (children: room, stay, payment, breakfast flag, folio summary) and a confirm handler. **Driving motivation** — operator-facing UX feedback on 2026-08-09 ("can we add modals for every step? like when I click Verify and Check-in button, a confirmation modal opens to confirm check-in, also in Review folio & check-out a modal pop-up also will show if the user if ready for check-out or need to pay for balance,etc"). The pre-CLS-01 admin surface had two visual smells that the spec called out: **(1) naked status transitions** — `Confirm booking` and `Verify & check in` fired `updateBookingStatus` directly on click with no confirmation affordance, so a mis-click on a busy desk silently moved a booking through a one-way status flip (especially costly for `checked-in`, where the room flips to occupied); **(2) split-path check-out** — the `Review folio & check out` button branched inside its onClick: `balance > 0` opened the legacy UnpaidCheckoutForm modal, `balance = 0` fired `handleStatusTransition("checked-out")` directly with no modal at all. A zero-balance check-out was a single click that flipped a booking to its final state with no "are you sure?" affordance — the worst UX of the three (the most consequential click, the least confirmation). The new pattern is one consistent shape: every lifecycle transition gets a confirmation modal that shows the relevant context, the desk reads + confirms or cancels. **Unified check-out modal (the new UCO-02/03 host)** — the pre-CLS-01 UnpaidCheckoutForm modal was a separate modal that only opened when balance > 0; the new check-out modal always opens, and the children body is conditional: `balance = 0` → green "Folio fully paid" callout, no reason form, primary CTA "Complete check-out"; `balance > 0 + not blocked` → amber balance callout + the UCO-02/03 reason form (shortcut chips + textarea capped at 500 chars) + warning-tone CTA "Check out with ₱X due"; `server returned "Front Desk cannot complete"` → red blocked callout + custom footer (Close button only — no confirm pair). The shell is `admin-app/src/components/ConfirmStatusModal.tsx` (new, ~120 lines: the title + subtitle + description + icon + children + Cancel/Confirm footer; the `footer?` prop overrides the default pair for the blocked path); the three modals + the three confirm handlers live in `BookingsPage.tsx`. The legacy `showUnpaidCheckoutForm` state + the legacy `<Modal title="Unpaid checkout — reason required">` body + the `unpaidCheckoutSubmitting` flag are deleted; renamed to `showConfirmCheckOut` + `confirmCheckOutPending` and lifted into the shared shell. **What the user sees** — every lifecycle transition now shows a small panel (room + stay + payment / folio summary) with a clear CTA + a Cancel button. A mis-click no longer flips a booking; the desk gets one final "are you sure?" affordance before any one-way status change. The check-out flow is one consistent shape regardless of balance — the desk no longer gets a different UX for the zero-balance case. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The status flips write the same `bookings/{id}` fields as before; `unpaidCheckoutReason` is still stamped immutably on departure (UCO-02/03 audit requirement); the blocked-when-admin-only gate is server-side (the `Front Desk cannot complete` server error still flips the modal to the blocked UI). **What this changes for the test surface** — one new `describe` block in `admin-app/src/__tests__/phase-11.7-confirm-forms.test.ts` with 7 source-text tests: the import, the 3 button-block no-longer-fires-direct (the regression guard catches a re-introduction of the naked transition), the 3 confirm handlers exist, the reason form is reachable inside the unified modal, the zero-balance branch hides the reason form + shows the green "Folio fully paid" callout, and the shell wraps the existing `<Modal>` (not re-implementing the focus trap or the ESC handler). The pre-existing `uses useTwoClickConfirm for destructive confirmations` test was updated to check for the 3 new state flags instead of the legacy `showUnpaidCheckoutForm` name. **What this changes for the audit trail** — nothing. The `unpaidCheckoutReason` write path is unchanged; the `unpaidCheckoutApprovedBy` admin gate is unchanged; the only thing the modal changes is the click-time affordance. **MDs** — `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md` §Lifecycle Transition Confirmation Modals (new section), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (this entry, ✅ shipped 2026-08-09 in the v0.266.0 release). **Gates** — UCO-02/03 (the reason form + the admin-only blocked gate are preserved verbatim inside the new shell's children body; the audit-trail stamp is unchanged), the existing `<Modal>` shell (the focus trap, ESC handler, backdrop dismiss, framer animations, mobile bottom-sheet are all reused — CLS-01 is purely a composition layer), the payment success modal (the post-verify flow's `Confirm Booking` / `Confirm with Balance` CTAs still work; the post-verify success modal is a separate flow from the drawer's primary action footer), the `handleStatusTransition` helper (still used by the legacy `payment-confirmed` → `confirmed` flow in the New Booking walk-in path — that's a server-driven flow, not a desk click, so no modal wrap needed; the `handleStatusTransition` direct call is still appropriate there), BOOKING-FLOW (no guest-side changes; the new modals are admin-only). **Rejected** — replace every existing modal in the drawer (record payment, verify payment, add charge, add refund, apply/reject discount, move room, add room, cancel booking, cancel store order) with the new shell (those are full forms with their own submit logic, error states, and success callbacks — the new shell is a confirmation panel, not a form; replacing them would be a much bigger refactor with no UX win; can be done as a separate cleanup if visual consistency becomes a problem), add a "skip this confirmation next time" checkbox per modal (a footgun — the mis-click protection is the whole point; a "skip" toggle would re-introduce the naked transition; the cost of one extra click per status flip is the right trade-off), put the check-out modal on its own URL/route (the drawer is the natural home; a separate route would break the staff's mental model of "I'm in a booking, I'm working on it"), use `window.confirm()` for the new confirmation modals (the phase-11.7 audit (2026-07-23) explicitly retired `window.confirm()` / `window.prompt()` from the admin app — the existing `useTwoClickConfirm` pattern is for destructive *form* flows, not for *status transition* confirmations; the new `ConfirmStatusModal` is the right granularity for the lifecycle shape), show the reason form *outside* the unified modal (e.g., as a separate "Add reason" step after the modal closes) (the UCO-02/03 audit requires the reason before the status flip fires, so the form has to be a precondition to the confirm button — splitting it out would be a worse UX than the legacy UnpaidCheckoutForm modal), pre-fill the reason field with a default value (the audit-trail value must be a real staff-written reason, not a default; the existing `UNPAID_REASON_SHORTCUTS` chips serve the common cases and the textarea is the free-text fallback — both unchanged from the legacy shape), show a per-room scope selector (the reservation-scope vs room-scope selector is already a separate decision (MRB-13 / decision #166); the drawer's check-out button already renders the existing `renderActionScope("reservation")` chip for N>1 reservations, and the new modal is per-booking — the scope choice happens at the row level, not inside the modal), drop the admin-only blocked path from the new modal (the admin-only gate is a server-side requirement, not a UI choice; the modal must surface the server's `Front Desk cannot complete` error so the desk knows to escalate; the custom footer prop is the 5-line carve-out that makes this work), require the desk to type a separate "I confirm check-out" string before the button enables (the existing pattern is "click the warning-tone CTA, the server is the source of truth for the gate"; a confirmation-string gate is a separate UX pattern, not part of the lifecycle transition shape), wire the check-in modal to the UCO-02/03 reason form (the UCO-02/03 form is specific to the unpaid-checkout audit; check-in is a different audit, doesn't need a reason; the check-in modal just shows the booking context + the breakfast flag, no form), unify the three lifecycle transition modals into a single dynamic `LifecycleTransitionModal` component (the 3 modals share the shell, but their context (children) is genuinely different — check-in shows room + stay + guests + breakfast, check-out shows folio + reason form; collapsing into a dynamic component would be a string-driven dispatch on `status` which is a worse code shape than 3 clean call sites of a shared shell), bump the BookingDrawer's `BookingDrawerActionFooter` component to take a `confirmationModal` prop (the footer is presentational — the confirmation-modal state belongs to the parent that owns the click handler, which is `BookingsPage`; lifting it into the footer would force a refactor of the 30+ other footer consumers or duplicate the prop default to "no modal" for everyone else).

| 209 | **RFO-01 Reservation-lookup form-open surface — wire the R- ref end-to-end (proposed + shipped 2026-08-10 on `fix/rfo-01-reservation-ref-lookup-surface`; v0.266.6 → v0.266.7):** the MRB-09 reservation-scope emails expose the `R-YYYYMMDD-NNNNN` reservation ref to the guest (subject line, body header, receipt PDF filename) but the `/my-booking` page could not actually use it — the form's ref field was hardcoded to `SI-…` only, the auto-lookup `useEffect` only read `?ref=`, and `lookupUrl()` in `email.ts` generated `?ref=<SI>&token=<token>` deep links keyed off the first child's per-booking ref + token. The R- ref the guest saw in the email subject was decorative. The pre-#209 server contract was already wired (`lookupSchema` accepted an optional `reservationRef` with the `R-\d{8}-\d{5}` regex per MRB-10 / decision #169, and `handleLookupBooking` dispatched on it with the email-second-factor gate against `reservation.leadGuestEmail` / the first child's `lookupToken` per `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts:9348-9426`) — only the client + the email deep link needed to learn the new shape. The fix has 3 client-side pieces + 1 server-test addition: **(1) `lookupUrl()` in `email.ts` branches on `booking.reservationRef` + `booking.guestEmail`** — when both are present the URL is `?reservationRef=<R>&email=<email>`; the legacy `?ref=<SI>&token=<token>` shape stays as the N=1 + per-child fallback (byte-equivalent magic links keep working). The lead guest email is `.toLowerCase()`-encoded to match the server's zod `.toLowerCase()` on read. **(2) The auto-lookup `useEffect` in `BookingLookupPage.tsx` reads `searchParams.get("reservationRef")` in priority over `?ref=`** — the R- deep link lands the guest on the page and the existing email second factor gates access (a bare R- with no email/token renders the form so the guest can fill in the missing piece instead of a hard error). The signature guard keys off the active ref so the two URL shapes never double-fire. **(3) The form's single ref input accepts both `SI-…` and `R-…`** — the input's label is now "Booking or Reservation Reference" and the placeholder shows both shapes (`SI-20260612-042 or R-20260815-00012`); the submit handler runs `RESERVATION_REF_REGEX.test(trimmedRef)` from `@spark-inn/shared/utils/references` and routes the lookup to the new `performLookup` 4th `reservationRef` arg. A bare R- with an empty email shows an inline error ("Please enter the email you used to book alongside the reservation reference.") that mirrors the server's 400 copy. The `performLookup` payload construction reads `reservationRef` first, falling through to the existing `ref + token` / `ref + email` / `ref alone` / `email alone` / `token alone` paths. **Driving motivation** — the 2026-08-10 audit found the spec was 90% shipped (server ready, MRB-09 emails visible) but the last 10% (client routing + email deep link) was the whole user-facing flow. A guest who saw `R-20260810-00001` in the subject line + body header + receipt PDF had no way to actually use it to look up their booking without scrolling down to find a per-child SI- ref they may not have copied. The R- is the public identifier; the SI- is the per-child implementation detail. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The server's `reservationRef` field is unchanged; the email's `lookupUrl` only changes the deep-link URL it generates. **What this changes for the test surface** — 1 new test file `guest-app/tests/api/rfo-01-reservation-ref-lookup-surface.test.ts` with 13 source-text tests across 4 describe blocks: the email's new `?reservationRef=…&email=…` branch + the legacy fallback, the page's auto-lookup useEffect (reservationRef priority, missing-credential early-return, prefill behaviour), the form's R- regex route + inline error copy + label/placeholder, and the `performLookup` 4-arg signature + the email-auth-mode wiring. The 148-test MRB-09 + MRB-10 + bookings-lookup suites still pass byte-equivalent (the legacy `?ref=` / `?token=` / `?email=` paths are unchanged). **MDs** — `plan/features/BOOKING-LOOKUP.md` §MRB-10 (note that the page reads `?reservationRef=` + the form accepts both ref shapes; the existing `Bare reservationRef (no credential)` edge case stays at 400 but the page renders the form first), `plan/features/EMAIL-PDF-STORAGE.md` §MRB-09 (the `lookupUrl()` description now calls out the reservation-scope branch + the N=1 byte-equivalence). **Gates** — H2 (the email magic link path is unchanged; the token's `lookupToken` is still the credential for the per-child N=1 case; the new reservation-scope path is a separate code branch), MRB-10 / decision #169 (the server contract is unchanged — `handleLookupBooking`'s reservationRef dispatch + the email-second-factor gate are the source of truth; the page + the email are the consumers that finally wire through them), MRB-09 / decision #168 (the reservation-scope email body + receipt PDF filename are unchanged; only the `lookupUrl()` helper that generates the "View my booking" CTA changes), BF-21 + RA 10173 (a bare R- ref is not enough to enumerate reservations — the server returns 400 without a credential, the page mirrors the same copy locally, and the `Booking not found.` 404 reply is unchanged when the credential mismatches), the existing per-child deep links (`?ref=<SI>&token=<token>`) stay byte-equivalent — the N=1 magic link in the existing `booking-submitted` / `payment-confirmed` / `booking-confirmed` / `checkin-reminder` / `booking-rescheduled` / `payment-rejected` / `booking-cancelled` / `discount-rejected` / `early-checkin-request` emails is unchanged because the `lookupUrl()` fallback path still runs. **Rejected** — make the email deep link carry `?reservationRef=<R>&token=<token>` (the first child's token) instead of `&email=<email>` (the lead guest's email) (the email is more shareable + matches the spec's "public identifier" framing; a token is per-child and not what the guest pastes into a form — the email-second-factor is also the more honest "what does the guest have?" answer: they have the email the booking was confirmed with), emit two CTAs in the email (one for R-+email, one for SI-+token) (the form accepts both shapes, so one CTA is enough; a "Reservation reference" + "Booking reference" pair on the same email would be confusing — the R- is the reservation's identifier, the SI- is a per-child implementation detail), drop the SI- magic link from the email entirely (the per-child magic link is still the strongest credential for the N=1 case; the existing `?ref=<SI>&token=<token>` path is byte-equivalent and lets the guest click straight from the email without typing anything), change the form to two separate fields (one "Booking ref" + one "Reservation ref") (the form's single-input design is the cleanest UX — the guest pastes whatever the email subject carries, the page figures out which one; a two-field design adds a decision the guest doesn't need to make), keep the existing form label "Booking Reference" + the SI- only placeholder (the label was misleading post-MRB-09 — the email subject shows a "Reservation reference" header + a Reservation Ref line on the receipt PDF, so a field labeled "Booking Reference" reads as a step backward; the new "Booking or Reservation Reference" label + the two-shape placeholder surface the contract honestly), let the page auto-lookup with a bare R- and a missing email (the server's 400 is load-bearing — a bare R- is enumerable at ~100k/day per property; the page mirroring the 400 copy locally is faster UX + keeps the rate limit clean), surface the new behaviour behind a feature flag (the change is fully client-side + the server contract is unchanged; a feature flag would only protect a future server-side rollout, which is not in scope), bump the version MAJOR (the change is wire-compatible — the server's `reservationRef` input has been in production since MRB-10 (2026-08-02); the email + page are consumers of an existing server feature; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning). **Audit amendment (same day, 2026-08-10) — the bare R- path is now accepted server-side, not just client-side.** The initial #209 spec kept the original MRB-10 / decision #169 contract (a bare R- returns 400 unless a credential is supplied) because the MRB-10 rationale ("~1k/day enumerable") sounded load-bearing. Post-shipment testing found the rationale was wrong: the 5-digit sequence widening in the H3 hardening batch (`shared/utils/references.ts:60`) raised the R- namespace to 99,999/day, identical to the SI- namespace — the same defenses (Turnstile + 10/min rate limit + 3-failure 1-hour backoff) already gate the SI- `ref`-alone path. The page's form copy reads "Enter your booking reference or the email you used to book", and the R- is the reservation's public identifier (subject line + body header + receipt PDF filename), so a guest who pastes just the R- expects it to work without a second factor. The amendment removes the `else { return res.status(400) ... }` branch from `handleLookupBooking` — the bare R- now falls through to the same first-child lookup + `enrichAndRespond` call the email + token paths use. The 404 reply is identical for not-found / credential-mismatch / R- alone cases so the response is not a ref-existence or email-existence oracle. The page's `handleSearch` is also updated: the previous "Please enter the email you used to book alongside the reservation reference" inline error is retired; a bare R- is submitted as-is with `performLookup("", trimmedEmail || undefined, undefined, trimmedRef)`. If the user did type an email, the server still uses it as a stricter second-factor gate against `reservation.leadGuestEmail`. **What this changes for the test surface** — the `mrb-10-reservation-lookup.test.ts` test "a bare reservationRef with no credential returns 400 with a clear message" is replaced with "a bare reservationRef with no credential falls through to enrichAndRespond (R- alone path)" which pins the new fall-through shape; the rfo-01 test file's "a bare R- ref without an email shows a clear inline error" test is replaced with "a bare R- ref without an email is submitted as-is (no inline error)"; 3 new server tests in the rfo-01 file pin the R- alone fall-through + the schema shape + the retired 400 message (16 total tests, was 13). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. **MDs** — `plan/features/BOOKING-LOOKUP.md` §MRB-10 (the Optional `reservationRef` input bullet is rewritten to call out the R- alone acceptance; the bare R- edge case row in §Edge Cases is updated from "400 with a clear message" to "200 with the reservation view"; the Data & Logic Checklist line for RFO-01 is updated to drop the "credential required" phrasing), this audit amendment, the #209 decision entry is now self-referencing. **Gates** — H3 hardening (the 5-digit sequence widening is what raised the R- namespace to parity with SI-; without it the bare R- path would still be enumerable at ~1k/day and the original MRB-10 rationale would hold), H2 (the email magic link path is unchanged; the token's `lookupToken` is still the credential for the per-child N=1 case; the new reservation-scope path is a separate code branch), MRB-10 / decision #169 (the SPEC is changed — the credential-required R- path is retired; the new contract is the one described in this audit amendment), MRB-09 / decision #168 (the reservation-scope email body + receipt PDF filename are unchanged; only the `lookupUrl()` helper that generates the "View my booking" CTA + the server's `handleLookupBooking` R- branch changed), BF-21 + RA 10173 (the response shape is unchanged for every other case; the bare R- path returns the same `kind: "reservation"` / `kind: "single"` shape every other path returns; the 404 reply is unchanged for not-found / credential-mismatch cases; no new PII surface), `plan/docs/SECURITY.md §Booking Lookup Security` (the enumeration defense list is the same: Turnstile + 10/min/IP rate limit + 3-failure 1-hour backoff; the R- alone path inherits the same defenses the SI- alone path already uses; no new rate-limit or backoff bucket needed). **Rejected** — require the email-second-factor gate on the R- alone path (the original MRB-10 rationale was wrong; the R- has the same enumeration risk as the SI- ref-alone path, which the project already accepts; requiring a credential here would be inconsistent with the SI- path and would create a UX trap where the form's header copy "Enter your booking reference or the email you used to book" stops being honest for the R- case), keep the 400 reply but soften the page's copy to be less prominent (the 400 IS the inconsistency — softening the copy would paper over the real bug), require both R- AND email for the R- alone path (i.e. a stricter shape than the SI- ref-alone path) (the form's header copy says "either or", not "both"; a stricter R- shape is the wrong way to go — the right move is to align R- with SI-, not to make R- stricter), add the R- alone path behind a feature flag (the R- alone path is a wire-compatible relaxation of the 400 response — the input shape is unchanged, the only difference is the response code; a feature flag would protect nothing meaningful and would split the codebase), bump the version MAJOR (the change is wire-compatible — the `reservationRef` input shape is unchanged; the response is the same `kind: "reservation"` / `kind: "single"` shape the email + token paths already return; the only difference is that a previously-400 input shape now returns 200 with the same response body; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).

| 210 | **CRL-06 + CRL-07 fix — cancellation snapshot fallback chain (proposed + shipped 2026-08-11 on `fix/crl-06-cancel-snapshot-fallback`):** the cancel-preview + destructive-cancel handlers used to read `cancellationPolicySnapshot` off each child booking and pass `null` straight to `evaluateCancelPreview` when the field was missing — which it always is, because the create flow writes the snapshot to the **reservation header** (`reservations/{id}.cancellationPolicySnapshot`, per CRL-05), never to the child booking doc. The helper's `fallbackContext.checkInDateKey: ""` is empty string, so `evaluateCancellation` falls through to `checkInMs = Date.now()` and the time math silently reports "0.0 hours before check-in" for every future-dated booking — a paid guest with a 19-day-out check-in would have their cancel reported with `policyRefund: 0` and the staff would have no signal that the policy owed money. The fix is a 3-link fallback chain in both `handleCancelPreview` and `handleCancelBooking`: **(1) child booking's own snapshot** (if the future write path starts stamping it, the child wins), **(2) the reservation header's snapshot** (the canonical source today, already loaded in the same read pass for the reservation-scope branch + a fresh `transaction.get` in the per-child branch), and **(3) a synthesized legacy-default snapshot** from the booking's own `checkIn` Timestamp for legacy pre-MRB-01 null-`reservationId` bookings (where there's no reservation header to read). The synthesized snapshot carries the same `cutoffHours: 48` / `refundPctBefore: 100` / `refundPctAfter: 0` / `source: "legacy-fallback"` defaults the helper already uses when the helper's own `fallbackContext` is null, so legacy bookings evaluate against the same rules — the only difference is the `scheduledCheckInTime` is derived from the booking's real `checkIn` field instead of `Date.now()`. The fix is a runtime fallback, not a backfill: every existing `bookings/{id}` in production continues to lack a snapshotted policy and the handlers do the right thing on read. The pre-fix code path had a comment claiming the fallback context was for "the legacy null-snapshot case" — that comment was right about the intent but the `checkInDateKey: ""` made the intent a lie; the synthesized snapshot in the handler makes the comment honest again. **Driving motivation** — the user found the bug by opening a cancel modal on `SI-20260810-00002` (a 19-day-out booking, ₱2,400, ₱0 collected). The system showed "0.0 hours before check-in" + "Policy refund: ₱0" + "No collected payment needs to be refunded or retained" — the third line is correct (no money was paid), but the first two lines were wrong, and would have been silently catastrophic on a paid booking. The destructive-cancel path had the same bug in 3 spots (reservation-scope liability + per-child liability + the per-child looked-up-booking shape) — fixing only the preview would have left the actual cancel still eating the refund for paid beyond-cutoff cancels. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The fallback is read-time only. The `cancellationPolicySnapshot` field on the child booking is still unwritten in the create flow (a follow-up could stamp it for defense-in-depth, but the runtime fallback makes it unnecessary). **What this changes for the response shape** — nothing. `evaluateCancelPreview`'s input shape is unchanged; the helper still reads `cancellationPolicySnapshot` per child; the only difference is the handler now populates that field with a real value instead of null. **What this changes for the test surface** — 1 new behavior test in `shared/__tests__/cancellation-preview.test.ts` (a 5-day-out check-in produces `hoursRemaining > 100` + `isBeforeCutoff: true` + `refundPct: 100`, proving the helper honours the snapshot's `scheduledCheckInTime`); 3 new source-text guards in `guest-app/tests/api/crl-06-cancel-preview.test.ts` (the preview handler's reservation-snapshot fallback + the legacy synthesized snapshot, plus the destructive-cancel's per-child branch reading the reservation header). All 553 shared tests + 40 crl-06 tests + 1247 admin tests pass. The 3 pre-existing failures in `bookings-staff-email-dedup.test.ts` (status code 400 instead of 200 on walk-in create) are unrelated to this fix — verified by `git stash` + re-run. **MDs** — `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md` §CRL-06 + §CRL-07 (the "snapshot lives on the reservation header" note gets a sibling bullet for the runtime fallback chain, so a future reader doesn't re-derive the 0.0-hours bug). **Gates** — CRL-05 (the create flow's snapshot is unchanged; the helper's input shape is unchanged; only the handler's read-side resolution is augmented), CRL-06 (the preview response shape is byte-equivalent for every booking that has a snapshot; the only behaviour delta is the now-correct time math for bookings that lack one), CRL-07 (the liability snapshot for the destructive cancel is now correct for every booking, not just the few that happen to have a child-level snapshot), BAR-02 (no new derived fields; the read-time fallback is local to the cancel handlers, not a project-wide derive rule), H1 hardening (no new auth surface; the preview + cancel endpoints are unchanged at the API level). **Rejected** — write `cancellationPolicySnapshot` to the child booking at create time + backfill existing bookings (the runtime fallback makes it unnecessary for correctness; the cost (every create path gains a 2nd write + a 1-shot backfill script + a `shared/utils/cancellation.ts` consumer that depends on the child-side field) is real and the benefit is zero — the reservation header is the canonical source and the handlers already load it), fix the helper to accept a "reservation snapshot" input + resolve the fallback inside the helper (the helper would need to know about reservations, which violates the current separation — the helper takes a flat `CancelPreviewChild` array; the reservation context lives in the handler; pushing the resolution down would couple the helper to the MRB-01 reservation concept), add a `hotelConfig` read in the preview handler to build a richer synthesized snapshot for the legacy case (the synthesized snapshot already uses the project's standard 48/100/0 defaults + the booking's real `checkIn` — the only thing `hotelConfig` would add is a non-default `checkInTime` / `timezone`, which the helper would re-derive from its own `getCheckInInstant` anyway; the cost of loading `settings/hotelConfig` on every cancel-preview call outweighs the marginal accuracy gain), inline a `helper.resolveSnapshot(child, reservation)` function in the handler to share the chain (the 3 fallback links are 3 lines of `||`; an extracted function is more code than the inlined chain at every call site, and the chains differ in the synthesized-snapshot branch — the reservation-scope cancellable children + the looked-up booking share one synthesized snapshot, the legacy case computes its own), drop the synthesized-snapshot branch entirely and only fix the reservation-scope case (the legacy null-`reservationId` path has the same bug, just with a different cause — the legacy pre-MRB-01 bookings have no reservation header to fall back to; without the synthesized branch, every legacy booking's cancel would still report 0.0 hours + 0% refund), bump the version MAJOR (the fix is a server-side correctness patch — no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).

| 211 | **CRL-06 follow-up — cancel-preview handler hoists `reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot` to the outer `try` scope (proposed + shipped 2026-08-11 on `dev` @ `d5d37f7`, follow-up to decision #210):** the CRL-06 + CRL-07 fix from earlier today (decision #210, commit `eca4737`) introduced a scope bug in `handleCancelPreview`. The new `reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot` const was declared **inside** `if (hasReservation) { ... }` (the reservation-scope branch where the reservation header is loaded + read into `reservationSnapshot`), but referenced at the **outer-scope** `lookedUpBookingForHelper.cancellationPolicySnapshot` ternary — the anchor's snapshot fallback chain, which lives below both the `if (hasReservation) { ... }` and `else { ... }` branches. Once the `if` block closed, the binding went out of scope; the outer reference tripped a `ReferenceError: reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot is not defined`, and `handleCancelPreview` returned 500 on every modern booking (the ternary at line 6811 short-circuits for legacy `!hasReservation` bookings, so the bug only fired for bookings that have a `reservationId` — i.e. every post-MRB-01 booking, the common modern path). The user-visible symptom on staging (`stg.sparkinnbohol.com`) was the page-level fallback "Could not load the cancellation preview" + the warning "The cancellation will still proceed if you tap confirm — the financial breakdown is unavailable" — the destructive cancel still ran (it computes its own liability snapshot from a different binding `reservationCancellationPolicySnapshotForLiability`, which is also declared in the outer `try` scope, so the same class of bug was avoided there), but the preview returned no `policyRefund` / `hoursRemaining` / `isBeforeCutoff` / `staffProcessingRequired` so the guest + staff had no signal of what the cancel would actually do. **Driving motivation** — same class of bug as RSC-01 (commit `0964a84`, 2026-08-11, the reschedule hoist): a binding hoisted into the wrong scope, the post-block reference tripping a `ReferenceError`. The user opened the cancel modal on staging and the browser dev console showed the 500 + the `reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot is not defined` ReferenceError. RSC-01 was the reschedule; this is the cancel-preview; both are the same day, same file, same "const declared inside `if`/transaction, referenced after the block closes" shape. The corrective technique is identical: hoist the binding to the outer scope, default to `null`, assign inside the block. **Fix — two lines change in `handleCancelPreview`:** **(1)** declare `let reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot: any = null;` at the top of the `try` block, alongside `reservation` + `cancellableChildren` + `allocationSubtotal`; **(2)** drop the `const` keyword on the existing line inside the `if (hasReservation) { ... }` branch so the assignment becomes a re-assignment to the outer binding instead of a new inner-scope `const`. The ternary at the anchor's snapshot pick is unchanged: `bookingData.cancellationPolicySnapshot || (hasReservation ? reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot : legacyFallbackSnapshotForAnchor)` — the legacy `!hasReservation` branch still picks `legacyFallbackSnapshotForAnchor` (the variable's default `null` is never read). The per-child `cancellableChildren` chain at `data.cancellationPolicySnapshot || reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot` is also unchanged — the inner-scope reference at that line was correct (the binding existed in the `if` block's scope, and the hoist preserves the name + the assignment shape). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The snapshot resolution is unchanged at the wire shape; only the lexical scope of the variable moved. **What this changes for the API contract** — nothing. The preview endpoint's response shape is byte-equivalent for every input; the only difference is that the previously-500 response now returns 200 with the real `preview` payload (the same shape the per-child `!hasReservation` path already returned). **What this changes for the test surface** — nothing. The existing CRL-06 source-grep tests in `guest-app/tests/api/crl-06-cancel-preview.test.ts` pin the **literal string** `reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot = reservationSnapshot.cancellationPolicySnapshot || null` (test at lines 317-318) and the **literal string** `data.cancellationPolicySnapshot || reservationCancellationPolicySnapshot` (test at lines 312-314); both literals still match the new shape — the only change is the `const` → bare-assignment on the assignment line + the new `let` line above the `if`. All 144 cancel/cancellation tests pass (40 crl-06 + 19 crl-02 + 25 crl-03-04 + 14 crl-07 + 17 crl-08 + 17 intercom + 12 mrb-13); the 3 pre-existing `bookings-staff-email-dedup.test.ts` failures are unrelated (verified by `git stash` + re-run on the unmodified `dev` HEAD). **MDs** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` "Recently shipped" section gains a sibling entry to RSC-01 since both are the same hoist-out-of-block pattern, same day, same file. **Gates** — CRL-06 (the preview response shape is now byte-equivalent for every input; the only behaviour delta is the previously-500 responses returning 200 with the real preview), CRL-07 (the destructive-cancel handler was never affected — it computes its own liability snapshot from a different binding `reservationCancellationPolicySnapshotForLiability`, which is also declared in the outer `try` scope, so the same class of bug was avoided there; the same-day commit `eca4737` shipped the fallback chain correctly in that handler), RSC-01 (same hoist-out-of-block pattern, same day, same file, different handler; the two are sibling bugs that share a single corrective technique), H1 hardening (no new auth surface; the preview endpoint is unchanged at the API level). **Rejected** — inline the snapshot at the outer call site as `reservationSnapshot?.cancellationPolicySnapshot || null` and skip the variable entirely (the variable is referenced twice — once inside the `if` for the per-child chain, once outside the `if` for the anchor chain; inlining only the outer reference would mean reading `reservationSnapshot` from the outer scope, but `reservationSnapshot` is also block-scoped to the `if` — the right fix is to hoist, not to chase the inner binding out), wrap the outer reference in a `try`/`catch` and return a degraded preview shape on `ReferenceError` (the right fix is to make the binding resolvable, not to paper over a scope bug with a `try`/`catch` — the degraded shape would silently hide every future reference error, including the same bug in a different binding), re-read the reservation header at the outer reference site to avoid the scope issue (an extra read + the "stale read" risk if the reservation header changes between the two reads — the hoist is one line and one variable), make the ternary at the anchor evaluate lazily via a helper that closes over the right binding (over-engineering for a 1-line scope fix), bump the version MAJOR (the fix is a server-side correctness patch that un-breaks a 500 response; no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).

| 212 | **RPT-04 — Reports page reads `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` + merges with `payments` (proposed + shipped 2026-08-11 on `dev` @ `03f6926`, follow-up to MRB-04 Phase 2.x / decision #159):** `handleAddRefund` writes refunds to **two** subcollections (per MRB-04 Phase 2.x, the "both, as separate paths" design) — `reservations/{id}/refunds/{refundId}` for new reservations (post-MRB-01, has `reservationId`) and `bookings/{id}/payments/{refundId}` for legacy pre-MRB-01 null-`reservationId` bookings. The Reports page (`admin-app/src/pages/ReportsPage.tsx`) was only subscribing to `collectionGroup(db, "payments")`, which catches the legacy path but misses every new-reservation refund. The Firestore rules already granted `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` read access to staff (RPT-03, `firestore.rules:475`) — the page just didn't query it. `LiabilityTab` already used `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` correctly (RPT-03 added the collectionGroup rule for it). Two sibling pages, two different collectionGroups, one of them incomplete — the classic dual-source-from-shipping-a-refactor regression. **Driving motivation** — the user opened the admin Reports tab on staging and the "Refunds" KPI card + the per-method breakdown (cash / gcash / bank / card / paypal / other) + the Daily Close transactions ledger all undercounted: the live read missed every new-reservation refund. The full backup export (admin-only XLSX) had the same blind spot — the per-booking `bookings/{id}/payments/` loop only catches the legacy path, so the spreadsheet's "Payments" sheet was missing the new-reservation refund rows too. **The fix — dual collectionGroup subscription + merge in three places:** **(1)** add a `useState<RawReportPayment[]>([])` `rawRefunds` state alongside the existing `rawPayments` state; **(2)** add a second `onSnapshot(collectionGroup(db, "refunds"), ...)` subscription that maps each `reservations/{id}/refunds/{refundId}` doc into the same `ReportPayment` shape (`type: "refund"`, `amount: negative`, the rest of the columns identical to the payments mapper). The record's `data.bookingId` is stamped at write time (`bookings.ts:7617`, the per-room child the refund was recorded against), so the `bookingDisplayById.get(bookingId)` lookup resolves the bookingRef for the display. The mapper handles both shapes — `reservations/{id}/refunds/{refundId}` uses `data.bookingId` (with the path's parent — the reservationId — as a defensive fallback), `bookings/{id}/refunds/{refundId}` uses the path's parent directly; **(3)** the `payments` useMemo merges both arrays via `[...rawPayments, ...rawRefunds].map(...)` so the existing display resolution (`bookingDisplayById.get(payment.bookingId)`) runs once over the union. `refundsTotal` + the per-method breakdown + the Daily Close ledger + the per-booking receivables all pick up the new-reservation refund rows automatically — no per-surface change needed. **(4)** the full backup export gains a `getDocs(collectionGroup(db, "refunds"))` after the existing per-booking `bookings/{id}/payments/` loop, pushing each doc to `paymentRows` with the same row shape (`Type: "refund"`, `Amount: negative`, the rest of the columns identical to the legacy refund rows). The `bookings.find((item) => item.id === bookingId)?.bookingRef` lookup resolves the bookingRef for the spreadsheet cell. The export's "Payments" sheet now contains every refund row, not just the legacy ones. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The dual subcollection is the canonical design (decision #159). **What this changes for the API contract** — nothing. The reports page's response shape is unchanged; the consumer just sees more rows. **What this changes for the test surface** — new `admin-app/src/__tests__/rpt-04-refunds-collectiongroup-merge.test.ts` (5 source-text guards): both `collectionGroup(db, "payments")` and `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` are subscribed; the `payments` useMemo merges via `[...rawPayments, ...rawRefunds].map(...)` with `rawRefunds` in the deps array; the new-reservation refund mapper resolves `data.bookingId` (with the parentDocumentId fallback); the full backup export reads `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` and pushes to `paymentRows` with `Type: "refund"`. The existing `rpt-03-liability-collectiongroup.test.ts` (7 source-text guards) still passes — the new test complements it. All 1252 admin tests pass; all 144 cancel/cancellation tests pass; typecheck clean for both apps. The 3 pre-existing `bookings-staff-email-dedup.test.ts` failures (BF-04 staff email dedup) are unrelated — verified by `git stash` + re-run on the unmodified `dev` HEAD. **MDs** — `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` "Recently shipped" section gains a sibling entry to the RSC-01 + CRL-06 follow-up entries since RPT-04 is the third sibling regression from shipping a refactor (the others: RSC-01 the reschedule hoist, CRL-06 follow-up the cancel-preview scope hoist, RPT-04 the refunds collectionGroup blind spot — all the same "shipping a refactor created an incomplete read" shape, all same day, all the same corrective technique: add the missing read at the consumer). **Gates** — MRB-04 (the dual subcollection is the canonical design; this fix is a read-side catch-up, not a model change; the `handleAddRefund` writer is unchanged), RPT-03 (the existing `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` Firestore rule is reused — no rules change needed; the page just now queries it), MRB-02 (the per-booking `data.bookingId` resolution is the same shape the per-room lookup uses; the record's `data.bookingId` is the per-room child the refund was recorded against), H1 hardening (no new auth surface; the refunds collectionGroup rule was already staff-gated). **Rejected** — read `reservations/{id}/refunds/{id}` per reservation (the export already iterates over `bookings`, not `reservations`; a per-reservation read would require loading the reservation header list separately, then resolving each refund's parent — the collectionGroup query is one round-trip and matches the live subscription's pattern), keep a separate "Refunds" sheet in the export XLSX for new-reservation refunds (splits the audit trail; the desk expects every refund on the same "Payments" sheet, same as the live view; a separate sheet would silently hide the bug class), migrate the legacy `bookings/{id}/payments/` refund entries to `reservations/{id}/refunds/` (the dual-source is the canonical design; legacy bookings don't have a reservation header to parent the new subcollection under; the runtime read-time merge handles both shapes cleanly), add a `payments/` collectionGroup read on `reservations/{id}/payments/` (the writer only writes to `payments/` for new reservations via the normal add-payment path, not the refund path; the `bookings/{id}/payments/` read the export already does covers the new-payment rows through the per-booking loop; no separate reservation-payments collectionGroup is needed), bump the version MAJOR (the fix is a server-side consumer catch-up — no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).


| 213 | **Intercom audio routing (Phase 13, proposed on `feature/intercom-audio-routing`):** the intercom has shipped with WebRTC voice calls since Phase 8 (decision #94 second-call-wins, decision #97 sound mute, the post-feature call ringtone + notification chime), but every audio surface — call audio, notification chime, call ringtone — has always followed the browser/system default output. On a desk machine with a USB headset plugged in, that's the opposite of what the operator wants: the call should land in the ears, the chime should land on the desk speaker. This feature splits the two surfaces and pins each to a per-staff output device via the Audio Output Devices API (`HTMLMediaElement.setSinkId`, `navigator.mediaDevices.selectAudioOutput`). **Surface model** — two surfaces, one master toggle, persisted on `guests/{uid}.audioRouting = { enabled, callOutputDeviceId, ringtoneOutputDeviceId, updatedAt }` + sibling `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` audit stamp. The owner-write allowlist on `guests/{userId}` (`firebase/firestore.rules` + `plan/docs/SECURITY.md §guests`) is extended with `audioRouting` + `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` so a staff member can change their own routing without admin help; `role`, `isActive`, and the staff-audit fields remain admin-only. **Implementation** — `admin-app/src/hooks/useAudioRouting.ts` opens one `onSnapshot(guests/{uid})` at the `AdminProvider` level; every consumer (AudioSettingsPage, IntercomInboxPage notification sound, the WebRTC remote stream created in `acceptCall`, the pre-answer ringtone) reads from the same live value. The notification sound path was previously Web Audio API (`fetch + decodeAudioData + createBufferSource`) — that buffer path can't honour `setSinkId` portably (Safari/Firefox don't), so it's replaced with a hidden `<audio>` element + `setSinkId` on the same URL. The call ringtone was previously an oscillator tree (`createOscillator` × 2 with 14 Hz LFO warble + `createGain` envelope) — that goes through `AudioContext.destination` which has no per-stream output device — so it's now rendered once to a 1.0s WAV blob via `OfflineAudioContext` (same 853/960 Hz envelope + same 0.4/0.2/0.4 cadence) and played through a hidden `<audio>` element with `setSinkId`. The `audioBufferToWav` helper is unit-tested for the WAV header shape so a future refactor that drops the header / bit depth / mono-mix doesn't silently regress. The WebRTC remote stream was already an `<audio>` element (just without `setSinkId`) — `acceptCall` now calls `applyAudioSink(remoteAudio, "call")` after assigning `srcObject`. **UI** — new `AudioSettingsPage` at `/audio`, sidebar entry between Settings and the bottom of the nav (Headphones icon), accessible to both front-desk and admin (not admin-only like the other `/settings` page — every operator with a headset needs it). The page has a master toggle, two device pickers (call / ringtone), per-picker "Test" button (plays a 0.5s 440Hz sine through the selected device + toast on success), a "Pick…" button that opens `selectAudioOutput` (Chrome/Edge only), and a "Reset to default" button. The page degrades to an unsupported-browser screen when `setSinkId` isn't on the prototype — Firefox + iOS Safari — and the runtime still saves preferences so the operator's intent is recorded even when the runtime ignores them. **Open questions deferred** — guest-side call audio routing (the same `setSinkId` pattern works on the guest app's hidden `<audio>` element, but the browser-support matrix makes the value small since most guests are on mobile Safari; defer until guest-side data shows complaints), per-shift device memory (`localStorage` cache keyed by `(uid, machineId)` so a rotating shift doesn't re-pick the headset every morning; defer until a hotel reports it), per-device volume offset (USB headset at 100% ≠ built-in speaker at 100%; defer — the chime is short and the operator can adjust system volume on either device). **MDs** — `plan/features/INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING.md` (new, ~270 lines: surface contract, browser support matrix, edge cases, open questions), `plan/features/INTERCOM-INBOX.md` §Notification sound + §Call audio + §Call ringtone (the three audio surfaces get explicit `applyAudioSink` callouts + a back-reference to the new spec), `plan/docs/BACKEND.md §guests/{userId}` (the new `audioRouting` + `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` fields), `plan/docs/TYPES.md §Staff` (the new `audioRouting?: AudioRouting` optional field), `plan/docs/SECURITY.md §guests` (the self-write allowlist extension), `firebase/firestore.rules §guests` (the `affectedKeys().hasOnly([...])` allowlist gains the two new field names), `plan/admin-app/CLAUDE.md` (the new `/audio` page row in the route table + the new `guests/{uid}` collection in the Firebase Usage table), `CLAUDE.md` (the new spec in the feature file list + the new read bundle row), `shared/types/index.ts` (the new `AudioRouting` interface, exported via `shared/index.ts` re-export). **Gates** — INTERCOM-INBOX (the call audio + notification sound + call ringtone paths all remain byte-equivalent at the listener for the default-output case; the new `<audio>` element + the pre-rendered WAV produce the same audio the previous Web Audio API code did; the only behaviour delta is the new `setSinkId` call which is a safe no-op on unsupported runtimes), INTERCOM-GUEST (the guest-side call audio is unchanged; the routing hooks into the admin-side `<audio>` element only; the guest-side `useAudioRouting` is a future extension tracked in the spec's Open Questions), FD-01 (E2E audit 2026-07-17: the `guests/{userId}` self-write allowlist is the right shape; the new fields are append-only, the existing `role` / `isActive` / staff-audit fields remain admin-only, so a non-staff member can't self-promote via this new field), H1 hardening (no new auth surface; the rule change is an allowlist extension on the existing owner-write branch, not a new write path), the audio-context-unlock pattern (the IntercomInboxPage's pointerdown/keydown unlock listener is preserved verbatim — the `<audio>` element path still needs a user gesture before the first `.play()` in every browser). **Rejected** — store the device IDs in `localStorage` (cross-device persistence is the value; a front-desk operator on a rotating shift would lose the headset pick on a different machine; the Firestore write is one round-trip and the snapshot hydrates in the same listener the rest of the auth-gated data uses), persist a single `outputDeviceId` field (the whole point is splitting call vs ringtone surfaces; a single field forces one device for both, which is the problem we're fixing), gate the Audio Settings page to admin-only (the front-desk operator is the primary user; making it admin-only would force a front-desk operator to ask an admin to pick their headset for them; the existing role-based access pattern on Rates / Members / Settings is for hotel-wide config, not personal device preferences), use `AudioContext.setSinkId` for the notification / ringtone paths (the Web Audio API's `setSinkId` has the same Safari/Firefox support gap as the media element version but adds a `MediaStreamAudioDestinationNode` + `<audio>` capture pipeline that has its own browser-specific quirks; the `<audio>` element path is the well-trodden ground that Chrome/Edge/Safari all support, and the OfflineAudioContext → WAV blob → `<audio>` element pipeline gives the same audio with a clean `setSinkId` story), route the call ringtone through a Web Audio API `MediaStreamAudioDestinationNode` + `<audio>` capture (works, but the Web Audio API graph has to be re-initialised on every routing change; the OfflineAudioContext pre-render path renders once and the WAV blob is reused for every subsequent play; the cost of rendering is amortised over the operator's session), migrate the existing `notif_sound_muted` localStorage key into the new Firestore field (the mute is per-device (a desk machine the operator doesn't want beeping overnight) and the routing is per-staff (the headset vs the speaker); the two are different axes and a single Firestore field would conflate them; the mute toggle stays in `localStorage` under `intercom-notification-muted` and the routing lives in Firestore; the Audio Settings page respects the mute flag by reading the same key — actually it doesn't yet, defer that to a follow-up; the current spec ships the routing feature and the existing mute toggle continues to work in the inbox header), ship a guest-side version in the same change (most guests are on mobile Safari where `setSinkId` doesn't work; the feature is a no-op for the common case; defer to a follow-up if guest-side data shows complaints; the spec's Open Questions section tracks this), make the Audio Settings page the new home for the `Bell` / `BellOff` mute toggle (the inbox header has a per-staff mute affordance with a tooltip that explains the localStorage backing; moving it to a separate page would lose the in-context discovery; the Audio Settings page is for *where* audio plays, the inbox header is for *whether* audio plays — two different concerns, two different surfaces), bump the version MAJOR (the feature is a new admin surface, not a wire contract change; no API change, no new guest-app field, no new data shape; `feat:` per Conventional Commits triggers a MINOR bump per `Husky` auto-versioning; the `audioRouting` + `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` fields are optional on `guests/{uid}` so existing docs continue to read byte-equivalent).

| 214 | **Concurrent front-desk call claim is an atomic `runTransaction` (proposed + shipped 2026-08-19 on `feature/call-staff-claim-transaction`; v0.273.4 → v0.275.0):** the `AdminContext.acceptCall` flow's claim step is wrapped in `runTransaction(db, async (tx) => { … })` instead of a plain `updateDoc`. The transaction atomically reads `calls/{roomId}` (via `tx.get(callRef)`), checks `data.status === "ringing"`, and writes `status: "active" + endedAt: null + acceptedBy: { uid, name, claimedAt: serverTimestamp() }` in one commit. The pre-#214 surface was a plain `updateDoc` of the answer + status — a best-effort last-write-wins race that let two front-desk staff both build a peer connection to the same guest (the second one's `peerConnection.setRemoteDescription` either failed or paired with stale SDP, the loser was left with a dead local connection, and there was no record of which staff actually answered). The new flow makes the first staff to commit the transaction the only one who proceeds to `getUserMedia` + `createAnswer`; every subsequent staff that tries the same room hits `data.status !== "ringing"` and the transaction aborts with `FirebaseError: Transaction failed all retries.` — the catch toasts "Call taken — Already answered by {Name}" (or "The guest hung up or another staff member answered first" if `acceptedBy` is not yet on the snapshot) and returns without building a half-built WebRTC connection. **Driving motivation** — operator question 2026-08-19 about the multiple-front-desks-on-the-same-call case. The pre-#214 behavior was acceptable for a single-staff hotel but created two concrete gaps for the 3-5-person desk: (1) the losing staff ends up with a non-functional banner that says "Connected" but has no working audio (the local peer connection is wired to a stale or never-arriving answer), and (2) the chat-thread audit trail ("Call answered at 2:14 PM · 3m 22s") does not record which staff member took the call. **Fix — four-part change.** **(1) `runTransaction` claim in `acceptCall`** — the first step of `acceptCall` is now the transaction. On success, `adminCallLocalAcceptRef.current = true` is set immediately (BEFORE `getUserMedia`) so the inbox state still attributes the call to this tab even if the post-claim `getUserMedia` fails. The final `updateDoc` that writes the SDP answer is trimmed to just `{ answer: { type, sdp } }` — the claim transaction is the single source of truth for `status: "active" + endedAt: null`, so a redundant re-flip would be a no-op. **(2) `acceptedBy` field on `calls/{roomId}`** — the transaction stamps `{ uid, name, claimedAt }` so the snapshot listener can hydrate the field for every tab. The snapshot mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:3795-3823` normalizes the Firestore timestamp into a `Date | null` and exposes it on the mapped `IncomingCall` as `acceptedBy: CallAcceptedBy | null`. Pre-#214 docs (no `acceptedBy`) map to `null`, so the renderer can fall through to the legacy Connected UI on legacy data. **(3) `IntercomInboxPage` three-state banner** — the banner branches on `incomingCall.status === "active" && incomingCall.acceptedBy?.uid !== currentUser?.uid` (the new `isClaimedByOtherStaff` flag). When true, the banner renders an informational grey surface ("Call Already Answered — Answered by {Name}. This banner clears when they hang up.") with NO Accept/Ignore/Mute/Disconnect buttons. The banner auto-dismisses when the winner's `declineCall` writes `status: "ended"` to the call doc — the snapshot listener's `activeCalls` filter excludes `ended` and `incomingCall` clears. The new testid is `data-testid="call-already-claimed-banner"` for downstream test pinning. **(4) Staff attribution in the call-answered audit trail** — `AdminContext.recordCallHistory` now writes a structured `callAnsweredByName: string | null` field on the `intercoms/{roomNumber}/messages` system doc (mirrors `calls/{roomId}.acceptedBy.name`), and prefixes the human-readable `text` with "Call answered by {Name} at {clock} · {duration}" when `adminCallLocalAcceptRef.current === true`. Pre-#214 messages + `call-missed` + `call-declined` keep the legacy un-attributed text. **`runTransaction` reads-before-writes contract** — the transaction body is `await transaction.get(callRef); if (status !== "ringing") throw; transaction.update(callRef, { … })`. The read completes before the write (FOL-03 pattern), so the SDK does not throw `Firestore transactions require all reads to be executed before all writes`. The transaction is also the gate: if it doesn't commit, the function returns BEFORE the no-offer branch's `adminCallAnsweredRef` set or the offer branch's `cleanupAdminCall` + `getUserMedia` + `createAnswer` chain. **Post-claim catch** — if the claim committed but a later step threw (`getUserMedia` denied, `createAnswer` failed, `updateDoc` rejected), the catch calls `cleanupAdminCall()` and writes `status: "ended" + endedAt: serverTimestamp() + endedReason: "accept-failed"` to the call doc. The `acceptedBy` stays on the doc as the audit trail of who tried to answer; `recordCallHistory` dispatches a `call-missed` system message because `adminCallAnsweredRef.current` is still false at this point (the call never actually connected). **Security rule gap** — the `calls/{roomId}` Firestore rule is `allow read, write: if true;` (the collection is fully open, the same as `intercoms` per the guest no-login model). The `acceptedBy` field is staff-writeable by any caller (matching the existing call-doc trust model). Future hardening could narrow this to `isStaff()` writes after the consent checkbox + per-room occupancy gate is in place (per FOL-01-style audit follow-up). **Known gap filed for next audit** — the `IntercomMessage` snapshot mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:3347-3366` has been silently dropping `messageType`, `callStartedAt`, and `callDuration` since the call-history-messages feature shipped (FOL-02 pattern — declared in the type, dropped on read, chat panel can't render the centered "Call answered" footer row). The #214 fix added `callAnsweredByName` to the mapper (the new field) but the pre-existing drops are a separate audit ticket; the chat panel's structured-system-message render is still dormant until that fix lands. **Test coverage** — 23 new source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-staff-claim-transaction.test.ts` pin the contract: (1) `acceptCall` body uses `runTransaction` with `tx.get(callRef)`; (2) the transaction throws on `data.status !== "ringing"`; (3) the transaction writes `acceptedBy: { uid, name, claimedAt: serverTimestamp() }`; (4) the catch toasts via `notify.info` and returns; (5) the post-claim catch writes `endedReason: "accept-failed"`; (6) the snapshot mapper hydrates `acceptedBy`; (7) the `IncomingCall` type declares `acceptedBy?: CallAcceptedBy | null` and the `CallAcceptedBy` sub-interface declares `uid + name + claimedAt?`; (8) `adminCallLocalAcceptRef` is a `useRef<boolean>(false)`, reset in `cleanupAdminCall`, set true on claim success; (9) `recordCallHistory` writes `callAnsweredByName` and prefixes the text with "Call answered by {staffName}"; (10) `IntercomInboxPage` imports `currentUser` from `useAdmin`, branches on `isClaimedByOtherStaff`, renders the "Call Already Answered" surface, uses the `call-already-claimed-banner` testid, and excludes Accept/Ignore/Mute/Disconnect buttons from the claim path; (11) `shared/types/index.ts` declares `callAnsweredByName?: string | null` on the canonical `IntercomMessage`. **MDs** — `plan/features/INTERCOM-INBOX.md` §Incoming call notification (the three-state banner + the claimer's display name in the loser UI + the `call-already-claimed-banner` testid), `plan/features/INTERCOM-GUEST.md` §Voice Call (note that the guest's UI is unchanged — the claim is a staff-side concern; the guest's peer connection still pairs with the first SDP answer that arrives at `calls/{roomId}.answer`), `plan/docs/BACKEND.md` (new `calls/{roomId}` schema section with the `acceptedBy` field, the `status` enum, and the `endedReason` audit stamps), `plan/docs/SECURITY.md §intercoms` (the `calls` collection rule + the audit-trail `acceptedBy` + the deferred hardening to `isStaff()` writes). **Gates** — INTERCOM-INBOX (the three-state banner is a strict superset of the pre-#214 surface; legacy pre-#214 call docs without `acceptedBy` continue to render the Connected UI byte-equivalent — verified by the `IncomingCall.acceptedBy` being `null` for legacy data), INTERCOM-GUEST (the guest's call flow is unchanged; the guest's peer connection still pairs with the first SDP answer regardless of who won the claim), INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING (the WebRTC remote stream + the call ringtone + the notification chime all continue to work for the staff member that won the claim; the loser tab never builds a peer connection so its `useAudioRouting` never attaches a remote stream), FOL-03 (the transaction body reads BEFORE writes; the helper is the gate that aborts the rest of the flow), FOL-02 (the snapshot mapper hydration contract extends to the new `callAnsweredByName` field; the pre-existing `messageType` / `callStartedAt` / `callDuration` drops are a separate audit ticket tracked in the Known gap paragraph above). **Rejected** — keep the pre-#214 last-write-wins `updateDoc` (the race leaves the wrong staff with a dead peer connection and no audit trail; the operator question 2026-08-19 is a real production concern for a 3-5-person desk; the `runTransaction` is the right primitive), use a Firestore security rule to gate the accept (the rule layer can detect `request.resource.data.acceptedBy` but cannot enforce "first wins" atomically — the SDK's `request.auth.uid` check would be a tie, not a claim;
