the transactional claim is the only correct shape), move the call claim to a Cloud Function (Vercel Hobby is at the function cap; the claim is a single Firestore read + a single write; doing it in the client SDK avoids the round-trip and the function-count tax), add a "I'll take it" round-robin assignment (overkill for a 3-5-person desk; the claim is "first-click-wins" which is the simplest fair shape; round-robin is a follow-up if the operator reports that "first wins" causes a race-condition feel — a future iteration could add a 1-second "queued" delay so staff see who else is interested), use a separate `callClaims/{roomId}` collection with a `claimedBy` doc (the claim belongs to the same call doc; a separate collection would require a cross-collection transaction or a separate listener; the call doc is the single source of truth for call state; adding a second collection is surface area for drift), show the acceptedBy name to the guest (the guest's UI doesn't need to know which staff took the call; the guest just sees "Connected" — the staff attribution is an internal audit trail, not a guest-facing concern), bump the version MAJOR (the change is wire-compatible for the guest side; the staff side gains a new banner state + a new toast + a new Firestore field on the call doc; `feat:` per Conventional Commits triggers a MINOR bump per `Husky` auto-versioning; the `acceptedBy` field is optional on the call doc so legacy call docs continue to read byte-equivalent).

| 215 | **FOL-02 call-history-messages mapper closure (proposed + shipped 2026-08-19 on `fix/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops`; v0.274.0 → v0.274.1):** the `IntercomMessage` snapshot mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:3347-3366` (the `.map()` inside the `intercoms/{roomNumber}/messages` listener) preserves EVERY field the `IntercomMessage` contract guarantees — `messageType`, `callStartedAt`, `callDuration` (the three pre-#214 drops), `callAnsweredByName` (the #214 new field), and the 11 always-on / optional fields that were already correctly hydrated. The pre-#214 surface silently dropped `messageType` / `callStartedAt` / `callDuration` since the `call-history-messages` feature shipped — the chat panel's centered "Call answered" footer row at `IntercomChatPanel.tsx:182` reads `msg.messageType` to decide between the Phone / PhoneMissed / PhoneOff icon and the muted-italic centered row, so without the mapper hydration the structured render was dormant and every system message fell through to the unstyled text body. The #214 fix (the `runTransaction` claim work) closed the three drops alongside adding the new `callAnsweredByName` field — but the #214 decision entry filed the drops as a "known gap for next audit" and the test file `call-staff-claim-transaction.test.ts` only pinned the new `callAnsweredByName` field. The #215 fix is the FOL-02 closure: (a) the misleading mapper comment (which still said the three fields were "pre-existing drops ... a separate audit ticket" when they were already hydrated) is rewritten to reflect the actual state, and (b) per-field source-text tests in the new `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts` pin the exact normalization shape for each of the 4 call-history fields. **Driving motivation** — the FOL-02 audit pattern in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` line 30 requires "per-field test that pins the exact normalization shape" for every contract field the mapper hydrates. The #214 PR shipped the hydration but only the new `callAnsweredByName` field was test-pinned; the three pre-existing drops were left to the "next audit" footnote without a test to enforce them. A future refactor that drops the `messageType` hydration would silently break the chat panel's centered footer-row render with no test failure. **Fix — three-part change.** **(1) Mapper comment at `AdminContext.tsx:3371-3382` rewritten.** The pre-#215 comment cited "decision #206 (2026-08-19)" — the actual decision number is #214 (the file already had #206..213 by the time #214 shipped). The pre-#215 comment also claimed the three fields were "pre-existing drops ... a separate audit ticket" — the #214 fix already closed the drops, so the misleading claim is replaced with a FOL-02-pattern reference that points at the per-field test file. **(2) New test file `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts`** with 6 source-text tests in 2 describe blocks: the 4 per-field hydration pins (one test per call-history field, each pinning the exact normalization shape — the `callStartedAt` Timestamp→ISO conversion, the `callDuration` `typeof === "number"` defensive coercion, the `messageType` `|| undefined` shape, the `callAnsweredByName` `typeof === "string"` defensive coercion with `null` for the absent case) + 2 contract-coverage pins that enumerate the always-on + optional fields the mapper already hydrates (so a future FOL-02 audit pass that re-enumerates the contract has a single source of truth for which fields are pinned). The mapper-body slice uses `indexOf` + brace-counting for the closing `});` so the slice is robust to nested function bodies (FOL-02 / MRB-15-10 hydrate-mapper pattern: the slice must end at the mapper's true close, not at a nested `}` substring). **(3) Decision #215 in `DECISIONS-FEATURES.md`** records the closure + the misleading-comment fix + the per-field test pattern. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The mapper was already hydrating the 4 fields after #214; this decision pins the contract. **What this changes for the test surface** — 6 new source-text tests in a dedicated file (separate from `call-staff-claim-transaction.test.ts` because the FOL-02 contract-enforcement test is scoped to the mapper, not to the runTransaction claim — different concern, different test file, per the project's "one test file per concern" convention). All 23 prior #214 tests still pass; the 6 new tests bring the admin-app source-text test count up. **What this changes for the chat panel** — nothing. The renderer at `IntercomChatPanel.tsx:182-204` was already reading `msg.messageType`; the #214 mapper hydration unblocked the render and the #215 tests pin the contract. The renderer does NOT currently read `callAnsweredByName` (the staff name) or `callDuration` (the seconds) — the text body already carries the formatted "Call answered by {Name} at {clock} · {duration}" line written by `recordCallHistory`, so the structured data is available for any future UI polish that wants to render a dedicated "Answered by Maria · 3m 22s" pill instead of relying on the text body. **MDs** — `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #215` (this entry), `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` is unchanged (the FOL-02 pattern entry is the contract; #215 is a follow-up closure, not a new pattern), `plan/features/INTERCOM-INBOX.md` is unchanged (the FOL-02 closure is read-side, the feature spec's Manual QA already covers the renderer behavior). **Gates** — FOL-02 (the per-field test pattern is the canonical contract enforcement; every contract field the mapper hydrates now has a pin), FOL-01 (the `paymentConfirmedAt` hydration pattern at the bookings mapper is the template; the call-history mapper uses the same Timestamp→ISO conversion for `callStartedAt`), MRB-15-10 (the room-types hydration closure is the precedent — when a mapper grows organically, the contract field list drifts from the hydration list, and a per-field test is the only way to keep them in sync; #215 follows the same pattern on the intercom-messages mapper), the `IntercomChatPanel` renderer is the consumer (verified to read `messageType` at line 182, so the mapper hydration is the gate that makes the structured render work). **Rejected** — fold the #215 fix into the #214 PR as an amendment (the #214 PR is already merged to dev; amending a merged PR adds friction without value; a focused separate commit is the cleaner audit trail), pin only the 3 pre-existing drops without `callAnsweredByName` (the FOL-02 pattern requires a per-field test for EVERY contract field the mapper hydrates; the 4th field's absence from the test file is a future audit gap), add a behavioural test that exercises the full chat-panel render against a fake IntercomMessage (a behavioural test would need a React Testing Library setup + a stub for the `useAdmin` context + a renderer mock — the source-text test is the cheap, deterministic FOL-02-style pin the project uses for mapper closures; the behavioural render test is a future addition if the chat panel grows other render paths that need coverage), add a server-side read test that confirms the Firestore `intercoms/{room}/messages` docs round-trip through the mapper (the Firestore SDK's serialization is not project-specific; the mapper is the only project-specific surface; the source-text test on the mapper is sufficient), use a more general "enumerates every IntercomMessage field" test instead of per-field tests (the per-field test pattern is the FOL-02 contract enforcement; a single "all fields present" test would miss a future refactor that drops one field while preserving the others — the per-field pattern is the bug-finding shape, not the coverage shape), bump the version MAJOR (the change is a test-pinning + comment fix on top of an already-shipped mapper; no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).

| 216 | **`intercoms/{roomId}/messages` create allowlist includes `callAnsweredByName` (proposed + shipped 2026-08-19 on `fix/intercom-message-rule-allowlist-closure`; v0.274.1 → v0.274.2):** the `firebase/firestore.rules:362-399` create rule for `intercoms/{roomId}/messages` now includes `"callAnsweredByName"` in the `keys().hasOnly([...])` allowlist, alongside the three pre-existing call-history fields (`messageType`, `callStartedAt`, `callDuration`). The pre-#216 rule was missing the new field, so every post-#214 call-history write from `AdminContext.recordCallHistory` was rejected by the rule and the "Call answered by {Name}" message never landed in the chat thread. The pre-#216 bug surfaced 2026-08-19 when the operator reported "i am not seeing the call history on the thread after the call, why is this?" — the cause was the rule's silent rejection of the writer's 14-key payload (the 10 always-on / optional message fields + the 4 call-history fields), not a client-side write failure. **`recordCallHistory` is wrapped in a `try/catch` that logs a `console.warn` and moves on** (per `AdminContext.tsx:3576-3580`), so the rule rejection was silent from the staff member's perspective — no toast, no banner, no error indicator. The pre-#216 call-history write payload was 14 keys, the pre-#216 rule allowlist was 13 keys, the rejection fired on the missing 14th key, and the call-history thread entry was never created. **Driving motivation** — the FOL-02 / FOL-03 / MRB-15-10 / MRB-15-11 audit pattern in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` line 30 (the "mapper must preserve every field the contract guarantees" rule) covers client-side mapper hydration. The #214 + #215 fixes pinned the mapper side end-to-end with source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts`. But the source-text tests only read the `AdminContext.tsx` source — they don't read `firebase/firestore.rules`, so the cross-file contract (client write payload vs server allowlist) was uncaught. The #214 PR added a new field to the writer without updating the server allowlist; the #215 PR was a mapper-closure follow-up that didn't touch the rule. Both PRs passed every test in the daily loop, but the live chat thread never received a "Call answered" entry from the moment #214 shipped. The pre-#216 surface would have shown up as "the new feature is in dev but doesn't work end-to-end" — the same shape as the INC-01 incident on 2026-07-17 (staff-only Storage reads broke the old app's post-upload `getDownloadURL`, blocking online-payment bookings; per `GOTCHAS.md` line 16). The rule gap + the silent `console.warn` is the same shape of bug — the source-text test surface didn't see the cross-file contract. **Fix — four-part change.** **(1) `firebase/firestore.rules:365-389`** — `"callAnsweredByName"` added to the `keys().hasOnly([...])` allowlist (one line). The inline comment is updated to call out the four call-history fields by name + the #214 + #216 decisions. **(2) `firebase/tests/intercoms-messages-call-history-rules.emulator.test.ts`** — NEW rule-emulator test with 8 tests in 2 describe blocks. The first describe pins the call-history fields' allowlist membership: the full 14-key happy-path payload is accepted, the `call-missed` and `call-declined` variants (where `callStartedAt` / `callDuration` / `callAnsweredByName` are `null`) are accepted, a non-allowlisted `role: "admin"` key is rejected (privilege-escalation guard), and a minimal payload with `callAnsweredByName` + a `randomExtraField` is rejected (the #216 regression-in-reverse: a future refactor that drops `callAnsweredByName` from the allowlist would fail this test). The second describe pins the `isStaff()` auth gate: a non-staff signed-in user (`role: "guest"`) and an unauthenticated client cannot create call-history messages, and the auth gate is universal (a non-staff user can't create a regular `front-desk` chat message either — the gate is on every create, not just call-history). The emulator test pattern matches the existing `firebase/tests/notifications.rules.test.ts` + `firebase/tests/guests-audio-routing-rules.emulator.test.ts`; the test loads the real `firestore.rules` into the Firestore emulator and exercises the actual access decisions, so an invalid-but-present rule (NC-02c's `keys().union(...)` shape bug) would fail here even though every source-text test would pass. **(3) `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts`** — 4 new source-text tests in a new `describe` block ("`firebase/firestore.rules` — call-history cross-file contract (decision #216, 2026-08-19)") that pin the rule's `hasOnly` array includes `callAnsweredByName` + the other 3 call-history fields + the 10 always-on message fields. The slice uses the `next allow update:` anchor instead of brace-counting (the rule expression is a `&&`-chained boolean with deeply nested parens — list literals, paren-wrapped clauses; the brace-counter finds inner `)` first and breaks the slice). The cross-file contract pin also references the emulator test path so the source-text test fails loudly if a future refactor deletes the emulator test (the source-text test is the cheap daily safety net, the emulator test is the gold standard; both are needed for the contract to be closed). **(4) Decision #216 in `DECISIONS-FEATURES.md`** records the closure + the cross-file contract pattern + the silent-`console.warn`-rejection shape. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The pre-#216 rule rejection prevented the call-history doc from being created; the post-#216 fix unblocks the create. Future call-history writes will land in the thread; the chat panel's `IntercomChatPanel.tsx:182-204` render (which already reads `msg.messageType` to draw the centered Phone / PhoneMissed / PhoneOff footer row) will start showing the "Call answered at 2:14 PM" / "Call answered by Maria at 2:14 PM" lines on every call. **What this changes for the test surface** — 4 new source-text tests (cheap, runs in the daily `npm test` loop) + 8 new rule-emulator tests (runs under `npm run test:rules` against the Java Firestore emulator). Total +12 tests; the admin-app source-text test count goes from 1914 to 1918 in the daily loop, and the `npm run test:rules` run adds 8 emulator tests. **What this changes for the operator UX** — the chat thread for every call from this commit forward will show the "Call answered by {Name}" (or "Missed call at {clock} · rang Ns" / "Call declined at {clock}") system message after the call ends. Calls from before the fix are unaffected (their docs were never written). **MDs** — `firebase/firestore.rules §intercoms/{roomId}/messages` (the inline comment references the #216 decision + the emulator test file), `firebase/tests/intercoms-messages-call-history-rules.emulator.test.ts` (NEW), `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts` (the 4 new cross-file contract pins), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #216` (this entry). **Gates** — INC-01 (per `GOTCHAS.md` line 16: rules and client ship together, or rules ship second. The #214 + #215 client changes are already in `dev` (commits `e5da26e` + `2e1c569`); the #216 rule change ships with the client, not before it. No risk of a tightened-rule-against-old-client regression). FOL-02 (the source-text test follows the per-field test pattern for the cross-file contract; a future refactor that drops any of the 4 call-history fields from the rule breaks exactly one test per field). NC-02d (the emulator test pattern is the project's gold standard for rule evaluation; source-text regex tests are the cheap daily safety net, emulator tests are the "invalid rule shape catches" pin). FOL-03 (the rule's read-write gate is unchanged; the allowlist is a `keys().hasOnly([...])` check, not a transactional read-then-write, so the FOL-03 "reads before writes" rule doesn't apply). **Rejected** — add `callAnsweredByName` to the rule via a more permissive shape (e.g. `keys().hasAny([...])` or removing the `hasOnly` gate) (a more permissive rule would let any signed-in user write a `role: "admin"` field to a chat message, breaking the privilege-escalation defense; the existing `hasOnly` shape is the correct gate; the fix is to add the one missing key, not to weaken the gate), fix the bug client-side by stripping `callAnsweredByName` from the writer (would silently lose the staff attribution the #214 work added; the data is the audit trail — losing it would break the chat-history contract end-to-end; the right fix is to unblock the write, not to drop the field), add a runtime test that calls the actual `recordCallHistory` function (would need a Firestore emulator + a React Testing Library setup; the source-text + emulator tests cover the contract at a cheaper layer; the runtime test is a future addition if the test surface grows), bump the version MAJOR (the change is a server-side rule correction; the client is unchanged; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning), file the cross-file contract pattern as a new GOTCHAS.md entry (the existing FOL-02 entry in `GOTCHAS.md` line 30 covers the client-side mapper hydration contract; the cross-file pattern (client write payload vs server allowlist) is the next-layer extension, but adding it to GOTCHAS is a separate audit pass — the #216 decision entry is the right shape for "the lesson learned", and a future FOL-06 audit could promote it to a GOTCHAS entry if the pattern bites again).

| 217 | **Call-history phantom "Missed call" + call-failed outcome (operator-reported bug fix, 2026-08-19; proposed on `fix/call-history-phantom-missed`):** the `acceptCall` flow in `AdminContext.tsx` no longer fires the call-history dispatch hook from inside its own body; a new non-dispatching helper `clearCallRefsOnly` handles the ref-zeroing that previously delegated to `cleanupAdminCall`. The pre-#217 surface was: `acceptCall` called `cleanupAdminCall()` (line 4077) BEFORE setting `adminCallAnsweredRef = true` (line 4106). The cleanup's async IIFE dispatched `recordCallHistory(roomId, "call-missed", durationMs)` against the in-flight ringing call's refs (`answered = false`, `ringingStart = T+0s`, `Date.now() - ringingStart ≈ 2s`). The dispatch fired the moment staff clicked Accept — producing a phantom "Missed call at HH:MM · rang 2s" entry in the chat thread on every accepted call. The pre-#217 dispatch was silent from the staff member's perspective because `recordCallHistory`'s outer `try/catch` swallows rule rejections as `console.warn`. The phantom message only surfaced in the chat thread (where it was visible to staff); the staff's own UI didn't toast, banner, or error on the spurious dispatch. Operator-reported 2026-08-19 with a screenshot showing paired `Missed call at 6:30 · rang 2s` + `Call answered by 'DK' at 6:30 · 8s` messages — the second one is correct, the first one is the bug. **Driving motivation** — the FOL-02 audit pattern covers read-side mapper hydration (#215 pinned that for the call-history fields) and the cross-file contract (#216 pinned the rule allowlist). But neither pattern catches a **call-site discipline** gap: a function calls a "tear-down + dispatch" helper from a context that is NOT a terminal lifecycle event. The #214 fix moved the dispatch hook into `cleanupAdminCall` to consolidate the call-history system message writes into one funnel — a sound refactor — but the funnel accidentally became reachable from a non-terminal site. The existing source-text tests pinned the **shape** of the funnel (it reads the refs, it calls `recordCallHistory`, the mapper hydrates the fields) but never pinned the **call sites** that invoke the funnel. A test of the form "acceptCall must not call cleanupAdminCall" would have caught this — but no such test existed. **The fix has four parts.** **(1) `clearCallRefsOnly()` helper extracted** at `AdminContext.tsx:3968-3998`. The helper zeros the six outcome refs and tears down the peer connection — but it does NOT contain the `recordCallHistory` dispatch IIFE. The dispatch hook lives exclusively in `cleanupAdminCall`. **Accept's success path uses `clearCallRefsOnly()` at line 4186**, not `cleanupAdminCall()`. The dispatch no longer fires at the moment of accept — the `call-answered` system message lands at the correct terminal event (operator disconnect / guest hangup / second-call-wins supersede / explicit decline / logout) instead of at accept. **(2) `adminCallHistoryDispatchedRef` exactly-once gate.** A new `useRef<boolean>(false)` ref gates the `cleanupAdminCall` IIFE dispatch. First entry: `if (adminCallHistoryDispatchedRef.current) return;` — short-circuits before reading any outcome refs. After resolving the outcome, the ref is set to `true` BEFORE the `await recordCallHistory(...)` yields (so a synchronous re-entry from StrictMode's second effect-cleanup invocation is a no-op even before the await). The ref is reset to `false` at the tail of `cleanupAdminCall` so the next call lifecycle can fire its own dispatch. Without this gate, React 18 StrictMode double-invokes effect cleanups in dev, and every call-history write would fire twice into the chat thread. **Production runs are not affected** (StrictMode is dev-only), but the dev loop would silently corrupt the chat history during the second-call-wins supersede exercise. **(3) `call-failed` outcome** for the accept-failed path (`getUserMedia` denied, `createAnswer` failed, `writeDoc` rejected). Pre-#217 the catch in `acceptCall` produced a misleading `call-missed` message — the staff DID press Accept, the claim transaction DID commit, but the post-claim audio plumbing failed. The new outcome distinguishes "the staff tried to answer and the audio stack failed" from "the call rang and no one pressed Accept" — both produce a system message but with semantically different content. The new `call-failed` branch in `cleanupAdminCall`'s outcome resolution fires when `adminCallLocalAcceptRef.current === true` AND `adminCallAnsweredRef.current === false` (i.e., the claim committed but the audio plumbing never finished). The text format is `"Call failed at HH:MM · after Ns"` (with the `after Ns` clause omitted when duration is 0/null) — the `after` phrasing distinguishes it from `Missed call at HH:MM · rang Ns`. The `IntercomMessage.messageType` union gains `"call-failed"`, the chat panel renders it with the `PhoneOff` icon + an `orange-600` tone (distinct from missed `amber-600` and declined `gray-400`), and the Firestore rule's closed enum at `firebase/firestore.rules:402-415` accepts the new value (without this rule update the `call-failed` write would be silently rejected — same shape of bug as #216). **(4) IntercomChatPanel dispatch tables.** The pre-#217 render path used chained ternaries (`messageType === "call-answered" ? Phone : messageType === "call-missed" ? PhoneMissed : PhoneOff`). The post-#217 path uses two `Record<...>` dispatch tables (`callIconByType` + `callToneByType`) — the closed `Record` shape forces TypeScript to error if a future messageType is added without a table entry, mirroring the closed-enum rule shape. The exhaustive-table pattern is the symmetric defense on both sides of the contract. **What this changes for the data model** — `IntercomMessage.messageType` gains a fourth value. Legacy docs (pre-#217) keep their existing values; new docs add the new value; the `keys().hasOnly([...])` allowlist is unchanged (no new field, only a new enum value). **What this changes for the test surface** — 25 new source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-history-phantom-missed.test.ts` pin: (1) `acceptCall` does NOT call `cleanupAdminCall()` on the success path (anchored on the line that creates the `RTCPeerConnection`); (2) `acceptCall` DOES call `clearCallRefsOnly()` on the success path; (3) `clearCallRefsOnly` exists, zeroes all six outcome refs, releases the peer connection + media stream, and does NOT contain the `recordCallHistory` dispatch IIFE; (4) `cleanupAdminCall`'s IIFE early-returns when `adminCallHistoryDispatchedRef.current === true`; (5) the dispatched ref is set to true BEFORE the `await recordCallHistory(...)` yields; (6) the ref is reset to false at the tail of `cleanupAdminCall`; (7) `cleanupAdminCall` resolves `call-failed` when `adminCallLocalAcceptRef.current === true` and `adminCallAnsweredRef.current === false`; (8) `recordCallHistory`'s parameter type widens to accept `"call-failed"`; (9) `recordCallHistory` formats the `call-failed` text as `"Call failed at HH:MM"` (+ optional `after Ns`); (10) the accept-failed catch comment references decision #217 (a future regression that restores the misleading pre-#217 wording fails this test); (11) the no-offer branch in `acceptCall` still sets `adminCallAnsweredRef = true` (regression sanity check); (12) `IntercomChatPanel` declares both dispatch tables (`callIconByType` + `callToneByType`) with all four messageType values; (13) `shared/types/index.ts` `IntercomMessageType` union includes `"call-failed"`. Plus 2 new emulator tests in `firebase/tests/intercoms-messages-call-history-rules.emulator.test.ts`: `call-failed` happy-path write succeeds, and a `messageType: "call-typo"` write fails the closed-enum rule (the regression-in-reverse for the new value). Plus 1 test in the existing `call-history-messages.test.ts` updated to match the new dispatch-table shape (the chained-ternary regex no longer matches the new code; the test now asserts each table entry exists, mirroring the `Record<...>` exhaustive-shape contract). **What this changes for the operator UX** — every accepted call now produces exactly ONE `call-answered` system message (at the terminal event). The phantom `call-missed` messages stop. The accept-failed path produces a `call-failed` message instead of a misleading `call-missed`. The chat-thread audit trail is now an accurate reflection of what actually happened. **MDs** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx` (the `clearCallRefsOnly` helper, the `adminCallHistoryDispatchedRef` ref, the success-path swap from `cleanupAdminCall` to `clearCallRefsOnly`, the `call-failed` outcome branch in the IIFE, the `Call failed at HH:MM` text format, the accept-failed catch comment rewrite), `admin-app/src/components/IntercomChatPanel.tsx` (the `callIconByType` + `callToneByType` dispatch tables + the `call-failed` orange-tone entry), `shared/types/index.ts` (the `IntercomMessageType` union gains `"call-failed"` + the comment block rewrites the docstring to mention the fourth outcome), `firebase/firestore.rules §intercoms/{roomId}/messages` (the closed-enum rule at line 408 adds `"call-failed"`), `firebase/tests/intercoms-messages-call-history-rules.emulator.test.ts` (2 new tests: `call-failed` happy path + closed-enum rejection), `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-history-phantom-missed.test.ts` (NEW, 25 source-text tests), `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-history-messages.test.ts` (1 test updated to match the dispatch-table shape), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #217` (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (the "Recently shipped" section gains the entry). **Gates** — INTERCOM-INBOX (the chat-thread audit trail is now accurate: every call produces exactly one system message with the correct outcome text; no behavioral delta for the live call UI), INTERCOM-GUEST (guest-side call UI is unchanged; the guest still pairs with the first SDP answer regardless of how the desk-side audit message renders), INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING (the audio routing remains pinned to the call device; the call-failed path doesn't reach the audio sink because the peer connection was never constructed), FOL-03 (the IIFE pattern is unchanged for the read-before-write transaction; the new `call-failed` branch doesn't introduce a new transactional surface), FOL-02 (the mapper continues to hydrate every contract field including the four call-history fields; #215 closed that pattern and #217 extends it with a new outcome value), NC-02d (the rule emulator test pins the closed-enum shape; the source-text test pins the cross-file client-vs-server enum sync; a future refactor that drops `"call-failed"` from either side fails a test). **Rejected** — only fix bug #1 and leave #2/#3/#4 as separate tickets (the operator's screenshot showed the phantom message; bug #1 is the visible surface. But bug #2 is in the same call site — fixing only #1 means the accept-failed path keeps producing misleading "Missed call" messages. And bug #3 is the structural fix that prevents future regression. And bug #4 is the contract tightening that makes the source-text tests deterministic. All four ship together because they're the same code path), use a per-tab "already dispatched" boolean on a class instance (the existing `useRef<boolean>` pattern matches the codebase's other call-lifecycle refs; a class instance would break the functional component shape and require a separate context provider), move the call-history dispatch to a Firestore trigger / Cloud Function (Vercel Hobby is at the function cap; the dispatch is a single Firestore write; doing it in the client SDK avoids the round-trip and the function-count tax), add a new `callOutcome` field with `{ kind, durationMs, reason? }` instead of using a string `messageType` union (a structured field would future-proof for richer audit metadata, but the current `messageType` union is the project-wide convention and changing the schema is out of scope for a fix-this-bug ticket), bump the version MAJOR (the change is wire-compatible for the guest side; the staff side adds a new outcome value but the existing outcomes continue to render byte-equivalent — verified by the `Record<...>` exhaustive shape matching the pre-#217 icon + tone mappings for `call-answered` / `call-missed` / `call-declined`; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning; the new outcome is additive — legacy chat threads continue to read and render byte-equivalent), file the call-site discipline pattern as a new GOTCHAS.md entry (the existing FOL-02 entry covers the mapper hydration contract; the call-site discipline pattern is the next-layer extension, but adding it to GOTCHAS is a separate audit pass — the #217 decision entry is the right shape for "the lesson learned", and a future FOL-06 audit could promote it to a GOTCHAS entry if the pattern bites again).

| 218 | **Active-call banner mute-state UX + disconnect button color (operator-reported bug fix, 2026-08-19; proposed on `fix/call-banner-mute-state-and-disconnect-color`):** the `IntercomInboxPage` active-call banner has three bug fixes that the operator flagged immediately after the #217 call-history fix landed (the operator could now actually see the call-history audit trail, which exposed the two banner UX gaps that had been there all along but were hard to spot when the audit trail was the operator's primary concern). **(1) Live mic status pill — always visible.** The pre-#218 banner showed the mic state in a `hidden lg:flex` indicator (desktop only) — the operator's mobile / tablet / smaller-laptop screens had no visual state cue. On those breakpoints, the only state cue was the mute toggle button's label, which read "Mute" / "Unmute" — the ACTION label, not the current state. Right after Accept, the operator saw "Mute" and read it as "I'm currently muted" even though `track.enabled` was `true` (browser default) and the mic was actually open. The operator's audio was working, but the operator couldn't tell, so they kept clicking Mute trying to "unmute" themselves. **Fix** — replace the `hidden lg:flex` chip with an ALWAYS-VISIBLE live status pill (no `hidden` class, no breakpoint gate): a coloured dot (`bg-emerald-500` for open, `bg-red-500` for muted) + a state label ("Mic open" / "Mic muted"). The pill uses `emerald-200/50/700` for the open state (standard "live" green mirroring Zoom / Teams / Meet) and `red-200/50/700` for the muted state. The pill carries `data-testid="call-mic-status-pill-{roomId}"` for downstream test pinning. The mute toggle button keeps the action label ("Mute" / "Unmute") so the two surfaces don't compete — together they read as "Mic open · Mute" / "Mic muted · Unmute" which is unambiguous on first read. The pill is rendered BEFORE the button in DOM order so the state cue lands first when the operator scans the banner. **(2) Disconnect button color fix.** The pre-#218 disconnect button used `bg-red-650 hover:bg-red-700` — `bg-red-650` is NOT in the Tailwind palette (the standard scale jumps 600 → 700, no 650). Tailwind silently dropped the class so the button rendered with NO background and was effectively invisible against the white card. The operator could still click it (the hit area was intact) but the visual affordance was missing — the button looked like text, not a CTA. **Fix** — `bg-red-650` → `bg-red-600` (the standard "danger" CTA shade) + `hover:bg-red-700` (the standard hover). Matches the rest of the app's red palette (ReportsPage refund cells, SettingsPage delete buttons). **(3) Mute button title attribute rephrased.** The pre-#218 unmuted title was "Mute mic. The guest will not hear you while muted." — describing the OUTCOME of clicking the button, not the current state. This was inconsistent with the muted-state title ("Mic is muted. The guest can't hear you. Click to unmute.") which described the current state + the action. **Fix** — the unmuted title is now "Mic is open. The guest can hear you. Click to mute." which matches the muted-state title's "state + click-to-toggle" pattern. The pre-existing `aria-label` and `aria-pressed` already follow the same pattern — this fix closes the title-attribute inconsistency. **What the user sees after the change** — on Accept, the banner shows a green "Mic open" pill + the blue "Mute" button + a clearly-red Disconnect button. The operator can see the live mic state at a glance on every breakpoint (no more "hidden lg:flex" gate). The Disconnect button is visually red and obviously a CTA. Toggling mute flips both the pill and the button label. **(Implementation)** — `admin-app/src/pages/IntercomInboxPage.tsx` (the `hidden lg:flex` indicator replaced with the always-visible pill + the disconnect button className fixed + the title attribute rephrased). No new components, no new dependencies, no Tailwind config changes — the `emerald-*` / `red-*` palette shades used are already standard in the existing project (used elsewhere in the codebase for the same semantic mapping). **(Tests)** — 16 new source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-banner-mute-state.test.ts` (3 describe blocks): **bug #1 (mic status pill)** — the `hidden lg:flex` indicator is GONE from the source (negative pin), the new pill has the `call-mic-status-pill-{roomId}` testid, the pill text says "Mic open" + "Mic muted" (one assertion per state), the pill uses `emerald-200/50/700` for the open state + `red-200/50/700` for the muted state (the standard semantic-colour mapping), the pill carries a coloured dot (`bg-emerald-500` / `bg-red-500`) that tracks the state. **bug #2 (disconnect color)** — the `bg-red-650` class is GONE from the source (negative pin), `bg-red-600 hover:bg-red-700` is present (positive pin), the `text-white` class is preserved so the label stays legible on the red background. **bug #3 (title rephrasing)** — the unmuted title says "Mic is open. The guest can hear you. Click to mute." (positive pin) + the muted title still says "Mic is muted. The guest can't hear you. Click to unmute." (positive pin, pre-existing wording preserved). Plus the regression-sanity pin for the two pre-existing surfaces — the mute button still has its `call-mute-toggle-{roomId}` testid, still has the Mic / MicOff icon swap, still has the "Mute" / "Unmute" action label; the disconnect button still has the PhoneOff icon + "Disconnect Call" label. Plus 2 updates to the pre-existing `feature-intercom-audio-routing.test.ts` to match the new banner: the "Voice connected indicators" comment anchor is now "Live mic status pill" (the comment that precedes the new pill), the `Audio Stream: Active` + `Mic Muted` text pins are now `Mic open` + `Mic muted` (the new pill text). All 1406 admin-app tests pass (1390 pre-#218 + 16 new #218 source-text additions). **(What this changes for the data model)** — nothing. The fix is read-side UI only — no Firestore fields change, no new admin-app state, no new context provider. The mic state still lives in `useState<boolean>(false)` on `AdminContext` and is reset on `clearCallRefsOnly()` + `cleanupAdminCall()`. **(What this changes for the audio contract)** — nothing. The audio plumbing is unchanged: `getUserMedia` returns a stream with `track.enabled === true` (browser default), `addTrack` adds the track to the peer connection, `toggleMicMute` is the only function that flips `track.enabled`. The #218 fix is purely about the operator's visual understanding of that state. **(What this changes for the operator UX)** — the operator can see the current mic state on every breakpoint, the Disconnect button is visually red and clearly a CTA, and the mute / unmute action is unambiguous because the state pill carries the current state and the button carries the action. **(MDs)** — `admin-app/src/pages/IntercomInboxPage.tsx` (the indicator + disconnect button + title fixes), `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-banner-mute-state.test.ts` (NEW, 16 source-text tests), `admin-app/src/__tests__/feature-intercom-audio-routing.test.ts` (2 test updates to match the new banner shape), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #218` (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (Recently shipped entry). **(Gates)** — INTERCOM-INBOX (the three-state banner from #214 / #215 / #216 / #217 is unchanged; the #218 changes are in the active-call sub-banner, not the "ringing / claimed-by-other / active" outer state machine), INTERCOM-GUEST (guest side is unaffected — the guest's UI doesn't have a mic toggle), INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING (the audio device routing is unchanged; the #218 changes are visual-only and don't touch the WebRTC or `setSinkId` paths), FOL-03 (no new transactional surface — the #218 changes are read-side UI), NC-02d (no new rule surface — the #218 changes are not security-relevant). **(Rejected)** — rename the button label to "Mute mic" / "Unmute mic" so the action is unambiguous on its own (would partially help but the root cause is the MISSING state cue, not the action label — the pill + button together is the symmetric fix), add a tooltip that always shows the current state on hover (tooltips are passive and require hover — operators on touch devices don't get hover, and the operator needs the state at a glance, not on hover), make the button always red regardless of state (defeats the purpose of the colour-coded state pill — green + red together is the conferencing-app convention for "you're live vs you're muted"), use a modal popover with the mic controls (a modal for a 1-click toggle is massive friction — operators mute/unmute frequently during calls), also fix the other `bg-red-650` instances scattered across `BookingsPage.tsx` + `SettingsPage.tsx` + `ReportsPage.tsx` + `DashboardPage.tsx` (each instance is its own operator-visible bug but they were not flagged by this operator report — a future audit pass could file them as a separate decision entry; the #218 fix is scoped to the call banner where the operator reported the bug), pin only the `bg-red-650` absence and not the `bg-red-600` presence (the absence pin catches the bug, the presence pin catches a future refactor that drops the disconnect button entirely from the active-call banner — both pins are needed for the contract), make the pill a `position: fixed` overlay so it doesn't take up banner space (the pill is small + the banner already has room for the three surfaces — a fixed overlay would obscure the chat thread on small screens), bump the version MAJOR (the change is purely visual — no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).

| 220 | **The admin Firestore listeners carry the role gates (RM-05, 2026-08-19):** a hard delete of a `rooms/{id}` is ADMIN-only — a front-desk session cannot delete a room type — and the members-list and room-CRUD listeners are scoped by the same rule, so the admin app's onSnapshot subscriptions never expose a write surface the role does not have. |
| 221 | **Booking cancellation requires a reason at the UI AND the server (BK-05, 2026-08-19):** the admin cancel flow requires a reason (`ConfirmForm`'s `reasonRequired`) and the server refuses a cancellation without one. A client-side-only requirement is bypassable by a direct POST, so the reason is validated on both sides. |
| 222 | **Static hero-image fallbacks carry the Google `=wN` size token (2026-08-19):** the static fallback URLs were missing the size token the live URLs use, so the fallback fetched the full-size original and the hero image regressed on a slow connection. |
| 223 | **The payment-proof + Senior/PWD ID uploads are bounded (B-10 + B-10c, 2026-08-19):** the upload path raced Firebase Storage with no timeout, so a stalled upload left the mobile guest stuck (it failed on mobile and worked on desktop). The upload is now bounded by a 90s timeout. |
| 224 | **The admin wordmark no longer overlaps the notification bell on mobile (#18, 2026-08-19):** the header root is `flex shrink-0` + `relative` and the bell wrapper is `shrink-0` + `relative`, so the wordmark and the bell cannot collide at narrow widths. |
| 225 | **QR print + download works on Safari iOS (Q-03 + #22, 2026-08-19):** the pre-#225 surface had two separate Safari iOS blockers — `handleDownloadPng` called `link.click()` (ignored by Safari) and the print path opened a blob URL Safari could not print. Both fall back to a Safari-safe path. |
| 226 | **`Member.memberSince` mapper normalizes Firestore Timestamp → ISO string (operator-reported bug fix, 2026-08-20; proposed on `fix/members-member-since-timestamp`):** opening the admin-app Spark Rewards Members page (`/members`) on production + staging crashed with React error #31: "object with keys {seconds, nanoseconds}". `{seconds, nanoseconds}` is the canonical Firestore `Timestamp` shape — React #31 fires when an object is rendered as a JSX child. **Operator-reported 2026-08-20** — every operator who opened `/members` on either environment hit the crash; the page rendered the empty-state card ("No members match the current search.") on some browsers and crashed silently on others. **Trace to the call-site** — `MembersPage.tsx:225` (`<p>{row.memberSince}</p>` in the mobile card) and `MembersPage.tsx:326` (`<span>{selectedMember.memberSince}</span>` in the drawer) render `memberSince` raw. The TS contract (`Member.memberSince: string`, `AdminContext.tsx:398`) said it was a string, but the runtime shape was a Firestore `Timestamp` object. **Why the mapper leaked a Timestamp** — `guest-app/server/handlers/members.ts:243,258,285` writes `memberSince: now` where `now = new Date()`; the Admin SDK auto-converts `Date` → Firestore `Timestamp` on store. The mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:3040` was `memberSince: data.memberSince || ""` — a raw pass-through that never converted the Timestamp to a string before landing on React state. With a Timestamp object, the `|| ""` guard never fired (objects are truthy), so React rendered `{seconds, nanoseconds}` as a JSX child. Every real Spark Rewards member in production has a Timestamp on this field (per `plan/features/SPARK-REWARDS.md:61`); the mobile card renders for every row, so the crash was universal. **`ReportsPage.tsx:1703`** already has the right pattern (`toDate(m.memberSince)?.toISOString()`) — confirms the toDate-guarded normalization is the canonical shape for this contract. **Fix — one-line mapper change.** Mirror the same `toDate`-guarded pattern that `MembersPage.tsx:58-60` uses for the drawer's `pointsHistory` and `IntercomPage.tsx:495` uses for `intercomMessages.timestamp`: `memberSince: data.memberSince?.toDate ? data.memberSince.toDate().toISOString() : (data.memberSince || "")`. The `toDate()` guard catches a Firestore Timestamp on the wire and converts it to an ISO string; the `|| ""` fallback handles a missing field; the else branch handles a legacy doc that already has `memberSince` as a plain string (no double-conversion). **What the user sees after the change** — the mobile card shows the member's join date (e.g. "2025-08-15"); the drawer shows the same date. No React crash, no empty state, no console error. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The mapper is the conversion boundary; the wire shape stays a Firestore Timestamp. **What this changes for the test surface** — 1 new test file `admin-app/src/__tests__/members-member-since-timestamp.test.ts` with 6 tests in 3 describe blocks (matching the FOL-02 pattern in `fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts`): (a) source-text positive pin: `memberSince: data.memberSince?.toDate ? data.memberSince.toDate().toISOString()` is present in the mapper; (b) source-text negative pin: `memberSince: data.memberSince || ""` is GONE from the mapper (catches a future refactor that re-introduces the raw pass-through); (c) source-text consumer pin: `MembersPage.tsx` references `row.memberSince` (mobile card at line 225) and `selectedMember.memberSince` (drawer at line 326); (d) runtime fixture pin: a Firestore Timestamp `{seconds, nanoseconds, toDate()}` round-trips to an ISO string via the same shape the mapper uses; (e) runtime backwards-compat pin: a non-Timestamp string passes through unchanged (no double-conversion via `.toISOString()` on a string). All 1465 admin-app tests pass (was 1459 before; +6 net). **Negative-test confirmation** — revert the mapper to the raw pass-through and the 2 source-text pin tests fail with the labeled messages "hydrates `memberSince` via a toDate()-guarded normalization (not a raw pass-through)" + "member mapper does NOT pass `memberSince` through raw (negative pin)". **MDs** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:3040` (the mapper fix + the 19-line docstring referencing decision #226 + the toDate-guarded shape), `admin-app/src/__tests__/members-member-since-timestamp.test.ts` (NEW, 6 tests), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #226` (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (Recently shipped entry). **Gates** — FOL-02 (the per-field hydration pattern is the same shape as `fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts` — per-field source-text pins + runtime fixture pins; the #226 entry adds the third per-field pin site), FOL-03 (no transactional surface — the #226 change is a read-side mapper), MRB-15-09 (the members listener already adopted the `getIdToken(true)` force-refresh pattern in #220; the #226 mapper change is unrelated to the listener), SPARK-REWARDS (the registration write path is unchanged — `memberSince: now` is still the canonical wire shape; the mapper is now the conversion boundary). **Rejected** — change the server write to `memberSince: now.toISOString()` (would close the bug at the wire but loses the Firestore Timestamp semantics — every other timestamp field in the members schema uses `Timestamp`; introducing a string-only field is inconsistent and would break a future `orderBy("memberSince")` query that expects a Timestamp), normalize the Timestamp at the render site (would scatter the conversion logic across every `{row.memberSince}` / `{selectedMember.memberSince}` site — three sites today, more tomorrow; the mapper is the single conversion boundary), bump the version MAJOR (the change is read-side — no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning), also normalize the other Timestamp-shaped fields on `Member` (`createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `memberSince`) (the other two are not currently rendered in the Members page; `createdAt` / `updatedAt` are admin-internal timestamps that surface via `ReportsPage.tsx` which already uses the `toDate()` helper — out of scope for this fix), add a runtime test that mounts `AdminContext` with a fake Firestore Timestamp and asserts the rendered DOM (would require loading the full React context + Firebase SDK in Node — too heavy for the source-text + runtime-fixture pattern that already pins the contract; the source-text pins catch the regression, the runtime fixture pins catch the shape change, and the operator's staging browser is the visual QA), expand the test to also pin `GuestAuthContext.tsx:117,144` (`memberSince: data.memberSince || ""` — same raw pass-through shape in the guest app's auth context) (the guest app's `/account/rewards` page renders `memberSince` via `RewardsPage.tsx`; a follow-up audit pass could file a parallel decision for that surface if the operator reports the same React crash there; the #226 entry closes the admin-app surface which is what the operator reported).

| 219 | **Mic mute state derives from live `track.enabled`, not from React `isMicMuted` (operator-reported bug fix, 2026-08-19; proposed on `fix/call-banner-mic-state-derived-from-track`):** the active-call banner's mic-state surface (the status pill, the mute button label/icon/styling/aria-pressed/title) is now driven by `getActualMicMuted()` which reads `track.enabled` directly — the live WebRTC track state, the only authoritative source of "is the mic actually transmitting right now". The React `isMicMuted` state is kept on the context (it still drives `toggleMicMute`'s first flip and resets on cleanup), but the banner no longer reads it for display. **Operator-reported 2026-08-19 (post-#218)** — the pill said "MIC OPEN" but the audio was muted. Root cause: the displayed state (`isMicMuted` useState) and the actual audio (`track.enabled`) had drifted apart. The drift came from something OTHER than `toggleMicMute` writing `track.enabled = false` — likely candidates: (a) WebRTC track renegotiation on the peer connection swapped the outbound track mid-call (the new track arrives with `enabled = true` by default but the operator's previous Mute click left the OLD track at `enabled = false`; the new track's `enabled = true` made the audio audible but `isMicMuted` still read `true` from the prior render, so the next Mute click would set the new track to `false` again — the displayed state would lag reality by one click); (b) a stale closure in an early render captured `isMicMuted = false` while `track.enabled` was already `false` from a prior session; (d) a browser/OS-level audio subsystem intervention. Whatever the cause, the operator could not see what the guest was hearing. The pre-#218 + #219 fix in two layers: #218 made the pill visible on every breakpoint so the operator COULD see the state; #219 makes the pill reflect the live audio state so what the operator sees is what's actually playing. **Fix — four-part change.** **(1) `getActualMicMuted()` helper added** at `AdminContext.tsx:3840-3856` — the function reads `adminMediaStreamRef.current.getAudioTracks()[0].enabled` and returns the negation. Idle state (no stream) returns `false`. The function is a `useCallback([])` so it's referentially stable across renders and the banner can call it without churning. **(2) `toggleMicMute` rewritten** to derive `nextEnabled` from the live `tracks[0].enabled` rather than from the React `isMicMuted` state — the function body's first flip computation reads the track, not the state. The `useCallback` dep array is now `[]` (was `[isMicMuted]`) because the function no longer reads any React state — it only reads the ref + the track. **(3) `AdminContextType` interface + return value gain `getActualMicMuted: () => boolean`** so the banner can subscribe. `isMicMuted` stays on the type + return value as a hint for any future consumer that wants the user's last-intent (e.g. analytics, a future mute-by-default toggle). **(4) `IntercomInboxPage` active-call IIFE captures `actualMicMuted` at the top of the render** (`const actualMicMuted = getActualMicMuted();`) and uses it for the pill text, pill border/fill/text, pill dot, button label, button icon, button styling, button `aria-pressed`, button `aria-label`, and button `title` — every visual + accessibility surface. The `useAdmin()` destructure intentionally does NOT pull `isMicMuted` (a comment in the destructure block documents why — `// `isMicMuted` is intentionally NOT destructured here`). The `toggleMicMute` click handler is unchanged — it still writes to `track.enabled` and updates `isMicMuted`. The `isMicMuted` reset on `clearCallRefsOnly` + `cleanupAdminCall` is unchanged — every new call still starts unmuted. **What the user sees after the change** — the pill, button label, button icon, button styling, button aria-pressed, and button title all reflect the LIVE `track.enabled` value. The pill says "Mic open" only when the mic is actually transmitting; it says "Mic muted" only when the track is actually disabled. The pill, button, and audio can never disagree. Operator's report (post-deploy): if the pill says "Mic open", the guest can hear them. If the pill says "Mic muted", the guest cannot. **Implementation** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx` (~30 lines: the `getActualMicMuted` helper, the `toggleMicMute` rewrite, the type extension, the context return value update) + `admin-app/src/pages/IntercomInboxPage.tsx` (~5 lines: the destructure change + the IIFE capture + the 9 display-site reads of `actualMicMuted` replacing the reads of `isMicMuted`). No new dependencies, no new components, no Tailwind config changes. **What this changes for the audio contract** — nothing. The audio plumbing is unchanged: `getUserMedia` returns a stream with `track.enabled === true`, `addTrack` adds the track to the peer connection, `toggleMicMute` is the only function that writes to `track.enabled`. The #219 fix is purely about the DISPLAY side — making the displayed state converge to the audio state that the WebRTC plumbing already maintained correctly. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. No Firestore fields, no React state shape changes (the `isMicMuted` state is still there — it's just not displayed anymore). **What this changes for the test surface** — 25 new source-text tests in `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-banner-mic-state-track-truth.test.ts` (3 describe blocks). **Block 1 — `getActualMicMuted` reads the live track state**: the function is exposed on the context return value, declared on the interface with `() => boolean` signature, reads `adminMediaStreamRef.current.getAudioTracks()[0].enabled`, returns `false` when there's no active stream, returns the negation (muted = !enabled). **Block 2 — `toggleMicMute` uses the live track state**: the `useCallback` has empty deps `[]` (no `isMicMuted` dep), reads `tracks[0].enabled` to compute `currentTrackEnabled`, computes `nextEnabled = !currentTrackEnabled`, still walks every audio track via `tracks.forEach(track => track.enabled = nextEnabled)`. **Block 3 — `isMicMuted` remains as a hint, not the display source**: the type contract still declares `isMicMuted: boolean`, the return value still exposes it, `cleanupAdminCall` still resets it via `setIsMicMuted(false)`, `clearCallRefsOnly` (the per-call helper from #217) also resets it. **Block 4 — IntercomInboxPage derives display from `actualMicMuted`**: the IIFE captures `getActualMicMuted()` at the top, the pill text + border/fill/text + dot colour derive from `actualMicMuted`, the button label + icon + styling + aria-pressed + aria-label + title all derive from `actualMicMuted`, the `useAdmin()` destructure intentionally does NOT include `isMicMuted` (anchored on the actual destructure lines, not the prose comment). Plus 3 test updates in `feature-intercom-audio-routing.test.ts` (the `toggleMicMute` useCallback block test pins the new `currentTrackEnabled = tracks[0].enabled` read + the empty deps array, the Inbox banner test pins the `const actualMicMuted = getActualMicMuted()` IIFE capture + the `aria-pressed={actualMicMuted}` + the `{actualMicMuted ? <MicOff` icon swap). Plus 2 test updates in `call-banner-mute-state.test.ts` (#218 regression sanity pins for the Mic / MicOff icon swap and the "Mute" / "Unmute" label — both now derive from `actualMicMuted` rather than `isMicMuted`). All 1431 admin-app tests pass (1406 pre-#219 + 25 new #219 source-text additions). **MDs** — `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx` (the `getActualMicMuted` helper, the `toggleMicMute` rewrite, the interface + return value updates), `admin-app/src/pages/IntercomInboxPage.tsx` (the destructure change + the IIFE capture + the 9 display-site reads), `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-banner-mic-state-track-truth.test.ts` (NEW, 25 source-text tests), `admin-app/src/__tests__/feature-intercom-audio-routing.test.ts` (3 test updates), `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-banner-mute-state.test.ts` (2 test updates), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #219` (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (Recently shipped entry). **Gates** — INTERCOM-INBOX (the active-call banner from #218 is unchanged; #219 changes only the SOURCE of the state the banner reads, not the banner shape or any of the surrounding lifecycle), INTERCOM-GUEST (guest side is unaffected), INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING (the audio device routing is unchanged; #219 doesn't touch WebRTC or `setSinkId`), FOL-03 (no new transactional surface — #219 changes are read-side UI), NC-02d (no new rule surface — #219 changes are not security-relevant). **Rejected** — keep the pre-#219 `useState<boolean>(false)` source-of-truth + add a `useEffect` that polls `track.enabled` on a timer to re-sync (a polling effect is the React-idiomatic way to "sync state to external" but it's wasteful for an event-driven surface — the track state changes only on user action, and the IIFE capture on every render already gives us the live value without a timer), read `track.enabled` inside a `useEffect` and force-sync `isMicMuted` (would re-introduce the dual-source problem the fix is trying to eliminate — `isMicMuted` would still lag by one render until the effect fires, plus the source-of-truth question is now "which one wins if they disagree?"), drop the React `isMicMuted` state entirely (would break the `useCallback([])` stable reference that toggleMicMute needs, plus the cleanupAdminCall reset would have to read from the track instead of the React state — workable but a wider blast radius than this fix needs to be), move the mic-mute toggle into a separate component so it has its own state (a refactor for one banner section is over-engineering; the IIFE capture is the minimum-blast-radius fix), use a state-management library like Zustand for the call state (the existing useState + useRef + useCallback pattern is the project convention; introducing Zustand is out of scope for a fix-this-bug ticket), bump the version MAJOR (the change is purely about which signal the banner reads for display — no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape, no new component; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).
| 227 | **The discount ID is pre-checked on payment verification (IDG, 2026-08-19):** a booking whose discount has not been verified shows a Verify / Reject control in the check-in surface, and the DASHBOARD's payment-verify gate reads `!discountVerified && !discountRejected` — so a re-verified-after-rejection booking falls through to the pending-payment gate instead of being counted twice. Full spec: `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §Discount ID Pre-Check on Payment Verification (IDG)`. |
| 228 | **Admin drawer folio allows re-verifying a rejected Senior/PWD discount (operator-reported UX bug; proposed on `fix/discount-reverify-after-rejection`, 2026-08-20):** after `handleRejectDiscount` (`guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts:5077+`) stamps `discountRejected: true` + `discountRejectedBy` + `discountRejectionReason` + restores `totalPrice` from `originalTotalPrice`, the booking's `discountType` field stays populated — but the folio's "Discount or voucher" card at `admin-app/src/pages/BookingsPage.tsx:5768-5811` still renders "Pending review" (amber) and hides the `Apply discount` button. The check-in chip at `:5838-5850` correctly says `✗ Rejected` on rejection; the folio chip at `:5781-5783` uses a different (incomplete) shape that ignores `discountRejected`. So a desk that verifies a Senior/PWD ID, then later rejects it, then later physically re-checks the ID (or accepts a freshly-resubmitted OSCA photo) has no UI path back to re-verifying — the folio chip stays "Pending review" forever and `Apply discount` stays hidden because `hasVoucherOrDiscount = selectedBooking.discountType || selectedBooking.voucherCode` is still truthy. **Operator-reported 2026-08-20:** "the ID has already been rejected; admin should still be able to apply discounts after rejection since maybe the ID is physically checked or resubmitted." **Why a UI-only fix is correct.** The check-in panel already runs the right `updateDoc` payload for re-verification at `BookingsPage.tsx:5914-5924`: `{ discountVerified: true, discountVerifiedBy: currentUser.email, discountRejected: false }`. The same write clears `discountRejected` — important because `shared/utils/bookingVat.ts:176` guards the senior/PWD VAT computation on `!booking.discountRejected`. The fix is a UI bridge from the folio chip into that existing check-in write. **Three UI changes — no data model change.** **(1) Folio chip 3-state.** Replace line `:5782`'s `discountVerified ? "Verified" : "Pending review"` 2-state ternary with the 3-state shape the check-in chip uses at `:5838-5850` (verified / rejected / pending). The chip text becomes `✓ Verified` (green), `✗ Rejected` (red), `⌛ Pending Review` (amber) — matching the check-in chip's wording and colour classes so the desk sees the same chip on both panels. **(2) `Re-verify ID` button.** When `discountRejected && RESCHEDULABLE_STATUSES.includes(selectedBooking.status)`, render a new `<button>` labelled `Re-verify ID` beside (or below) the existing `Apply discount` slot. Opens a `<ConfirmForm>` titled "Re-verify this rejected discount?" whose body cites the prior rejection (`<strong>{discountRejectedBy}</strong> · {discountRejectionReason}` — audit-cluster fields already hydrated by FOL-02 at `AdminContext.tsx:1335-1448`, decision #198). Confirming runs the SAME `updateDoc` payload as the existing "Approve Discount" button at `:5914-5924`. No new server route, no new field. The lifecycle is closed-and-reopened: `discountVerified` flips true, `discountVerifiedBy` stamps, `discountRejected` clears. No new email action; no re-send of the original rejection. **(3) `Apply discount` button stays hidden on rejection.** The desk should re-verify the original ID, not re-apply a discount type the ID just rejected. The button only re-shows when `!hasVoucherOrDiscount` OR after a successful re-verify (`discountVerified && !discountRejected`) — the existing `!hasVoucherOrDiscount && RESCHEDULABLE_STATUSES.includes(status)` guard is unchanged. **Downstream gates unaffected.** IDG (#227 reserved) reads `!discountVerified && !discountRejected` for its dashboard gate — a re-verified-after-rejection booking falls through to IDG's "every senior/PWD room is either verified or rejected → buttons re-enable" branch, same as a first-time verify. CRL-02..09 unaffected (cancellation audit metadata is a separate cluster). MRB-12 (`apply-discount` segmented control for reservation scope) is unaffected because the re-verify is always booking-scoped (`discountType` lives on the booking doc, not the reservation header). **Source of truth.** The `discountVerified` flip is the durable "verified at some point" signal per FOL-01 (#197); the audit cluster (`discountRejectedBy` / `discountRejectionReason`) is the WHY. No new timestamp field — adding `discountReverifyAt` at 14-room scale is over-engineering when the boolean flip already carries the durability (the FOL-01 pattern). **Tests** — `admin-app/src/__tests__/dsc-05-discount-reverify-after-rejection.test.ts` (NEW, ~10 source-text guards mirroring FOL-02 / MRB-15-10: the 3-state branch on folio line 5782, the `✗ Rejected` chip matches line 5843 verbatim, the `Re-verify ID` render condition + label, the `Apply discount` stays-hidden-on-rejection anti-regression, the confirm body cites the audit cluster, the confirm handler calls `updateDoc` matching the line-5914 shape verbatim, the audit cluster is hydrated via FOL-02 admin mapper); the `phase-12-booking-drawer-folio-interaction.test.ts` line-97 assertion split into "hides apply discount when verified" + "shows Re-verify ID when rejected" (replace, don't duplicate). **MDs** — `admin-app/src/pages/BookingsPage.tsx:5768-5811`, the new test file, the split existing test, `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §Discount Verification (Senior/PWD)` (new state-machine bullet: pending → verified OR pending → rejected → re-verified), this entry, the ROADMAP ship note + commit SHA. **Gates** — FOL-01 (`discountVerified` boolean = durable verified signal), FOL-02 (audit cluster already hydrated), DSC-04 (atomic apply endpoint — unaffected), MRB-12 (apply-form scoped, re-verify is per-booking). **Rejected alternatives** — auto-clear `discountRejected` at reject time (loses the WHY); expose a re-submit-with-new-photo flow (out of scope; existing `discountIdPhotoUrl` persists); add a server-side `POST /api/bookings/reverify-discount` endpoint (over-engineering — no new writer needed); add `discountReverifyAt` Timestamp (FOL-01 establishes the convention); show `Apply discount` on rejection for a different discount type (wrong shape — original ID needs re-verification, not a different ID); bundle into IDG #227 (out of IDG's scope — different surface); use a modal popover instead of ConfirmForm (heavier than this fix needs; Cancel / Reject / Approve paths already use ConfirmForm); bump version MAJOR (`fix:` triggers PATCH per `Husky` auto-versioning). **(Re-)Proposed 2026-08-20.** Spec lives in this entry + the ROADMAP entry + the `BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md` state-machine bullet. Implementation lands on `fix/discount-reverify-after-rejection` in the same branch as the spec; ship note + commit SHA appended to ROADMAP.md on merge.


| 229 | **Discount ID pre-check on dashboard payment verification (owner decision 2026-08-20, option (a) — hard block on dashboard alert only; shipped 2026-08-20 on `feat/idg-discount-id-precheck`):** the dashboard "pending payment alerts" card now hard-blocks the `Verify and record payment` + `Reject payment proof` buttons when any room in the `PendingPaymentItem.rooms[]` group carries a `discountType ∈ {"senior", "pwd"}` AND `!discountVerified && !discountRejected`. An amber callout names the affected room(s) with an `Open booking` deep-link to the drawer, and the card's `dueAmount` reads the **pre-discount** total (`originalTotalPrice` instead of `totalPrice`) so the verify amount is honest if the staff later rejects the ID. **Driving motivation — the BIR/OSCA compliance story (RA 9994 / RA 9442 / RA 7277).** Today, the only checkpoint on the Senior/PWD discount ID is the drawer's check-in panel at `BookingsPage.tsx:5780-5844` — but nothing forces staff to verify the ID before money verification on the dashboard. The pre-IDG surface let staff verify a ₱2,000 payment on a senior-discounted booking (effective total ₱2,000), and a later ID rejection re-prices to `originalTotalPrice` (e.g. ₱2,500) — the verified ₱2,000 sits as a confirmed payment toward a now-bigger bill. The IDG gate moves the verification moment **upstream** of money verification (NOT into the booking flow — the established `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md §Booking Flow` rule "Discount IDs are verified at check-in" still stands for the check-in panel). **Owner decision 2026-08-20: option (a) — hard block on dashboard alert only.** Options (b) "soft warning + deep-link" + (c) "block only verify, keep reject enabled" + (d) "log as roadmap follow-up" + (B) "gate the drawer's check-in too" + (E) "add a server-side reject in `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment`" were rejected per the rationale at `plan/project/ROADMAP.md §Discount ID Pre-Check on Payment Verification (IDG)`. **Fix — four-part change spanning one new file + one existing page + two existing test files.** **(1) New `admin-app/src/utils/pendingPaymentDiscountGate.ts`** — pure-derivation helper module with two exports: `hasUnverifiedDiscount(item: PendingPaymentItemLike): boolean` (iterates `item.rooms`, returns `true` iff any room is `senior`/`pwd` AND `!discountVerified && !discountRejected`) and `getDueAmountPreDiscount(item): number` (sums `originalTotalPrice` for the unverified rooms + `totalPrice` for the rest, with `Number.isFinite` filter + `> 0` guard so `NaN` / `Infinity` / negative values are dropped). Declared structurally (the `PendingPaymentRoomLike` + `PendingPaymentItemLike` types) so the helper has zero coupling to the `DashboardPage` module — the admin-app test pins the runtime contract, the dashboard source-text guard pins the wire shape. **(2) `admin-app/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx` — three sub-changes.** *(2a)* Extend the `PendingPaymentRoom` type with the 4 IDG fields: `discountType: DiscountType | null` + `discountVerified: boolean | null` + `discountRejected: boolean | null` + `originalTotalPrice: number | null`. The data is already hydrated by the FOL-02 admin mapper (decision #198, `AdminContext.tsx:1474-1482`); the dashboard just plumbs the fields into the room-derivation loop so the alert card doesn't need a second Firestore read. *(2b)* Two `rooms[]` derivation sites (the legacy single-row site at `:352-397` and the N>=1 reservation site at `:430-472`) populate the 4 fields with the same defensive coercions the FOL-02 mapper uses (`Number(...)` for the price, `typeof === "boolean"` coercion for the verify/reject flags, `null` for unknown). *(2c)* The alert card's `pendingPayments.map((item) => {...})` render extracts the gate into a local `const idgBlocked = hasUnverifiedDiscount(item)` + `const idgDueAmount = idgBlocked ? getDueAmountPreDiscount(item) : item.dueAmount`. When `idgBlocked` is true: the `dueLabel` swaps from "Reservation due" / "Outstanding" to **"Due (pre-discount, ID pending)"** so the staff knows the math is provisional; the `dueAmount` line reads `formatPrice(idgDueAmount)` instead of `formatPrice(item.dueAmount)`; an amber callout `<div role="alert" data-testid="idg-discount-gate-banner">` renders above the action row with `border-amber-300 bg-amber-50` Tailwind classes + lists the affected room numbers + an `Open booking` `<button data-testid="idg-open-booking-cta">` that deep-links via `navigate(\`/bookings?bookingId=${encodeURIComponent(item.leadBooking.id)}\`)` (the existing `BookingsPage.tsx:841` `searchParams.get("bookingId")` reader picks it up). Both the `Reject` and the `Verify & Record` buttons gain `disabled={idgBlocked}` + `aria-disabled={idgBlocked}` + a `disabled:cursor-not-allowed disabled:opacity-50` Tailwind class + a tooltip that swaps from the normal copy to *"Verify the senior/PWD ID before [verifying|rejecting] payment — open the booking to verify or reject the ID."* when the gate is active. **The `Proof` button is NOT gated** (the staff can still preview the uploaded proof even with an unverified ID — they might need to cross-check the GCash reference against the receipt). **No gate on the drawer's check-in flow** — the established `GOTCHAS.md §Booking Flow` "Discount IDs are verified at check-in" rule stands. **No server-side reject** — the per-card gate is purely UI; the server contract is unchanged (a future walk-in paying full `originalTotalPrice` at the desk without uploading proof is still allowed, per the existing flow). **(3) `admin-app/src/__tests__/pending-payment-discount-gate.test.ts` (NEW, 18 runtime tests).** The pure-helper behavioural tests per `plan/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md §Testing`. The `PendingPaymentRoomLike` shape is declared structurally in the test file (no import dance) so the test pins the contract at the runtime level. 18 tests in 5 describe blocks: **(A)** every N=1 non-discounted → `false` (2 tests); **(B)** unverified senior/pwd → `true` (4 tests: 1-room senior, 2-room with 1 senior + 1 non-discounted, pwd sibling shape of senior, null-verified defensive case); **(C)** verified OR rejected → `false` (4 tests: 1-room senior verified, 1-room senior rejected, mixed verified+unverified (ANY unverified still blocks), 2-room where both verified); **(D)** `getDueAmountPreDiscount` math (5 tests: pre-discount sum for unverified rooms + `totalPrice` for the rest, `totalPrice` sum when no rooms are unverified, `totalPrice` sum when no room has a discount, `totalPrice` fallback when `originalTotalPrice` is `null`, raw `originalTotalPrice` when it underflows the post-discount total); **(E)** defensive coercions (3 tests: non-numeric `originalTotalPrice` → 0, non-numeric `totalPrice` → 0, empty `rooms[]` → gate inactive + 0 due). The runtime tests catch the shape-of-helper drift the v0.264.9 retrofit pattern was designed to catch (the regex-only test file would have caught a future "I renamed the helper" refactor but not a future "I changed the gate's boolean logic" refactor — the runtime pin catches both). **(4) `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-05-dashboard-discount-gate.test.ts` (NEW, 8 source-text guards).** The dashboard wiring pins per the FOL-02 / MRB-15-10 / DSC-05 pattern: the `hasUnverifiedDiscount` import is on the import line; the `getDueAmountPreDiscount` import is on the import line; both buttons render with `disabled={idgBlocked}` + `aria-disabled={idgBlocked}`; the amber banner has `amber-\d+` + `border-amber-\d+` Tailwind classes inside a `{idgBlocked && (<div ...>)}` conditional; the `Open booking` CTA deep-links via `navigate(\`/bookings?bookingId=\`)`; the "Due (pre-discount, ID pending)" label is rendered when the gate is active; the pre-IDG "Reservation due" label is preserved when the gate is inactive (anti-regression). Plus 4 source-text guards in `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-05-dashboard-pending-payments.test.ts` updated to declare the 4 new `PendingPaymentRoom` fields (the FOL-05 source-text pin pattern — mirrors the existing `totalPrice: number;` / `status: string;` pins at the same site). **Test totals — +30 net admin-app tests** (18 runtime + 8 source-text + 4 source-text), 1537/1537 admin-app tests pass; tsc clean. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The 4 IDG fields are already on the `Booking` contract (FOL-02 hydrates them); the `PendingPaymentRoom` type is the FOL-05 dashboard-internal projection. **What this changes for the server contract** — nothing. No new endpoint, no new field, no new writer. The dashboard's `useMemo` re-reads on the next `bookings` snapshot echo (the FOL-02 admin mapper already hydrates `discountVerified` / `discountRejected` / `originalTotalPrice`), so no new Firestore subscription. **What this changes for the check-in surface** — nothing. The drawer at `BookingsPage.tsx:5780-5844` keeps the `discountVerified` + `discountRejected` status pill + the Verify/Reject actions exactly as today. DSC-05 (decision #228, the 2026-08-20 folio re-verify-after-rejection fix) composes cleanly: a re-verified-after-rejection booking falls through to IDG's "every senior/pwd room is either verified or rejected → buttons re-enable" branch, same as a first-time verify. **What this changes for the email + notification side** — nothing. The gate is purely client-side. **What this changes for the operator UX** — a `payment-uploaded` senior/PWD booking now shows the discount-ID pre-check on the dashboard alert; the staff opens the drawer → verifies or rejects the ID there → returns to the dashboard; once every senior/pwd room is cleared, the gate deactivates and the payment buttons re-enable. The verify amount reads `originalTotalPrice` (pre-discount) while the gate is active so the staff sees the HONEST amount if a later ID rejection re-prices the booking. **Implementation** — ~120 lines changed across 4 files: `admin-app/src/utils/pendingPaymentDiscountGate.ts` (NEW, ~100 lines including the 30-line module docstring), `admin-app/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx` (~20 lines: 4-field type extension + 2 derivation-loop sites + alert card gate wiring), `admin-app/src/__tests__/pending-payment-discount-gate.test.ts` (NEW, ~210 lines + 18 runtime tests), `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-05-dashboard-discount-gate.test.ts` (NEW, ~150 lines + 8 source-text guards), `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-05-dashboard-pending-payments.test.ts` (4 new source-text guards appended to the IDG-02 describe block). **MDs** — `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §Discount ID Pre-Check on Payment Verification (IDG)` (test-coverage block updated with the actual shipped counts + the `Shipped 2026-08-20 on feat/idg-discount-id-precheck` note + the spec-stub → #229 numbering flip), `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md §Booking Flow` (the "Discount IDs are verified at check-in" entry extended with the IDG rule + the "verify at check-in" wording preserved for the check-in surface + the IDG dashboard payment-verify surface added as a separate bullet, both pointing at `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §IDG` + `plan/project/ROADMAP.md §IDG` for full spec), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (this entry, the implementation record), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (the IDG-01..04 ship note + version bump). **Gates** — FOL-02 (the 4 IDG fields are already hydrated by the admin mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:1474-1482`; the dashboard just reads them through the `PendingPaymentRoom` shape), FOL-05 (the dashboard alert card + the `PendingPaymentItem` derivation + the `openVerifyForm(item)` + the `verifyScope` state are unchanged; the IDG fix adds a gate ON TOP of the existing surface), MRB-12 (the admin reservation affordance + the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate paid-amount wire are unchanged; the IDG fix doesn't touch the reservation-header read), DSC-05 (a re-verified-after-rejection booking composes cleanly — IDG's "every senior/pwd room is either verified or rejected → buttons re-enable" branch is the same as a first-time verify). **Rejected** — **(b) soft warning + deep-link** — staff can dismiss and proceed;
