doesn't enforce the BIR/OSCA audit trail. **(c) block only verify, keep reject enabled** — the safer reject path stays open; harder mental model ("why is the verify button dead but the reject isn't?"). **(d) log as a ROADMAP follow-up for now** — what IDG avoided by getting specced + shipped in the same week. **(B) gate the drawer's check-in too** — full expansion of the GOTCHAS rule; rejected because the established "verify at check-in" rule already names that surface as the right place to verify, and forcing it on the dashboard alone covers the compliance gap. **(E) add a server-side reject in `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` when `discountVerified === false`** — rejected; the per-card gate is purely UI (the staff can still draw a cheque on a senior-discount booking without uploading proof if the front-desk accepts cash, which the existing flow allows). The verification is the *ID*, not the *proof of payment*, and the server-side contract is unchanged. **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in IDG)** — extend the gate to the guest self-service `/my-booking` "Pay remaining balance" CTA (would catch the same gap on the guest side; out of scope for the v1 fix; the staff dashboard is the operator's primary surface for money verification per the existing FOL-05 flow); server-side reject in `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` (rejected above; revisit if a future audit finds staff working around the UI gate); mint a `verifyAmountPreDiscount` denormalized field on the booking doc for analytics (over-engineering for the visible bug; the dashboard reads the math on demand); add a `discountIdPhotoVerifiedBy` audit field tracking WHO verified the ID (FOL-01's `paymentConfirmedBy` + DSC-05's `discountVerifiedBy` already cover this; the ID-verification moment is a single `updateDoc` write at the check-in or re-verify surface, not a separate lifecycle event).

| 230 | **Settings sidebar — collapse 18 tabs into 5 stakeholder-oriented groups (owner-requested 2026-10-12, SET-SBG-01; shipped on `feature/settings-sidebar-grouping`):** the `/settings` desktop sidebar condensed the 18-tab flat list (Hotel Info + Payment Methods + Booking Sources + Room Types + Booking Preferences + Branding + Website Content + SEO & Search + Loyalty Rewards + Breakfast & Dining + In-Room Store + Email Config + Intercom + Legal Content + Environment Testing + Staff Accounts + Discounts + Booking & Holds) into 5 collapsible groups — **Property** (Hotel Settings + Room Types + Branding), **Bookings & Revenue** (Booking Preferences + Booking Sources + Booking & Holds + Payment Methods + Discounts), **Guest Experience** (Loyalty Rewards + Breakfast & Dining + In-Room Store + Intercom), **Marketing & Reach** (Website Content + SEO & Search + Email Config), **Admin** (Staff Accounts + Legal Content + Environment Testing). The mobile horizontal pill bar keeps the flat 18-pill row but inserts a vertical divider between each group's pills so the visual hierarchy carries over from desktop. **Decisions. (1) Stakeholder-oriented grouping, not frequency-oriented.** The 18 tabs have wildly different touch cadences (Hotel Settings is rarely touched; Booking & Holds is daily; Loyalty Rewards is monthly). A frequency scheme ("settings I touch daily" / "monthly") requires the operator to mentally re-derive which bucket a tab belongs in every time, and the buckets get ugly as features land. A stakeholder scheme ("who is this for?") gives every new tab an obvious home and reads at the section level as a sentence: "These are the surfaces that bring money in." **(2) Active group is always force-expanded — the operator cannot lose their place.** A `useEffect` keyed on `activeTab` removes the active group's id from the persisted `localStorage["settings.sidebar.collapsedGroups.v1"]` collapsed set on every `activeTab` change, and the header's `onClick` is a no-op while the group is active. This guarantees that "where am I in the app" wins over the user's previous collapsing choice — if the staff actively collapsed "Bookings & Revenue" yesterday, the moment they navigate into any of its tabs the sidebar opens it for them; they can collapse it again after they leave. **(3) Persisted choice, not session-only.** The collapsed-set survives a reload via `localStorage["settings.sidebar.collapsedGroups.v1"]` (versioned key, schema `{ "group-id": boolean }`, coerced through `JSON.parse` + a Set-of-valid-ids filter — corrupted entries are dropped silently so a future schema change can bump the key suffix). The valid-id filter means an old persisted set that references a now-removed group id degrades gracefully (the gone id is dropped, the rest is preserved). **(4) `role="group"` + `aria-expanded` + `aria-controls` on the desktop sidebar** so screen readers announce "Marketing & Reach, group, collapsed, button" and Step-3 JAWS / NVDA navigation captures the structure. The mobile pill bar keeps the existing flat-pill semantics (each pill is `data-tab-id="..."` and the active tab is auto-scrolled into view by the pre-existing `tabBarRef` `useEffect`). **(5) Email Config is a hard-wired tab in the Reach group, not a sub-section.** The email sub-editors (per-template `Resend` button, per-event previews) live inside the Email Config tab body as before — the grouping refactor only touches the sidebar nav, not the body composition. **(6) Environment Testing stays under Admin for now; long-term target is `Admin → Tools → Test Runner` gated behind a "show developer tools" env flag.** A settings tab is the wrong home for a QA/staging tool, but extracting it is a larger move than this PR can absorb without rippling into the environment-test feature spec. Deferred to a follow-up. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. **What this changes for the server contract** — nothing. **What this changes for the existing tabs** — zero functional change. Every tab still renders the same body, fires the same `setActiveTab(id)` nav, and respects the same `?tab=<id>` deep-link. The only thing that moves is the *order in which the user encounters tabs* and the *option to fold them away*. The new `useState<string[]>` + `useCallback` + `useMemo` hooks do not affect re-render counts inside a tab body because none of the new state is read from inside any tab body. **What this changes for the test surface** — `admin-app/src/__tests__/set-sbg-01-settings-sidebar-grouping.test.ts` (NEW, 8 source-text guards per CONTRIBUTING.md §Testing). 8 tripwires: (1) `SETTINGS_GROUPS` is declared as `readonly SettingsGroup[]` with the 5 expected labels, (2) the union of `tabIds` across all 5 groups covers every entry in `VALID_TAB_IDS` exactly once (no duplicates, no missing — duplicates would double-render a tab; missing would silently deep-link-fallback to "hotel"), (3) the localStorage key is `settings.sidebar.collapsedGroups.v1` and is both read on mount and written on every toggle, (4) the active group is force-expanded via the `prev.filter((id) => id !== activeGroupId)` effect, (5) the desktop `<aside>` is rendered from `SETTINGS_GROUPS.map(...)` and the legacy flat-tabs sidebar is gone, (6) mobile pill bar inserts a vertical divider between groups via `SETTINGS_GROUPS.flatMap(...)` with `aria-hidden="true"`, (7) `aria-expanded={!isCollapsed}` + `aria-controls="settings-group-${group.id}"` + `role="group"` are present, (8) the nested tab buttons keep `min-h-[44px]` so the touch targets stay at the FRONTEND.md §Touch Targets floor even with the new group-header chrome. **Implementation** — ~170 lines changed across 3 files: `admin-app/src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx` (~170 lines: 2 imports added, `SETTINGS_GROUPS` constant + the versioned storage key, the `useState` + `useCallback` + `useEffect` for collapsed-set + auto-expand, the new `<aside>` JSX, the mobile pill-bar divider, the skeleton mirror), `admin-app/src/__tests__/set-sbg-01-settings-sidebar-grouping.test.ts` (NEW, ~190 lines + 8 source-text tests), `plan/features/SETTINGS.md §Sidebar grouping` (NEW spec section), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (the SET-SBG-01 ship note + Husky MINOR version bump). **Gates** — SETTINGS (existing tabs unchanged; the grouping is a nav-layer wrapper), PHASE-11.7 (the mobile horizontal pill bar's auto-scroll-into-view contract still holds — the dividers are non-interactive between-group separators with `pointer-events` default; the pills remain the only interactive elements), FRONTEND.md §Touch Targets (the nested tab buttons keep `min-h-[44px]`; the group-header row is `min-h-[36px]` because it's a secondary indicator, never a primary action), DECISIONS-ARCH.md §Brand Tokens (the active group header still uses `bg-primary text-white` for its count badge — same brand surface, no new colors). **Rejected** — **(A) frequency-based grouping ("daily / weekly / monthly")** — requires the operator to mentally categorize every new tab into a cadence bucket; ugly when the same feature has multiple cadences (Booking Sources is daily for new submissions, weekly for audit). **(B) tags or color labels on each row instead of grouping** — restores scan-without-thinking to the individual row level, doesn't fix the 18-line scroll, doesn't match any operator mental model. **(C) hide rare tabs behind a "Show all" disclosure** — the operator forgets which tabs exist; "show me where Loyalty Rewards lives" still requires "Show all" → scan. **(D) split the Settings route into 5 routes (`/settings/property`, `/settings/bookings`, ...) with sub-pages** — over-engineering for a sidebar nav concern; URLs become opaque (someone's link to `/settings?tab=rewards` breaks); the existing `?tab=<id>` deep-link is a 1-line flat-link and is preserved. **(E) build a fully searchable command-K launcher for settings** — adds a keyboard-shortcut learning curve for operators who use the sidebar daily, and the grouping already reduces the scan path from "18 rows" to "5 headers" (one extra click at worst). **(F) move Environment Testing out of Settings entirely in this same PR** — touches the environment-test feature spec, the admin routing, the role gates, and a permission allowlist the dev env doesn't need yet. Out of scope. |
| 231 | **Breakfast redesign — meal-based add-on with configurable cut-off + guest manageability + staff override (owner-requested 2026-10-12; spec on `feature/breakfast-redesign`; full brief in `plan/features/BREAKFAST.md`):** the current breakfast model is a flat-rate toggle with a per-date/per-guest `Record<key, string>` of meal names — a shape that fits one rate and one menu but not a boutique hotel that wants per-meal pricing, guest-side editability, a daily cut-off, kitchen handoff, and refund liability. Owner + client closed a 10-decision design conversation and approved the redesign as Phase 13. **Decisions. (1) Per-meal catalog with hotel-wide prices** — `settings/breakfastConfig.meals: BreakfastMeal[]` (was `silogItems[]`) gains `price`, `description`, `displayOrder`; existing `id / name / isActive` fields preserved; snapshots are immutable so a deactivated meal cannot be re-priced. (2) **Configurable cut-off, default 8:00 PM, hotel timezone** — `cutoffTime: "HH:MM"` (default `"20:00"`) + `cutoffTimezone` (defaults to `hotel.config.ts` timezone, falls back to `Asia/Manila`); applies to add, change, AND remove; **server-side enforcement** via a new `computeBreakfastCutoffState` helper (the server is the source of truth). (3) **Per-guest, per-date granularity, exactly one meal per slot** — no "No meal" placeholder; leaving a slot empty means skip. Children + extra-bed occupants are ordinary meal slots (the previous `breakfastIncludesChildren` toggle is removed). (4) **Step 1 "Room + Breakfast" toggle removed** — Step 1 is rooms + dates only; Step 2 gains a new "Breakfast" section with a per-date × per-guest meal grid; sidebar updates live; applies to public + corporate + walk-in flows. (5) **Discounts apply per the existing scope matrix** (decision #146) — Senior/PWD, voucher, Spark Rewards, corporate rate; the `allowOnlineSeniorPwdClaim` toggle remains the gate for online Senior/PWD claims, same as for room rates. (6) **Always rolls into the running booking balance** — no separate online-payment moment; removing an unpaid meal reduces the amount due; removing a paid meal opens a refund liability. (7) **Guest manageability via `/my-booking/breakfast`** — new guest post-booking page reusing the existing `ref + lookupToken` magic-link auth; cutoff countdown per date, locked dates read-only, served slots read-only; batched `POST /api/bookings/breakfast/update` is idempotent on `clientBatchId`. (8) **Front-desk + admin override with required reason** — confirmation modal collects free-text reason (min 5 chars); the reason writes to the audit entry + surfaces in the kitchen list's "Late changes" column; the `frontDeskOverrideEnabled` setting is a defensive kill switch (default true). (9) **Snapshot immutability** — `breakfastSelections[key].mealId / mealName / price` are frozen at order time; deleted menu items still show on existing bookings, tagged "(no longer offered)". (10) **Refund liability extends the existing refunds subcollection** — `OnsitePayment.category: "cancellation" \| "breakfast"` (NEW) + `breakfastRefundContext` (NEW, optional); no new collection; the existing Refunds admin view gains a `category` filter chip; the dashboard gains a pending-refunds badge. (11) **Kitchen handoff at cut-off** — printable admin view at `/admin/kitchen-list` (two columns: "Finalized at 8 PM" + "Late changes"); the dedicated real-time kitchen display is a follow-up only if the printable view is insufficient after a week of use. (12) **No migration** — production has zero bookings; legacy `hasBreakfast: boolean`, `breakfastRate: number`, `breakfastIncludesChildren: boolean`, `ratePerPersonPerNight`, `breakfastIncludesChildrenDefault` are deleted from the create path + Settings save + type defs in the same PR; read paths default new fields to `{}` / `0` / `false` for any legacy test data. (13) **Audit subcollection, not a field on the booking doc** — `bookings/{id}/breakfastAudit/{auditId}` for every add/change/remove/serve/unserve/override action; avoids the GOTCHAS "fan-out writes to a single array field" race. (14) **No new Vercel function** — the 6 new routes (1 public config read, 1 guest manage read, 1 guest update, 1 staff update, 1 mark-served, 1 kitchen list) are all handlers in the existing catch-all. (15) **No new email template** — the existing 8 W4.4 audit-extension templates + the `buildReservationEmailView` helper extend to render the new shape. **Tickets proposed:** BR-01..BR-08 (data model, Settings, Step 2 + Step 1 cleanup, guest manage page, staff drawer, refunds, kitchen list, email/PDF/Reports). Realistic solo-dev timeline: 6–8 weeks. **Gates:** GOTCHAS §Firebase (no fan-out writes; no client-side recompute of `breakfastTotal`), CRL-01 (refund idempotency reuses `OnsitePayment` + `handleAddRefund`), PEX-01..06 (24h post-Confirm hold + in-flow hold + 8 PM breakfast cut-off are three independent lifecycles; none intersect), MRB-01 (`guestIndex` is over the reservation's aggregate guest list, not per-room; kitchen list prints one row per guest, not per room), VERCEL-FUNCTION-LIMIT (catch-all dispatcher; 12-function cap unchanged), SECURITY (public manage gated on `ref + lookupToken` magic-link; staff routes staff-Token gated; Turnstile on every mutating call). **MD sync:** `BREAKFAST.md` (NEW), `SETTINGS.md §Breakfast & Dining`, `BOOKING-FLOW.md`, `BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md`, `EMAIL-PDF-STORAGE.md`, `BACKEND.md`, `TYPES.md`, `ROADMAP.md`. **Rejected:** keep flat-rate (cannot support per-meal pricing / manageability / refund liability); auto-pay online at order (inconsistent with rooms; ugly refund path; walk-in + staff-override split the model); require a "No meal" placeholder (forces the guest to fill every slot; "leave the slot empty" is simpler + supports partial-stay breakfast); build dedicated real-time kitchen display in v1 (premature); migrate the legacy fields (production is empty; clean delete is better than a parallel-models-forever alternative); add a separate `breakfastRefunds` collection (the existing `OnsitePayment` table with a new `category` field is the simpler path). |


| 231 | **Guest booking flow — the Discount Options card is removed entirely when the online Senior/PWD path is disabled (owner request 2026-10-13; shipped on `fix/hide-discount-options-when-online-claims-off`):** when `settings/hotelConfig.seniorPwdOnlineEnabled === false`, `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` now renders **no Discount Options card at all**. The pre-#231 surface kept the card shell + the "Discount Options" heading + a note ("Senior/PWD online claims are currently unavailable. Eligible guests may present a valid ID at check-in to receive the mandated discount.") while hiding only the selector + ID upload inside it — so a disabled toggle left a visible empty card with a "come back at check-in" instruction. The card container is now gated on `seniorPwdOnlineEnabled`, and the three now-redundant inner `seniorPwdOnlineEnabled &&` guards (picker / multi-room hint / ID upload) are deleted because the outer gate owns the condition. **Why:** an empty card reads as a broken or punitive feature and adds a step to the guest funnel; the front-desk path that actually honors RA 9994 / RA 10754 is unaffected, so the note advertised a path the guest can still use without needing to be told about it up front. **Unchanged:** the 20% rate stays hardcoded; the walk-in / drawer / post-booking `apply-discount` path is never gated by this toggle; the server still rejects `discountType ∈ {senior, pwd}` on online creates while disabled (`guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts:2046`); the client still auto-reverts a stale selection to `none` if the toggle flips mid-session. **Tests:** `guest-app/tests/api/post-booking-discount-controls.test.ts` now pins the card-level gate + two negative guards on the removed copy (so the note cannot silently return); `guest-app/tests/api/guest-per-individual-discount-guard.test.ts` drops the `seniorPwdOnlineEnabled` prefix from the multi-room-hint guard. **Spec:** `plan/features/RATE-MANAGEMENT.md §Discount rules`. |
| 232 | **BR-03: the public breakfast config route (2026-10-12):** `GET /api/bookings/breakfast/config` returns the meal catalog + cut-off + default instructions for the guest Step 2 UI and the Manage Breakfast page. Cached 60s on the server, no auth (public site content), rate-limited per IP at 60/min. The handler is a thin read — it loads `settings/breakfastConfig`, normalizes it, and serves the rich shape (isEnabled + cut-off time + cut-off timezone + default instructions + a meals array) that replaced the old flat per-person rate. The admin's per-date `overrideCutoff` flag on a `BreakfastSelectionEntry` is the staff escape hatch that lets the desk place a meal on a date whose cut-off has passed. |

This is the guest-facing half of the breakfast lifecycle. |
| 234 | **BR-05: the staff-side breakfast visibility + refund endpoints (2026-10-13):** `POST /api/bookings/breakfast/mark-served` (`{ bookingId, slotKey, action: "serve" | "unserve", reason }`) is the staff-side kitchen-visibility endpoint for the breakfast service, and `POST /api/bookings/breakfast/mark-refund-paid` is the staff-side "Mark as paid" endpoint for the BR-01 `breakfastRefundContext` refund-liability entries. |
| 235 | **BR-06: the shared refund-write helper (2026-10-13):** the underlying refund-write logic moved into `lib/refunds.ts` (`markRefundPaid`), which is the SINGLE source of truth for writing a refund — so the admin refunds view, the liability projection and the paid-marking path cannot disagree about a refund's state. |
| 236 | **BR-07: the kitchen prep endpoint + the admin Kitchen list page (2026-10-13):** `GET /api/bookings/breakfast/kitchen-list?date=YYYY-MM-DD` and `POST /api/bookings/breakfast/kitchen-list/acknowledge` power the admin `/admin/kitchen-list` page. The GET handler reads two streams — `finalized` (every `bookings/{id}/breakfastSelections[date-*]`) and the late-change stream — so the kitchen sees the production list and the changes made after it was printed. |
| 237 | **"I Need More Time" — the guest may extend the in-flow checkout hold (IMT-01..04; specced 2026-10-13, shipped 2026-10-14 on `fix/phase-12-imt-01-hold-dead-end` + `feature/phase-12-imt-02-hold-extend-endpoint` + `feature/phase-12-imt-03-hold-extend-cta`):** the guest-facing checkout hold can be extended without losing entered information or restarting the flow. **(a) The countdown was already shipping** — IFH-01 (2026-10-08) delivered the whole `bookingHolds/{id}` lifecycle, the banner, the hook, and the routes; the missing piece was only the button. **(b) A confirmed bug came first:** an expired hold was an unrecoverable dead end. `holdId` was minted once per page instance and never re-rolled, `onPickNewRoom` only rewrote `?step=select-room`, and the expired doc re-read as HTTP 200 `status: "expired"` so the hook's 404 → `POST /api/holds/start` branch never fired — producing a red-banner loop escapable only with a hard reload. IMT-01 mints a fresh id on that CTA. **(c) Revival is EXPLICIT-ONLY, and this is the load-bearing rule.** The id is never re-rolled on a plain re-entry and the hook never auto-invokes `extendHold`, because auto-reviving would make the countdown unexpirable — a guest sitting on the expired banner and reloading would get a fresh window forever, turning the timer decorative. Encoded in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md §Booking Flow` and pinned negatively. **(d) Extension is ADDITIVE from `max(now, expiresAt)`** — a guest with 13:30 left keeps that 13:30 and gains a window; a reset-from-now would destroy it, the opposite of the request. An expired hold restarts from now. **(e) `POST /api/holds/extend` takes `holdId` + `reservationId` ONLY** (`.strict()`); the window comes from the doc's own `holdMinutes` snapshot, so no unauthenticated caller can request a longer hold than the hotel configured — which is also what makes `MAX_IN_FLOW_HOLD_EXTENSIONS = 3` enforceable. Rate-limited 10/IP/min, tighter than start's 30. **(f) `"consumed"` is never extendable** — a committed booking's hold must not be resurrectable from a stale `?hold=` link; any other status is refused fail-closed. **(g) Staff see nothing** (owner answer 2026-10-13): a front-desk extend would be reassurance theater against a SOFT hold, and the surviving-booking case would pay the FOL-02 mapper-field tax to display a number belonging in a Firestore query. `extensionCount` still exists because the server enforces the cap from it and the guest banner reads it — functionally required, not analytics. No dedicated `extendedAt` field. **(h) Zero infrastructure cost:** no new Vercel function (a branch in the existing catch-all) and no Firestore rules change (`bookingHolds` has no `match` block; the write goes through the Admin SDK). Also fixed in passing: the ticking banner was an `aria-live` region updating every second, and a BACKEND.md drift claiming admin reads on `bookingHolds` were open when the collection is default-deny. 41 + 18 tests. |

| EC-01 | **Spark Rewards early check-in requests surface as persistent admin alerts + dashboard widget (owner decision 2026-08-21, single combined PR; shipped on `feature/early-checkin-admin-alerts`):** today, a member's early check-in request only notifies staff via a single email to `ADMIN_EMAIL` (per `SPARK-REWARDS.md §Early check-in perk`). When the desk is busy, the email gets buried and the request sits unanswered. Owner asked for a UI equivalent to the existing pending-payments dashboard alert. **Decisions.** **(1) Delivery channel — email + notifications collection + dashboard widget, NOT intercom.** The dormant `isEarlyCheckInRequest` intercom-message flag stays dormant per `SPARK-REWARDS.md §Out of scope`. **(2) PR shape — single combined PR, NOT split (bell-first + widget-second).** Splitting would touch the `NotificationType` union twice. **(3) Server write — `await writeNotification(...)` in the existing `/api/email?action=early-checkin-request` handler, NOT a new endpoint.** Mirrors the NC-01 post-ship fix. **(4) Admin action — reuse `AdminContext.resolveEarlyCheckin`.** **(5) Decline UX — shared `ConfirmForm` with required reason, NOT `window.prompt()`.** Per `plan/admin-app/CLAUDE.md §Mobile UX building blocks`. **(6) Deep-link target — `/dashboard?focus=early-checkin&bookingId=…`, NOT `/bookings?bookingId=…`.** **(7) Widget visibility — hidden when no requests are pending.** Per `DASHBOARD-OVERVIEW.md §Edge Cases`. **(8) PII safety — title is `Early check-in requested — ${bookingRef}` only.** Per Hard Rule. **Verified.** `admin-app` 1581/1581 + `shared` 569/569 + `tsc -p admin-app` + `tsc -p api` all clean; pre-existing `test-runs-staging-refresh` + `audit-guest-sev2-2026-07-07` failures present on `dev` HEAD before this PR (verified by stash + checkout dev + npm test); new `dashboard-early-checkin.test.ts` adds 10 source-text pins. No new Vercel function (reuses `POST /api/bookings/early-checkin-resolve`); `guest-app/api/[...route].js` rebuilt + committed. MD sync: `SPARK-REWARDS.md §Phase 12 addendum`, `NOTIFICATION-CENTER.md §UI Checklist`, `DASHBOARD-OVERVIEW.md §UI + Data/Logic`, `ROADMAP.md` (Phase 10B 14/15), this decision entry.


| EC-01b | **EC-01 FOL-02-class fix — admin `bookings` mapper hydrates `earlyCheckIn` from the snapshot (operator-reported 2026-08-21; proposed on `fix/ec-01-mapper-hydration`, v0.287.0 → v0.287.1):** the EC-01 ship at v0.287.0 landed the bell + dashboard widget + server write correctly, but the dashboard widget never rendered in production. The operator saw the bell alert on the member's request and the `notifications` doc arrived, but the widget was hidden. **Root cause (FOL-02 class — `GOTCHAS.md §Firestore snapshot mapper hydration`):** the admin `bookings` snapshot mapper at `AdminContext.tsx:1482+` explicitly enumerates every field it reads from the Firestore doc. The admin `Booking` type at `AdminContext.tsx:372` declared the optional `earlyCheckIn` field (matching the guest-app server write at `guest-app/server/handlers/email.ts:2338-2346`), but the mapper never read `data.earlyCheckIn` from the snapshot. Result: every booking's `earlyCheckIn` was `undefined`, the dashboard widget's `b.earlyCheckIn?.status === "requested"` filter was always `false`, and the widget stayed hidden. **Why the original test missed it.** The EC-01 source-text test at `dashboard-early-checkin.test.ts` pinned the **consumer** side (`DashboardPage.tsx` keys its filter on the field) but never checked the **producer** side (`AdminContext.tsx` hydrates the field). Source-text tests on the consumer don't catch mapper drops on the producer — the FOL-02 lesson applied to a different field on the same mapper. **Why the bell still worked.** The `notifications` doc write lives in the `/api/email?action=early-checkin-request` handler (`guest-app/server/handlers/email.ts:2353+`, awaiting `writeNotification`) and the bell reads from the `notifications` `onSnapshot` at `AdminContext.tsx:3680+` — a separate read surface from the bookings mapper. The server write + the bell + the deep-link routing all worked because they don't depend on the bookings mapper. The widget was the only consumer that read via the bookings mapper, so it was the only surface that broke. **Fix — one defensive-coercion line at the end of the mapper:** `earlyCheckIn: data.earlyCheckIn ?? null`. The `?? null` guard floors legacy bookings without the field to "no early check-in request" so the widget's `?.status` filter short-circuits cleanly. The same shape as FOL-02's `reservationId: data.reservationId ? ... || null : null` and BSP-01's `breakfastServed: data.breakfastServed || {}` hydrations at the same site — the same hydration discipline across the mapper. **Regression test — `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-class-early-checkin-mapper-hydration.test.ts` (NEW, 6 source-text pins):** (1) the hydration `useEffect` is present + locatable; (2) the mapper reads `data.earlyCheckIn` (the assertion that failed in RED); (3) the dashboard widget filter is keyed on the hydrated field; (4) the `Booking` type declares all 7 fields (`status` + `requestedTime` + `notes` + `requestedAt` + `resolvedAt` + `resolvedBy` + `staffNote`); (5) the widget never reads the field from a separate listener (no `onSnapshot` double-cost); (6) wire-path sanity — shared `NotificationType` union still includes `early-checkin-request`. **Pattern for future fields.** Any new optional field on the `Booking` contract must be added to **both** the type declaration AND the snapshot mapper, with the same defensive coercion the writers use. The `fol-02-class-*.test.ts` naming pattern is the canonical place to add a new pin (the `FOL-02` test file at `fol-02-admin-bookings-mapper-missing-fields.test.ts` already covers the MRB-01 reservation cluster; new field additions land in a sibling file named after the field class, not as new assertions inside the existing file — keeps the regression tests grep-discoverable by the field they protect). **Verified:** admin-app 1587/1587 (was 1581, +6 from the new regression file), `tsc -p admin-app` clean, pre-existing `test-runs-staging-refresh` + `audit-guest-sev2-2026-07-07` failures on `dev` HEAD unchanged, no server change → no api bundle rebuild needed (Husky `check:api-bundle` verified). **MD sync:** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (this entry), `GOTCHAS.md §Firestore snapshot mapper hydration` (no change — the existing pitfall description already covers this bug class, the EC-01 ship was the test for whether agents had internalized it; the lesson is "always pin the producer side of the data path in the test for any new mapper field", which is the test discipline gap this fix exposes). **Gates:** FOL-02 (the same hydration discipline — type declaration + explicit mapper read + defensive coercion — applies; the FOL-02 test file's slice shape was mirrored for the new file's slice), MRB-15-10 (the field-additions-test-contract pattern — new mapper fields land in a sibling test file named after the field class), BSP-01 (the defensive-coercion line shape + the `?? null` vs `|| {}` pattern matches the existing breakfastServed hydration at the same site), NC-01 (no `writeNotification` call site was touched; the 7 existing awaited sites are unchanged). **Rejected:** `(a)` run a runtime round-trip test with the Firestore emulator — the source-text pin + the live production bundle verify (`grep -c "earlyCheckIn" /tmp/prod-bundle.js` shows the new line is in the minified bundle) are sufficient contract enforcement; the runtime harness would add ~150 lines for a surface that already has two layers of verification. `(b)` add a `useEffect` in `DashboardPage` that subscribes to the booking doc directly — would double the read cost AND drift from the canonical booking state (the same anti-pattern the BSP-01 lesson warns against). `(c)` move the mapper to a custom hook to make future field additions less error-prone — premature for a single new field; revisit if a third FOL-02-class bug surfaces within the next quarter. **`git log` trail:** `f2a0eed chore(dev): merge EC-01 FOL-02-class fix — earlyCheckIn mapper hydration (v0.287.1)` → `430bb8e fix(admin): hydrate earlyCheckIn in bookings mapper — EC-01 widget renders (FOL-02 class)` → Husky version bump `6c02583`.


| EC-02 | **Spark Rewards early check-in perk: booking-flow entry point + admin disable toggle (operator-requested 2026-08-21; proposed + shipped on `feature/early-checkin-booking-flow-toggle`, v0.287.1 → v0.288.0):** the EC-01 ship at v0.287.0 added the bell + dashboard widget + server notification, but the guest entry point was RewardsPage-only. Operator requested two surface changes that shipped together: **(1) extend the perk to Step 4 of the booking flow** (the confirmation page, single-booking flow with no picker since the URL params carry the bookingRef) **with explicit disclaimer copy** ("not guaranteed, subject to approval, email will be received for the approval or rejection"), AND **(2) add a global admin toggle** in Settings → Rewards tab to disable the perk entirely (admin-only Settings page, same UI shape as the existing points + member-discount toggles). **Decisions.** **(1) Server gate (defense-in-depth, Hard Rule #1)**: the `/api/email?action=early-checkin-request` handler now reads `settings/rewardsConfig.earlyCheckInEnabled` on every request and returns 403 if false. The gate runs BEFORE the booking write so a disabled toggle never lands a doc. Mirrors the existing `memberDiscountEnabled` / `pointsEnabled` reads at `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts:2582` / `:11102`. Failure to read the config defaults to true (logged + perk proceeds) so a transient read failure doesn't accidentally lock members out — same pattern as the existing `bookings.ts` catch. **(2) No new server route**: the booking-flow button POSTs to the existing `/api/email?action=early-checkin-request` route; no new Vercel function (Hobby plan stays at the 12-function cap). **(3) Toggle defaults to true**: the `settings/rewardsConfig.earlyCheckInEnabled` field is optional + the guard reads `!== false`, so pre-EC-02 deployments keep the perk on until the admin explicitly turns it off (non-breaking per Hard Rule §non-breaking-only-for-existing-data). **(4) RewardsPage also gated on the toggle**: consistency — the disable has to hide the perk on BOTH the booking flow and the RewardsPage, otherwise a member who reloads the page with stale state could still submit from the rewards surface. **(5) Strong disclaimer copy in 3 places**: (a) inline next to the booking-flow button, (b) repeated inside the modal so the member sees it before hitting submit, (c) in the staff email intro so the receiving operator knows the workflow they're entering ("requires your approval. The guest will receive an email once you approve or decline."). **(6) Single-booking flow, no picker**: Step 4 only has one booking in scope (the URL params carry the bookingRef), so the existing RewardsPage multi-booking picker is unnecessary here — the modal is a single time + notes field. **(7) Admin toggle has sub-text**: matches the existing pattern (member discount toggle has no sub-text; the new toggle has a 1-line sub-text explaining the scope: "When off, the button disappears from the booking confirmation page and the server rejects new requests with 403. Pending + historical requests on existing bookings stay visible until resolved.") so the admin understands the toggle's effect before flipping it. **(8) Test the server gate too**: the new test file `early-checkin-booking-flow-toggle.test.ts` pins the 403 path with a source-text guard (`earlyCheckInEnabled\s*[!=]==\s*false` + `return\s+res\.status\(403\)`), not just the UI hide — defense-in-depth is invisible if the test doesn't cover both surfaces. **Verified:** admin-app 1598/1598 (was 1587; +11 from new file), shared 569/569, `tsc -p admin-app` + `tsc -p api` + `guest-app` typecheck all clean. Pre-existing `test-runs-staging-refresh` + `audit-guest-sev2-2026-07-07` failures on `dev` HEAD unchanged. No Vercel function added. Husky `check:api-bundle` verified + version bump to 0.288.0 (feat: → MINOR bump). **MD sync:** `SPARK-REWARDS.md §Phase 12 addendum §2` (this entry's implementation record). **Gates:** NC-01 (the `await writeNotification` call site at `email.ts:2386+` is unchanged — the new gate block sits BEFORE it and is awaited inside a try/catch that defaults to true on read failure, mirroring the existing pattern at `bookings.ts:2582-2586`), FOL-02 (the admin `bookings` mapper hydration at `AdminContext.tsx:1725+` is unchanged — no new `Booking` field added; the toggle lives on `settings/rewardsConfig`, not on a booking doc), MRB-15-10 (the rewards-config shape is additive — no field was removed from the existing surface), NC-02d (the `notifications` rules at `firebase/firestore.rules` are unchanged — no new field on the existing schema). **`git log` trail:** `d7dfa2f feat(spark-rewards): early check-in request button on booking flow + admin disable toggle (EC-02)` → Husky version bump `c1df90` → MD sync commit pending → merge to `dev` → push.


| IFH-01 | **In-flow hold (Steps 2 + 3 countdown banner) — soft UX signal, not a hard inventory lock (owner-requested 2026-10-08; shipped on `feature/hold-countdown-banner`):** the public booking flow's Step 1 → Step 2 transition moved the user into the form with no visible signal that the room was being held. Owner asked for a countdown banner — "Your room is held for you until 10:56 AM · Time left: 10:18. Nobody else can book it while you finish and pay." — to set urgency. **Decisions.** **(1) Soft hold, NOT a hard inventory lock.** The banner copy is honest: "We'll lock it in the moment you confirm. If it goes through, you're set." (Tightened in HCT-01, decision #270 — the previous "if another guest books first, we'll let you know and you can pick a new room" spelling was longer than necessary.) The authoritative double-booking guarantee is unchanged (the Firestore transaction in `handleCreateBooking` per `plan/features/AVAILABILITY-LOCKING.md`). The `bookingHolds/{id}` collection is **not** consulted by the public availability endpoint and is **not** read by the create transaction — two guests can both see the room as available and both have an active hold; the transaction picks the first commit and the second gets a "Room no longer available" error on Confirm. This keeps the existing safety net untouched and avoids the complexity of a hard lock (which would need availability-endpoint filtering, transaction-time checks, and a Janitor sweep to free abandoned holds before they're actually available again). **(2) Ephemeral collection, not a field on `reservations/{id}`.** The reservation header doesn't exist until the booking transaction commits (MRB-01 stamps it inside `handleCreateBooking`). Pre-creating the header on the Step 1 → Step 2 transition would force MRB-01 to handle a "draft" stage that every downstream reader would have to ignore. A dedicated `bookingHolds/{id}` collection keeps the hold lifecycle (start → consume OR expire) isolated from the reservation/booking lifecycle. **(3) Idempotent start endpoint, client-preallocated `holdId`.** Same pattern as MRB-02's `reservationId`: the client preallocates a UUIDv4 in Step 1's `useState` lazy init, threads it through the URL as `?hold=<id>`, and the `useInFlowHold` hook calls `POST /api/holds/start` on first read. The start endpoint is idempotent on the same `holdId` — a retry returns the existing doc unchanged. **(4) 15-minute window, hardcoded constant.** `IN_FLOW_HOLD_MINUTES = 15` in `shared/utils/bookingInFlowHold.ts` with bounds 5..30. Industry norm for in-checkout holds is 10..20 min (Booking.com ~20, Expedia ~30, most hotel direct sites at 15); the form is short (2..5 min for a returning guest, 5..8 min for a first-timer reading the terms), so 15 gives a comfortable buffer without letting inventory sit sticky. The constant will move to a per-hotel settings field in a future commit (Settings → Hotel Config → `inFlowHoldMinutes`); for v1 it's a shared constant. **(5) Consume is best-effort, OUTSIDE the booking transaction.** `handleConsumeInFlowHold(holdId, reservationId)` fires after the booking transaction commits. A failed consume (network blip, hold lapsed between booking commit and the consume call) is logged at `warn` level and never rolls back the booking. The hold is a UX signal, not a hard lock — a stale hold doesn't matter. The consume marks the doc `status: "consumed"` and stamps `inFlowHoldId` + `inFlowHoldMinutes` onto the booking doc for analytics. An idempotent replay (same `reservationId` + same `requestFingerprint`) does NOT re-consume — the original commit already consumed it, and the second consume would 409 with "already-consumed". **(6) Public endpoint surface: `POST /api/holds/start` (Turnstile-gated, 30/IP/min) + `GET /api/holds/read` (60/IP/min) — no new Vercel function.** Both routes are registered in the existing catch-all at `apiRouter.ts`, so the Vercel function count stays at the Hobby plan's 12-function cap. The Janitor sweep for stale `"active"` holds past their `expiresAt` is a future follow-up (will mirror the existing PEX-06 hold-expiry cron). **(7) No Firestore `onSnapshot` from the guest client.** `useInFlowHold` uses a 1Hz `Date.now()` tick + a 30-second resync re-fetch via `GET /api/holds/read`. The 1Hz tick is cheap, matches the existing booking-lookup polling pattern, and avoids needing a permissive Firestore rule on `bookingHolds/{id}`. The 30-second resync guards the rare clock-skew case. **(8) No `HoldCountdownBanner` on Step 1 or Step 4.** The banner is gated to Step 2 + Step 3 only (the form sections). Step 1 doesn't show it because the user is still picking — no hold is started. Step 4 hides it because the booking has committed (`status: "consumed"` → the hook returns `isConsumed: true` → the banner renders nothing). **(9) No walk-in hold.** `/api/bookings/create-walkin` does NOT accept a `holdId` — the desk has the keys, no UX timer is needed. The walk-in schema is unchanged; pre-walk-in hold callers can be added in a future commit if the desk flow needs it. **(10) Two new booking fields, both optional.** `bookings/{id}.inFlowHoldId: string | null` (link back to the consumed hold) and `bookings/{id}.inFlowHoldMinutes: number | null` (snapshot of `IN_FLOW_HOLD_MINUTES` at create time, so future reports can attribute expired holds to a specific window even if the constant changes). Both fields are `null` for walk-in bookings and for legacy pre-hold-banner callers. **Verified:** `npm run check:api-bundle` clean (Husky pre-commit), `tsc -p api` + `guest-app` typecheck clean. Husky version bump for the new feature (MINOR per Conventional Commits). **MD sync:** `BOOKING-FLOW.md §In-flow Hold` (UX checklist + lifecycle), `AVAILABILITY-LOCKING.md §In-flow Hold` (the interaction with the existing locking guarantee), `BACKEND.md §bookingHolds/{holdId}` (the collection schema), `API-ROUTES.md §Hold Routes` (the new endpoints), `TYPES.md §Booking Hold` (the `BookingHold` type + the two new `Booking` fields), `ROADMAP.md` (the feature log), this decision entry. **Gates:** PEX-01 / PEX-02 / PEX-06 (the in-flow hold is a separate, soft lifecycle from the 24h post-Confirm hold — the two never intersect, the in-flow hold's consume is best-effort and never rolls back the booking transaction), MRB-01 (the in-flow hold does not require a "draft" stage on the reservation header — the ephemeral `bookingHolds/{id}` collection keeps the hold lifecycle isolated), MRB-02 (the in-flow hold reuses the same client-preallocation + idempotent-replay pattern as the reservation id), FOL-03 (the consume call is OUTSIDE the booking transaction, so the FOL-03 read-order rule does not apply; the in-transaction write path is unchanged), EC-02 (the new booking flow `HoldCountdownBanner` and EC-02's confirmation-page early-check-in button are independent surfaces — no shared state). **Rejected:** `(a)` add a hard inventory lock — adds complexity (availability-endpoint filtering, transaction-time checks, Janitor sweep) and the existing transaction-based guarantee already prevents double-booking. `(b)` use Firestore `onSnapshot` for the live countdown — would require a permissive security rule on `bookingHolds/{id}` (every anonymous guest can listen to every hold) and the polling pattern is cheap + matches existing usage. `(c)` stamp `inFlowHoldExpiresAt` on the reservation header — same MRB-01 "draft stage" problem as option (a) above. `(d)` add the hold to the `admin_settings` Settings UI in this same PR — premature; the shared constant works for v1 and the per-hotel setting is a clean follow-up. `(e)` add the hold to the Step 1 page too — the user is still picking at Step 1, no room is selected yet, starting a hold before the Step 1 → Step 2 transition adds noise without value.

the interface fields are a narrow window of read-tolerance for any test data or pre-production demo content. **Verified.** `npx tsc -p admin-app --noEmit` 0 errors · `npx tsc -p guest-app --noEmit` 0 errors · `npx tsc -p shared --noEmit` 15 pre-existing baseline errors (all pre-date BR-08) · `vitest shared/__tests__/` 42 files / 613 tests pass (includes `br-08-closeout.test.ts` 13/13) · `vitest guest-app/tests/api/` 146 files / 1733 pass + 206 skipped (the skipped ones are the legacy audit-trail tests preserved as `it.skip`) · `vitest admin-app/src/__tests__/` 138 files / 1717 pass + 14 pre-existing baseline failures (none new from BR-08). **Files changed.** 69 modified + 2 new (the `br-08-closeout.test.ts` + `br-08-email-templates.test.ts` tripwires), all scoped to `admin-app/` + `guest-app/` + `shared/` per the parent's scope constraint. **MDs.** `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` Phase 14 row updated to `✅ BR-01..BR-08 shipped 2026-10-14` + the per-ticket breakdown entries flipped from ⬜ to ✅ with commit hashes. **Rejected.** `(a)` keep `calculateBreakfastAddOn` as a back-compat shim — every caller has migrated; | 239 | **Booking flow — explicit 5-step layout with a dedicated "Add-ons" step (BR-09, owner-requested 2026-10-14, shipped on `feature/breakfast-redesign` via `e539f44`; `feat:` MINOR bump v2.0.0 → v2.1.0):** the public + corporate + admin walk-in + admin calendar-create booking flows render 5 distinct visual steps instead of the legacy 4 — **(1) Select Room**, **(2) Add-ons** (newly extracted), **(3) Guest Details** (old Step 2 minus breakfast, with the per-guest count dropped from a bold `<TextField>` to a muted `<p data-testid="guest-count-label" class="text-xs text-gray-500">`), **(4) Review**, **(5) Confirmation**. **Why now:** the BR-12 "other add-ons" feature (airport transfer v1) needs a dedicated step that doesn't share a step with guest details. BR-09 establishes the layout so BR-12 can land as a sibling section below the breakfast grid without restructuring the flow again. **Decisions.** **(1) Add-ons step name, not Breakfast or Extras.** Forward-looking: BR-12 adds a second section (airport transfers) to the same step. "Add-ons" reads correctly with one section and with several. **(2) Muted per-guest label, not bold input.** The "Number of guests" `<TextField>` was redundant — the value is derived from the distributed room cart (`{ roomName, occupancy }[]`) via `renderGuestCountLabel()`. A muted gray-on-white label reads as derived context, matches the FRONTEND.md §UX Philosophy "show, don't ask" rule, and removes one editable field from the funnel. The internal `guestDetails.guestCount` state is preserved so `setOccupancy` continues to maintain the create payload + the booking-write validation downstream. **(3) Numeric `?step=N` back-compat with indefinite sunset.** Pre-BR-09 shared links (e.g. `?step=2` = old "Guest Details") now route through `normalizeStepKey()` which maps the numeric form to the closest current step. The numeric form lives indefinitely — no sunset ticket — so bookmarked URLs continue to work for the lifetime of the deployment. **Soft-cart-fallback:** `?step=2` with no rooms in cart redirects to Step 1 via the `distributedRoomCart.length === 0` guard, so a guest with a stale link never sees an empty form. **(4) Step indicator uses `flex flex-wrap` instead of a hardcoded column count.** The legacy `sm:grid-cols-4` broke under 5 steps. The new flex layout adapts to any future step count (e.g. if BR-13 ever splits Add-ons into multiple sub-steps). **(5) Walk-in + calendar-create modals: minimal-split only.** The walk-in + calendar-create modals added `walkinStep` / `calendarStep` state, a stepper, and Continue/Back nav. The breakfast grid was extracted into Step 2. The rest of the form fields (contact, room stays, payment) stay on Step 1 / Step 3 because the modal is a single `<form>` whose full visual re-shuffle would balloon the diff and risk the BR-05 staff-drawer tests. Full visual split is filed as BR-09.1 (a separate, smaller refactor). **(6) Corporate "Add-ons" is a placeholder.** The corporate rate is a flat rate with no per-date breakfast grid, so the new corporate Add-ons step renders "No add-ons available" today + scales to host BR-12 sections when BR-12 lands. Parity with the public flow's step model is intentional even though the content is currently empty. **(7) `Place booking` CTA on the Review step.** The pre-BR-09 "Confirm Booking" / "Confirm Corporate Booking" copy was inconsistent across the two flows; BR-09 standardizes on "Place booking" (the corporate flow matches). **(8) Zero infrastructure cost.** No new Vercel function (the existing `handleCreateBooking` is unchanged) and no Firestore rules change. **(9) Admin-app StepIndicator is a fresh mirror of the guest-app shape (`admin-app/src/components/StepIndicator.tsx`, NEW in BR-09).** Same `flex flex-wrap` layout, same accessible semantics (`aria-current="step"` + `aria-label`). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing (`bookingHolds`, `reservations`, `bookings` schemas are all unchanged). **What this changes for the server contract** — nothing. **What this changes for the create-call payload** — zero. `handleCreateBooking` reads the same `distributedRoomCart` + `guestDetails` shape; the breakfast section renders inside the Add-ons step but writes the same `breakfastSelections` / `breakfastTotal` shape into the booking payload (BR-03 contract). **Verified.** `npx tsc -b admin-app` 0 errors · `npx tsc -b guest-app` 0 errors · `vitest`: 4357 pass / 18 pre-existing failures unchanged (the 18 are pre-BR-09 baseline failures — IMT/early-checkin/members-redeem-points, none touched by BR-09) · `+31 new BR-09 tests (22 guest-app + 9 admin-app) all pass`. **Files changed.** 6 modified (`BookingPage.tsx`, `CorporateBookingPage.tsx`, `StepIndicator.tsx` x 2 — guest + admin, `BookingsPage.tsx`, `CalendarPage.tsx`, `br-03-step2-breakfast-section.test.ts` header-note) + 3 new (`br-09-step-flow.test.ts`, `br-09-walkin-calendar-step-flow.test.ts`, `admin-app/src/components/StepIndicator.tsx`). **MDs updated.** `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` Active & Recent row + Phase 14 BR-09 entry marked ✅; this decision #239 entry. **Rejected.** **(A)** "Breakfast" or "Extras" as the Step 2 name — they read correctly with one section but break if BR-12 lands. "Add-ons" is the durable label. **(B)** Replace the muted label with a read-only `<TextField value={guestCount} readOnly />` — a read-only input still renders as an input affordance (cursor, hover, focus ring) and reads ambiguously (is it editable?). The `<p>` is unambiguous. **(C)** Sunset the numeric `?step=N` form at a future date — operators with 3-year-old shared links would lose them; the fallback is harmless. **(D)** Auto-roll a fresh `holdId` on every entrance to the Add-ons step — would interact badly with the IMT-03 "I need more time" hold-extend path (a step transition would force a re-mint). The current "soft cart fallback → Step 1" is the right pattern when the cart is empty.

| 244 | **Breakfast cut-off toggle + 'No add-ons, continue' ghost button (BR-10, owner-requested 2026-10-14, shipped on `feature/breakfast-redesign`; `feat:` MINOR bump v2.1.0 → v2.2.0 via the Husky Conventional-Commits hook):** two small additive additions to the BR-09 Add-ons step. **(1) `cutoffsEnabled: boolean` on `BreakfastConfig`** — admin-side switch in the Breakfast & Dining tab of `SettingsPage.tsx`, sitting next to the existing `cutoffTime` input (mirrors the `Front desk can override the cutoff` toggle, `bg-primary` for the active state per FRONTEND.md, helper text "When off, guests can edit breakfast meals up until the morning of service."). The shared `computeBreakfastCutoffState` helper accepts an optional `cutoffsEnabled` on its input; when the flag is `false` the helper short-circuits to `{ isLocked: false, reasonOrCutoff: "cutoffs-disabled" }` while still computing `cutoffAt` (so the UI can render the disabled-state hint). Every known caller (BookingPage, ManageBreakfastPage, BookingDrawerWorkspace, WalkinBreakfastGrid, the kitchen-list internal helper, breakfast-manage.ts, breakfast-mark-served.ts, breakfast-staff-update.ts via `assertNotLocked` in breakfast-selections.ts) threads the flag through — no caller is allowed to bypass the helper with a side-channel `now >= cutoff` comparison (pin: `every caller of computeBreakfastCutoffState threads cutoffsEnabled` + `no caller hard-codes isLocked`). **Why now:** operations needed a single-click "relax cut-offs" lever for last-minute check-ins without staff having to type a per-guest override reason for every meal (the BR-05 `overrideCutoff + overrideReason` path stays). The new toggle is a global per-hotel setting, not a per-stay lever, which matches the operations scope. **(2) "No add-ons, continue" ghost button** — parallel CTA on the Add-ons step in all four BR-09 surfaces (BookingPage public flow, CorporateBookingPage, BookingsPage walk-in modal Step 2, CalendarPage calendar-create modal Step 2), styled with the muted gray-ghost recipe (`bg-transparent text-gray-500 border border-gray-300 hover:bg-gray-50`) per FRONTEND.md §Button Variants. Both buttons call the IDENTICAL destination (URL-based for the public + corporate flows: `step=guest-details`; callback-based for the admin modals: the same `setWalkinStep` / `setCalendarStep` advance arrow — every `onClick` handler byte-identical, pinned by the BR-10 tripwires that count the `setWalkinStep((current) => (current < 4 ? ...))` arrow references inside the dual-button block). No new step key, no shortcut for skip. **Why now:** the BR-09 step-flow refactor split Add-ons out as a dedicated step, and a guest who wants to skip the breakfast grid entirely was left with only a "Continue to guest details" button — the new ghost button gives that flow an explicit affordance without minting a new step or a separate confirmation. **Decisions. (1) Cut-off bypass is a config switch, not a per-stay override.** A per-stay override (e.g. "this booking can edit after cutoff") would have ballooned the booking schema + the audit trail; a config switch is operationally equivalent and zero-schema. **(2) `cutoffsEnabled` is required on `BreakfastConfigSchema`, not defaulted in the schema.** The reader (`normalizeBreakfastConfig`) defaults missing values to `true` for legacy docs, but the writer must always be explicit — a silent Zod default would let a careless refactor lose the field. **(3) `reasonOrCutoff: "cutoffs-disabled"` label, not a special-cased field on `CutoffState`.** Single optional enum-stable label field (the JSDoc explicitly reserves the right to add more labels without an interface break). A discriminated union was rejected as overkill for two states. **(4) The helper computes `cutoffAt` even when the toggle is off.** The rule still runs — the UI wants the "would-be cutoff moment" hint, and a future ui decision (e.g. "render the disabled hint as a slightly dimmer version of the normal countdown") can drop in without re-touching the helper. `minutesUntilCutoff` is set to `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` on the disabled arm so any renderer that pipes the countdown into a "minutes remaining" line gets "always open" instead of a negative number. **(5) Kitchen-list handler reads the toggle from Firestore on every uncached request.** The BR-07 kitchen-list always used the BR-01 default `cutoffTime` + `cutoffTimezone` for performance (one less Firestore hop per kitchen page load). BR-10 re-reads the toggle on every uncached request (cached 30s in-process) so the late-change split honors the admin-side flip immediately; the existing `cutoffTime` + `cutoffTimezone` defaults stay (no extra hydration). A Firestore blip falls back to conservative `true` (cut-offs enforced). **(6) Ghost button uses the muted gray-ghost recipe, NOT the `components/GhostButton.tsx` brand-primary variant.** `GhostButton` renders `border-primary text-primary` — visually competing with the primary "Continue to guest details" CTA on the same row. FRONTEND.md §Button Variants specifies the muted recipe for the secondary-affordance case; we inline the muted classes in the booking flow + the admin modals rather than introducing a `MutedGhostButton` component (one new component would force four new uses to keep parity, and a per-component variant is premature without more use cases). **(7) Walk-in + calendar-create modals: minimal-split only.** The dual-button block mirrors the same minimalist pattern as the BR-09 step-flow refactor — the ghost button is conditionally rendered (`{walkinStep === 2 && (...)}` / `{calendarStep === 2 && (...)}`) so the Step 1 + Step 3 modals still render a single primary CTA and Step 2 renders the dual CTAs. No visual re-shuffle beyond the conditional. **(8) Booking-drawer breakfast tab is intentionally NOT touched.** The drawer is post-booking and staff-only; the user-requested UX is for the public + staff WRITE-time funnels (guest + walk-in + calendar-create). A drawer-side ghost button would be confusing (a "no add-ons" affordance when the booking has already been placed + the staff is reviewing state). Filed separately if needed. **(9) Zero infrastructure cost.** No new Vercel function (the existing `handleSaveBreakfast` save flow covers the toggle + the `breakfast-manage.ts` / `breakfast-mark-served.ts` / kitchen-list handlers all read through `computeBreakfastCutoffState`), no Firestore rules change (`settings/breakfastConfig` is already admin-writable), and no new shared types or schemas beyond the optional `cutoffsEnabled` field + the `CutoffState.reasonOrCutoff?: "cutoffs-disabled"` (back-compat optional). **(10) Dual-button layout: primary left, ghost right on `sm:` breakpoints; stacked primary-above-ghost on `< sm:`.** The primary reads first on mobile (visual scan is top-down) and the primary sits at the read-position on desktop (thumb-reachable on mobile, click-target on desktop). A full-width-each stacked layout on mobile matches the house pattern for bottom-bar CTAs (verified against the FilterChip grid in the Rooms page). **Verified.** `npm run typecheck` clean on both workspaces · `npm run test:fast`: shared 45 files / 674 tests pass (+19 BR-10 cutsff-enabled), guest-app `test:api` 157 files / 1962 tests pass (+14 BR-10 dual-add-ons-buttons; 14 skipped unchanged; zero new failures), admin-app `src/__tests__/` 148 files pass / 1774 tests pass (+10 BR-10 walkin-calendar-skip-button; 1 pre-existing `dashboard-overdue-checkouts.test.ts` env-load failure unchanged). BR-09 tripwire tests (22 guest-app + 9 admin-app + 15 breakfast-cutoff characterization) still pass — no regression on the 5-step layout or the existing cut-off helper. **Files changed.** 17 modified (12 source + 5 server) + 3 new test files (`shared/__tests__/br-10-cutsff-enabled.test.ts` 19 assertions, `guest-app/tests/api/br-10-dual-add-ons-buttons.test.ts` 14 assertions, `admin-app/src/__tests__/br-10-walkin-calendar-skip-button.test.ts` 10 assertions). **MDs.** `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` Active & Recent row + Phase 14 BR-10 entry marked ✅ + the "Last updated" header rewritten; `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` this #244 entry. **Rejected. (A)** Per-stay `cutoffsDisabled` flag on the booking doc — would balloon the booking schema + the audit trail + the BR-08 close-out contract; a global config switch is operationally equivalent and zero-schema. **(B)** Sunsetting the toggle after a fixed window — operators with seasonal cut-off patterns (e.g. "off in summer, on in winter") need the toggle to live indefinitely, mirroring the existing `frontDeskOverrideEnabled` setting's contract. **(C)** Auto-detecting cut-off bypass from a low `breakfastSelections` population (e.g. "if < 10% of guests order breakfast, silently turn cut-offs off") — too implicit; a hotel that wants low-traffic days should flip the toggle explicitly. **(D)** Use the existing `components/GhostButton.tsx` brand-primary variant — visually competes with the primary CTA on the same row (see Decision (6) above); the muted recipe is the correct secondary-affordance treatment per FRONTEND.md. **(E)** Mint a new `STEP_KEYS.skipAddOns` step — the destination is the same `guest-details` step; a new step is a URL-state bloat for a UX affordance. **(F)** Render the dual-button pair on every step in the modal (Step 1 / 3 / 4) — the BR-09 minimal-split pattern is still the right scope; the dual CTAs belong on the Add-ons step only. **(G)** A confirmation modal on skip — the brief was explicit ("The grid is already locked-or-edit per `computeBreakfastCutoffState` so the only path forward is to handle the locks"), so a confirmation would be redundant friction. **(H)** Move the cut-off enforcement logic out of the shared helper into a per-app wrapper — the cut-off rule is the single source of truth the helper documents; wrapping it per-app would invite silent drift between the guest-app client render + the server-side gate + the manage page + the staff drawer. The helper remains the canonical gate and every caller threads the flag.


| 245 | **Other Add-Ons feature — airport transfer v1, scoped for the BR-13 generic-add-on refactor (BR-12, owner-requested 2026-10-14, shipped on `feature/br-12-other-add-ons`; `feat:` MINOR bump v2.2.0 → v2.3.0 via the Husky Conventional-Commits hook):** the new "Other Add-Ons" feature ships a flexible add-on catalog with airport transfer as the first non-breakfast add-on. v1 readies the schema for the BR-13 generic-add-on refactor (when a second add-on **type** — seaport transfer — lands) — the data model is intentionally close to the breakfast shape so BR-13 is a non-breaking refactor, not a rewrite. **Decisions. (1) Per-add-on `cutoffHoursBeforeCheckIn`, NOT a hotel-wide `otherAddOnCutoffsEnabled` toggle.** Every add-on has its own lead window (default 24h). The per-add-on `isEnabled` switch is the only cut-off-adjacent toggle for v1. The shared `computeOtherAddOnCutoffState` helper mirrors `computeBreakfastCutoffState` exactly: returns `{ isLocked, cutoffAt, minutesUntilCutoff, reasonOrCutoff?: "addons-disabled" | "cutoff-passed" }`. When `isEnabled === false`, the helper short-circuits to `isLocked: true, reasonOrCutoff: "addons-disabled"` (mirrors the breakfast `"cutoffs-disabled"` label). The BR-13 refactor may add a hotel-wide `otherAddOnCutoffsEnabled` toggle; for v1, the per-add-on `isEnabled` is operationally equivalent and zero-schema. **(2) Additive `Booking` fields — NEVER mutate the BR-08 frozen breakfast fields.** New fields: `otherAddOnSelections?: Record<dateKey, OtherAddOnSelectionEntry[]>`, `otherAddOnTotal: number`, `hasAnyOtherAddOns: boolean`, `otherAddOnServed?: Record<selectionKey, { servedAt, servedBy }>`. The pre-BR-12 bookings read with `otherAddOnTotal: 0`, `hasAnyOtherAddOns: false`, no selections. Back-compat is automatic. **(3) Per-day × per-add-on `quantity`, NOT per-day × per-guest slot.** Each entry's `quantity` is the number of guests using the add-on that day (default = the booking's total guest count, editable 0..guest count). This is the BR-12 divergence from the breakfast per-guest-slot shape — breakfast is guest-slot-based (each guest gets one meal per day); other add-ons are quantity-based (N guests use the add-on together). The shape difference is what makes BR-13 a refactor and not a schema migration. **(4) Separate Firestore collection `addOns/{addOnId}`, NOT a sub-field of `breakfastConfig`.** The two collections have different shapes (`breakfastConfig` is a single doc with a `meals: BreakfastMeal[]` sub-array; `addOns` is a collection of independent `AddOn` docs). A unified Settings tab would force a schema migration that BR-08 just closed out. The new "Other Add-Ons" Settings tab mirrors the existing Breakfast & Dining tab exactly and gives the add-on catalog its own visual identity. **(5) Pre-seed on first visit.** When the admin opens the "Other Add-Ons" Settings tab for the first time and the `addOns` collection is empty, the handler writes the `AIRPORT_TRANSFER_PRESEED` row (name "Airport transfer", ₱500/person, 24h cutoff). The public `/api/bookings/addons/config` endpoint falls back to the pre-seed read-only — a fresh deployment can render the Step 2 + Manage Other Add-Ons UI without waiting for the admin. The first Settings save turns the pre-seed into a real doc. **(6) Unified `markRefundPaid` helper gains the `"otherAddOn"` category discriminator.** The helper now handles three categories (`"cancellation"`, `"breakfast"`, `"otherAddOn"`). The same UPDATE-existing-pending-entry path applies (breakfast + other add-on are per-booking refunds; cancellation is per-reservation or per-booking depending on MRB-04 phase). The audit row lands in `otherAddOnAudit` (the BR-12 subcollection) with `action: "mark-served"` (the closest analog in `OtherAddOnAuditEntry`). The `OtherAddOnMarkRefundPaidSchema` Zod schema validates the body shape; the `otherAddOnRefundContext` blob on the refund doc mirrors the `breakfastRefundContext` shape exactly. **(7) Zero infrastructure cost.** Five new routes register in the existing catch-all `apiRouter.ts` (the Hobby-plan 12-function cap stays; see `plan/docs/VERCEL-FUNCTION-LIMIT.md`). Routes: `GET /api/bookings/addons/config` (60/min/IP), `GET /api/bookings/addons/manage` (10/min/IP, lookup-token gated), `POST /api/bookings/addons/update` (10/min/IP, Turnstile-gated), `POST /api/bookings/addons/staff-update` (30/min/IP, STAFF-Token + Turnstile-gated), `POST /api/bookings/addons/mark-served` (60/min/IP, STAFF-Token + Turnstile-gated), `POST /api/bookings/addons/mark-refund-paid` (30/min/IP, STAFF-Token + Turnstile-gated). The Firestore rules change is zero — `addOns`, `bookings/{id}/otherAddOnSelections`, `bookings/{id}/otherAddOnServed`, `bookings/{id}/otherAddOnAudit` all follow the existing pattern (admin-writable for the catalog, booking-writable for the subcollections). **(8) No kitchen list for other add-ons.** Add-ons are "served at check-in / by-arrival". The `mark-served` action stamps `otherAddOnServed` + `otherAddOnAudit` for the booking-drawer "Served at HH:MM" badge + the receipt, but no printable list view ships in v1. The kitchen-list component (`breakfast-kitchen-list.ts`) stays BR-05-only. A dedicated add-on preparation workflow is filed as a follow-up. **(9) Booking-drawer "Other Add-Ons" tab is read-only for v1.** The tab renders per-day selections + per-add-on subtotals + served badges. Full staff edit UI (parallel to `WalkinBreakfastGrid`) is a follow-up — the staff-update API is wired and the data model supports it, but UI parity with the breakfast grid is a separate ticket. The booking drawer's `activeSection` union grew one entry (`"addons"`) with a `Sparkles` icon (lucide-react). **(10) No new email templates for v1.** The existing "Confirmation" / "Update" / "Refund paid" templates get an additional paragraph for other add-ons. A dedicated `add-on-refund-paid` email template is a follow-up if operations finds the conflation confusing. **(11) No Walk-in modal Step 2 mirror (`WalkinOtherAddOnGrid`) for v1.** The walk-in + calendar-create modals' Step 2 dual-button block (BR-10) stays. The walk-in flow has a different UX (staff-curated, not guest-curated) and BR-12 v1 focuses on the public + guest-manage + staff-drawer surfaces. The follow-up is filed as an open item. **(12) No Reports → Sales → Add-Ons sub-tab for v1.** The Reports sub-tab is a follow-up because the existing Reports page doesn't have an Add-Ons surface yet. The data is queryable (the per-day × per-add-on `otherAddOnSelections` map + the denormalized `otherAddOnTotal`); only the UI surface is missing. **(13) Server-computed total, never client-trusted.** Both `recomputeOtherAddOnTotal(selections)` + `hasAnyOtherAddOns(selections)` are server-side helpers (`shared/utils/otherAddOnTotal.ts`). The booking write stamps `otherAddOnTotal` + `hasAnyOtherAddOns` server-side inside the same Firestore transaction as the selection write — the same pattern BR-01 established for `breakfastTotal` + `hasAnyBreakfast`. The client can preview the total locally but the server is the source of truth. **(14) Idempotency on `clientBatchId` (60s window).** The guest Manage Other Add-Ons save endpoint uses the same `idempotencyLog/{addons-update:{bookingRef}:{clientBatchId}}` pattern BR-04 established for breakfast. A retry within 60s returns the prior result without re-applying the writes. The idem window matches the BR-04 breakfast window — a single retry rule across the add-on surface so support staff don't need to remember two different windows. **(15) Audit subcollection names match the existing pattern.** `bookings/{id}/otherAddOnAudit` mirrors `bookings/{id}/breakfastAudit` exactly. The `OtherAddOnAuditEntrySchema` action enum mirrors `BreakfastAuditEntrySchema` minus the `serve` / `unserve` actions (other add-ons don't have the kitchen workflow) and adds `mark-served` (the closest analog for the booking-drawer served stamp). **(16) `cutoffHoursBeforeCheckInSnapshot` is frozen at first add.** Mirrors `breakfast.overrideCutoff: false` + `overrideReason: null` from the BR-01 entry — the per-selection snapshot is frozen so a later admin edit to the catalog lead time never rewrites the per-booking lock. **(17) Spec doc size: ~580 lines** (`plan/features/OTHER-ADD-ONS.md`) mirroring `BREAKFAST.md`'s structure: Decisions Locked + Data Model + Cutoff Model + Server Contract + Shared Helpers + Shared Zod Schemas + Guest UI + Admin UI + Email/PDF/Receipt + Edge Cases + Migration + Open Items + Implementation Order + Verification + Rejected Alternatives. **Verified.** `npx tsc -p guest-app --noEmit` 0 errors · `npx tsc -p admin-app --noEmit` 0 errors · `npx tsc -p shared --noEmit` 15 pre-existing baseline errors (none new from BR-12) · `vitest shared/__tests__/` 49 files / 726 tests pass (includes `br-12-data-model.test.ts` 13 + `br-12-cutoff-helper.test.ts` 8 + `br-12-total-helper.test.ts` 14 + `br-12-zod-schemas.test.ts` 17 = 52 new BR-12 tripwires) · `vitest guest-app/tests/api/` includes `br-12-addons-config.test.ts` 27 + `br-12-addons-manage.test.ts` 29 = 56 new BR-12 tripwires · `vitest admin-app/src/__tests__/` 147 files / 1768 tests pass + 2 pre-existing `@config` import failures unchanged. BR-09 + BR-10 tripwire tests still pass — no regression on the 5-step layout, the dual-button pattern, or the `cutoffsEnabled` toggle. **Files changed.** 18 new (5 shared utils + 6 server handlers + 1 server lib + 1 guest page + 5 test files + 1 spec MD) + 9 modified (shared types + shared schemas + shared index + apiRouter + refunds lib + guest App.tsx + admin BookingDrawer + admin AdminContext + admin BookingsPage). **MDs.** `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` Phase 14 row + the new BR-12 entry marked ✅; `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` this #245 entry; the new `plan/features/OTHER-ADD-ONS.md` spec. **Rejected. (A)** Generic-add-on abstract base class with `breakfast: BreakfastSelections` + `seaport: OtherAddOnSelections` discriminated union on the `Booking` interface — premature abstraction. The BR-13 refactor will pull the common patterns when a second add-on **type** lands, not before. **(B)** Hotel-wide `otherAddOnCutoffsEnabled` toggle for v1 — operationally redundant with the per-add-on `isEnabled` switch. The BR-13 refactor may add the toggle if operations asks for it. **(C)** "Other add-ons" sub-tab inside the existing Breakfast Settings tab — mixed-purpose UI. The new "Other Add-Ons" Settings tab mirrors the existing Breakfast tab exactly and gives the add-on catalog its own visual identity. **(D)** Add-on email template dedicated to the `mark-refund-paid` action — the v1 piggy-back on the existing breakfast refund-paid email copy is operationally acceptable. A dedicated template is a follow-up. **(E)** Walk-in modal Step 2 mirror (`WalkinOtherAddOnGrid`) — out of scope for v1. The walk-in flow has a different UX (staff-curated, not guest-curated) and the BR-12 v1 focuses on the public + guest-manage + staff-drawer surfaces. **(F)** Generic add-on admin Settings tab with both breakfast + other add-ons — the two collections have different shapes (`breakfastConfig` is a single doc with a meals sub-array; `addOns` is a collection of independent docs). A unified tab would force a schema migration that BR-08 just closed out. **(G)** New Vercel function per route — Hobby-plan 12-function cap (see `plan/docs/VERCEL-FUNCTION-LIMIT.md`). All 5 new routes register in the existing catch-all dispatcher. **(H)** Kitchen list parallel for other add-ons — add-ons are "served at check-in / by-arrival" and don't need a kitchen list. The `mark-served` action stamps the badge + the receipt, but no printable list view ships in v1. **(I)** Booking-drawer full edit UI for other add-ons — out of scope for v1. The data model + the API are wired; the UI parity with `WalkinBreakfastGrid` is a follow-up ticket. **(J)** Reports → Sales → Add-Ons sub-tab for v1 — the data is queryable; only the UI surface is missing. Follow-up. **(K)** Adding `addOnId` to `OnsitePayment` for the add-on refund context — the `otherAddOnRefundContext` blob is the discriminator (mirrors `breakfastRefundContext`), and `OnsitePayment` is admin-app-only. The BR-13 refactor may unify the two refund-context shapes into a single `addOnRefundContext` if a second add-on type with refund semantics lands. **(L)** Per-add-on email notifications (e.g. "Your airport transfer is confirmed for Dec 1") — the v1 piggy-backs on the existing confirmation email's "Other Add-Ons" paragraph. A dedicated per-add-on email template is a follow-up if operations wants per-add-on emails.


| 246 | **Stay-range normalization — a single `normalizeStayRange()` helper owns every guest-facing check-in/check-out pair (DR-01, owner-reported 2026-10-14; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the owner could set Check-in Oct 16 with Check-out Oct 9 and proceed. Three independent holes let that through. **(1) One-directional guard** — `DateRangePicker` clamped a typed check-out falling before `checkIn + 1`, but nothing handled **check-in being moved onto or past an already-set check-out**. Native `<input type="date" min>` constrains what the picker lets you *click*; it never corrects a value that is already out of bounds, so the inverted pair rendered and submitted. **(2) Unvalidated URL seeding** — `BookingPage` took `searchParams.get("checkIn")` raw, and `CorporateBookingPage`'s own `isValidDateKey` regex only checked the `YYYY-MM-DD` *shape*, so a shared or hand-edited `?checkIn=2026-10-16&checkOut=2026-10-09` opened the flow on garbage dates and the first availability query already used them. **(3) Not cosmetic — the server accepted it**: `createBookingSchema` validated both dates as free-form strings (`.min(1).max(40)`) and the night count was `Math.max(checkOut - checkIn, 0)`, producing a real, confirmed **0-night booking** instead of an error. `handleRescheduleBooking` already guarded this correctly. **Fix — five parts.** **(a)** NEW pure, string-based `shared/utils/stayRange.ts` — `normalizeStayRange()` plus `isValidIsoDate` / `addDaysToIsoDate` / `diffNightsBetweenIso` / `isValidStayRange`. Every date crossing the guest-app boundary is an ISO hotel-local date key, which compares correctly with `<`/`>` as plain strings (fixed-width zero padding), so the clamp logic needs no `Date` parsing; day math re-anchors at `T00:00:00Z` and mutates via `setUTCDate`, keeping it free of DST drift. **(b)** `DateRangePicker` became **self-healing rather than handler-guarded**: it renders `resolved.checkIn`/`resolved.checkOut` (the normalized values), so an invalid pair from a parent can never put an inverted range on screen, not just when the guest touches the inputs; it pushes the correction back to the parent so URL params + price summary + availability query all agree; the inputs are bounded by a property-timezone past floor and a 30-night booking horizon, with `min={checkIn + 1}` on check-out and check-out capped at a 30-night booking horizon. **(c)** Owner-chosen auto-correct semantics: when check-in moves onto/past check-out the picker **preserves the last valid night count** (a 2-night stay stays 2 nights) rather than collapsing to 1 and silently changing the price the guest agreed to — the Booking.com/Airbnb convention; it falls back to `checkIn + 1` only when there is no prior valid range. A latched inline hint (`role="status"`) explains every correction, because a guest who watches check-out slide unexplained will assume the picker is broken. **(d)** URL seeding on `BookingPage` + `CorporateBookingPage` now routes both params through the normalizer; the shape-only `isValidDateKey` regex is deleted. The remaining sibling surfaces are bounded too: `CorporateStaysPage`'s preferred check-in/out pair (previously two fully independent inputs with **zero** `min`/`max`) is now linked with a live inline error — and because it is a corporate *inquiry* (a requested window), not a bookable stay, it surfaces the constraint rather than silently rewriting the client's dates; `BookingLookupPage`'s extend-stay field clamps on change and disables its CTA while the range is invalid. **(e)** Server boundary: `handleCreateBooking` now 400s on a non-ISO date or a range shorter than one night, closing the 0-night booking hole. **Tests** — 17 behavioural cases in NEW `shared/__tests__/stay-range.test.ts` (including the exact regression: Oct 9 → Oct 11 preserved as Oct 16 → Oct 18; plus a fuzz-style sweep asserting no input pair can produce an inverted or zero-night range) + 15 source-text guards in NEW `guest-app/tests/api/dr-01-stay-range-guard.test.ts` pinning that the create guard runs *before* the booking transaction, that the picker renders the resolved values, and that the reschedule guard is not regressed away. 3 pre-existing source-text tests were re-pinned to the stronger contract (`nbs-2026-08-08-corporate-error-recovery` F10 ×3, `audit-batch-3-bi12-bi16` BI-12, `rfo-01-reservation-ref-lookup-surface` import line) — each kept its original intent, with the shape-only regex assertions replaced by normalizer ones. Total guest-app 1975 → 1990 passing. **MDs** — `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (DR-01 entry), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md` (§Stay-range normalization), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` #245 (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md`. **Gates** — AVAILABILITY-LOCKING (no change to the create transaction; the guard rejects before it), BOOKING-UNIFIED (untouched), BREAKFAST (BR-08/09/10 untouched; the normalizer is date-only), CORPORATE-BOOKING (the F10 URL-fallback contract is preserved, now strictly stronger), FOL-03 (no new transactional surface), SECURITY (no PII; the guard adds no logging). **Rejected** — auto-correct to `checkIn + 1` (owner-rejected: silently downgrades a 2-night stay and changes the agreed price), normalize only inside `onChange` (keeps the inverted range reachable via URL params or any future call site that bypasses the handler), silently auto-correct the corporate *inquiry* pair (it captures what the client wants, not a bookable stay — rewriting it would misreport the request), reuse `getNumNights`'s `Math.max(diff, 0)` clamp as validation (that clamp is what *hid* the 0-night booking), reject past check-in server-side (out of scope — same-day/walk-in and past-stay admin flows legitimately send those; the client picker already floors at today), a calendar widget with disabled ranges (a large UX change for no correctness gain over mutual `min`/`max`), MAJOR bump (no API contract change for valid clients — only the previously-accepted-invalid case now 400s; `fix:` triggers PATCH per Husky auto-versioning). |
