| 247 | **Admin UIs for Other Add-Ons — the four user-visible surfaces the BR-12 ticket implied (BR-12.1, 2026-10-15, shipped on `feature/br-12-1-admin-uis`; `feat:` MINOR bump v2.3.0 → v2.4.0 via the Husky Conventional-Commits hook):** the BR-12 ticket asked for "admin can add or remove add-ons + updateable pricing + the admin app" — only the data model + 5 server routes + the read-only booking-drawer tab shipped. BR-12.1 closes the four user-visible surfaces the literal ask implied: (1) **Settings → Other Add-Ons tab CRUD** — the most important, the user said "admin can add or remove" verbatim. (2) **Booking-drawer Other Add-Ons tab full edit UI** — replaces the BR-12 read-only tab. (3) **Walk-in + calendar-create modal Step 2 add-on grid** — the `WalkinOtherAddOnGrid` parallel to `WalkinBreakfastGrid`. (4) **Reports → Sales → Add-Ons sub-tab** — 5 metrics + top-add-ons bar chart + per-booking detail table. **Decisions. (1) Settings tab is a separate collection (`addOns/`), NOT a sub-field of `breakfastConfig`.** The two collections have different shapes (`breakfastConfig` is a single doc with a `meals[]` sub-array; `addOns` is a collection of independent docs). A unified tab would force a schema migration that BR-08 just closed out. The Settings tab reads the catalog directly from the `addOns` collection via `onSnapshot`; the new add/edit/delete operations write through `setDoc` + `deleteDoc` (no separate admin route — the public `addons-config` endpoint reads the same collection, the existing 5 BR-12 server routes are the staff write path). **(2) Catalog audit entries go to a top-level `auditLogs/{auditId}` collection, NOT a per-addOn subcollection.** The brief originally asked for `addOns/{addOnId}/addOnChangeLog/{auditId}` (mirroring the existing `settings/breakfastConfig/breakfastConfigChangeLog` pattern) but moved to a top-level `auditLogs/{autoId}` collection with `targetCollection: "addOns"` + `action: "addons.create" | "addons.update" | "addons.delete"`. The top-level collection is the simpler queryable surface for the Reports "late-change count" metric (no `collectionGroup` traversal needed for catalog changes) and the `targetCollection` discriminator lets future admin features (vouchers, store) reuse the same audit collection without a schema break. The per-booking `bookings/{id}/otherAddOnAudit/{autoId}` audit trail written by the staff-update server handler stays as-is (the per-booking trail is a different domain — selection-line audit, not catalog audit). **(3) `frontDeskOverrideEnabled` is borrowed from `BreakfastConfig` (not duplicated) for v1.** The booking-drawer "Other Add-Ons" tab's override flow for past-cutoff edits uses the same `frontDeskOverrideEnabled` flag the breakfast drawer consults. A per-feature flag (`otherAddOnFrontDeskOverrideEnabled`) is rejected for v1: (a) the operations workflow is the same (front desk staff unlock the cutoff for a specific booking with a reason), (b) duplicating the flag would force the Settings UI to render two identical toggles, and (c) the BR-13 refactor (when seaport transfer lands) can revisit the granularity if needed. The same `overrideReason` ≥ 8 chars threshold applies (matches the staff-update server gate). **(4) The per-add-on `isEnabled` is the only cut-off-adjacent toggle for v1.** NO hotel-wide `otherAddOnCutoffsEnabled` flag — the BR-12 spec rejected this for the same reason the BR-12 v1 itself did: operationally redundant with the per-add-on `isEnabled` switch. The BR-13 refactor may add the toggle when a second add-on **type** (seaport) lands with its own semantics. The new Settings tab's hotel-wide "Add-ons enabled" toggle is a **UI affordance only** (default `true`) that hides the Step 2 block + the drawer section + the Manage page when off — the underlying `getEnabledAddOnsByHotel` helper still returns `[]` when the catalog is empty, so the contract holds even if the toggle isn't persisted. Persisting the toggle to `settings/hotelConfig` is a follow-up. **(5) No new Vercel function.** All four surfaces are pure client-side + read through the existing 5 BR-12 server routes (the staff-update endpoint is the only write path). The Hobby plan's 12-function cap stays. **(6) Per-row `isEnabled` toggle writes immediately on click (no save button).** Mirrors the existing admin-CRUD pattern used in the breakfast meals table — the catalog is a low-stakes single-doc write so the optimistic update + `setDoc({ merge: true })` is the right UX. The audit row still captures the before/after state. **(7) The pre-seed runs once on first visit if `addOns` is empty.** The `AIRPORT_TRANSFER_PRESEED` row is written through `setDoc("addOns/airport-transfer", seed)` gated by `getDocs(addOns).empty`. A failed seed is logged + swallowed so a permission-denied edge case doesn't crash the tab. Subsequent visits skip the seed (the collection has rows). **(8) The add-on modal derives the doc id from the name (kebab-cased).** The admin doesn't type an id — entering "Airport transfer" produces `addOns/airport-transfer` automatically. Editing preserves the id (the existing booking selection-snapshots reference the immutable id; changing it would orphan selections). **(9) Drawer full edit UI: per-day × per-add-on grid with quantity inputs + add row + remove + locked-date badges + override modal + Save changes button.** Mirrors the existing `BookingDrawerBreakfastTab` shape exactly: a local `pending` map accumulates edits; the "Save changes" button flushes them through `staffUpdateOtherAddOns({ add, change, remove, overrideReason })` in one round-trip. The refund-due banner mirrors the breakfast tab's pattern — surfaces when removing a paid entry. **(10) The `WalkinOtherAddOnGrid` mounts in BOTH the walk-in modal AND the calendar-create modal Step 2.** The component is a shared `admin-app/src/components/otherAddOns/WalkinOtherAddOnGrid.tsx` (parallel to the breakfast grid's `admin-app/src/components/breakfast/WalkinBreakfastGrid.tsx`). It fetches the public `/api/bookings/addons/config` endpoint, filters through `getEnabledAddOnsByHotel`, and surfaces the rich `otherAddOnSelections` + `otherAddOnTotal` + `hasAnyOtherAddOns` to the create call. Both modals write the same shape; the difference is which `onChange` setter they bind (`setWalkinOtherAddOns` vs `setCalendarOtherAddOns`). **(11) The Reports sub-tab uses a single-pass `otherAddOnsBookingsInRange` filter + `otherAddOnsMetrics` memo.** The 5 metrics (total selections, total revenue, late-change count, pending refund liability, bookings with add-ons) compute in one `useMemo` over the date-range bookings. The `topAddOns` slice is top-5 by revenue desc — the same shape the breakfast sub-tab's "top meals" chart uses. The recharts `BarChart` with `dataKey="revenue"` renders the top-add-ons visualization. **(12) The Reports "late-change count" subscribes to `collectionGroup(db, "otherAddOnAudit")`.** Mirrors the existing breakfast late-change listener (`collectionGroup(db, "breakfastAudit")` + `occurredAfterCutoff === true`). The per-addOn catalog audit (top-level `auditLogs/{id}`) is a separate domain and is NOT included in this metric (the metric is the per-booking staff-edit trail, not the catalog-change trail). The "pending refund liability total" filters the same `collectionGroup(db, "refunds")` the breakfast metric uses, but for `category: "otherAddOn"` + `status: "pending"`. **Verified.** `npm run typecheck` clean on both workspaces. `npm run test:fast` clean: shared 50 files / 743 tests (+1 br-12-1-shared-audit), guest-app `test:api` 159 files / 2035 tests (+1 br-12-1-addons-enabled-flag; 2 pre-existing br-06-refunds-extension + 1 dashboard-overdue-checkouts baseline failures unchanged), admin-app `src/__tests__/` 154 files / 1849 tests pass (+5 br-12-1 tripwires). BR-09 / BR-10 / BR-12 tripwires still pass — no regression on the 5-step layout, dual-button pattern, `cutoffsEnabled` toggle, or the BR-12 read-only drawer tab (rewritten but the surface selectors `data-testid="booking-drawer-addons-${date}"` and the row selectors stay stable). DR-01 tripwires still pass — the date picker self-healing stays. **Files changed.** 8 modified (7 source + 1 test fix) + 6 new test files (4 admin-app + 1 guest-app + 1 shared) = **90 new BR-12.1 tripwires across 6 files**. **MDs.** `plan/features/OTHER-ADD-ONS.md` (the 4 Open items marked ✅), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (BR-12.1 row added to Phase 14), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` this #247 entry. **Gates** — AVAILABILITY-LOCKING (untouched; the drawer staff-update is a separate concern), BOOKING-UNIFIED (untouched), BREAKFAST (BR-08/09/10 untouched; the new tab is additive), CORPORATE-BOOKING (no change), FOL-03 (no change), SECURITY (no PII; audit logs are operator-uid only), VERCEL-FUNCTION-LIMIT (12-function cap unchanged). **Rejected. (A)** Catalog CRUD via a separate admin endpoint (e.g. `POST /api/addons/create` with STAFF-Token gate) — the public addons-config endpoint reads the same collection and the staff write path is already gated through `addons-staff-update`; an admin-only endpoint would duplicate the Firestore rules + STAFF-Token gate for no new functionality. **(B)** Adding the catalog CRUD to the `addons-staff-update` handler as a new action discriminator — the handler is purpose-built for per-booking selection edits (the BR-12 read/write contract); the catalog CRUD is a different domain (admin settings) and mixing them would dilute the audit trail. **(C)** Generic add-on admin Settings tab unifying breakfast + other add-ons — the two collections have different shapes (the breakfast one is a single doc with a `meals[]` sub-array; the other is a collection of independent docs); a unified tab would force a schema migration that BR-08 just closed. **(D)** Per-feature `otherAddOnFrontDeskOverrideEnabled` flag (mirroring the `frontDeskOverrideEnabled` flag we borrow) — adds a Settings UI duplicate + the operations workflow is identical (front desk unlocks the cutoff for a specific booking with a reason); the BR-13 refactor can revisit the granularity if needed. **(E)** Per-add-on override override (an extra override modal per-row rather than a single save-time modal) — over-engineered for v1; the BR-12 v1 spec calls for a single Save changes flow with a single override modal that captures the reason before the flush. **(F)** Persisting the "Add-ons enabled" hotel-wide toggle to `settings/hotelConfig` in this same PR — the toggle is a UI affordance for v1 (the underlying `getEnabledAddOnsByHotel` helper returns `[]` when the catalog is empty, so the contract holds without the persisted toggle); persistence is a follow-up. **(G)** Adding the new add-ons to the existing BR-12 audit-log comment block in `shared/schemas/booking.ts` — the existing comment block documents the per-booking audit; the new top-level `auditLogs/{id}` collection is a separate domain (catalog audit) and gets its own dedicated schema. **(H)** Reusing the BR-09 `addOnsStep` URL key for the "Add-ons enabled" toggle on the public flow — the toggle is admin-only; the public flow's existing "No add-ons available" copy already handles the disabled case through the empty-catalog signal. **(I)** Adding the new add-ons to the existing admin-app `addons` sub-tab on the Bookings page — the Bookings page doesn't have a sub-tab structure; the new surfaces are (a) the Settings tab, (b) the booking-drawer tab, (c) the walk-in + calendar-create modals, and (d) the Reports sub-tab. All four are user-visible + complete. **(J)** Drawing the top-add-ons chart from the live `addOns` collection (per-row revenue) — the chart is per-booking revenue, not per-catalog doc; the source is `bookings/{id}.otherAddOnTotal` + the per-day × per-add-on selection map. |


| 248 | **Date picker — the stay length becomes a visible `Nights` stepper; the silent auto-move is no longer needed (DR-01 follow-up, owner-reported 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** after #245 the owner reported the behaviour still "feels weird". The logic was correct but the **presentation** was the problem: two independent date fields with a *silent* auto-move, where check-out jumped by an invisible `lastValidNights`. The guest had no way to see or set the number driving the move, so the correction read as arbitrary — which is also why the hint message existed (it explained a value the guest never chose). A survey of how the majors actually behave (design teardowns, not first-hand audits) found **two independent fields plus post-hoc explanation is the pattern nobody uses**: **Airbnb** uses a single state-machine calendar where the first click sets arrival and the UI auto-toggles to departure, making inversion *structurally impossible*; **Booking.com** (two boxes, closest to our architecture) adjusts inside a visible highlighted range, with a 30-night cap and 365-day horizon — which lines up with what #245 built; **Agoda** renders arrival date plus a **Nights** ticker as a first-class control; **TripAdvisor** highlights the span in the same view. **Notably, no major site ships a "we changed your date" message** — they make the state visible or unreachable rather than explaining it afterwards. **Fix — surface the length, drop the message that existed only to compensate.** The picker now renders a **Nights** stepper between the two dates, built to match the existing Adults/Children steppers on the booking page (same markup, 44px touch targets, `bg-gray-100` buttons, `Moon` icon in `text-primary`, `aria-label="Add/Remove one night"`, disabled at 1 night and at the horizon). Because check-out is now visibly `checkIn + nights`, the `nights-preserved` hint is **deleted** — it explained something the visible control already explains, which is exactly the noise a redundant hint is. Hints are retained only for corrections the guest genuinely cannot infer from the inputs: the horizon cap, the past-date clamp, and a malformed link. The stepper's cap is measured from **check-in**, not today (`diffNightsBetweenIso(checkIn, horizon)`) — a check-in 20 days out inside a 30-night horizon has 10 nights of room, so the raw `MAX_STAY_NIGHTS` would over-promise. Vertical orientation stacks Check-in → Nights → Check-out so it reads as a sentence; horizontal keeps the two dates side by side with the stepper beneath. The `nights-preserved` *behaviour* is unchanged — only its explanation is gone. **Tests** — 11 new (7 source-text pins on the stepper's presence, wiring, cap arithmetic, 44px targets and the deleted hint; 4 behavioural checks of the stepper math, including the check-in-relative horizon cap). Guest-app 1993 → 2059 passing. **MDs** — `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (rule 8), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md` (stepper checklist items), `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` #246 (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md`. **Gates** — BREAKFAST (BR-08/09/10 untouched), AVAILABILITY-LOCKING (no change to the create transaction), CORPORATE-BOOKING (the picker is shared, so the corporate page gains the stepper too — correct, same contract), FOL-03 (no new transactional surface), SECURITY (no PII). **Rejected** — build a full Airbnb-style single-widget range calendar (the most correct answer, but it means hand-writing keyboard-grid navigation, ARIA grid roles, month navigation, availability dots and mobile behaviour, and a hand-rolled a11y calendar is a genuine regression risk against the native `<input type="date">` we already have, which is the right call on mobile), keep the hint and just make it auto-dismiss after ~6s (treats the symptom — the problem is that the value was invisible, not that it was unexplained), reset to a fixed 1 night when check-in moves (owner-rejected earlier; it silently changes the price), put the stepper above check-out in vertical mode (breaks the Check-in → Nights → Check-out reading order), auto-dismiss hints on a timer (a time-based auto-dismiss is untestable at the source level and adds a timer to a component that has none). **⚠️ SUPERSEDED by #249** — the `Nights` stepper this entry describes was removed the same day; it shipped, then was replaced by the range calendar, which makes an inverted range unrepresentable. This entry also duplicated #246 (both describe the same `Nights` stepper, appended concurrently by two working sessions). Retained for history. |
| 249 | **Date picker becomes a single-overlay range calendar; the nights stepper is removed (DR-02, owner-requested 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the owner correctly rejected the #246 premise — **there is no nights picker in Agoda**. Their screenshot of agoda.com settled it: Agoda uses a **two-month calendar (October | November) with the entire stay rendered as one contiguous highlighted band**, check-in ringed, check-out filled, and two text boxes above mirroring the selection. No ticker, no scroller. #246 was built on a single 2019 Mobiscroll design teardown that described both incorrectly for what Agoda does today, and I had leaned on it too hard — then steered the owner *away* from the calendar, which was the correct pattern all along. #246 is superseded: the **nights stepper is deleted**. **Why the calendar is structurally better, not just prettier:** with two independent fields there is always a moment where the two hold values that disagree, and every piece of machinery built to repair that — the clamps, the `nights-preserved` correction, the "we changed your date" message — existed only to fix a state the UI let you reach. A range calendar has no such moment: the first click sets arrival, the UI moves to departure, and an inverted range is **not representable**. **Implementation** — `react-day-picker@10` in `mode="range"` (chosen over hand-rolling for keyboard-grid navigation and ARIA roles, which a custom calendar regresses). Two read-only text boxes above mirror the selection; a range summary line shows `Oct 15 — Oct 20 · 5 nights`. Bounded `today .. today+30` via `disabled={{ before: startMonth, after: endMonth }}`, two months side by side only at >=768px (at 640px they wrap into what reads as one broken column). **A real bug this surfaced — caught in the browser, not by the compiler.** A calendar's range mode reports the **first** click of a new selection as `{ from: day, to: day }` — same day, *not* `to: undefined`. Committing on any `to` therefore committed a 1-night range from a single click, which fired an availability refetch; `BookingPage` swaps itself for a full-page "Checking room availability" loader, which **unmounts the picker**, so the guest's click silently vanished and the UI snapped back. `tsc` was clean and the suite was green — the repo has no jsdom/RTL harness, so a selection bug is invisible to both. Fixed by extracting a **pure exported resolver** `resolveRangeSelection()` (`pending` / `complete` / `cleared`) into `shared/utils/stayRange.ts`, where a same-day pair is always `pending`. The draft range is also kept separate from the committed pair, so pricing never chases a provisional date. **Brand compliance** — react-day-picker ships a **blue** selected range, which would put an off-brand colour on the most prominent element in the booking flow (hard rule: primary CTAs use `config.colors.primary`). Themed via `--rdp-accent-color` / `--rdp-accent-background-color` on the wrapper plus a `.spark-date-range`-scoped override for the month-nav buttons, which the variables alone do not cover. No hex is hardcoded — a per-client white-label change flows through. **Timezone** — the calendar operates on `TZDate` pinned to `config.timezone`, so a guest in any timezone sees the property's days; `getFullYear`/`getMonth`/`getDate` on a `TZDate` read tz-local parts, which makes the ISO round-trip exact. **Preserved** — `normalizeStayRange()` still guards everything arriving from outside the calendar (shared `?checkIn=` links, past dates, horizon overruns), the server-side 400 stays, and the corporate *inquiry* pair + extend-stay field stay as they are. **Tests** — `resolveRangeSelection` gains 9 behavioural cases (shared suite 17 -> 26) covering the same-day regression, backwards second click, cleared state, past-date floor, horizon refusal, and a sweep asserting no input pair ever completes as zero-night or inverted; the DR-01 source-text suite is rewritten for the calendar (23 pins) including an explicit anti-regression that no `max={...checkOut}` / `min={...checkIn}` coupling is reintroduced. Verified in a real browser at 640px and 1100px. Guest-app 2053 passing. **MDs** — `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (rule 9), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md`, `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` #247 (this entry), `plan/project/ROADMAP.md`. **Gates** — AVAILABILITY-LOCKING (untouched), BREAKFAST (untouched), CORPORATE-BOOKING (shared picker gains the calendar — correct, same contract), FOL-03 (no new transactional surface), SECURITY (no PII), WHITE-LABEL (theming reads `config.colors.primary`, no hardcoded hex). **Rejected** — hand-rolling the calendar (worse a11y), keeping the nights stepper *alongside* the band (two competing controls for one value), retaining the explanatory hints that the band makes unnecessary, `max={{ before }}` style per-input bounds (that coupling is what froze the picker in the first place). **Supersedes** #246 (nights stepper) and the #245 auto-correct presentation; #245's `normalizeStayRange` + server guard remain in force. |
| 250 | **The range calendar gets a real booking horizon, a fresh-start click model, and a mobile sheet (DR-03, owner-reported 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the owner reported two symptoms — “I can only pick October and November dates” and “on mobile the calendar is not scrollable and is not seen entirely”. Both were real, and unpicking them surfaced **four** further defects that the compiler and a green suite could not see. **(1) The booking horizon and the maximum stay length were one number.** `MAX_STAY_NIGHTS = 30` was doing double duty as both the stay-length cap *and* the calendar's `endMonth`, so `maxCheckOut = today + 30` capped reachable dates at Nov 14 — **December was unreachable by construction**, and a working pager read as a broken one. A second consequence: `stayCap` was `min(maxNights, horizonNights)` and was applied **only** on the inverted branch, so a long-but-valid range (a legitimate 60-night stay) passed unchecked. They are now separate policies — `DEFAULT_MAX_STAY_NIGHTS = 30` (a length) and `DEFAULT_BOOKING_HORIZON_DAYS = 365` (a distance, matching Booking.com) — enforced as two independent clamps, each with its own adjustment reason (`nights-clamped` vs `horizon-capped`) so the hint names the limit that actually bound. The night clamp deliberately uses `maxNights` rather than `stayCap`: clamping to `stayCap` would fold the horizon back in and make every over-long range report `nights-clamped`, hiding which limit was responsible. **(2) The calendar absorbed clicks — the real reason far-future months felt unreachable.** `addToRange` tests `isAfter(date, from)` **before** `isAfter(date, to)`, so once a complete range is on screen *every* click later than its check-in is taken as “move the check-out here” and the old `from` is kept. A guest who wanted a different December stay silently stretched their current one instead; widening the horizon alone would not have fixed it. The picker now drives the range from `onDayClick`: a click completes a range **only** when a pending check-in is waiting, and otherwise starts a new stay — the reading every major site uses. **(3) `classNames` shallow-merges.** `{ ...getDefaultClassNames(), ...props.classNames }` means naming a slot **replaces** its default. The DR-02 theming fix had set `classNames={{ root: "spark-range-root" }}`, which deleted `rdp-root` and silently un-defined every `--rdp-*` variable the stylesheet declares on it — day cells collapsed from 42px to their text width and the calendar rendered unstyled and tiny. Fixed to `root: "rdp-root spark-range-root"` (keep the default, append the hook). Related: theming must target the element that **declares** the custom properties. An ancestor `--rdp-accent-color` is useless because the derived range vars resolve `var(--rdp-accent-color)` at their point of declaration, and substitution resolves where it is written, not where it is used. **(4) Pin `timeZone` when handing the library `TZDate`s.** Without it the picker's internal `DateLib` runs in the *browser's* timezone while our values are pinned to `config.timezone`, so the two disagree: a deep-linked December stay rendered with nothing selected, and a picked one painted `range_middle` across every day from the start of the grid. `timeZone={config.timezone}` aligns both sides. **(5) The pair is committed atomically.** The picker fires `onCheckInChange` then `onCheckOutChange`; both handlers close over the *same* render's `checkIn`, so the second write rewrote the URL from a stale value and clobbered the check-in the first had just written (`?checkIn=2026-10-15&checkOut=2026-12-24` on a December booking). Added `onRangeChange(checkIn, checkOut)`, called once on commit, and wired it in `BookingPage` + `CorporateBookingPage` wherever the URL is derived from these values. **(6) Mobile is a sheet, desktop stays inline.** The calendar is a fixed bottom sheet (`top-16`, `overflow-y-auto`, `overscroll-contain`) with a dimmed backdrop, an X, and an explicit brand-orange **Done**; at `md+` it reverts to the inline card. Inline on a phone it fell below the fold and was clipped by the sticky cart bar. **Tests** — shared suite 26 -> 34 cases (7 new DR-03 cases including the December regression, the horizon/night-cap split, and that neither message quotes the other's number; one stale DR-01 case was rewritten because its premise — that the 30-day horizon was the cap — no longer holds). Source-text pins gained four contracts: the horizon/night-cap split, the fresh-start click model, the atomic commit, and the timezone pin. **Verified in a real browser** at 550px: December reachable and selectable, the 20 -> 24 band painted in `config.colors.primary`, and `?checkIn=2026-12-04&checkOut=2026-12-09` matching the displayed stay. **MDs** — `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (rule 10), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md`, this entry, `plan/project/ROADMAP.md`. **Gates** — AVAILABILITY-LOCKING (untouched), CORPORATE-BOOKING (same picker, same contract), SECURITY (no PII), WHITE-LABEL (theming still reads `config.colors.primary`, no hardcoded hex). **Rejected** — widening only the horizon (defect 2 would have swallowed it); clamping with `stayCap` (wrong reason reported); keeping `onSelect` and pre-clearing the range on open (loses the visible current selection); widening the sheet to full height on desktop (the inline card is correct there). **Supersedes** the theming paragraph of #249 — `rdp-root` must be retained alongside the hook class, which #249 got wrong. 
| 251 | **SET-SBG-01 follow-up: the Settings sidebar grouping + the `addons` tab (2026-10-15):** the Settings tab set is grouped into sections for the admin sidebar; the Other Add-Ons tab (BR-12.1, #247) was added post-merge and the group map + the flat `tabs` metadata are kept in sync by the SET-SBG-01 tripwire. **That tripwire was rewritten in the October 2026 audit closeout (decision #293)** because it used to compare a hardcoded copy of the group tabs with itself — it now reads `VALID_TAB_IDS` + `SETTINGS_GROUPS` out of the source and derives the count. |
| 252 | **The calendar becomes genuinely controlled, and a refused click explains itself (DR-04, owner-reported 2026-10-15; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the owner picked a check-in, paged two months forward, clicked a check-out, and reported "the check-out date is selected on the picker but it did not populate the check-out date field". Both halves of that sentence were true simultaneously, and both were bugs. **(1) `selected` was being silently ignored — the calendar was the liar.** DR-03 drove the range from `onDayClick`. react-day-picker's `useRange` decides controlled vs uncontrolled with `useControlledValue(selected, onSelect ? selected : undefined)` and only calls `setSelected` internally when `onSelect` is ABSENT — so without it the component is uncontrolled, `selected={draft}` is read **once** as an initial value and then discarded, and every click ran the library's own `addToRange`. The picker was painting ITS stale range (old check-in -> clicked day) on top of a draft that had already moved: two disjoint orange bands while the fields showed one pending check-in. The generalisable rule is the one worth keeping: **`value` without `onChange` is an initial value, not a control** — wire the callback the component actually branches on rather than reaching for a neighbouring event and assuming it became controlled. Moving to `onSelect` puts the calendar in controlled mode, so the painted range is always exactly our `draft`. **(2) The library's computed range is discarded anyway.** `addToRange` checks `isAfter(date, from)` BEFORE `isAfter(date, to)`, so a completed range absorbs any later click as "move the check-out here". The handler therefore takes only `triggerDate` and derives the range itself, exactly as DR-03 did. **(3) The refusal is now explained, not swallowed.** A stay longer than `MAX_STAY_NIGHTS` returned `pending`, dropped the check-out, and said nothing — indistinguishable from a broken picker, which is how it read. It now keeps the check-in (so the guest can immediately pick again) and renders the reason in a `role="alert"` banner, **both** in the page flow and inside the sheet, because on phones the sheet covers the flow copy. It deliberately does NOT silently clamp the check-out to the cap: an invisible date move is the DR-01 failure mode in a new outfit. This is DR-01 rule 4 applied to a live calendar rather than to a corrected date. **Tests** — the DR-03 fresh-start pin was re-pointed from `onDayClick` to `onSelect` and now also pins the ignored-range signature plus an anti-regression that `onDayClick` is not reintroduced; one new pin covers the alert banner and its dual placement. Guest-app 2065 -> 2066 passing. **Verified in a real browser at 764px** — the phantom band is gone (a pending check-in renders as a single ringed day); a 2-months-forward check-out that exceeds the cap shows the alert and keeps the check-in; a valid 29-night stay across a month boundary commits to `?checkIn=2026-10-21&checkOut=2026-11-19` and renders the band. **MDs** — `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` (rule 11), `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md`, this entry, `plan/project/ROADMAP.md`. **Gates** — AVAILABILITY-LOCKING (untouched), SECURITY (no PII), WHITE-LABEL (no colour change). **Rejected** — passing a no-op `onSelect` alongside `onDayClick` (works, but leaves two handlers racing and obscures why the calendar is controlled); silently clamping an over-long stay to the cap (hides a real limit and moves a date the guest can see); disabling out-of-cap days in the calendar (a 30-day disable rule per check-in is far more machinery than telling the guest, and does not explain itself). **Supersedes** the `onDayClick` half of #250. 
| 253 | **The maximum stay length becomes a per-hotel setting, and the reschedule bypass it never had is closed (MSL-01, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the owner asked whether the 30-night cap could become an admin setting with `0` = no limit. It could, and cheaply, because `settings/hotelConfig` is already open at both ends: `firebase/firestore.rules` grants wildcard public read on `settings/{documentId}`, and both the guest app (`usePublicSiteContent`) and the three server write paths already read that doc. **The point of the change is not flexibility — it is that "how long may a guest stay" stops being a code deploy and becomes a Settings edit.** **(1) `resolveMaxStayNights()` owns the whole raw-value mapping** (`shared/utils/stayRange.ts`), because `0` is poisoned in two independent ways. A literal `0` reaching `stayCap = Math.max(Math.min(0, horizon), 1)` yields `stayCap === 1` and collapses **every stay in the system to one night** — silently, with no error and no failing test. `Infinity` is the safe representation (`n > Infinity` is never true; `min(Infinity, horizon)` degrades to the horizon). Separately, `Number(undefined)` is `NaN`, so a resolver that only tested finiteness would read **every un-migrated hotel as unlimited** and quietly open existing installs to unbounded stays. Hence four distinct answers: absent/`null`/`""` → 30, `0` → `Infinity`, fractional → floored-then-guarded (a `0.5` floors to `0` and would otherwise re-enter as unlimited), garbage → the default, **never** unlimited. The server fails CLOSED: a Firestore read error yields the default cap, not unlimited. **(2) A live bypass was found and closed.** The cap was enforced in `handleCreateBooking` and `handleCreateWalkin` — but `handleRescheduleBooking` only ever checked the lower bound (`numNights < 1`). A front-desk user could stretch a legal 2-night booking to 200 nights and skip the cap the guest-facing flow enforced entirely. This corrects a scoping assumption from the design review, which had read the second guard site as reschedule rather than walk-in. All three paths now resolve the same setting and share one `buildMaxStayNightsError` so they refuse identically. **(3) The DR-01 test pins were deliberately INVERTED.** That suite asserted `expect(pickerSrc).toMatch(/const MAX_STAY_NIGHTS = 30/)` under the label "THE DECEMBER REGRESSION". A future agent seeing it fail after this change would most naturally re-hardcode `30` — silently resurrecting the exact bug the file exists to prevent. It now asserts the picker must **not** hardcode the cap. A test that pins an implementation detail becomes a trap the moment the detail is deliberately removed; the pin has to be re-pointed at the invariant, not deleted. **Verified before building** (two questions the design review had left open): **30 is NOT load-bearing in pricing** — every call site computes `ratePerNight × numNights`, with no weekly/monthly tier and no length-triggered discount, so raising or removing the cap cannot distort an invoice. And **Spark Inn does take long-stay guests** — `BookingRegistrationForm.tsx:206` offers `"long-stay relocation"` as a purpose-of-stay value, so that guest is bookable by phone but was previously **not** bookable on the public site. That was the concrete bug the owner's instinct was pointing at. **The shipped default stays 30.** No existing install's behaviour changes until an operator touches the field — which is the point: the business decision becomes configuration, not a release. **Docs/copy corrections this forced:** `MAX_STAY_NIGHTS` in `shared/constants` no longer describes what the system enforces and now says so; the Settings tab intro said "Two hold windows" and the button said "Save Hold Windows", which would have been a lie once the form also saved a cap (both now "Booking Limits"); and the editor states the cap is **not** snapshotted, unlike the hold windows, so it applies to stays already checked in. **Tests** — shared `stay-range.test.ts` 34 → 45 cases, pinning the trap directly: the same 5-night range is asserted at **1 night** with a raw `0` and at **5 nights** with the resolved value. Guest-app `dr-01` 30 passing. **Rejected** — removing the cap entirely (loses the guard against a fat-fingered 200-night booking; the operator can set `0`); applying the setting only on create (leaves the reschedule bypass open); normalising the value in `usePublicSiteContent` instead of at each boundary (the hook is a dumb override layer and the `0` mapping must live where the clamp is); reusing `clampIntegerInRange` for the editor (its `min` is `>= 1`, and `0` is a legal, meaningful value here); raising `MAX_ADVANCE_DAYS` at the same time (a separate policy with no owner ask behind it). **Supersedes** the `MAX_STAY_NIGHTS` half of #250's policy description — the number is now a fallback, not the rule. |

| 254 | **Shared booking shell — compact mobile stepper, safe-area sticky footer, compact hold banner (MBUX-01, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the first slice of Phase 15 (`plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md`). Scope is deliberately the SHELL only — Step 1 room cards, the breakfast grid, Step 3/4 content, and the confirmation hierarchy are MBUX-02..08, and the BR-10 dual CTA consolidation is MBUX-05. **Renumbered from #253.** The Phase 15 plan reserved #253, but MSL-01 shipped the same day and consumed it; the reservation was stale, so MBUX takes the next free number. **Decisions. (1) Mobile shows five CONNECTED POSITIONS and one caption; desktop keeps every label.** The pre-MBUX-01 `StepIndicator` rendered all five labels inside `flex flex-wrap`, which cannot fit one line at phone width — it wrapped into a two- or three-row block above the fold on every render, and it stated the current position a second time via the page's own `Step N of 5` eyebrow. Below `sm:` the labels collapse to `sr-only`, the five circles share the width evenly via `flex-1`, 1px connectors join them, and a single `aria-hidden` caption reads `Step N of 5 · Current step name`. The eyebrow became `hidden sm:block` so the position is stated once, not twice. From `sm:` up the layout is unchanged, so desktop + the existing wide-screen QA matrix are byte-equivalent. **(2) The current step keeps its NUMBER, not a check.** Checks-only circles were rejected in the spec: they make the current position ambiguous. Completed pairs the check glyph with an `sr-only` "Completed", so state is never carried by colour alone. `aria-current="step"` marks the current item and `<ol>`/`<li>` semantics are preserved. The label element stays in the a11y tree at every width (`sr-only` → `sm:not-sr-only`) — collapsing it to `hidden sm:inline` would delete every step's accessible name on phones. **(3) The eight sticky footers were extracted, and the extraction is the point.** All four steps on both the public and corporate pages carried a byte-identical wrapper string, so the safe-area rule could only ever be aspirational. `BookingStickyFooter` owns `pb-[calc(1rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]`, a full-width mobile action slot, and an optional `detail` strip (Step 1's occupancy-recovery guidance, which previously squeezed the primary action). This also fixes a real defect: the flat `py-4` meant a notched iPhone's home indicator sat ON the primary action, which `FRONTEND.md §Admin App — Mobile UX Checklist` already forbade for sticky footers. Both shells moved from `pb-32` to `pb-[calc(8rem+env(safe-area-inset-bottom))]` so the content reserve matches the footer's own height. **(4) The mobile hold banner is one line, and the countdown is never rendered twice.** The active state collapses to `Room held · 13:21 remaining` below `sm:`, with the full explanation behind an `aria-expanded` info affordance; from `sm:` up the explanation is unconditional and the old layout is unchanged. The MM:SS pill is `sm:inline-flex` so the mobile line and the pill never both sit in the accessibility tree — two live copies of a ticking counter would be read twice a second. The extend CTA, the expired state's two explicit actions, the `role="region"` wrapper, and the single `sr-only` announcement live region are all untouched. **(5) An extension failure is never hidden behind the collapsed explanation.** `extendError` renders as a SIBLING of the collapsible region, not a descendant — a mobile guest who expanded nothing and still needs to know the extend failed would otherwise meet a silently broken CTA. **Rejected:** animating the connectors (nothing to animate is worth a `useReducedMotion` guard; the connector is a static rule); collapsing the eyebrow at every width (it is the desktop position statement); moving the hold banner into the footer (it is a flow-level status, not a per-step decision); deleting Step 2's two CTAs here (that is MBUX-05's behaviour change, not a layout extraction). **Tests:** `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-01-booking-shell.test.ts` — 41 source-text pins across the stepper, the eyebrow visibility on both pages, the footer extraction + safe-area reserve, and the banner's collapse/threshold/error-placement contracts. Its assertions run against comment-stripped source so a doc comment cannot satisfy (or trip) a code pin. **Verified:** `guest-app` 2108 passed (the one failure, `br-06-refunds-extension`, is pre-existing on `dev` — confirmed by stash + rerun), `shared` 782/782, `admin-app` BR-09 9/9, `tsc -p guest-app` clean, `npm run build -w guest-app` clean, and the emitted CSS verified to carry `not-sr-only`, `padding-bottom:calc(1rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))`, and the `13.5px` connector offset. **No new Vercel function and no schema/type change**, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.

| 255 | **Step 1 room cards become the selection target, and mobile gains a multi-room cart tray (MBUX-02 + MBUX-03, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the second Phase 15 slice (`plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md §Step 1 — Select Rooms`). **Shipped together on purpose.** MBUX-02 makes the card selectable; MBUX-03 is what makes that trustworthy. Split, a guest could select three room types with no way to see the result — the per-card counters MBUX-02 deletes were the only sum visible on a phone. Shipping 02 alone would have made multi-room selection visibly WORSE than before. **(1) The `Room Only` panel is removed; `Room Only` survives as a value.** Each card carried two controls for one decision — a `Rooms` quantity stepper and a `Room Only` `RateOption`. Since BR-03 (decision #232) deleted `Room + Breakfast`, the panel stopped being a choice: its untick branch was reachable only by clicking the single lone panel twice, and its switch-rate-in-place branch was unreachable entirely. It is now a duplicate of the stepper beside it. The panel, the `selectRoomType` handler, and the `RateOption` component are deleted; `rateChoice: "room-only"` remains on every cart item, so the API shape, `serializeBookingRoomCart` URL state, and server validation are untouched. **Only the guest-facing label is gone.** The duplicate type pill (which rendered the same `type.label` as the heading directly beneath it) is also removed, as is the copy `Click a rate to add a room. Click the same rate again to remove it.` **(2) `updateRoomQuantity` is now the single mutation path for add and remove.** The unselected card offers one full-width `Select this room`; the selected card shows `N selected`, the per-type total for the stay, and the stepper. Decrement-to-zero restores the unselected state because `isSelected` derives from `cartQuantityByType` — there is no separate clear path. A selected card takes a primary border. `aria-current="true"` marks it, deliberately NOT `aria-current="step"` — a card selection is not a navigation position. **(3) EXB-11.3's invariant is preserved and re-pinned.** No room may be pre-selected on page load; `Select this room` is the user-explicit add. This is why the EXB-11.5 suite was rewritten rather than deleted — the selection-sync effect is now the ONLY thing keeping `selectedRoomType` coherent after a decrement-to-zero, since the untick branch that explicitly skipped that sync is gone. `exb-11-5-rate-option-toggle.test.ts` keeps its filename (two docs reference it) and now records the supersession, pins the new surface, and holds negative pins so the panel cannot quietly return. **(4) The cart grouping was extracted, not copied.** `groupCartByType` + `RoomCartLines` moved out of an IIFE inside the Step 1 sidebar into a component. Adding a second cart surface by copying that block would have guaranteed the two drift; both now render the same component and the same single `deriveRoomTypeCapacityFit` helper, so the tray cannot contradict the card's Fits / Tight / Doesn't fit chip. The sidebar passes `idPrefix="cart-line"`, the tray `"cart-tray-line"`. **(5) The tray lives above the sticky footer, collapsed, gated `lg:hidden`.** It renders nothing when the cart is empty; collapsed it carries the four facts that change the next decision (room count, fit state, aggregate total, expand affordance) plus the adults/children rollup; expanded it shows the same grouped `N× Deluxe Room` lines as the sidebar — never positional `Room 1` (CHD-12). `hidden` (not clipped) keeps collapsed lines out of the accessibility tree. Its aggregate total is the SAME `displayTotal` the sticky footer uses — a second total derivation would be free to disagree with the figure the guest is about to be charged. From `lg:` up the sidebar already shows the full cart, so the tray is `lg:hidden` rather than duplicating every line on screen. **Rejected:** keeping the panel and renaming it (it would still be a duplicate control); a "click the same card again to remove" gesture (reinstates the meaningless double-tick EXB-11.5 added); putting the tray in the page body (scrolls away — the exact regression MBUX-01 just fixed elsewhere); a per-type total helper (the existing `roomOnlyTotal` × quantity is exactly correct for N identical rooms over the same dates, and a new helper would be a second pricing path); shipping MBUX-02 without MBUX-03 (worse multi-room UX than today). **Preserved:** CHD-11's per-type capacity chip and rooms-needed nudge, the CHD-11 occupancy gate and `adjust-room-cta`, the availability bound on increment, EXB-11.4's extra-bed stepper and soft floor, BR-03's room-rate-only breakfast preview, WRV-01's mixed-rate breakdown panel. **Tests:** new `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-02-03-step-1-rooms.test.ts` (19 guards: the grouping contract, single-helper capacity derivation, both surfaces sharing one component, the `lg:hidden` mount, the collapsed/`hidden` region, the shared `displayTotal`, and the seven preserved Step 1 invariants); `exb-11-5-rate-option-toggle.test.ts` rewritten (15 guards incl. negative pins against the panel's return); `exb-11-3-no-default-room-type-on-load.test.ts` retargeted to the `Select this room` path. **Verified:** guest-app 2132 passed (the one `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`), `tsc` clean, `vite build` clean. **No `guest-app/server/` or `shared/` change, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.**

| 256 | **Optional admin-managed breakfast meal photos — the image contract, public normalization, Storage policy, and admin upload controls (MBUX-04, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the third Phase 15 slice. **The image is optional and additive**: every existing meal without an image stays valid, and the guest Step 2 menu strip (MBUX-05) is the only consumer — MBUX-04 deliberately ships the contract without the guest rendering, because a test pinning guest markup before that slice would just be a guess. **(1) The Zod schema was the real trap.** `BreakfastMealSchema` is the gate every admin catalog save passes through, and Zod's `z.object` **strips unknown keys by default**. Adding `imageUrl` to the TypeScript interface alone would have produced the worst possible failure: the upload would succeed, the admin would see the preview, and the URL would be silently deleted the next time anyone touched dining settings — with no error anywhere. `imageUrl` is therefore declared in `BreakfastMealSchema` with `.url().max(2000).optional()`, and an empty string is **rejected** rather than stored, because "no image" is expressed by OMITTING the field. A test asserts the URL survives `safeParse`; that is the assertion that fails first if the field is ever dropped from the schema again. **(2) The read boundary drops malformed values instead of forwarding them.** `safeImageUrl` in `normalizeBreakfastConfig` returns `undefined` for anything that is not an absolute `http(s)` URL — empty string, relative path, bare filename, non-string, and notably `javascript:` / `data:` (a catalog row hand-edited in Firestore must not become an injection vector). The normalizer returns `undefined` rather than `""` because the guest fallback keys off the field's PRESENCE: a meal with `imageUrl: ""` would render a broken `<img src>` instead of the no-image tile. `undefined` keys are dropped by `JSON.stringify`, so a meal without an image costs nothing on the wire. **(3) The snapshot invariant holds structurally, not by convention.** Bookings must never carry a catalog image — a later image edit must not rewrite the appearance of a historical booking, and totals must not be recomputable against it. `breakfastSelections` entries are built **field-by-field** (`mealId` + `mealName` + `price`) with no spread, so there is no path by which a new catalog field can leak in. This is asserted directly against the construction site rather than trusted. **(4) Storage is public-read / staff-write, scoped per meal.** `breakfast-meals/{mealId}/{fileName}` mirrors the payment-method-QR discipline: the `{mealId}` segment means a replace on one meal can never overwrite another meal's object, even though the admin cannot choose the path. Public read because the guest menu renders the thumbnail inline and the reference is an ordinary download URL; write restricted to the shared `isStaff()` helper. **(5) Upload order is the correctness argument.** Upload → repoint the row → THEN delete the superseded object. Reversing either step can leave the guest menu strip pointing at a deleted file — a broken image in front of a paying guest. Removal clears the row first so the guest immediately falls back. Storage deletes are best-effort: an orphaned object is a cleanup nit, not a broken booking path. **`uploadBreakfastMealImage` deliberately does NOT write Firestore** — it returns the URL and the Settings editor merges it into the row, so the catalog keeps exactly one writer that always passes through `BreakfastConfigSchema`; a second writer could bypass validation. Input is capped at 8 MB pre-compression (a meal photo is a real photograph, unlike a monochrome QR) and re-encoded to JPEG at 1200×1200 / q0.82, with the admin preview on a consistent 4:3 crop. The file input resets after each pick so re-selecting the same file fires `change` again — otherwise "replace" silently no-ops after a failed compression. **(6) Uploads key off `meal.id`, never `meal.name`.** Names are editable free text; a renamed meal must not orphan or overwrite another meal's images. **Rejected:** storing `""` for no image (breaks presence-based fallback); putting the image in the booking snapshot (violates the invariant); one shared image folder without a meal segment (a replace could clobber another meal); a Firestore write inside the upload helper (a second catalog writer that bypasses the schema); validating on the server only (an optimistic admin UI needs the client-side gate, and the server never sees the file). **Tests:** new `shared/__tests__/breakfast-meal-image.test.ts` (16 **behavioural** tests — the normalizer and schema are pure functions, so accept/drop/reject is asserted against real input rather than a regex); new `admin-app/src/__tests__/mbux-04-meal-images.test.ts` (21 source-text guards: the Storage rule, the upload path, the ordering, and the snapshot absence). **Verified:** shared 798/798, admin 1879/1879, guest-app 2132 passed (the single `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`), `tsc` clean for both apps. **Bundle note:** this slice changes `shared/`, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is regenerated. See the ROADMAP entry — the regenerated bundle also carries an unrelated dependency-resolution delta (`iobuffer` → `atob` via `jsonwebtoken`) that reflects the current `package-lock.json`, not this feature.

| 257 | **Step 2 rebuilt for phone widths: Breakfast menu strip, responsive selectors, copy-to-remaining-days, and ONE context-aware CTA (MBUX-05, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the fourth Phase 15 slice. This is where MBUX-04's meal photos first become visible to a guest. **(1) The menu strip sits ABOVE the date cards and its cards are non-interactive.** Native `<option>` elements cannot render images consistently on mobile, and the spec rejected photos in dropdowns outright — so the photos need a surface above the selectors. Interleaving them would repeat the same catalogue across every one of the N dates × M guest slots, which is the exact repetition the mobile refresh exists to remove. Horizontal scroll with `overscroll-x-contain` (a horizontal flick must not chain into the page's vertical scroll on iOS), `loading="lazy"`, a consistent 4:3 crop, and a branded-neutral tile when a meal has no image, the URL 404s, or the network drops. The meal NAME is always rendered as text, so a missing photo never removes information. **Cards are `<div>`s, not buttons** — a tappable photo beside a selector is indistinguishable from "pick this meal", and this feature must not create a second path for selecting one. It reads the SAME active, display-ordered list the selectors read, so a photo can never appear for a meal that cannot be chosen. **(2) The guest selector row was structurally broken on phones, not merely ugly.** A fixed `min-w-[160px]` label beside a `flex-1` select left the native select about 150px wide at 360px, truncating the option text so the control read as broken. Now stacked on mobile (label above, full-width select) and side-by-side from `sm:` up, where there is room for the old shape. The empty option is `No breakfast`, and the "skipping is allowed" explanation moved ONCE above the grid instead of repeating inside every option on every date card. **(3) Copy-to-remaining-days is a pure helper because its rules are invisible in a regex.** A multi-night stay repeats the same guest matrix N times — the most tedious thing in the booking flow on a phone. `copyBreakfastDateToRemaining` encodes four rules that a source-text pin would happily pass while the helper quietly kept a stale target value: **`No breakfast` is a real choice and COPIES** (an empty source slot clears the target rather than leaving yesterday's meal behind); **locked dates are excluded by the CALLER** (lock state is a cut-off computation the caller already owns — re-deriving it here would create a second source of truth that could disagree); **each copied entry is RE-STAMPED, not shared by reference** (copying the entry object would let a later mutation of one date silently rewrite another's); and **`updatedDates` counts only real changes**, so the confirmation can never overstate what the tap did. Its clock is injected so the tests are deterministic. The guest app has no toast system, so the confirmation is an inline `role="status"` live region rather than a transient overlay. The action appears only on a date that has at least one explicit selection (nothing to copy otherwise) and only when a later EDITABLE date exists. Copied dates stay fully editable — it writes the same map the selectors write. **(4) BR-10's dual CTAs are retired on the public flow only.** Both buttons advanced to the IDENTICAL `step=guest-details` URL and differed only in style and label. The ghost button told a guest who had skipped breakfast that there was a special "skip" affordance implying the primary would handle their choices differently — it would not. That implication is the whole defect, and one action removes it without removing any capability: skipping is now simply "have no selections and press Continue". The single CTA is context-aware (`Continue without add-ons` at zero, `Continue with N add-ons · total` otherwise) and quotes `breakfastTotal`, the SAME value the review step and create payload use, so the CTA cannot display a figure that differs from the bill. No new step key is minted. **Corporate keeps its BR-10 pair until MBUX-09**, and `br-10-dual-add-ons-buttons.test.ts` was rewritten to pin that split deliberately rather than left to rot — it now records why the ghost button was wrong and asserts the corporate pair still exists. **(5) Two spec items were honestly reported rather than faked.** The "existing other add-ons as progressive cards" requirement is **vacuous**: Step 2 has breakfast only — the other-add-ons catalogue is not implemented in the guest flow — so `addOnCount` is derived from breakfast selections alone rather than hardcoded to a wider formula that does not exist yet. The optional thumbnail **preview modal** (the spec says "may open") is deferred; the strip's no-photo / loading / failed states are the contract MBUX-10 must verify. **Rejected:** photos inside `<option>` (mobile browsers render image-rich native options inconsistently — explicitly rejected by the spec); making strip cards selectable (creates a second selection path with no clear undo); leaving the 40-character explanation in every `<option>` (it repeated N × M times); deriving the lock filter inside the copy helper (second source of truth for cut-offs); copying the entry object by reference (shared mutable state across dates); a toast for the confirmation (the guest app has none). **Tests:** new `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-05-step-2-addons.test.ts` — 35 guards of which **13 are behavioural** against the copy helper and 22 are source-text pins for the strip, the selectors, the copy placement rules, and the consolidated CTA. **Verified:** guest-app 2168 passed (the single `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`), `tsc` clean, `vite build` clean. **No `shared/` or `server/` change, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.**

| 258 | **Step 3 reduced to essential guest details: ONE authoritative consent control, a compact secure-handling line, and an incomplete state that names the first correction and takes focus there (MBUX-06, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the fifth Phase 15 slice. **(1) The duplicate consent checkbox is deleted, and this was a real bug, not cosmetic tidying.** Two consecutive steps asked the same question with two different state variables: `guestDetails.consent` on Step 3 and `termsConsent` on Step 4. The server's `guestDetailsSchema` validates only the FIRST. That is the defect NBS-2026-08-08 (F5) was filed against — a guest could tick the Step 4 box, pass the UI, and receive a server 400 "Privacy policy consent is required." F5's fix was to AND both checkboxes together, and its own comment explicitly deferred the real fix: *"The duplicate-checkbox UX is a pre-existing smell — a future refactor should consolidate to a single source of truth."* MBUX-06 is that refactor. `guestDetails.consent` is now the single value read by the Confirm gate, the create payload, and the missing-consent CTA copy — and the server validates that same field. The generalisable lesson, recorded in the code comment: **whenever the client gates on a value the server does not validate, the gate is decorative.** The one line the deleted checkbox carried that Step 3 does not have — "I understand that my booking is subject to the cancellation policy selected" — is not silently dropped: the cancellation-policy disclosure sits directly above and is always rendered. The guest READS the policy there instead of ticking it a second time. **(2) The large privacy explanation becomes a compact secure-handling line.** The consent checkbox now carries the whole obligation in one sentence, so a second boxed paragraph repeating it was pure weight on a phone. The RA 10173 reference is KEPT — it is the compliance-relevant fact — but the layout is a single inline line with a decorative `ShieldCheck`, and the sentence carries the meaning rather than the icon. **(3) The Continue button is no longer permanently disabled.** The pre-MBUX-06 footer rendered a `disabled` Continue when the form was incomplete. A disabled button is unfocusable, so keyboard and screen-reader users could not reach it at all, and a tapping user got a dead control whose only feedback was a footer summary several scroll-heights away. It is now an actionable button that reveals the first offending field's inline error, scrolls it into view, and moves focus there. Field order matches visual order; the consent checkbox is reached only after every field is valid. `guestCount` has no focusable control on this step (the cart lives on Step 1), so it is surfaced through the inline guest label rather than by stealing focus. The footer summary now names the FIRST correction — "First name is required", "Enter a valid email address", "Accept the privacy and terms consent below" — instead of the useless "Complete required fields and consent", and both the summary and the focus target derive from the same `guestErrors` object in the same order so they cannot disagree. **(4) What was already correct and is asserted as preserved, not re-implemented.** The four primary contact fields already carry `autoComplete` tokens (`given-name` / `family-name` / `email` / `tel`); the special-requests accordion is already collapsed by default (SR-01..05, 2026-10-12); member identity fields are already prefilled and `readOnly`; inline validation is already touched-field gated; the cart-readiness gate already blocks Continue to review. Rewriting working code to "match the spec" would have added risk for no gain, so these are pinned as regression guards instead. **Rejected:** hiding the Step 4 checkbox with CSS (a hidden control still implies a required action — deleting it is what makes the single-control claim true); keeping both boxes and ANDing them (that was F5's workaround and it is what this slice exists to end); a `disabled` button plus a banner above the form (still unreachable by keyboard); copying the policy sentence into Step 3 (the disclosure is already always rendered on Step 4, and duplicating legal copy across steps invites them to drift); focus-on-load rather than focus-on-attempt (stealing focus before the guest has interacted is hostile to screen-reader users). **Tests:** new `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-06-step-3-details.test.ts` (24 guards, incl. negative pins that `termsConsent` cannot return and that the Continue button cannot silently regain `disabled`); `nbs-2026-08-08-booking-error-recovery.test.ts` F5 rewritten to record the consolidation and to pin that the gate AND the payload read the same field — the exact defect it was filed for. **Verified:** guest-app 2193 passed (the single `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`; a `convert-inquiry-read-order` failure appeared in one intermediate run and did not reproduce on re-run or in isolation — a pre-existing cross-test flake, unrelated to this change), `tsc` clean, `vite build` clean. **No `shared/` or `server/` change, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.**

| 259 | **Step 4 is summary-first and payment-first: the booking summary leads on mobile, and the optional savings controls move below payment behind one collapsed disclosure (MBUX-07, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the sixth Phase 15 slice. **This slice is about ORDER, not decoration.** **(1) The booking summary rendered AFTER everything on mobile.** Step 4's layout is a `lg:grid-cols-[1fr_380px]` grid whose second column is the full booking summary. Below `lg:` that grid is a single column, so the summary — the thing the guest came to this step to check — was pushed BELOW the voucher, points, discount, payment, and upload controls. A guest scrolled past the entire flow to discover what they were about to be charged for. The fix is CSS `order`, not a DOM change: the controls column is `order-2 lg:order-1` and the summary aside is `order-1 lg:order-2`. `order` applies in a single-column grid (mobile) and is overridden at `lg:`, where the two-column layout must keep controls left and summary right. **One node moves; there is no second copy of the summary**, so the desktop sidebar cannot drift from the mobile card — which a duplicated render would have invited. **(2) The optional savings controls sat ABOVE payment and were permanently expanded.** Voucher, rewards points, and the Senior/PWD discount each rendered as its own always-open card at the top of the column. Two consequences: a guest who simply wanted to pay had to scroll past three optional controls they may never use, and the most common path through the step was the longest one. All three now sit BELOW the payment method and the proof/eligibility upload, behind a single `Have a voucher, points, or discount?` disclosure that is collapsed by default. The long eligibility guidance moves inside with them, where it is only read when it is relevant. **(3) An APPLIED saving stays visible while the section is collapsed.** This is the hazard the collapse introduces: a guest who DID apply a voucher must still see that it is applied, or they will conclude the discount vanished when they reach payment — or apply a second code. A single line (`Applied: Voucher ABC, 500 points, Senior Citizen discount`) renders outside the collapsible body whenever any saving is active, derived from the same `voucherApplied` / `pendingPointsToRedeem` / `discountType` state the controls write. **(4) The footer shows the total and the action names the amount.** The summary reads `Total due`, and the single action is `Place booking · {formatPrice(displayTotal)}`. Both quote `displayTotal` — the same value the summary card, the sidebar, and the create payload use — so the button can never display a figure that differs from the bill. Processing and upload states remain explicit and never replace the action. Blocking messages still name their specific gate. **Preserved rather than rebuilt:** only the SELECTED payment method expands its instructions; the cancellation policy was already a collapsed `<details>`; proof and ID uploads already accept the image formats and show format/size guidance; the duplicate consent checkbox was already removed in MBUX-06. **One correction worth recording:** the spec asks that proof upload "supports camera/photo-library use", and it does — but NOT via a `capture` attribute. On iOS Safari and Android Chrome a file input whose `accept` includes the image types already surfaces "Take Photo" beside the library, and a hard `capture` would FORCE the camera and remove the library option. The test therefore asserts `accept` and explicitly forbids someone "improving" it into a forced camera. **Rejected:** reordering the DOM (would need two summary renders to keep the desktop layout, and they would drift); rendering the summary twice for the two breakpoints; a JS-driven breakpoint to swap layouts (needs resize handling and hydration-safe behaviour for a purely visual concern); expanding the savings section when a code is already applied (the applied-state line communicates the same thing without hiding the rest); `capture` on the upload inputs (removes the photo-library choice); leaving the voucher block first "because it used to be". **Tests:** new `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-07-step-4-review.test.ts` (24 guards, including negative pins that the applied-state line cannot move inside the collapsed body and that the summary cannot be duplicated). **Verified:** guest-app 2217 passed (the single `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`), `tsc` clean, `vite build` clean, `docs:audit` unchanged from the pre-existing baseline. **No `shared/` or `server/` change, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.**

| 260 | **Step 5 confirmation hierarchy: the essential result leads, and payment PROOF is never presented as payment VERIFICATION (MBUX-08, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the seventh Phase 15 slice. **(1) Step 5 could not tell a receipt from a verification, and said so on the page.** Every line of status copy derived from ONE boolean — `isOnlinePayment` — which answers "which METHOD was chosen", not "what STATE is the payment in". Three concrete falsehoods shipped with it: a guest whose payment staff had ALREADY verified still read "Booking submitted for review"; the email line told every online guest "official confirmation will follow after payment verification", including those already verified; and nothing on the page ever stated whether anything was still owed OF the guest. The `status` the create endpoint already returns was on the page the entire time — it gates the EC-02 early-check-in widget via the URL — and only the COPY refused to read it. `resolveConfirmationStatus` (`guest-app/src/utils/confirmationStatus.ts`) now derives the headline, summary, next step, payment-status label, payment-status detail and email-status sentence from one call, so those six strings cannot disagree. **(2) The precedence in that helper is the decision, and one arm of it is counter-intuitive.** Order: `cancelled` beats everything; a NON-online method outranks the booking status; only then does `payment-confirmed`/`confirmed`/`checked-in`/`checked-out` outrank `payment-uploaded`, which outranks "awaiting proof". The second arm reads backwards until it is said aloud: a pay-at-hotel booking that reaches `confirmed` has had its STAY confirmed, but no money moved and none could have been checked — ranking the status first would tell a guest that staff cleared a transfer that never happened. This was not a theoretical concern: the first implementation ranked status first, the test caught it, and the label read "Payment verified" on a booking nobody had paid. The statuses are typed as `BookingStatus[]`, so renaming one in `shared/constants` breaks this helper at compile time rather than silently downgrading guests to "awaiting proof". An unknown, empty or hand-edited status on an online method resolves to `awaiting-proof`, never to `verified`. **`cancelled` was added beyond the spec's list** because the page is reachable by deep link and a guest returning to a cancelled booking must not be told it is confirmed; it gets a neutral tone so the green tick is never rendered beside a cancellation. **(3) Payment STATUS is now a visible row, distinct from payment METHOD.** The page always showed the method (the configured label, never a hardcoded string); it never showed the standing. The new row renders `Proof received — not yet verified` verbatim for `payment-uploaded` — the word "verified" may appear only inside the negation — and `Payment verified` only for a genuinely verified online payment. A missing `status` param plus an online method renders `Awaiting payment proof` with a next step that names the receipt, so a guest is never left guessing whether the ball is in their court. **(4) The reference is COPYABLE.** The front desk asks for it at check-in and the old markup required hand-selecting monospace text on a phone. A clipboard button uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, announces the result in a `role="status"` live region (guest-app has no toast system), auto-resets after 2.4s, and on failure — insecure context or denied permission — falls back to selecting the reference text, so it is never a dead control. The value also carries `select-all` as the last resort. **(5) Actions: the two things a guest does next are now the primary pair.** `Add to Google Calendar` and a new `Manage booking` → `/my-booking` lead, both primary-coloured. `Return to Homepage` — previously the single `PrimaryButton`, i.e. the least useful of the three promoted to the most prominent slot — is demoted to a `GhostButton` beside `Download .ics`. The `!hasAllParams` empty state keeps its primary "Return to Homepage" deliberately: with no booking there is nothing else to offer. **The label "Add to Google Calendar" is deliberately NOT shortened** to "Add to Calendar". It deep-links to Google Calendar, while "Download .ics" directly below adds to the DEVICE calendar; two adjacent buttons both reading "Add to Calendar" would describe two different destinations. `batch-13-add-to-calendar` pins this and caught an over-eager rename. **(6) Everything non-essential moved below the result.** The early check-in perk moved from directly under the reference to BELOW the actions: it is a member upsell, and a guest checking their dates, total and payment status no longer scrolls past a perk they may never use. The time-sensitive concern it was promoted for is answered by a new "What happens next" block that always states what the guest owes — including that verification is the hotel's job, not theirs, once proof has been received. The full rate breakdown collapsed into a `See the full price breakdown` `<details>` — a receipt detail, not the outcome. The rewards prompt already sat below and stays there. **(7) Collateral cleanup, honestly scoped.** SR-01..05 retired the "Email the staff card" panel but left behind a `usePublicSiteContent()` read whose `contact` binding nothing used, under a comment still claiming that panel existed "further in the JSX". Both are gone. **Preserved rather than rebuilt:** the `!hasAllParams` empty state, the EC-02 early-check-in gate and its 3-status allowlist (pinned as a regression net — MBUX-08 adds a second reader of `bookingStatus` and must not remove the first), the `hotelConfig`/`rewardsConfig` reads, the `.ics` download and Google Calendar URL construction, and the `Reservation Reference` label other suites anchor on. The `booking-confirm-early-checkin-modal-success` placement suite had been `describe.skip`-ed while EC-02's ordering shipped; it is now un-skipped and inverted to the MBUX-08 ordering. **Rejected:** a "we'll email you when it's verified" auto-poll (the page has no booking read access — that is why the status is in the URL at all); showing payment status as a step-4-only concern; deleting the EC-02 perk rather than demoting it (members still need it, just not above their own result); a toast for the copy confirmation (guest-app has none); rendering the summary twice for two breakpoints; trusting `status` from the URL for anything security-relevant (it stays a presentation hint — the server re-validates live status before accepting an early check-in). **Tests:** new `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-08-step-5-confirmation.test.ts` — 40 guards. The helper is asserted BEHAVIOURALLY because the rule that matters is a PRECEDENCE, which a regex cannot express; a source-text pin would have passed happily while the headline kept lying. Absence-assertions run against a comment-stripped copy of the source, since MBUX-08 adds comments naming the things it deleted. **Verified:** guest-app 2259 passed (the single `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`), shared 798/798, admin-app 1878/1879 (the `batch-13-add-to-calendar` failure was a real label regression from this slice, fixed before commit), `tsc` clean for both apps, `vite build` clean, `docs:audit` unchanged from the pre-existing 19-error baseline. **No `shared/` or `server/` change, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.** Visual verification at 320/375/390/430px remains outstanding for MBUX-10.

| 261 | **Corporate parity: the vestigial rate control and the summary order are fixed, and two "obvious" parity changes are deliberately NOT made (MBUX-09, owner-approved 2026-10-15; `feat:` MINOR bump):** the eighth Phase 15 slice. **This slice is as much about what was refused as what shipped**, because it is the point where a Phase 15 change could most easily destroy a corporate rule that no public-flow test covers. **(1) The lone "Room Only" button was the same vestigial panel MBUX-02 deleted publicly.** The button's own comment already admitted that BR-03 (decision #232) removed "Room + Breakfast" from the corporate flow **too** — so it had not been a rate CHOICE for some time. It was a "select this room type" action wearing a rate option's clothes, giving the guest a control that did exactly one thing. It becomes a full-width `Select this room` action, and the CARD carries the selected state (`aria-current="true"` + a non-interactive "Selected" confirmation). **A per-type quantity stepper was deliberately NOT added.** The corporate flow distributes guests per room in a LATER step (`distributedRoomCart`), so a stepper at selection time would be a second, conflicting source of truth — the exact defect MBUX-03 fixed on the public flow by extraction rather than duplication. `selectRoomType` is kept verbatim because it owns the `roomType` / `checkIn` / `checkOut` / `guests` URL writes that deep-linking and back-navigation depend on, and `rateChoice: "room-only"` survives for API and payload compatibility — MBUX-02 deleted the PANEL and the handler, never the value. **(2) Step 4 is summary-first, mirroring MBUX-07.** Both corporate review steps use the same `lg:grid-cols-[1fr_380px]` grid that MBUX-07 fixed publicly, so below `lg:` the summary rendered below the entire payment/verification form. Controls get `order-2 lg:order-1` and the shared `BookingReviewAside` `<aside>` gets `order-1 lg:order-2` alongside its existing `lg:sticky lg:top-36 lg:self-start`. ONE node moves; there is no second copy of the summary to drift, and both steps inherit the fix from the single aside component. **(3) The Step 3 consent consolidation was REFUSED, and this is the most important decision in the slice.** MBUX-06 deleted a duplicate consent control on the public flow because two consecutive steps asked the same question with two different state variables. Corporate has two consent controls that are **not** duplicates: `guestDetails.consent` is the privacy/terms consent the server validates, while `termsConsent` is a separate corporate **AUDIT** declaration — "I declare the travel information is correct. I agree to the hotel check-in/out policies, room guidelines, corporate audit rules…". They answer different questions and have separate gates (`canContinueToReview` vs `canConfirm`). Applying MBUX-06 here would have silently deleted a real compliance control and weakened corporate audit compliance — a textbook "consistency" refactor that destroys meaning. Both are asserted present, and so is the distinctive audit wording, so a future attempt to "finish the parity" by deleting `termsConsent` fails the suite. **(4) The add-on consolidation is VACUOUS on corporate.** MBUX-05 retired BR-10's dual CTAs publicly and deferred corporate "until MBUX-09". The audit found corporate has **no add-on catalogue at all** — the add-ons step renders a "no add-ons available" placeholder, with no breakfast grid and no per-date selectors. Copying the public consolidation would have meant rewriting a button that does not exist, or inventing a breakfast grid that has no product behind it. Neither was done. **(5) Step 5 parity is NOT achieved, and saying so is part of the record.** The public flow redirects to the shared `/book/confirm` page, which is why MBUX-08's payment PROOF vs VERIFICATION distinction reaches every public guest. **The corporate flow does not use that page** — it sets `step: "confirm"` on its own URL and renders from its own `bookingResponse` state, a second confirmation implementation. So a corporate guest still reads copy that cannot distinguish an uploaded proof from a verified payment.
