Closing that is real outstanding work, pinned by a test that documents the current shape rather than a claim of parity. **Preserved and asserted:** the access-code gate, the `Negotiated rate` badge, the BI-04 fallback that never renders a zero price when a type has no negotiated rate (`ratePerRoomType[type] ?? type.corporateRate || type.pricePerNight`), the `distributedRoomCart` guest-distribution model, the URL writes, the member identity lock (email/first/last read-only, phone deliberately editable), and the MBUX-01 safe-area reserve. **Rejected:** copying MBUX-06's consent deletion across (destroys a compliance control); adding a quantity stepper (second source of truth against the distribution step); importing the public add-ons UI to corporate (no catalogue exists to render); pointing corporate at `/book/confirm` as a one-liner (its inline step carries corporate-specific fields — company name, billing arrangement — that the public confirmation does not carry). **Tests:** new `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-09-corporate-parity.test.ts` (25 guards, roughly half of them negative: the vestigial control cannot return, no stepper can be added, `termsConsent` and its audit wording cannot be deleted, `updateRoomQuantity` cannot be introduced). Absence-assertions run against a comment-stripped copy of the source. **Verified:** guest-app 2284 passed (the single `br-06-refunds-extension` failure is pre-existing on `dev`), `tsc -p guest-app` clean, `vite build` clean, `docs:audit` unchanged from the pre-existing 19-error baseline. **No `shared/` or `server/` change, so `guest-app/api/[...route].js` is untouched.**

| 262 | **Phase 15 close-out: cross-surface contract, two bug fixes found in the close-out pass, and the known browser matrix is honestly deferred (MBUX-10, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `docs:` no bump):** the close-out slice of Phase 15. **(1) Cross-surface contract test (19 guards) pins the invariants the slice suites cannot reach on their own.** Shared shell components (`StepIndicator`, `BookingStickyFooter`) are imported by BOTH `BookingPage` AND `CorporateBookingPage`; the hooks-rule regression net (which caught a production React #310 this week — see below) is asserted across every page with a non-trivial hook surface; the CSP permits the Google auth iframe script in BOTH `vercel.json` files; the bundle's `VERSION2` is asserted ≤ the source `VERSION` (it can lag but must never lead — the discipline is "rebuild at release cutover, not per commit" to avoid the ~20k-line env-drift diff); MBUX-09's two refused items are still in place (the corporate audit `termsConsent` declaration and the vacant add-ons placeholder); MBUX-08's `cancelled` state still renders with a neutral tone; the public privacy `termsConsent` is still deleted. **(2) Two real production bugs were found and fixed in the close-out pass and shipped before this decision.** First: the React #310 on `/book` — a `useState` (`breakfastCopyNotice`, MBUX-05) had been declared AFTER the loading-skeleton early return in `BookingPage`. The first render hit the early return and skipped the hook; the second render reached it; React's hook-order check fired and unmounted the whole tree. Reproduced live against the staging project, fixed by moving the useState above the early return, with a load-bearing comment that documents why the position is not negotiable, and a regression net that would have caught the bug. Second: the Google auth iframe CSP block — `script-src` listed `www.google.com` but not `apis.google.com`, and Firebase Auth's `signInWithPopup` injects the latter at runtime. Google sign-in was broken in production while email/password was unaffected, which is why it was easy to miss. Fixed by adding the host to `script-src` in BOTH `vercel.json` and `admin-app/vercel.json`. **(3) The full verification sweep is clean.** `tsc -p guest-app` and `tsc -p admin-app` both clean; `vite build` clean; guest-app 2308 passed / 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` test, unchanged on `dev`); shared 798/798; admin 1879/1879; `docs:audit` unchanged from the pre-existing 19-error baseline (zero new). The browser-QA matrix at 320/375/390/430px, tablet, and desktop — explicitly listed in the MBUX-10 spec — could not be executed in this session. The dev server is running against the staging Firebase project and the Step 1 page renders cleanly without the React #310; that is the partial verification this slice shipped, and the matrix itself remains **outstanding, owner to confirm which Firebase env to drive the pass against** (the repo ships `.env`, `.env.local`, `.env.spark-inn-stg`, and `.env.spark-inn-prod`). Nothing in this slice is `feat:`-shaped; the only version bump is the `fix:` that landed at #260's pre-existing audit declaration and the new `fix:` for the React #310 (carried in `76ad458`), which this decision records the close-out for.
| 263 | **`recordCallHistory` catch surfaces a toast, not a silent `console.warn` (FCH-01, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.15.2` → `2.15.3`):** the deferred follow-up from decision #216, shipped now that the Phase 15 close-out is closed. **(1) The exact shape that hid #216 is itself the lesson.** Pre-#216 the `recordCallHistory` catch at `AdminContext.tsx:4539` wrapped the chat-history write in a `try/catch` that logged `[call-history] failed to record {outcome} for {roomId}: ...` as a `console.warn` and moved on. When the #216 Firestore rule rejected the call-history write (the rule's `keys().hasOnly([...])` allowlist was missing `callAnsweredByName`), the warn was silent from the staff member's perspective — no toast, no banner, no error indicator. The operator only found out by manually inspecting the chat thread. The #216 rule fix unblocked the common case, but any FUTURE write failure (Firestore quota, network blip, a new rule field mismatch, the `intercoms/{roomId}/messages` parent doc missing, etc.) would still go to console without a UI signal. **The shape is the lesson, not the specific bug.** **(2) FCH-01 makes the catch visible to the staff member it concerns.** A new `notify.error("Call history not recorded", \`The call-history entry for room {roomId} could not be saved. See the browser console for details.\`)` call is added INSIDE the same `catch` block. The toast is the staff-facing PRIMARY signal; the existing `console.warn` is the SECONDARY diagnostic for the operator's devtools. Both surfaces remain because the toast carries strings, not error stacks — the operator still needs the actual error object in the console to debug. The toast message points at the console explicitly so staff don't double-book the failure into a chat thread or a Reports entry. The toast mirrors the existing `acceptCall` "Call taken" pattern at `AdminContext.tsx:5152`, re-using the same `notify.error(title, message?)` signature from `Toast.tsx` (the error variant already produces `role="alert"` + `aria-live="assertive"` for screen-reader announcement). **(3) The dispatch contract is unchanged.** Still best-effort — never rethrows (a future refactor that adds `throw error;` at the catch tail would let a Firestore failure break the call cleanup flow, which is exactly the bug the try/catch was added to avoid). Still never blocks the call cleanup. The active-call banner / hang-up already succeeded before `recordCallHistory` runs. **(4) New source-text regression net** `admin-app/src/__tests__/fch-01-record-call-history-toast.test.ts` (10 guards): braces-aware slice of the `recordCallHistory` body; `notify.error` title `"Call history not recorded"`; the `roomId` is interpolated into the message; the toast message points at the browser console; `console.warn` keeps the `[call-history]` log prefix AND the actual error object as second arg (so the operator's devtools still receives the error stack + Firestore error code); the catch does NOT rethrow; `AdminContext.tsx` imports `notify` from `../components/Toast` (the same module as the existing toast call sites); the Toast module's error variant uses `role="alert"` so screen readers announce failures immediately. The pre-existing `call-history-messages.test.ts:88` `console.warn` pin remains — the `[call-history]` log prefix is the operator's diagnostic and removing it would lose the actual error. **(5) Test totals.** 1895 admin-app tests pass + 16 skipped (the 2 pre-existing `api-base-url` + `dashboard-overdue-checkouts` test files fail to load due to a `@config` vitest alias issue, unrelated to this slice — verified by stash + re-run). `tsc -p admin-app` clean. **Rejected.** **(a) Replace the `console.warn` with the toast** — losing the error object in the console would make future debugging harder; the warn must stay. **(b) Show a banner/modal instead of a toast** — too heavy for a best-effort audit-trail write; the toast matches every other admin-app failure surface (`acceptCall` "Call taken", voucher toggle, payment method, store item, etc.). **(c) Re-throw on catch** — would break the call cleanup flow; the helper is best-effort by contract. **(d) Push the failure to a separate `auditLogs/{autoId}` collection** — over-engineered for a "this audit trail entry didn't land" signal; a follow-up audit could add it if the pattern recurs. **(e) Surface a banner on the inbox listing the rooms with failed audit entries** — the toast is sufficient; if the pattern persists across many rooms, the inbox banner is a future slice, not a FCH-01 concern. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#263, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Open Follow-Ups → FCH-01 bullet rewritten + last-updated line bumped).
| 264 | **Mobile UX review fixes — scroll-to-top scope, summary strip on top with Edit affordance, branched cart-distribution error copy (MBUX-11, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.15.3` → `2.15.4`):** three small but real product fixes captured from the owner's mobile review of `/book` Step 1. **(1) `App.tsx ScrollToTop` no longer scrolls on every search-param mutation.** The pre-fix effect watched the whole `search` string via the `useLocation` hook's effect dependency and called `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` on every change — but the booking flow persists cart state (`adults`, `children`, `quantity`, etc.) into the same search string, so every +/- button and every room-card selection triggered a scroll-to-top. The owner explicitly flagged this: *"when I click select room or make changes using minus and plus sign, it always scrolls back to top which is annoying."* The fix tracks the previous pathname + previous `step` query-param value via `useRef`s; only fires `window.scrollTo(0, 0)` when either changed. Cart mutations and steppers no longer touch the viewport. **Not a refactor of the booking flow itself** — the cart's URL persistence is the BR-09 multi-step URL contract and stays; the scroll side-effect is the only thing that changed. **(2) New `StaySummaryStrip` component (`guest-app/src/components/StaySummaryStrip.tsx`)** mounts directly under the `StepIndicator` on mobile (the right of the hero on `md:` and up). Carries the selected date range + night count + guest count + an Edit pencil that smooth-scrolls to the existing stay-details `<aside>` (now carrying `id="booking-stay-details-aside"` + `scroll-mt-32`) with a brief ring highlight — mirrors the existing Adjust-room CTA pattern at `BookingPage.tsx:4656`. Strip is `lg:hidden` so the desktop sidebar stays the primary surface on `lg:` and up without duplicating the summary lines. Agoda-style top-strip + edit affordance per the owner review: *"the date selection should I think be on top part of the page for mobile but maybe make it hidden or collapsed by default since it was already populated before step 1, maybe add an edit button of something like that just like Agoda."* **(3) The cart-distribution failure message branches on the actual cause now, instead of always showing the same generic sentence.** New `cartDistributionErrorMessage` helper at `BookingPage.tsx:952-968` picks the right text from four specific causes + a legacy fallback: **(A)** no rooms → *"Pick at least one room to start your stay."* **(B)** more rooms than guests can fill → *"You have N rooms but only N guests — each room needs at least one adult. Remove a room or add another guest."* **(C)** unassigned adults remain → *"N adults are not assigned to a room yet — pick a different room or remove a guest."* **(D)** unassigned children remain → *"N children are not assigned to a room yet — pick a different room or remove a child."* **(legacy)** the original *"Add enough available rooms to fit every guest, with at least one adult assigned to each room."* stays as the defensive last case (should not fire in normal use). The warning `<p>` now also carries `role="alert"` so screen readers announce the failure immediately (was missing pre-MBUX-11). The owner explicitly asked for a cause-specific message: *"maybe tell user too much rooms where added maybe update the number of guests or check."* **Collateral.** `mbux-07-step-4-review.test.ts` updated to accommodate the new `id` attribute on the aside (the structural contract — one aside with the right order classes + an id — is unchanged; the test was just too rigid about which attribute appears first). **Three new source-text regression nets (38 guards total) — `tests/api/mbux-11-scroll-scope.test.ts` (10 guards), `tests/api/mbux-11-stay-summary-strip.test.ts` (16 guards), `tests/api/mbux-11-cart-error-cases.test.ts` (12 guards).** Each pins the SHAPE not the pixels; the visual acceptance matrix (does the strip look right at 375px) is the MBUX-10 manual browser pass when the user confirms the Firebase env to drive it against. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2333 guest-app tests pass + 246 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline. **Rejected.** **(a) Drop the new strip's Edit button** — the owner explicitly asked for an "edit button of something like that just like Agoda"; skipping the button means the strip is purely informational and the guest has to scroll down to find the picker. **(b) Make the strip sticky on top instead of inline** — the sticky behaviour would compete with the page header on scroll and create a stacking-context conflict with `BookingStickyFooter`; the inline mount at the top of the section is sufficient because the StepIndicator already anchors the user to the top of the Step 1 view. **(c) Open the date picker in a modal on Edit** — a modal would duplicate the existing `<DateRangePicker>` + Adults/Children steppers; the scroll-to-aside pattern reuses the existing controls and is the same pattern the Adjust-room CTA already uses. **(d) Always-on inline error (no branching)** — would have lost the cause-specific feedback the owner requested; the legacy message stays as the defensive guard for the rare case none of A–D match. **(e) Add a "Edit guests" CTA button to the unassigned-guests message** — same scope creep as (a); the message itself points at the obvious next action ("pick a different room or remove a guest") without needing a button. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#264, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-11 as shipped).
| 265 | **Breakfast menu switches to a responsive grid on mobile (MBUX-12, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.15.4` → `2.15.5`):** the pre-MBUX-12 `BreakfastMenuStrip` was a horizontal-scroll carousel (`flex snap-x snap-mandatory overflow-x-auto` + 128px per-card width). On a 375px phone that put 2.5 cards visible and cut the third one in half — exactly the *"Longsilog is half off-screen"* shape the owner flagged on Step 2. The fix is a responsive CSS grid: **2 cols on mobile, 3 cols on `sm:`, 4 cols on `lg:`**. The per-card `w-32 sm:w-40` (128px/160px) is replaced by `w-full` so the grid cell owns the width. The aspect ratio (4:3), `loading="lazy"`, the `UtensilsCrossed` fallback icon, the `failedIds` error-recovery state, and the brand-neutral tile styling all stay — the strip is a read-only catalogue, not a selector, so adding a hover state or making the tiles interactive would have been the wrong move. **The fallback icon you see in the owner's screenshot is the expected shape when staging has no meal photos uploaded yet** — that's a content gap (MBUX-04 admin upload path), not a code bug. The skip-a-day explanation below the strip moves from `mt-4` to `mt-5` so the breathing room is preserved now that the strip no longer has its own internal `pb-1`. **Collateral.** `mbux-05-step-2-addons.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-12 assertion pinned `overflow-x-auto` + `overscroll-x-contain` + `snap-x` (the carousel classes that are now gone); rewritten to pin the new responsive grid shape. The pre-MBUX-12 negative pins live in the new test file. **New source-text regression net `tests/api/mbux-12-breakfast-menu-grid.test.ts` (11 guards) — pins the grid utility classes (`grid grid-cols-2 gap-3 sm:grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-4`), the per-card `w-full` sizing, the absence of carousel classes (`snap-x`, `overflow-x-auto`, `overscroll-x-contain` — comments stripped via `stripComments` to avoid the JSDoc prose accidentally satisfying the negative regex), the `UtensilsCrossed` fallback + `failedIds` error recovery, the brand-neutral tile styling (gray-100 ring + gray-400 icon, no hover state), the `aria-label="Breakfast menu"` on the section, the empty `alt=""` on the decorative `<img>`, and the `mt-5` on the skip-a-day explanation. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2344 guest-app tests pass + 246 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline. **Rejected.** **(a) Keep the carousel and shrink the per-card width** — at 96px per card, 3.9 cards would fit on a 375px phone, but the meal name would truncate to ~10 characters ("Longsilog" → "Longsi…" with no price visible), which is worse than the grid. **(b) Make the tiles interactive (clicking a meal pre-selects it across all slots)** — the strip is a read-only catalogue; pre-selection across slots would be a huge UX change that hides the per-date × per-guest selection shape and would need its own decision entry. **(c) Show a "View all meals" modal** — adds a layer for a 3-meal catalogue; mobile grid is sufficient until the catalogue grows past ~6 meals. **(d) Hide the strip on mobile entirely** — the strip is the only place a guest sees the meal photos; hiding it would lose the MBUX-04 admin-upload value. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#265, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-12 as shipped).
| 266 | **Savings disclosure moves above the payment method card on Step 4 (MBUX-13, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.15.5` → `2.15.6`):** the pre-MBUX-13 Step 4 (Review & Pay) layout put the "Click to upload receipt photo" button BEFORE the "Have a voucher, points, or discount?" disclosure. A guest who already sent payment via QR + uploaded the receipt had nothing to gain by then opening the disclosure — they would only discover they had a discount / voucher AFTER they had committed to the full price. The owner explicitly flagged this on mobile: *"for mobile, does the Have a voucher, points, or discount? have a good placement already? i think its better to have it before the upload screenshot part? so that user wont send first before applying the discount"*. The fix reorders only the placement — the disclosure's collapsed-by-default shape from MBUX-07 (decision #259) is preserved. **Why ABOVE the payment method card, not just above the upload specifically:** the upload is gated on picking a payment method first (it doesn't render until `currentPaymentMethod` is set), so a "before the upload" placement would still put the disclosure below the method-picker card — the guest would have already committed to a payment path. Above the entire method-picker card gives the cleanest "before you commit to sending money" placement. **What stays:** same `<details>` element, same summary header text, same `ChevronDown` rotation animation, same `data-testid` attributes (`optional-savings-disclosure`, `optional-savings-summary`, `optional-savings-applied`), same inner content (voucher / points / Senior-PWD picker / ID upload), same collapsed-by-default state. **Collateral.** `mbux-07-step-4-review.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-13 assertions pinned "payment method BEFORE disclosure" + "upload BEFORE disclosure" (the MBUX-07 "savings below payment" shape). Reversed to pin the new MBUX-13 "savings BEFORE payment method" shape. The "one disclosure wraps voucher / points / discount" + "collapsed by default" + "cancellation policy last" assertions are unchanged. **New source-text regression net `tests/api/mbux-13-savings-disclosure-above-payment.test.ts` (13 guards) — disclosure sits before the payment method card + before the upload (the new ordering); same testids + className + summary text + applied line + content cards (all preserved); NO `open` attribute on the opening `<details>` (MBUX-13 is placement only, not state); MBUX-13 rationale comment is in place; exactly one `optional-savings-disclosure` (no accidental block duplication during the reorder). **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2357 guest-app tests pass + 246 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline. **Rejected.** **(a) Also expand the disclosure by default on mobile** — kept as MBUX-14, a separate future slice if requested. MBUX-13's mandate was placement only; expanding-by-default is a different decision that affects whether the disclosure acts as a billboard for the discount options (the brand-voice question) vs a compact one-tap affordance (the MBUX-07 collapse philosophy). Doing both at once would conflate the two. **(b) Move the disclosure above the guest details** — would put optional savings ahead of required guest details, which is backwards: a guest who abandons at the consent step should not have been prompted about vouchers first. **(c) Remove the disclosure entirely on mobile** — a discount exists on every booking; removing it removes a feature. **(d) Auto-scroll the disclosure into view when the user picks a payment method** — gimmicky and breaks the scroll-position discipline from MBUX-11 (cart mutations must not move the viewport). **(e) Use a tooltip on the upload button that says "did you apply a discount?"** — same underlying problem (the user said "user wont send first before applying the discount"), but a tooltip is invisible to the user in the moment they need it; the disclosure before the payment card is the right shape. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#266, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-13 as shipped).
| 267 | **Savings disclosure expanded by default on Step 4 (MBUX-14, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.15.6` → `2.15.7`):** the deferred state flip from MBUX-13 (decision #266). The pre-MBUX-13 / pre-MBUX-14 / MBUX-07 (decision #259) state was collapsed-by-default — a guest had to tap the "Have a voucher, points, or discount?" disclosure to even see the picker. On mobile this hid the Senior/PWD + voucher + points options from guests in a hurry, who would submit their booking without realizing a discount was available. MBUX-13 moved the disclosure ABOVE the payment method card so guests see it before committing to a payment path, but kept the collapsed state and explicitly deferred the state flip ("MBUX-13 is a placement fix only, not a state fix"). MBUX-14 picks up that deferred flip. **Behaviour change.** The `<details>` opening tag now carries an `open` attribute so the disclosure renders expanded by default on every viewport (mobile + desktop). The disclosure still collapses on tap (the chevron rotation animation + the `group-open:rotate-180` wiring are unchanged), so a guest who has already applied nothing and doesn't want to see the options can collapse it. **What stays.** The disclosure still wraps the Voucher / Points / Senior-PWD picker + ID upload as a single block; the className, the `data-testid`, the summary text, the chevron rotation animation are all unchanged; the "Applied: …" line for guests who already applied a voucher / points / discount is unchanged; the placement from MBUX-13 (above the payment method card) is unchanged — MBUX-14 is state-only. **Collateral.** `mbux-13-savings-disclosure-above-payment.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-14 assertion pinned "no open attribute" (MBUX-13 deferred the flip); flipped to pin "open attribute IS present". `mbux-07-step-4-review.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-14 "the disclosure is COLLAPSED by default" assertion flipped to "the disclosure is OPEN by default (per MBUX-14, decision #267)". The `<details>` opening-tag anchor regexes throughout the MBUX-13 + MBUX-07 + MBUX-14 suites were tightened to anchor on the LAST `<details>` BEFORE the testid (not the FIRST `<details>` in the file), because `BookingPage` has three `<details>` elements (the hold-countdown banner, the optional-savings disclosure, the cancellation policy) and a non-greedy `[\s\S]+?` was matching from the wrong start. **New source-text regression net `tests/api/mbux-14-savings-disclosure-open-default.test.ts` (13 guards) — the `open` attribute is present on the disclosure's `<details>` opening tag (with the negative space pin so the `group-open:rotate-180` Tailwind class doesn't satisfy the regex); the MBUX-14 rationale comment is in place; the disclosure's className + testid + summary text + chevron rotation + inner content (voucher / points / Senior-PWD / ID upload) all unchanged; the hold-countdown banner's `<details>` and the cancellation-policy `<details>` stay collapsed (MBUX-14 only flipped the optional-savings disclosure); the MBUX-13 + MBUX-07 cross-test contracts both pin the open-by-default attribute. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2370 guest-app tests pass + 246 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline. **Rejected.** **(a) Mobile-only `open` via viewport detection** — would add a `useMediaQuery`-style hook to conditionally carry the `open` attribute on `<lg:` and drop it on `lg:`+; the cost is 5-10 lines of state for an asymmetric UX (desktop guest sees the disclosure closed, mobile guest sees it open) with no clear semantic justification. **(b) Auto-expand the disclosure based on the cart contents** — if the cart has >1 room, the per-individual Senior/PWD picker auto-reverts to "none" (the existing `cartIsMultiRoom` guard), so expanding when the guest already can't pick Senior/PWD is misleading. The simpler "always open" is honest. **(c) Add a subtle "Did you check for a discount?" hint to the submit button** — gimmicky and a11y-hostile (a button title that doesn't fit the button label is read by screen readers as a separate label, which is confusing). The expanded disclosure is the right place for the prompt. **(d) Force the disclosure open via JavaScript on focus** — needs JS, complicates the test surface, and the HTML `open` attribute is the native idiom. **(e) Ship MBUX-13 + MBUX-14 as one slice** — the placement fix (MBUX-13) was independently shippable and the state flip (MBUX-14) needed its own rationale comment + decision entry. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#267, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-14 as shipped).
| 268 | **Hold-expired banner stacks vertically on mobile (MBUX-15, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.15.7` → `2.15.8`):** the pre-MBUX-15 hold-expired banner layout was written for desktop and leaked onto mobile. The outer container was `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-3` — on a 375px phone the text block (with `flex-1`) got squeezed into a narrow column next to the two buttons, and the buttons themselves were narrow because they shared the row with the squeezed text. The owner flagged this with a screenshot of the banner on mobile and asked for a UI improvement. **Layout changes (HoldCountdownBanner.tsx, expired-state block):** outer container `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-3` → `flex flex-col gap-3 sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap sm:items-center sm:gap-3` (stacks on mobile, restores inline on `sm:`+); buttons container `flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2` → `flex flex-col gap-2 sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap sm:items-center sm:gap-2` (buttons stack vertically on mobile, each full-width, inline on desktop); each button `inline-flex min-h-11 ...` → `inline-flex min-h-11 w-full items-center justify-center ... sm:w-auto` (full-width on mobile, auto-width on `sm:`+). **What stays (MBUX-15 is layout only, not emphasis):** the "Your hold has expired." headline unchanged; body copy (extend / can't-extend branches) unchanged — the IMT spec reads long but reads cleanly at full mobile width now that the buttons sit below the text; IMT-03 primary/secondary emphasis preserved (the "I need more time" filled `bg-primary` stays primary, "Pick a new room" ghost `border-red-300 bg-white` stays secondary); the `<motion.div role="alert">` + `data-testid="hold-countdown-banner"` + `data-state="expired"` accessibility surface unchanged; the `min-w-0 flex-1` text-block anchor preserved so the desktop-only inline shape still shrinks without horizontal overflow; the active-state countdown + Retry layout, the transient-error Retry layout, and the loading-skeleton no-layout logic all untouched. **Source-text regression net `tests/api/mbux-15-hold-expired-mobile-stack.test.ts` (11 guards) — outer container uses `flex-col` for the mobile stack + restores `sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap sm:items-center` on `sm:`+; buttons container uses `flex-col gap-2` on mobile + restores `sm:flex-row sm:flex-wrap sm:items-center` on `sm:`+; the "I need more time" primary button is full-width on mobile (`w-full sm:w-auto`) and keeps the IMT-03 `bg-primary` filled emphasis; the "Pick a new room" secondary button is full-width on mobile and keeps the IMT-03 `border-red-300 bg-white` ghost emphasis; the headline + body-copy branches + `min-w-0 flex-1` text-block anchor are preserved; `role="alert"` + `data-testid="hold-countdown-banner"` + `data-state="expired"` accessibility surface is preserved. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2381 guest-app tests pass + 246 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline. **Rejected.** **(a) Shorten the body copy to "Need more time? Ask for more."** — the IMT spec deliberately carries the "if another guest gets the room first, we'll let you know before you pay" reassurance because it's the #1 question guests ask when the timer hits 0:00. Trimming it is a separate copy decision, not a layout fix. **(b) Use a sticky bottom-sheet on mobile instead of stacking in-flow** — adds a new visual mode (modal-style surface) for a 1-screen case; the in-flow stack is sufficient and the user already lands in the booking flow with a sticky footer at the bottom. **(c) Move the buttons to the right side of the headline instead of below** — that's the pre-MBUX-15 shape that produced the squeezed-text problem. **(d) Reduce the `gap-3` on mobile** — the gap is fine; the problem was the inline-not-stacked shape, not the gap. **(e) Drop the `min-w-0 flex-1` text-block anchor** — that's the contract for the desktop inline shape (it lets the text shrink instead of overflowing). Removing it would break the `sm:`+ view. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#268, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-15 as shipped).

| 269 | **Breakfast cut-off gets a visible, in-context explainer on Step 2 (MBUX-16, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `feat:` MINOR bump `2.15.9` → `2.16.0`):** the cut-off policy that locks the per-date breakfast cards had ZERO in-page explanation. A guest who arrived late in the day saw meal cards in a disabled state with no on-page reason and had to guess — most guessed "this site is broken" rather than "the cut-off window for tonight has passed". The owner asked: *"for breakfast cut-off can you add an explanation why the breakfast add-ons is unavailable or closed due to the cut off policy?"* **(1) A new helper, `formatCutoffTime`, owns the raw-time → display-time mapping.** Lives at `guest-app/src/utils/formatCutoffTime.ts` (own file rather than inlined in `BookingPage.tsx` so the test suite can import it directly without rendering the whole page). Takes "HH:MM" 24h, returns "H:MM AM/PM" (the in-page expectation: "20:00" → "8:00 PM"). **Falls back to the raw input on malformed value** — a misconfigured admin dining-settings value cannot crash the page; if it can't parse, it renders verbatim. The helper is the only place that knows the display rule, so a future locale change or 24h preference is a one-line edit. **(2) Per-date sub-line on locked dates inside the menu grid.** Each meal card that is locked for a given night now renders a small `text-xs text-amber-700` sub-line below the meal name: *"Cut-off was 8:00 PM the day before — skip this day or contact the front desk."* The cut-off time is interpolated via `formatCutoffTime`, so the rendered string always matches the admin-configured value. **Renders only on the cards that are locked** — open dates stay clean (no copy on a date that's bookable is meaningless noise), and the per-date anchor (`date.isLocked`) is the same predicate the disabled state uses, so the explainer cannot drift from the actual lock. **(3) One-line help text under the menu heading when `dates.some(c => c.isLocked)`.** A second piece of context that frames the explainer at the section rather than the per-card level: *"Some dates are past the cut-off. You can still book the stay — just skip breakfast for those nights."* This is the framing sentence — it tells the guest the booking is still possible, which is the actual unknown. **(4) Wording is short, neutral, and frames the cut-off as an OPERATIONAL fact, not a system failure.** "Cut-off was 8:00 PM the day before" is the policy stated plainly — no apology theater, no "we apologize for the inconvenience", no "this option is currently unavailable" tech-bro copy. The "contact the front desk" branch is intentional: a guest whose plan depends on a late arrival can resolve it with one call rather than abandoning the booking. **Source-text regression net `tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` (19 guards) —** the `formatCutoffTime` helper round-trips `"20:00"` → `"8:00 PM"`, `"08:00"` → `"8:00 AM"`, `"12:00"` → `"12:00 PM"`, `"00:00"` → `"12:00 AM"`, and `"23:59"` → `"11:59 PM"` (boundary cases); falls back to raw input on malformed strings (`"abc"`, `""`, `"99:99"`); the helper file lives at `guest-app/src/utils/formatCutoffTime.ts` (the import surface path is pinned, not the implementation detail — a rename that keeps the export name is fine); the per-date sub-line copy `"Cut-off was"` + `formatCutoffTime`'s output + `"the day before"` is interpolated into the locked-date branch of the menu grid; the help text `"Some dates are past the cut-off"` is rendered ONLY when `dates.some(c => c.isLocked)` is true (the help text branch is absent in the open-only path); the `text-xs text-amber-700` styling class names are present on the sub-line so the warn tone matches the disabled state; the open-date branch stays clean (no "cut-off" copy, no help text, no `text-amber-700`) — the assertion is the negative space; the helper is exported as a NAMED export (consistent with `plan/docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` named-exports-for-components rule for utils). **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2400 guest-app tests pass + 246 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline (MBUX-16 adds 0). **Rejected.** **(a) Inline the helper in `BookingPage.tsx`** — the helper is a pure transform with no React, no hooks, no JSX, and has 6 distinct input cases worth pinning. Inlining would force the test suite to render the whole `BookingPage` (which loads a Firestore snapshot and a Firebase emulator) just to assert "20:00" → "8:00 PM", which is the kind of test fragility the suite explicitly avoids. The helper file is 24 lines and the import cost is one line. **(c) "Sorry, breakfast for this date is no longer available"** — apology theater + system-failure framing; the cut-off is a policy, not an outage. **(d) Just "Cut-off passed" with no time** — the time IS the information; "passed" without "when" forces the guest to dig into the booking policy. **(e) Replace the disabled cards with a "Breakfast unavailable" message** — loses the menu structure (meal names, descriptions, photos) for the next morning, which is the case where a guest CAN still add breakfast. **(f) Auto-add a "breakfast skipped" line item to the cart on locked dates** — overrides the guest's intent; the cart is explicit, the policy is to skip, the choice belongs to the guest. **(g) Put the explainer in a tooltip on hover** — phone-only surface, no hover; the inline sub-line is the right mobile idiom. **(h) Add a per-card "Why is this locked?" link to the booking policy page** — out of the moment; the guest has to leave the menu grid to learn what the per-card copy already tells them. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#269, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-16 as shipped).
| 270 | **Hold banner copy: shorter honesty escape hatch (HCT-01, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `refactor:` PATCH bump `2.16.0` → `2.16.1`):** the active-state second paragraph of the Steps 2 + 3 hold banner pre-HCT-01 read "We'll lock it in the moment you confirm — if another guest books first, we'll let you know and you can pick a new room." The owner flagged the second clause as wordy: the long-form "if another guest books first, we'll let you know and you can pick a new room" spelled out a race the operator knows about but the guest doesn't need to anticipate. Owner asked for a shorter sentence that still did NOT make a hard-reservation promise. **Decisions.** **(1) New copy: "We'll lock it in the moment you confirm. If it goes through, you're set."** The "If it goes through, you're set" half is the conditional escape hatch — it preserves the honesty signal without spelling out the rare race. The conditional protects against the same trap the CCCS (Singapore consumer protection authority) flagged Agoda for in their June 2025 ruling: a short, lock-promising line with no escape hatch was called "misleading" because it created "a false sense of urgency" without telling the consumer what happens if the lock fails. The new copy keeps the conditional in spirit (the "if it goes through" half says the lock is not unconditional) without the handover-formula phrasing. **(2) Tripwire tests updated, NOT deleted.** Two source-text guards pinned the old phrasing: `tests/api/in-flow-hold.test.ts:285-290` (with a comment specifically calling out that "a future 'tighten the copy' change is forced to also update the decision entry" — this comment was the trigger that landed HCT-01 as a deliberate decision rather than a silent edit) and `tests/api/mbux-01-booking-shell.test.ts:229-231` ("keeps the honest copy — 'another guest books first' still ships"). Both matchers now assert on `/If it goes through/` — the conditional half of the new copy. The tripwire is preserved (any future copy change that removes the conditional will fail the test and force an updated decision entry). **(3) Headline paragraph is unchanged.** "Your room is held for you until {expiresAt}." stays verbatim — that line is the active-state status, not the honesty hedge. **(4) `refactor:` PATCH bump, not `feat:` MINOR or `fix:` PATCH-with-implication.** This is a tightening of copy, not a behaviour change or new surface. The user-visible behaviour of the hold banner is identical: same soft-hold status, same countdown, same conflict-on-confirm path. The decision lives in the 260s-numbered slot because it's a copy-tightening iteration on the IFH-01 banner, not a Phase 15 mobile-UX slice. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#270, this entry; IFH-01 entry's quoted copy updated to point at #270); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md` (intro paragraph + checkbox copy); `plan/features/AVAILABILITY-LOCKING.md` (§In-flow Hold paragraph); `guest-app/src/components/HoldCountdownBanner.tsx` (JSDoc header quote + active-state second paragraph). **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; guest-app tests pass (baseline + 0 new failures — both tripwires green); `docs:audit` unchanged from the 19-error baseline. **Rejected.** **(a) Just "We'll lock it in the moment you confirm." with no escape hatch at all** — overpromises a hard reservation; would put the copy in the same shape CCCS called Agoda out for. **(b) "We'll lock it in the moment you confirm. If another guest books first, we'll let you know." (keep the explicit race, drop the "pick a new room")** — still 17 words, the owner asked for shorter. **(c) "Confirmed at the moment of booking."** — factually misleading, no conditional, sounds like a guarantee. **(d) Re-order so the escape hatch comes first ("If it goes through, you're set. We'll lock it in the moment you confirm.")** — reads backwards; the natural narrative is "what we'll do" → "what it means", not the reverse.

| 271 | **Desktop `/book` repaired after the MBUX 11–16 mobile-first refresh (UX-DESK-01, owner-flagged on screenshots 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.16.1` → `2.16.2`):** the MBUX-07 (decision #259) mobile-first refresh shipped `order-2 lg:order-1` on the Step 4 controls column with the matching `order-1 lg:order-2` reserved for the summary aside. MBUX-11 (decision #264) added the same pattern to the Step 1 Stay details aside on 2026-10-15. The `BookingReviewAside` component (which renders in Steps 2, 3, AND 4) was never given its half of the contract. Three desktop-only bugs fell out of that single missing class — owner caught two on screenshots on 2026-10-16 ("Due to the MBUX changes, the desktop version was ruined. Can you check?"). **Decisions.** **(1) Step 4 Review & Pay: the booking summary on the wide main column, payment on the narrow sidebar, was the inverse of what the spec calls for.** `BookingReviewAside` defaulted to `order: 0`, which is LOWER than the controls' `lg:order-1` (= 1) — the auto-placement rule "lower order → column 1" sent the aside into the 1fr main column and the payment column into the 380px sidebar. With the GCash / Bank Transfer buttons squeezed into ~80px each (380 ÷ 4 per the BF-10 `lg:grid-cols-4` left over from the PayPal-as-4th-method audit), "Bank Transfer" wrapped onto three lines and the payment UI became unreadable. UX-DESK-01 fixes this with the spec-mandated `order-1 lg:order-2` on the aside element — controls (1) win column 1 (1fr LEFT main, where the guest commits the booking), aside (2) lands in column 2 (380px RIGHT sidebar, the reference surface). **Owner-led reframing that landed the design direction:** the owner explicitly asked on 2026-10-16 *"should the payment and discount which is currently in the sidebar be the one at the center since it is the main focus during review and pay?"* — the answer is yes, the controls + payment + upload own the wide main column on desktop, the booking summary is the reference sidebar on the right. Mobile behaviour is preserved — aside (1) FIRST → TOP, controls (2) SECOND → BELOW (matches MBUX-07's "summary first on mobile" rule). Safe for Steps 2 + 3 (which render the same component) because their forms come first in DOM order with default `order: 0`; the aside at order 2 on lg still lands in column 2 the way it did before, and at mobile the aside at order 1 still lands below the form (form-first on Steps 2 + 3 — matches existing design; only Step 4 needed "summary first on mobile"). **(2) Step 1 room cards collapsed into the 320px column on desktop.** `<section className="lg:grid-cols-[320px_1fr]">` placed the 320px sidebar in column 1 and the 1fr main in column 2, and the aside's `lg:order-2` (MBUX-11 mobile-first) sent the room cards into column 1. Inside each card `md:grid-cols-[280px_1fr]` reserved 280px for the image and only ~40px for the text, so room type names like "Standard Room" wrapped to one letter per line and the desktop column went visually unreadable — owner screenshot caught this. UX-DESK-01 swaps the template to `lg:grid-cols-[1fr_320px]` — the 1fr main is now column 1 (room cards fill the full ~800px width the original design intended), and the 320px sidebar lands in column 2 (RIGHT). Mobile behaviour is unchanged: below `lg:` the section collapses to a single column and the existing `order-1` / default `order: 0` stack still places room cards at the top. **(3) Payment Method grid was hard-pinned to `lg:grid-cols-4`** — the BF-10 (2026-06-26) audit commit added PayPal as a 4th method and pinned the grid to `lg:grid-cols-4`. With the typical 2-method config (GCash + Bank Transfer), `lg:grid-cols-4` forced each button into 25% of the container width — in the pre-UX-DESK-01 sidebar (380px) that worked out to ~80px per button; after UX-DESK-01's Step 4 layout flip the controls column is ~800px wide, but a 4-col grid with only 2 items would still leave 2 dead columns on the right. UX-DESK-01 makes the column count adaptive to the enabled method count — 1 method → 1 col (full-width button), 2 methods → 2 cols at `sm:`, 3 methods → 2 cols at `sm:` + 3 cols at `lg:`, 4+ methods → 2 cols at `sm:` + 4 cols at `lg:`. Each `lg:grid-cols-N` is gated by an explicit count predicate (`=== 3`, `>= 4`) rather than a dynamic template-literal class because the Tailwind JIT compiler needs the full class names in source. The `sm:grid-cols-2` fallback covers the typical 2-method config without any predicate. **Collateral.** `tests/api/mbux-07-step-4-review.test.ts` tightened — the pre-UX-DESK-01 "exactly one aside with order-1 lg:order-2" assertion was broadened to a pattern that ALSO matched the Step 1 aside MBUX-11 added (which carries the same `order-1 lg:order-2` but with an `id="booking-stay-details-aside"` and a trailing `scroll-mt-32`). UX-DESK-01 narrows the pattern to `<aside className="order-1 lg:order-2 lg:sticky lg:top-28 lg:self-start">` (no id, no scroll-mt-32) so it pin the `BookingReviewAside` specifically, not the broader "either Step 1 or Step 4 aside" match. The two-complementary-orders count assertion is updated to the same narrowed pattern. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; `vitest run` for `tests/api/mbux-07-step-4-review.test.ts` 24/24 green; full `vitest run` shows the pre-existing admin-app failures (the `@config` vitest alias issue — `dashboard-overdue-checkouts` + `api-base-url`) unchanged from `dev` HEAD, verified by `git stash` + re-run. **Rejected.** **(a) `feat:` MINOR bump** — desktop layouts are restoring the pre-MBUX-07 desktop behaviour, not adding a new surface; no new API, no new component, no user-visible behaviour change (the mobile-first booking flow that MBUX-11..16 shipped is unchanged). The owner flagged the regressions on screenshots, which is the textbook `fix:` shape. **(b) `release:` MAJOR bump** — no public API change, no data model change, no breaking schema; the two regressions were un-shipped desktop behaviour, not a new system. **(c) Refactor Step 1's mobile order too (move the aside to `order-first` so the Stay details renders ABOVE the room cards on mobile)** — out of UX-DESK-01 scope; mobile behaviour is unchanged and the Step 1 mobile order is its own design question that MBUX-11 explicitly deferred. A future "summary-first on Step 1 mobile" decision would be a separate slice. **(d) Add a 5-method `lg:grid-cols-5` tier** — no production hotel config has 5 enabled methods (the existing Pay at Hotel + GCash + Bank Transfer + PayPal are the 4-tier ceiling); speculative complexity for a non-existent shape. **(e) Move the `BookingReviewAside` element to use a unique `data-testid` so the test pattern can be scoped without relying on class-name inference** — would touch every Steps 2/3/4 render branch and add three testids for a single source-text test that already has a clean narrow-class pattern; the class-name pin is the lower-blast-radius fix. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#271, this entry); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md` (Step 1 intro paragraph re: the 1fr/320px column + Step 4 summary-first + payment-method-grid adaptive-cols notes).

| 272 | **Breakfast cut-off pill replaces the MBUX-16 per-date sub-line + the redundant "Skipping a day" reassurance line is dropped (MBUX-17, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.16.2` → `2.16.3`):** the MBUX-16 (#269) cut-off explanation shipped a top-of-section help text AND a per-date sub-line on every locked card. Both carried the cut-off TIME — the same `formatCutoffTime` interpolation appeared twice on the page whenever any date was locked. Owner flagged this on a screenshot: *"can we improve this part? it's too text-y and tight for mobile"*. Owner explicitly asked for two changes: (a) "maybe we can just add a 'ordering closed due to cut-off' pill for the date that the ordering is closed", and (b) "remove the skipping a day is fine - leave any slots as 'No Breakfast' since I think it is already implied since it is the default selection already". **Decisions. (1) Locked-date marker is a single compact pill, not a two-line stack.** The pre-MBUX-17 per-date right-aligned column carried line 1 (`"Ordering closed"`, `text-gray-600 text-xs`) AND line 2 (`"Cut-off was 8:00 PM the day before — skip this day or contact the front desk."`, `text-amber-700 text-xs`). MBUX-17 collapses both into one inline pill: `<span data-testid="breakfast-cutoff-pill" className="inline-flex items-center gap-1 rounded-full bg-amber-100 px-2.5 py-1 text-xs font-semibold text-amber-800">Ordering closed due to cut-off</span>`. The pill carries the full reason inline — the "due to cut-off" suffix replaces the verbose sub-line. The top-of-section help text (which appears when ANY date is locked) is now the SINGLE source for the cut-off TIME — no more duplication between the two surfaces. **The unlocked-state line stays byte-equivalent** (`Order by ${formatStayDate(date)}`) — MBUX-17 is locked-only, the `dates.some(c => c.isLocked)` gate is preserved. **(2) The pill uses the in-file `rounded-full + amber-100 + amber-800 + inline-flex items-center gap-1 + px-2.5 py-1 text-xs font-semibold` vocabulary** that already ships on the in-file `Tight — at the cap` / `Doesn't fit your group` / `Fits your group` pills (`BookingPage.tsx:4354-4357`) — so the cut-off indicator reads as part of the same visual vocabulary rather than a separate one-off element. No new icon, no new accent color, no new component. **(3) The pill is a `<span>` (inline), not a `<p>` (block)** — the right-aligned column on the date card header expects an inline sibling to the unlocked `Order by Oct 24` line so they share the same vertical rhythm. The `mb-05` "stacked label above full-width select" layout for the per-guest dropdowns is unchanged. **(4) The pre-MBUX-17 "Skipping a day is fine — leave any slot as 'No breakfast'" line above the date cards is REMOVED.** The default `<option value="">No breakfast</option>` on every guest slot (the first entry in the meal list) already communicates the same thing: a guest who never opens a dropdown already gets the no-meal choice for that guest/date. The reassurance paragraph was redundant mobile real estate — ~40px of vertical space above the per-date grid that changed no outcome. The `mt-5` slot the line used to occupy is now taken by the mbux-16 help text when at least one date is locked (mutually exclusive — the help text only appears when a date is locked, otherwise the gap collapses to nothing). **Why this is `fix:` PATCH, not `feat:` MINOR or `refactor:` PATCH.** This is a UI tightening of the MBUX-16 surface, not a new feature; no new API, no new component, no new test surface (the MBUX-16 tripwires get refocused onto the pill). The owner flagged the regression on a screenshot of the cut-off text being duplicated, which is the textbook `fix:` shape. **(5) `fix:` PATCH also matches the MBUX-12..15 cycle (#265-268), which used `fix:` PATCH for the same shape of mobile-UX review iterations on already-shipped MBUX surfaces.** The MBUX-01..10 initial Phase 15 implementation used `feat:` MINOR; MBUX-11..17 review iterations use `fix:` PATCH. **Collateral.** `tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-17 per-date sub-line tripwires (`data-testid="breakfast-cutoff-explanation"`, `skip this day or contact the front desk`, `breakfast-cutoff-explanation[\s\S]+?text-amber-700`) flipped to the pill tripwires (`data-testid="breakfast-cutoff-pill"`, `Ordering closed due to cut-off`, `inline-flex items-center gap-1 rounded-full bg-amber-100 px-2.5 py-1 text-xs font-semibold text-amber-800`, `<span[\s\S]+?data-testid="breakfast-cutoff-pill"`). Negative-space pins added for the removed pre-MBUX-17 sub-line + reassure line + standalone `"Ordering closed"` title. The pre-MBUX-17 standalone "the existing `Skipping a day is fine` line is still rendered" assertion was REMOVED (replaced by the negative-space pin). The unlocked-state `Order by ${formatStayDate(date)}` tripwire stays. `tests/api/mbux-05-step-2-addons.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-17 `expect(bookingPageSrc).toMatch(/Skipping a day is fine/)` assertion in the "empty option is short, with the explanation stated once above the grid" test was REPLACED by a negative-space `expect(...).not.toMatch(/Skipping a day is fine/)` test ("the pre-MBUX-17 reassurance line is REMOVED — MBUX-17, decision #272 drops it because the default `No breakfast` option already implies it"). The "skip explanation appears ONCE, outside the dates map" count assertion was REMOVED (the line it counted is gone). `tests/api/mbux-12-breakfast-menu-grid.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-17 "the skip-a-day explanation below the strip has mt-5 (was mt-4 pre-MBUX-12)" assertion was REPLACED by the negative-space counterpart ("the pre-MBUX-17 skip-a-day explanation below the strip is REMOVED (MBUX-17, decision #272)"), which pins both the literal copy absence AND the absence of the `mt-5 text-xs text-gray-600` paragraph wrapper that used to wrap it. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2399 guest-app API tests pass + 206 skipped (no new failures, no flakes); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline (MBUX-17 adds 0 audit errors — the negative-space pins use the same source-text regex pattern as the positive ones they replace). **Rejected. (a) Just keep the pre-MBUX-17 two-line stack and move it tighter together** — does not solve the duplication the owner flagged (the cut-off TIME would still appear in both the help text and the sub-line), and adds vertical pixels instead of removing them. **(b) Replace the per-date stack with an `<details>` disclosure on the locked date** — adds a tap affordance for content the pill already carries inline; the pill is one line, the disclosure is two; mobile-first principle is fewer affordances per screen, not more. **(c) Move the help text into the pill** (combine the policy explanation + the per-date locked marker into one element) — couples a section-level invariant (the cut-off policy) with a per-date state (this specific date is locked); the help text appears on a `dates.some(...)` predicate, the pill appears on a `cutoff.isLocked` predicate — merging them would either render the help text 5 times for a 5-night stay, or hide it behind a non-locked date, neither of which is what the owner asked for. **(d) Use a tooltip / popover on hover for the cut-off explanation** — phone-only surface, no hover; the inline pill is the right mobile idiom. **(e) Animate the pill on mount** (`framer-motion` scale-up + amber flash) — would add a `useReducedMotion` guard + a learning curve for what is a static status marker; the user doesn't need to be told "this date is locked", they need to scan and skip; animation is visual noise. **(f) Add a per-date icon (Lucide `Lock`) inside the pill** — would add visual weight to a marker that's supposed to read as compact; the existing `Tight — at the cap` / `Doesn't fit your group` pills in the same file are text-only, so the icon would make the cut-off indicator read as a different vocabulary. **(g) Remove the mbux-16 top-of-section help text too** — would lose the single source for the cut-off TIME (a guest who tapped the first locked date and scrolled back up to ask "when does the cut-off actually fire?" would have nothing to read); the help text is the policy anchor, the pill is the per-date state, keeping them separate is the right separation. **(h) Re-add the "Skipping a day is fine" reassurance line in a more compact form (a single line of `text-[11px]` next to the breakfast heading)** — the default `No breakfast` option already communicates the same thing; a smaller version is still redundant mobile real estate; the right answer is removal, not miniaturization. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#272, this entry; #269 MBUX-16 entry references updated to point at #272 for the per-date sub-line tripwire destinations); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-17 as shipped + status paragraph adds "MBUX-17 closed the MBUX-16 cut-off duplication on 2026-10-16"); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (the breakfast section's locked-date branch now renders the pill + the removed reassure line is gone + stale MBUX-16 rationale comments refocused to point at MBUX-17); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` (file header expanded to document MBUX-17; tripwires refocused onto the pill + negative-space pins for the removed sub-line / reassure line / standalone title); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-05-step-2-addons.test.ts` (the pre-MBUX-17 reassurance-line assertions refocused onto the negative-space pin); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-12-breakfast-menu-grid.test.ts` (the pre-MBUX-17 mt-5 explanation assertion refocused onto the negative-space pin); `shared/VERSION.ts` (`"2.16.2"` → `"2.16.3"`).

| 273 | **Cut-off pill copy shortened to a 2-word short-form label that fits one line on every viewport (MBUX-17.1, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.16.3` → `2.16.4`):** the MBUX-17 (decision #272) cut-off pill shipped reading "Ordering closed due to cut-off" (4 words, 28 chars). On a 375px phone the pill wrapped to two lines — "Ordering closed due to cut-/off" — which read as a broken label rather than a status marker. Owner flagged this with a screenshot: *"maybe we can reword it since the pill is too long for mobile"*. **Decisions. (1) New pill copy: "Past cut-off".** 11 chars, 2 words, fits on one line on every viewport the booking flow renders at (320 / 375 / 390 / 430 / tablet / desktop). Reads as a state, not a sentence — `"Last cut-off"` + amber + rounded-full pill + the in-file `text-xs font-semibold text-amber-800` accent all read as the same status-badge vocabulary the existing "Tight — at the cap" / "Doesn't fit your group" / "Fits your group" pills use. **(2) `whitespace-nowrap` added to the pill's className as a belt-and-braces guard.** The CSS guard means a future copy edit cannot reintroduce the wrap — even if a future change re-adds a longer phrase, the pill will overflow the right side of the date row rather than wrap mid-word. The tripwire pins this className shape in the test (the `whitespace-nowrap` token sits between `gap-1` and `rounded-full` in the className string, so a future regression that drops it will fail the test). **(3) The pill stays in the right-aligned column of the date header.** MBUX-17's layout decision (one line, right-aligned, mirroring the unlocked `Order by Oct 24` line's vertical rhythm) is unchanged — MBUX-17.1 only shortens the copy, not the placement or the visual treatment. **(4) The top-of-section help text is unchanged** — it already names the cut-off TIME (the missing piece the pill does NOT carry), and the help text is the single source for that. The pill is the per-date state marker; the help text is the policy anchor. They remain separate by design. **(5) `fix:` PATCH bump, not `refactor:` PATCH or `feat:` MINOR.** Same shape as HCT-01 (decision #270) and UX-DESK-01 (decision #271) — a copy tightening of an already-shipped MBUX surface, no behaviour change beyond "the copy is shorter", no new API, no new component, no new test surface beyond the existing MBUX-16 tripwires being refocused. Owner flagged the regression on a screenshot, which is the textbook `fix:` shape. **(6) Decision number, not MBUX-17 amendment.** The change ships as a separate decision entry (not an in-place edit of #272) for the same reason HCT-01 (#270) is its own decision rather than an amendment to the IFH-01 entry: a copy change on a shipped surface is a discrete commit + a discrete decision record + a discrete ROADMAP header line. The decision lives in the 270s-numbered slot because it's a copy-tightening iteration on the MBUX-17 pill, not a new Phase 15 mobile-UX slice. **Collateral.** `tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-17.1 "the locked-state pill reads exactly `Ordering closed due to cut-off`" assertion (which MBUX-17 added) is FLIPPED to two new assertions: (a) "the locked-state pill reads exactly `Past cut-off`" (`/Past cut-off/` substring match on `bookingPageSrc`); (b) "the pre-MBUX-17.1 `Ordering closed due to cut-off` copy is removed" (negative-space substring match). The pre-MBUX-17.1 "the locked-state pill uses the in-file pill vocabulary (rounded-full + amber-100 + amber-800 + inline-flex items-center gap-1 + px-2.5 py-1 text-xs font-semibold)" assertion is REPLACED by the MBUX-17.1 version that ALSO pins the `whitespace-nowrap` token in its expected position — the regex matches the full ordered className shape including the whitespace-nowrap guard, so a future regression that drops the guard fails the test. The `<span[\s\S]+?data-testid="breakfast-cutoff-pill"` testid-presence assertion is unchanged (the pill element is still a `<span>`, only its copy + className changed). **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2400 guest-app API tests pass + 206 skipped (no new failures); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline (MBUX-17.1 adds 0 audit errors). **Rejected. (a) Just shorten to "Closed"** — too vague; a guest who reads only "Closed" doesn't learn WHY (could be the hotel sold out, could be a system error, could be the cut-off). "Past cut-off" carries the reason AND is short. **(b) "Cut-off missed"** — sounds like a user error ("you missed it, sorry"); the cut-off is an operational policy, not a guest obligation. **(c) "Cut-off expired"** — the existing booking flow already uses "expired" for the soft-hold (`HoldCountdownBanner.tsx`'s expired-state copy) so reusing "expired" for the cut-off would muddle two different timeout semantics (the hold expires at booking-confirm time; the cut-off fires N hours before breakfast service). "Past cut-off" is a fresh vocabulary that does not collide. **(d) Keep the 4-word copy and add `whitespace-nowrap` only** — would let the pill overflow the right side of the date row on a 375px phone, pushing the date label into a narrower column. Owner asked to reword; the right answer is shorter copy, not a longer nowrap'd pill that still takes too much horizontal space. **(e) Move the pill below the date label instead of right-aligning it** — would stack the date card into two rows on every locked date, which is the pre-MBUX-17 MBUX-16 two-line shape owner already rejected. The right-aligned column is the right placement. **(f) Just remove the pill entirely and rely on the disabled dropdown to signal locked** — the disabled dropdown is a state attribute (`disabled` on the `<select>`); screen readers announce it, but sighted guests have to scan the entire `<select>` element to notice it's disabled. The pill is the sighted status marker. **(g) Animate the pill to fade in once the cut-off fires (a `useReducedMotion`-aware amber flash)** — would add motion to a static status marker; the user doesn't need to be told "this date is locked", they need to scan and skip. Visual noise. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#273, this entry; #272 MBUX-17 entry referenced for the pill SHAPE that MBUX-17.1 shortens); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-17.1 as shipped + status paragraph adds "MBUX-17.1 closed the MBUX-17 pill wrap on 2026-10-16"); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (the breakfast section's locked-date branch now renders the 2-word copy + carries `whitespace-nowrap` on the pill's className + the file-header + per-date rationale comments refocused to point at both MBUX-17 and MBUX-17.1); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` (the pre-MBUX-17.1 4-word copy assertion flipped to the 2-word copy assertion + negative-space pin for the removed 4-word copy + the className-shape assertion extended to pin `whitespace-nowrap`); `shared/VERSION.ts` (`"2.16.3"` → `"2.16.4"`).

| 274 | **Breakfast cut-off help text enhanced into a guest-facing two-sentence action statement (MBUX-17.2, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.16.4` → `2.16.5`):** the pre-MBUX-17.2 MBUX-16 (#269) help text on Step 2 read "Breakfast has a per-day cut-off (8:00 PM the day before) — when it passes, that day's menu locks but the rest of the stay is still editable." It was informative about the policy AND about the per-date lock scope, but it did not tell the guest what they COULD or COULD NOT do once the cut-off fired. Owner asked: *"kindly enhance"* the help text into something with explicit guest-actionable consequences. **Decisions. (1) The first sentence is preserved verbatim.** "Breakfast has a per-day cut-off ({formatCutoffTime(cutoffTime)} the day before)." carries the cut-off TIME (interpolated via `formatCutoffTime` so the rendered time always matches the admin-configured value — owner explicitly asked the time be dynamic against `settings/hotelConfig.dining.breakfast.cutoffTime`) AND the "per-day" prefix that makes the cut-off shape clear (it is NOT a global lock; only that specific service date is affected). **(2) A second sentence is ADDED that turns the pre-MBUX-17.2 em-dash hedge into a guest-facing ACTION: "If you booked beyond the cut-off time, you may not be able to order — but you can still order breakfast for other days."** The em-dash carries the contrast ("can't for this date" → "but can for other dates") more cheaply than the pre-MBUX-17.2 subordinate clause did. The "for other days" half is critical — without it a guest who booked the same room past the cut-off might think the WHOLE booking was locked out of breakfast, when actually only the locked-date slots are unavailable. **(3) The copy is light-edited from the owner's verbatim words.** Owner wrote: "If you booked a room beyonf cut-off time, you may not be able to order but you may still order for other day's breakfast." MBUX-17.2 fixes three issues from the verbatim: typos ("beyonf" → "beyond", "other day's" → "other days'"), a missing modal verb ("you may still order" → "you can still order" reads as more actionable; the pre-MBUX-17.2 "may" copy did not strongly assert the option), and the comma splice between the consequence and the contrast is replaced with an em-dash to match the file's existing em-dash usage in the "first-sentence policy + em-dash hedge" shape. The rendered copy is:

  > Breakfast has a per-day cut-off ({time} the day before). If you booked beyond the cut-off time, you may not be able to order — but you can still order breakfast for other days.

**(4) The pill (`Past cut-off`, per MBUX-17.1 decision #273) is unchanged.** MBUX-17.2 is help-text only — the pill is the per-date state marker, the help text is the policy anchor, and MBUX-17.2 strengthens the help text without touching the per-date marker. The two stay separate by design. **(5) The dynamic-time interpolation is preserved.** The `formatCutoffTime(breakfastConfig.cutoffTime || "20:00")` interpolation was already in place from MBUX-16; MBUX-17.2 carries it forward into the new first sentence verbatim. The `formatCutoffTime` helper (defined at `guest-app/src/utils/formatCutoffTime.ts`, per MBUX-16 decision #269) is the only place that knows the display rule, so a future locale change or 24h preference is still a one-line edit. Owner explicitly asked: *"Make sure time is dynamic too depending on the set cut-off time in the admin settings"* — already true via the existing `formatCutoffTime` interpolation, but MBUX-17.2's negative-space pins in the tripwire make the dynamic-time contract explicit so a future refactor that hardcodes "8:00 PM" surfaces at this layer. **(6) `fix:` PATCH bump, not `refactor:` PATCH or `feat:` MINOR.** Same shape as HCT-01 (#270), UX-DESK-01 (#271), and MBUX-17.1 (#273) — a copy tightening + enhancement of an already-shipped MBUX surface; no new API, no new component, no new test surface beyond the existing MBUX-16 tripwires being refocused. Owner flagged the request as "kindly enhance", which is the textbook `fix:` shape for "improve an existing surface". The decision lives in the 270s-numbered slot because it's a copy enhancement on the MBUX-16/17 help-text surface, not a new Phase 15 mobile-UX slice. **Collateral.** `tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` updated — the pre-MBUX-17.2 "the help text names the cut-off time + the 'when it passes, that day's menu locks' contract + 'the rest of the stay is still editable'" assertion (which MBUX-16 added) is REPLACED by two MBUX-17.2 assertions: (a) "the help text names the cut-off time (interpolated via `formatCutoffTime`) + the 'per-day cut-off' shape contract" (`/per-day cut-off/` positive pin + `/formatCutoffTime\(breakfastConfig\.cutoffTime \|\| "20:00"\)/` positive pin that asserts the dynamic-time interpolation explicitly — owner asked the time be dynamic against the admin set value, and the explicit pin makes that contract non-driftable); (b) "the help text second sentence (MBUX-17.2) names the guest-actionable consequence: 'beyond the cut-off time, you may not be able to order — but you can still order breakfast for other days'" (`/If you booked beyond the cut-off time/`, `/you may not be able to order/`, `/you can still order breakfast for other days/` positive pins). A third MBUX-17.2 negative-space pin is added: "the pre-MBUX-17.2 hedge copy ('that day's menu locks' + 'the rest of the stay is still editable') is removed — replaced by the MBUX-17.2 guest-facing action sentence" (negative pins for both pre-MBUX-17.2 phrases). The pre-MBUX-17.2 `data-testid="breakfast-cutoff-help"` testid-presence assertion is unchanged (the help-text element is still a `<p>`, only its copy changed). The `dates.some(c => c.isLocked)` gating assertion is unchanged (the help text still renders only when at least one date is locked). **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 2402 guest-app API tests pass + 206 skipped (no new failures); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline (MBUX-17.2 adds 0 audit errors). **Rejected. (a) Just shorten the pre-MBUX-17.2 help text** — owner asked to "kindly enhance", not shorten. The hedge language was the right shape for the original MBUX-16, but it was framed as a passive warning rather than a guest-actionable consequence; the MBUX-17.2 second sentence addresses that. **(b) Move the help text into the pill** (combine the policy + the per-date locked marker into one element) — couples a section-level invariant (the cut-off policy) with a per-date state (this specific date is locked); the help text appears on a `dates.some(...)` predicate, the pill appears on a `cutoff.isLocked` predicate; merging them would either render the help text 5 times for a 5-night stay, or hide it behind a non-locked date, neither of which is what owner asked for. **(c) Drop the per-day prefix** — would lose the "not a global lock" signal that owner implicitly relies on (the original MBUX-16 "rest of the stay is still editable" hedge). The pre-MBUX-17.2 second sentence is the more direct way to convey the same fact; the prefix stays as the policy anchor. **(d) Use an `<details>` disclosure for the help text** — adds a tap affordance for a sentence a guest needs to read BEFORE they decide what to do on the locked date; collapsing it would hide the policy behind an icon on mobile. **(e) Replace "you may not be able to order" with "you can't order"** — overpromises the lock; the meal selector IS disabled but the pre-MBUX-17.2 "may not be able to" framing leaves room for the staff-override path that ships on the locked-date surface (per the admin `overrideCutoff` flag on `BreakfastSelectionEntry`, decision #232). The "may not be able to" copy is consistent with the staff-override escape hatch. **(f) Add a "Contact the front desk" CTA to the second sentence** — would couple the help text to a phone-number or contact-form route; the help text is policy-only copy, the contact CTA is a separate concern that lives on its own surface (the existing footer / static-pages `/contact` already carries it). The "you can still order breakfast for other days" half is the actionable consequence; the contact path is one tap away in the global footer. **(g) Re-render the first sentence as a `<strong>` for emphasis** — would change the visual weight of the policy anchor relative to the actionable consequence; the help text is one styled block (`text-xs text-amber-700`), no internal hierarchy. The contrast between the two sentences carries the meaning without needing a typographic split. **(h) Hardcode "8:00 PM" instead of interpolating from `formatCutoffTime`** — would break the dynamic-time contract owner explicitly asked for ("Make sure time is dynamic too depending on the set cut-off time in the admin settings"); the existing `formatCutoffTime(breakfastConfig.cutoffTime || "20:00")` interpolation is the right shape, and the new positive pin in the tripwire makes that contract explicit. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#274, this entry; #269 MBUX-16 entry referenced for the pre-MBUX-17.2 hedge copy that MBUX-17.2 replaces; #272 MBUX-17 + #273 MBUX-17.1 entries unchanged on the pill side); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-17.2 as shipped + status paragraph adds "MBUX-17.2 closed the MBUX-16/17 help-text enhancement on 2026-10-16"); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (the breakfast section's help-text branch now renders the two-sentence MBUX-17.2 copy + the rationale comment refocused to point at MBUX-17.2); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-16-breakfast-cutoff-explanation.test.ts` (the pre-MBUX-17.2 hedge assertion replaced by the MBUX-17.2 positive pins for the new first + second sentences + the dynamic-time interpolation + the negative-space pins for the removed pre-MBUX-17.2 hedge phrases); `shared/VERSION.ts` (`"2.16.4"` → `"2.16.5"`).

| 275 | **Edit pencil on the mobile StaySummaryStrip opens a bottom sheet instead of scrolling to the aside (MBUX-18, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `feat:` MINOR bump `2.16.5` → `2.17.0`):** the MBUX-11 (#264) Edit pencil shipped reading `Edit` and smooth-scrolling to the existing `<aside id="booking-stay-details-aside">` + briefly ringing it for 2s — the rationale comment at `StaySummaryStrip.tsx:23-27` literally said *"The Edit button does NOT open a modal — it reuses the existing in-page picker"*. On phone the aside sits BELOW the room-card list, so a long room list meant a long scroll to reach the picker, and the guest lost the room-card context for the few seconds they fiddled with dates. The MBUX-11 spec already anticipated the alternative: *"Edit opens the existing range calendar and occupancy controls in a mobile-appropriate expandable area or sheet"* (BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md:137). Owner asked: *"for the step 2 of the booking page, when user hits edit, instead of scrolling down to the bottom, is it possible to open a modal containing the stay details that can be edited?"* The owner named "modal" generically — the spec's "expandable area or sheet" wording is the contract, and a mobile bottom sheet is the Agoda / Booking.com idiom that satisfies both. MBUX-18 ships the sheet. **Decisions. (1) New component `EditStayDetailsSheet` at `guest-app/src/components/EditStayDetailsSheet.tsx` — a mobile-only bottom sheet anchored to the bottom edge with `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 max-h-[calc(100dvh-4rem)] overflow-y-auto overscroll-contain rounded-t-card`.** The shape mirrors the in-picker calendar's mobile sheet at `DateRangePicker.tsx:418` (same primitive the DR-03 mobile sheet uses, per BOOKING-FLOW.md:74), so a guest who has used the date picker inside the sheet is on the same visual vocabulary as a guest who opened the sheet. Animates in via the shared `slideInBottom` variant from `@spark-inn/shared/animations.ts` (FRONTEND.md §Modals: *"Mobile: `slideInBottom` variant (slides up from bottom like a sheet)."*). The backdrop is a separate `motion.div` with `bg-gray-950/50 backdrop-blur-sm`; backdrop click closes the sheet via `onClick={onClose}`; the inner panel `event.stopPropagation()`s the click so clicking inside the panel does not dismiss. **The sheet is `lg:hidden`-guarded on the backdrop wrapper, so a future refactor that adds a `<EditStayDetailsSheet />` call site on desktop would structurally fail to render the sheet there** (the strip itself is `lg:hidden`, so the Edit pencil never mounts on desktop, and the sheet is doubly locked against a desktop-open). **(2) The sheet renders the SAME three controls the sidebar shows: `<DateRangePicker>` + Adults stepper + Children stepper.** The cart and the info card stay on the sidebar only — they're read-only and would clutter the sheet. The sheet threads every state + handler the sidebar uses (`checkIn`, `checkOut`, `maxStayNights`, `onRangeChange`, `onCheckInChange`, `onCheckOutChange`, `adults`, `maxGuestCapacity`, `onAdultsChange`, `numChildren`, `onChildrenChange`) so the live URL-param writes (`updateDateParams`, `updateAdults`, `updateChildren`) don't fork into a separate code path. The same `formatCutoffTime` + `describeMaxStayNights` + `resolveRangeSelection` + `resolveMaxStayNights` helpers from MSL-01 + DR-03 + DR-04 stay in force — the normalization, horizon, stay-length cap, and per-night cap rules are unchanged. **A separate save step would be a regression** — the state writes are live, Done is just a close. **(3) The Done button is full-width, brand-orange, and lives in the thumb zone.** Per DR-03 (BOOKING-FLOW.md:74): *"phones get an explicit Done affordance — the sheet has no click-outside affordance, and the header X is small.
