Full width because it is the primary action and sits in the thumb zone at the bottom of the sheet."* The button's `style={{ backgroundColor: config.colors.primary }}` keeps the hard rule from CLAUDE.md — *"All primary CTAs = `config.colors.primary`. Use the `primary` Tailwind token, never hardcoded hex values"*. The X close is labelled (`aria-label="Close"`, the same convention `Modal.tsx` uses), so a screen-reader user can dismiss without a sighted affordance. **(4) Dialog semantics: `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal="true"` + `aria-labelledby="edit-stay-details-title"`.** The `aria-labelledby` points at the heading id (`"Edit stay details"`), per FRONTEND.md §Accessibility rule 6: *"Modals and drawers trap focus while open; focus returns to trigger on close"*. A future refactor that drops the `role="dialog"` would lose the screen-reader announcement of the sheet's purpose. **(5) The `StaySummaryStrip` becomes a dumb trigger.** The pre-MBUX-18 `targetId?: string` prop + `scrollIntoView` + `ring-2 ring-primary ring-offset-2` + `setTimeout(..., 2000)` dance is RETIRED. The new `onEdit?: () => void` prop carries the trigger; the parent (`BookingPage`) owns the sheet's open state via a top-of-component `useState(false)` (NOT inside the `if (isSelectRoomStep)` branch — React 19 hooks must run in the same order every render). The optional-chain `onEdit?.()` in the strip's handler means a parent that forgets to wire the prop is a silent no-op rather than a crash — the worst case is a developer-visible regression (the strip tap does nothing), not a guest-visible dead control. The strip's `handleEdit` body now reads `function handleEdit(): void { onEdit?.(); }` — five lines instead of nine, and the whole pre-MBUX-18 scroll/restore pattern is gone. **(6) `BookingPage` carries the sheet state + the props wire.** A new `useState` declaration right after the existing `IMT-01 holdId` useState at `BookingPage.tsx:402-408`; the `StaySummaryStrip` call site gains an `onEdit={() => setIsEditStayDetailsOpen(true)}` callback; the new `<EditStayDetailsSheet />` render sits directly below the strip in the same `<section>` block. The pre-MBUX-18 comment at `BookingPage.tsx:4147-4152` ("the `id` is the scroll target for the StaySummaryStrip's Edit button on mobile") is extended to note that the Edit pencil no longer scrolls here — the `id` + `scroll-mt-32` remain as a defensive fallback for the BookingStickyFooter's URL-driven deep-link scroll + accessibility tools. A future refactor that drops the id would lose that contract, so the MBUX-11 tripwire still asserts `aside#booking-stay-details-aside` and the `scroll-mt-*` class. **(7) The pre-MBUX-18 scroll/restore dance on the strip is pin-inverted to negative space.** The MBUX-11 tripwire (`tests/api/mbux-11-stay-summary-strip.test.ts`) is updated: the `targetId?: string` + `targetId = "booking-stay-details-aside"` default pins are RETIRED (the prop is gone); the `el.scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" })` + `el.classList.add("ring-2"...)` + `window.setTimeout(...)` + `el.classList.remove("ring-2"...)` pins are REPLACED by negative-space pins — `not.toMatch(/scrollIntoView/)`, `not.toMatch(/classList\.add/)`, `not.toMatch(/window\.setTimeout/)` — so a future refactor that quietly reintroduces the scroll behaviour fails the test. The pre-MBUX-18 positive pin for `el.scrollIntoView` is REPLACED by a positive pin for `function handleEdit[\s\S]*?onEdit\?\.\(\)` so the new shape is asserted positively AND negatively. **This is the right pattern for a behaviour flip on a shipped surface**: keep the tripwire, invert the assertions to negative space, add a new companion tripwire for the new shape. **(8) New source-text regression net `tests/api/mbux-18-edit-stay-details-sheet.test.ts` (23 guards) — the `EditStayDetailsSheet` file exists with a named export; the props contract pins every state + handler the sidebar uses (`open`, `onClose`, `checkIn`, `checkOut`, `maxStayNights`, `onRangeChange`, `onCheckInChange`, `onCheckOutChange`, `adults`, `maxGuestCapacity`, `onAdultsChange`, `numChildren`, `onChildrenChange`); the bottom-sheet primitive pins (`fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0` + `max-h-[calc(100dvh-4rem)]` + `overscroll-contain` + `rounded-t-card`) so a future refactor that uses a centered modal would fail; the `slideInBottom` import from `@spark-inn/shared` so the animation primitive is the shared one (a future refactor that hand-rolls a `y: 32` variant would fail); the `lg:hidden` guard on the backdrop wrapper so a desktop-open is structurally prevented; the `role="dialog"` + `aria-modal="true"` + `aria-labelledby="edit-stay-details-title"` semantics; the close button's `aria-label="Close"` + `<X />` icon; the backdrop `onClick={onClose}` + the inner-panel `event.stopPropagation()` so clicking inside does not dismiss; the embedded `<DateRangePicker>` carries the same props surface as the sidebar (so the live URL-param writes match); the Adults stepper's min-1 / max-`maxGuestCapacity` clamps match the sidebar's (per CHD-05 + CHD-11.5); the Children stepper's min-0 / max-10 clamps match the sidebar's (per CHD-11.2); the Done button is full-width + brand-primary + `onClick={onClose}` (close-only, no save); the three `data-testid` hooks (`edit-stay-details-backdrop` / `-close` / `-done`) are exposed for future behaviour tests; the BookingPage integration pins the import + the `useState(false)` declaration + the `<EditStayDetailsSheet>` render with all the picker / stepper props + the StaySummaryStrip's `onEdit` callback that opens the sheet. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 3262 guest-app API tests pass + 222 skipped + 1 failed (the pre-existing `br-06-refunds-extension` baseline failure, unchanged on `dev`); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline (MBUX-18 adds 0). **Rejected. (a) Center modal everywhere (mobile + desktop)** — works on desktop but on phones the calendar feels cramped and the iOS keyboard fights the backdrop; the bottom sheet is the native mobile idiom. Desktop keeps the existing sidebar-as-edit-surface pattern, so no desktop change is needed (the strip is `lg:hidden` and the sheet is `lg:hidden`-guarded). **(b) Bottom sheet on mobile + centered modal on desktop** — overkill. The sheet is mobile-only by construction; on desktop the strip doesn't render, so the user uses the sidebar directly without an Edit affordance. The dual-mode surface would add a desktop modal the user never sees, with no benefit. **(c) Just remove the Edit pencil entirely** — loses the affordance on a screen where the dates/occupancy controls sit below a long room list; the pencil is the single tap that brings the controls into view. Removing it would force the guest to scroll every time they want to change a date. **(d) Inline expand below the strip instead of a sheet** — collapses the room-card list below the strip rather than overlaying it; the sheet preserves the room-card list state (the guest can dismiss and re-open) and keeps the controls in the same place across opens. The inline expand would push the room cards down by the expanded height on every open, which feels jarring on a phone. **(e) Reuse the existing `Modal` component (`guest-app/src/components/Modal.tsx`)** — that component renders a centered modal (`max-w-2xl` + `rounded-card-lg` + `items-center justify-center`), which is the wrong shape for a mobile phone sheet; reusing it would force the centered styling + lose the bottom-sheet primitive the in-picker calendar already uses. The new component is the right shape, not a wrapper. **(f) Move the sheet state into the strip** — would couple the strip's dumb-trigger contract to a stateful component; the pre-MBUX-18 strip was a presentation component with no internal state, and that's the right shape for the strip (it's reusable on Step 1, Step 2, etc., in any future surface that needs a compact stay summary). The state belongs in the parent. **(g) Animate the strip itself when the sheet opens** — adds motion to a static UI element without a state change on the strip; the sheet's own animation is the right place. **(h) Use a `<details>` disclosure inline instead of a sheet** — works on desktop but loses the mobile bottom-sheet idiom; `<details>` is a browser-native expand-in-place primitive, not a modal sheet. **(i) Auto-open the sheet on first visit (deep-link friendly)** — adds a state-driven open that the user didn't ask for; the guest's first action is to read the room list, not to fiddle with dates. The sheet opens only on an explicit Edit tap. **(j) Show the existing cart inside the sheet** — the cart is read-only on Step 1 (it's a summary of room selections, not a date/occupancy control); putting it in the sheet would clutter a 6.7" phone and duplicate the sidebar's surface. **(k) Show the existing info card inside the sheet** — same reasoning; the info card is contextual help for the date picker, and the sheet already carries the date picker. The header line ("Edit stay details") is the contextual help for the sheet itself. **(l) Move the Done button's colour to a Tailwind class instead of `style={{ backgroundColor: config.colors.primary }}`** — the Tailwind `bg-primary` utility compiles to the same hex at build time, but the `style` attribute is the explicit shape the CLAUDE.md hard rule mandates for brand-primary CTAs (the same shape the Done button in `DateRangePicker.tsx:502` and the `PrimaryButton` component use); a future refactor that switches to `bg-primary` would still work today but the existing shape is the canonical one and the test pins it. **(m) Add a top-of-sheet "Edit stay details" sticky header** — the sheet already has a header row with the X close; a sticky header on top of that would add a second header row on a 6.7" phone and waste ~56px of vertical space. The existing single-header shape is sufficient. **(n) Pull the sheet into a custom hook (`useEditStayDetailsSheet`)** — over-engineering for a single boolean state + an onEdit callback; the hook would carry the same `[open, setOpen]` state and add an indirection that the parent can already do in two lines. **(o) Also expose Edit on the BookingReviewAside on Steps 2/3/4** — explicitly rejected by owner (option 3 of the three-scope questionnaire at the start of this slice: *"Step 1 + sheet only on mobile (no desktop change)"*). Steps 2/3/4's sidebar stays read-only for now; a future slice can add the same affordance there if the user asks. **(p) Reuse the `Modal` component's `scaleIn` variant on a tablet breakpoint (`sm:`..`lg:`)** — the strip is `lg:hidden`, so tablets render the strip, not the sidebar; a tablet breakpoint in the sheet would render a modal for a tablet-only slice, which is asymmetric UX for no clear semantic reason. The mobile-only shape is honest. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#275, this entry; #264 MBUX-11 entry referenced for the pre-MBUX-18 scroll-to-aside behaviour that MBUX-18 retired); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-18 as shipped + status paragraph adds "MBUX-18 closed the Step 1 mobile-UX review follow-up (Edit affordance) on 2026-10-16" + §Step 1 "Compact stay summary" expanded with the MBUX-18 sheet contract); `guest-app/src/components/EditStayDetailsSheet.tsx` (new file, 198 lines — the mobile bottom sheet); `guest-app/src/components/StaySummaryStrip.tsx` (header comment flipped to point at MBUX-18; `targetId?: string` prop removed; `onEdit?: () => void` prop added; `handleEdit` body simplified to `onEdit?.()`); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (the import list gains `EditStayDetailsSheet`; the `IMT-01 holdId` useState is joined by a new `isEditStayDetailsOpen` useState(false); the `<StaySummaryStrip />` call site gains `onEdit={() => setIsEditStayDetailsOpen(true)}`; a new `<EditStayDetailsSheet />` render sits directly below the strip in the same `<section>`; the aside's per-MBUX-11 comment at line 4147 is extended to note that the Edit pencil no longer scrolls here — the `id` + `scroll-mt-32` remain as a defensive deep-link fallback); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-11-stay-summary-strip.test.ts` (the `targetId?: string` + `scrollIntoView` + `ring-2` + `setTimeout` positive pins are RETIRED; the scroll/ring/setTimeout pattern is REPLACED by negative-space pins (`not.toMatch(/scrollIntoView/)` etc.); the new `onEdit?:` + `onEdit?.()` shape is asserted positively; the `<StaySummaryStrip />` props contract is extended to assert the `onEdit` callback wired to `setIsEditStayDetailsOpen(true)`); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-18-edit-stay-details-sheet.test.ts` (new file, 23 guards — the full contract for the new sheet); `shared/VERSION.ts` (`"2.16.5"` → `"2.17.0"`).

| 276 | **Hide the BookingPage aside's three editable blocks (`<DateRangePicker>` + Adults/Children steppers) on mobile (MBUX-19, owner-approved 2026-10-16; `fix:` PATCH bump `2.17.0` → `2.17.1`):** the MBUX-18 (#275) sheet made the strip's Edit pencil the canonical mobile edit surface. But the BookingPage `<aside id="booking-stay-details-aside">` ALSO rendered the same `<DateRangePicker>` + Adults/Children steppers inline, just below the strip on mobile (`order-1` on the parent `<section>` puts the aside ABOVE the room cards). The pre-MBUX-19 mobile shape was two editable surfaces stacked: the strip at the top (Edit pencil → sheet) + the aside just below it (inline controls). The BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md:139 rule explicitly forbids that: *"The page does not repeat the same stay summary in both a header card and sidebar card."* The MBUX-18 entry (`#275`) flagged the duplication but did not address it ("The cart and the info card stay on the sidebar only — they're read-only and would clutter the sheet") because the owner had not yet asked. Owner asked: *"can we then hide the one at the bottom?"* The natural follow-up to MBUX-18. MBUX-19 hides the aside's three editable blocks on mobile. **Decisions. (1) The three editable blocks (`<DateRangePicker>` + Adults `<label>` stepper + Children `<label>` stepper) are wrapped in a single `<div className="hidden lg:contents">` inside the aside.** On `< lg:` the wrapper is `display: none`, which removes all three blocks together — one CSS rule, no JSX duplication. On `lg:` the wrapper dissolves into the parent's `space-y-6` stacking context. The cart (`<RoomCartLines>`) and the info card (`<Info size={18} className="mt-0.5 shrink-0 text-primary" />`) stay OUTSIDE the wrapper as siblings — they're read-only on Step 1 (the cart is a summary of room selections, the info card is static help text) and don't duplicate the sheet. The "Stay details" `<h2>` heading stays outside the wrapper too — it covers the cart + info card on both surfaces. **Why `hidden lg:contents`, NOT `hidden lg:block`**: `lg:block` would change the wrapper's `display` to `block`, which adds an extra anonymous box inside the parent's `space-y-6` stacking context — the picker would then be separated from the stepper below by a DOUBLE gap (parent's `space-y-6` counts the wrapper as a real child and adds 24px between it and the next sibling, then the next sibling has its own 24px before its children). `lg:contents` removes the wrapper entirely from the layout on `lg:`, so the three controls slot back into the parent's `space-y-6` stacking context without an extra wrapper box. On `< lg:` the wrapper is `display: none`, which removes all three blocks together. **The `display: contents` rule has one known accessibility caveat** (it removes the box from the accessibility tree on browsers that don't support the `aria-hidden` + `display: contents` combination) — this is irrelevant here because on `lg:` the wrapper has no semantic role anyway (it's just a CSS grouping helper), and on `< lg:` it's `display: none` so the accessibility tree skips it. The triple-edit-block visibility is a layout concern, not an a11y one. **(2) The aside's `id="booking-stay-details-aside"` + `scroll-mt-32` remain unchanged** — they're a defensive deep-link fallback for the BookingStickyFooter's URL-driven scroll + accessibility tools, exactly as the MBUX-18 #275 entry committed. MBUX-19 does not touch them. The MBUX-11 source-text tripwire (which still asserts `aside#booking-stay-details-aside` + `scroll-mt-*`) passes without modification. The MBUX-18 source-text tripwire (which asserts the `<EditStayDetailsSheet />` props wire + the StaySummaryStrip's `onEdit` callback) also passes — MBUX-19 doesn't touch the sheet wire either. **(3) No new component, no new test surface beyond the new MBUX-19 tripwire.** MBUX-19 is a single-attribute change to the aside's JSX + a single new source-text regression net. No new API, no new component, no new state, no new file beyond the tripwire. The MBUX-19 tripwire pins: the wrapper's exact opening tag `<div className="hidden lg:contents">` (a future refactor that adds an extra class fails the equality check); the three editable blocks INSIDE the wrapper (`<DateRangePicker>` + `checkIn={checkIn}` + `onRangeChange=` + `onCheckInChange=` + `onCheckOutChange=` for the picker, `>Adults<` + `Remove one adult` + `Add one adult` + `disabled={adults >= maxGuestCapacity}` for the Adults stepper, `Children (0–11)` + `Decrease children count` + `Increase children count` + `disabled={numChildren >= 10}` for the Children stepper); the cart + info card OUTSIDE the wrapper (`<RoomCartLines` + `<Info size={18} className="mt-0.5 shrink-0 text-primary"` rendered after the wrapper's closing `</div>`); the absence of `hidden lg:block` (which would break `space-y-6`); the rationale comment in the BookingPage source that references the BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md duplicate-summary rule + the `lg:contents` vs `lg:block` rationale; the unchanged `aside#booking-stay-details-aside` + `scroll-mt-*` defensive fallback. The tripwire uses a small bracket-walker helper (`sliceAsideEditableWrapper`) to anchor assertions on the wrapper's slice, so a future refactor that moves the three blocks out of the wrapper fails with a meaningful diff (not "the wrapper is missing somewhere in a 5800-line file"). **The slice construction is a brace-balanced walker** (not a regex) because the file uses JSX, and a regex would fail on the inner `<div>...</div>` stack inside the picker + stepper rows — the walker counts `<div ...>` opens vs `</div>` closes, with the `charAfter = " "` / `"\n"` / `"\t"` / `">"` guard so it doesn't false-positive on substrings like `<divide`. The walker is the right primitive for "find the matching close tag in JSX without a parser". **(4) `fix:` PATCH bump, not `refactor:` PATCH or `feat:` MINOR.** Same reasoning as HCT-01 (#270), UX-DESK-01 (#271), MBUX-17.1 (#273), MBUX-17.2 (#274) — a tightening/cleanup of an already-shipped MBUX surface; no new API, no new component beyond the new tripwire file (which is a test, not a surface). The user-visible behaviour on mobile changes (the aside becomes shorter, the picker + steppers are no longer visible inline), but the surface is the same shape (the heading + cart + info card stay). The `feat:` shape (heuristic) is for new components or new behaviour; MBUX-19 is a refinement of MBUX-18's surface, not a new surface. **Verification.** `tsc -p guest-app` clean; 3250 guest-app API tests pass + 222 skipped (no new failures — MBUX-19's 11 guards all pass); `docs:audit` unchanged at the 19-error baseline (MBUX-19 adds 0). **Rejected. (a) Hide the entire aside on mobile** — would also hide the cart (read-only, useful for confirming selections) + info card (static help, useful for first-time guests). MBUX-19 keeps both visible. **(b) Hide just the `<DateRangePicker>` and keep the steppers visible** — splits the edit surface: guest opens the sheet for dates, but Adults/Children stays inline on the aside. Worse UX (two surfaces to edit) and worse symmetry (one block hidden, two not). MBUX-19 hides all three blocks together via the wrapper. **(c) Wrap each block individually with `hidden lg:block`** — three wrappers for three blocks. Same `display: block` problem at each boundary (each wrapper adds an anonymous box to `space-y-6`). Single wrapper is the right primitive. **(d) Use `lg:flex` instead of `lg:contents`** — `lg:flex` changes the wrapper to `display: flex` on `lg:`, which would also break `space-y-6` (a flex container's children are laid out by the flex algorithm, not by the parent's `space-y-6` margin rule). The three controls would then stack with no spacing between them (because `space-y-6` only adds `margin-top` to flex children it considers siblings, not children of a flex container — Tailwind's `space-y-*` uses `:not([hidden]) ~ :not([hidden])` selectors that don't match flex children). **(e) Move the cart + info card INSIDE the wrapper** — the cart and info card are read-only; they should stay visible on mobile. The wrapper is the edit-block boundary, not the section boundary. **(f) Hide the heading on mobile** — the heading is the section anchor for the cart + info card; removing it would leave the cart looking orphaned. **(g) Hide the heading AND the wrapper on mobile** — same as (a), loses the cart + info card. **(h) Use `lg:grid` instead of `lg:contents`** — would force the three blocks into a grid layout on `lg:`, changing the desktop sidebar's vertical stack into a horizontal grid. The picker + steppers have no business being in a horizontal grid — each one needs its own row for the calendar + the +/- buttons. **(i) Move the editable blocks to a sibling component (`<StayDetailsControls>`)** — would deduplicate the block from the EditStayDetailsSheet's body, but adds a new component for a single use site. The DRY win is small (the wrapper is 100 lines, the sheet's picker + steppers are another 100 lines; sharing them requires a UI component with 6 props, not a 100-line helper). A new shared component is a bigger blast radius than MBUX-19 needs. **(j) Add an Edit pencil to the aside on mobile instead of hiding the picker** — would create a second Edit affordance (one in the strip, one in the aside) — the same duplication the BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md rule forbids. **(k) Move the strip's Edit pencil INTO the aside** — collapses the strip + aside into one surface. The strip is the mobile-first compact summary; the aside is the desktop sidebar. They have different purposes on different surfaces and should stay separate. **(l) Re-introduce the pre-MBUX-18 scroll-to-aside Edit behaviour on the strip and hide the wrapper for both mobile AND desktop** — would drop the MBUX-18 sheet from the surface entirely. The sheet is the right answer to "one-handed mobile edit"; re-introducing the scroll-to-aside would re-introduce the long-scroll problem on phone. **(m) Use CSS `@media (max-width: 1023px) { aside.editable { display: none } }` instead of Tailwind's `hidden lg:contents`** — adds a custom CSS rule for one element, breaking Tailwind's utility-first convention (FRONTEND.md §Tokens: *"Use Tailwind utilities for all styling — custom CSS only for animations + brand tokens"*). Tailwind's `hidden lg:contents` is the right primitive. **MDs.** `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md` (#276, this entry; #275 MBUX-18 entry referenced for the sheet wire that MBUX-19 leaves untouched + the BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md:139 duplicate-summary rule that MBUX-19 enforces; #264 MBUX-11 entry referenced for the defensive deep-link fallback that MBUX-19 preserves); `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (§Recently-shipped header line); `plan/features/BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md` (status line lists MBUX-19 as shipped + status paragraph adds "MBUX-19 closed the MBUX-18 duplicate-aside-edit-controls follow-up on 2026-10-16" + §Step 1 "Compact stay summary" expanded with the MBUX-19 aside-editing contract); `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (the aside's three editable blocks are wrapped in `<div className="hidden lg:contents">` at line 4203; the rationale comment block at lines 4161-4199 documents the MBUX-19 contract + the `lg:contents` vs `lg:block` rationale + the BOOKING-MOBILE-UX.md:139 rule reference); `guest-app/tests/api/mbux-19-hide-aside-edit-controls.test.ts` (new file, 11 guards — the wrapper's exact opening tag, the three editable blocks INSIDE, the cart + info card OUTSIDE, the absence of `lg:block`, the rationale comment, the unchanged `aside#booking-stay-details-aside` + `scroll-mt-*`); `shared/VERSION.ts` (`"2.17.0"` → `"2.17.1"`).

| 277 | **The server owns the breakfast price + name: the create path snapshots the LIVE catalog, never the request (OCT-C1, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the October 2026 audit found that `validateBreakfastSelections` checked only `mealLookup.has(entry.mealId)` and then persisted `entry.price` — the CLIENT's wire value. The wire schema explicitly accepted `price` and `mealName` from the request (`entryShape`, `z.number().finite().min(0)` + `z.string()`), so a guest could POST `price: 0` and book free breakfasts; the tampered value flowed into `breakfastTotal` → `totalPrice` → VAT → the folio → the receipt PDF → the confirmation email. The live catalog's price sat right there in `mealLookup` and was never consulted. **The fix sources BOTH the price and the name from the catalog meal.** `mealName` was equally client-controlled, so the receipt could be made to name a meal the hotel never offered. The manage path already re-read `serverPrice` from the catalog and wrote a price-mismatch audit entry (`breakfast-manage.ts:712`); the create path now matches it, so **the catalog price is the only price on every path**. The wire `price` + `mealName` became OPTIONAL and are accepted-and-ignored, so a cached guest bundle keeps working. The booking still keeps its snapshot forever (BR-03's snapshotting rule) — the catalog is re-read only at the moment the order is placed. **Verified behaviourally, not by source-text:** `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c1-breakfast-price-authority.test.ts` (8 guards) imports the pure validator and asserts that a client `price: 0` still yields 350, that an inflated price is ignored, that a client `mealName` is replaced by the catalog name, that a request omitting the price is filled in, and that the total is the sum of the CATALOG prices. 6 of the 8 fail against the pre-fix code. **Rejected:** re-pricing the booking later from the catalog (breaks the snapshot contract and would silently change a guest's price after they booked); trusting the client's price with a sanity cap (a cap is still the client's number); removing the `price` field from the wire schema (a breaking change for a cached guest bundle). **Files:** `guest-app/server/lib/breakfast-selections.ts` (the `entryShape` price/name → optional + the catalog-sourced snapshot in `validateBreakfastSelections`); `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c1-breakfast-price-authority.test.ts` (new).

| 278 | **The server owns the other-add-on snapshots: price, name, unit and cut-off all come from the live catalog (OCT-C2, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the same class of bug as #277, on the BR-12 other-add-on paths. The add op read `priceSnapshot: Math.max(0, Number(op.priceSnapshot ?? liveAddOn.price) || 0)` — the `??` fallback was DEAD CODE, because the client ALWAYS sends `priceSnapshot`. The guest's price won, was snapshotted permanently onto the booking (so a later "the catalog price changed after the order" check could not even detect it), and flowed into `otherAddOnTotal` → the balance. A guest could buy a ₱500 airport transfer for ₱1. The same op also preferred the client's `nameSnapshot` and `unitSnapshot`, and — most seriously — `cutoffHoursBeforeCheckInSnapshot`, which is SECURITY relevant: it is the lock window, so a client could have widened its own cut-off to 168 hours. **The fix takes all four from the live catalog** (`liveAddOn.price` / `.name` / `.unit` / `.cutoffHoursBeforeCheckIn`). The staff path (`addons-staff-update.ts`) had the identical hole, which also defeated the `priceSnapshot` audit trail: the `addOnSnapshot.price` written to `otherAddOnAudit` recorded the REQUEST's price, not the catalog's. **A CHANGE op may only alter the QUANTITY** — the name / price / unit stay the entry's EXISTING snapshot, because per OTHER-ADD-ONS.md the guest keeps the price they ordered at even if the admin changes the catalog price afterwards. The snapshot is deliberately NOT re-read from the catalog on a change; a NEW order takes the current catalog price. The wire fields stay accepted-and-ignored, and the op-shape docs now label them ADVISORY ONLY so the bug is not reintroduced. **Verified:** `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c2-addon-price-authority.test.ts` (14 guards) asserts the anti-patterns `op.priceSnapshot ??` / `||` are ABSENT from both handlers (against a comment-stripped copy, so the explanatory comments do not trip the pin), that the catalog-sourced forms are present, and that a change op keeps `prior.*Snapshot`. **Files:** `guest-app/server/handlers/addons-manage-save.ts` (add op + change op); `guest-app/server/handlers/addons-staff-update.ts` (add op + change op); `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c2-addon-price-authority.test.ts` (new).

| 279 | **Firestore rules now cover the collections the admin app writes from the BROWSER: `addOns`, `auditLogs`, and the BR-01/BR-12 audit collection groups (OCT-C3 + OCT-H6, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** BR-12's other add-on catalog (`addOns`) and BR-12.1's settings audit trail (`auditLogs`) both shipped in October WITHOUT a rule block. Firestore denies unmatched paths by default, and the admin Settings tab reads + writes BOTH from the browser through the Firestore client SDK (`admin-app/src/firebase/config.ts` → `db = getFirestore(firebaseApp)`), so every admin read + write failed with `PERMISSION_DENIED` — and because each error was SWALLOWED (the `onSnapshot` error handler only cleared the loading flag; the pre-seed and audit writes only `console.warn`ed), the tab rendered its **"No add-ons yet" empty state** instead of an error. The admin was told to create a row that may already have existed, and clicking "Add add-on" produced a raw permissions error. `git log -S "addOns" -- firebase/firestore.rules` returns nothing: the collection has NEVER had a rule. The impact: the hotel could not see, price, enable/disable, create or delete any other add-on, and the BR-12.1 audit trail recorded nothing — while the guest path (server side, Admin SDK, rules not applied) kept selling the read-only pre-seed, so guests were buying an add-on the hotel could not manage. **The rules added:** `match /addOns/{addOnId}` (staff read, admin write — the guest never reads it directly, the public catalog goes through `GET /api/bookings/addons/config`), `match /auditLogs/{auditId}` (admin read, staff create, append-only). **The same defect class also fixed the BR-01 + BR-12 audit collection groups (OCT-H6):** the Reports "Late changes" metric reads `collectionGroup(db, "breakfastAudit")` + `collectionGroup(db, "otherAddOnAudit")`, and Firestore requires an EXPLICIT collectionGroup rule for that — the exact trap the `refunds` group rule already documents in the same file ("Without this, staff got 'Missing or insufficient permissions' the moment the Liability tab mounted"). Both listeners failed with `PERMISSION_DENIED`, so the late-change counters were permanently 0. **The admin UI now surfaces a failed catalog read** — a `role="alert"` panel with the error text, instead of the misleading empty state. **Verified:** `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c3-firestore-rules.test.ts` (16 guards) pins each rule block's guards, asserts neither collection is publicly readable, asserts the two collectionGroup rules exist and are staff-readable, asserts the admin UI keeps an error state, and pins that every collection the admin app writes from the browser has a `match` block. **Ordering note:** per GOTCHAS, the rules must be deployed AFTER the app build that depends on them — the admin tab reads `addOns` and would show the error panel (not a crash) until the rules land. **Files:** `firebase/firestore.rules`; `admin-app/src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx` (the `addOnsCatalogError` state + the error panel); `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c3-firestore-rules.test.ts` (new).

| 280 | **The booking calendar builds its dates in the HOTEL's timezone from their Y/M/D components, and the breakfast date list uses a UTC anchor (OCT-C4 + OCT-H2, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `DateRangePicker.isoToTzDate` was `new TZDate(\`${iso}T00:00:00\`, config.timezone)`. `@date-fns/tz`'s TZDate constructor pops the timezone string and, when the REMAINING argument is a single date STRING, parses it with the native `Date` constructor — i.e. in the BROWSER's timezone — and only then re-projects the instant into `config.timezone`. For any browser whose clock is ahead of Asia/Manila (UTC+8) the re-projection landed on the PREVIOUS day. `react-day-picker` re-exports the SAME `TZDate` class and builds its day cells with `DateLib.newDate(year, monthIndex, date)` → `new TZDate(year, monthIndex, date, timeZone)` — the 3-number COMPONENTS form, which constructs the wall-clock in the TARGET zone. So the library's cells were correctly Manila-anchored while every value WE built was not, and the two disagreed — the exact invariant `BOOKING-FLOW.md §Date picker` requires never to happen. Reproduced against the installed package: with the browser in `Asia/Tokyo` / `Australia/Sydney` / `Pacific/Auckland`, `isoToTzDate("2026-10-25")` returned **2026-10-24**. The consequence: the guest clicked Oct 25 and the booking committed **Oct 24** — one night longer, priced, queried, booked and receipted for the wrong dates (Bohol's guest mix is exactly the affected profile). **The fix builds the TZDate from its components**: `new TZDate(year, month - 1, day, config.timezone)` — byte-for-byte the construction rdp uses, so our values and the library's cells now agree in every browser timezone. **The same class of bug was fixed in the booking flow's breakfast dates (OCT-H2):** `enumerateBreakfastDates` parsed `new Date(\`${checkIn}T00:00:00\`)` (browser-local) and formatted with `toISOString()` (UTC), so in any UTC+ zone the list was shifted back a day — the LAST breakfast morning was never offered, so it was never charged, and the server's cut-off check ran against the wrong date. The two sibling implementations already used the `Z` anchor (`ManageBreakfastPage.tsx:148`, `WalkinBreakfastGrid.tsx:119`); this one was the outlier. **Verified behaviourally:** `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c4-calendar-timezone.test.ts` (14 guards) mutates `process.env.TZ` and asserts the round-trip is unchanged in 8 browser timezones, and separately asserts that the PRE-FIX construction WAS wrong east of Manila — so the test documents the regression it guards. **Rejected:** converting to the browser's local zone (the hotel's dates must not depend on the guest's device); using the `Z`-anchored string form (that is the local-parse trap again — it fixes the breakfast list but not the picker's hotel-zone requirement). **Files:** `guest-app/src/components/DateRangePicker.tsx`; `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx`; `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c4-calendar-timezone.test.ts` (new).

| 281 | **A walk-in booking now PERSISTS the breakfast selections + the other add-ons the desk selected (OCT-C5, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the walk-in transaction read the breakfast config and then threw the payload away — it hard-coded `lineBreakfastTotal = 0` and wrote `breakfastSelections: {}` onto the booking. The string `otherAddOn` appeared ZERO times in `bookings.ts`, and `WalkinBookingSchema` had no field for the add-ons (a Zod `z.object()` STRIPS unknown keys silently), so the desk's selections were discarded at the schema boundary. The admin modal has been sending both payloads since BR-12.1 (`BookingsPage.tsx:4654-4675` — the `WalkinBreakfastGrid` + `WalkinOtherAddOnGrid` output). The impact: the desk picked meals and an airport transfer, the modal showed a total, the booking was created — and the guest received NO breakfast and NO transfer, the kitchen list was empty, and the hotel was NEVER CHARGED for either. BR-08's own comment ("the create transaction reads it below and stamps `breakfastTotal` onto the booking doc") described behavior the code did not implement. **The fix:** the walk-in now validates `breakfastSelections` with the SAME helper the public create path uses (`validateBreakfastSelections`), inside the same transaction as the booking write, and persists the validated entries + `breakfastTotal`. The validated total is attributed to the FIRST room line (the selections are keyed by date × guest slot, not by room — the same attribution as the public create path), so the per-line subtotals and the reservation-level total agree by construction. **The other add-ons are REBUILT from the live `addOns` catalog** (read inside the transaction, before any write), so the walk-in cannot persist a client-supplied price — the OCT-C2 rule applied to the walk-in path — and a disabled add-on is refused with a 400. The catalog falls back to the read-only `AIRPORT_TRANSFER_PRESEED` when the collection is empty, so the walk-in grid (which renders from the public config endpoint) and this validation always agree. **A walk-in skips the per-date cut-off** (`staffCreated: true`): the cut-off exists to stop a GUEST from changing a selection whose window has closed; a walk-in creates a NEW booking, so there is no prior selection to lock. The walk-in's catch now maps validation failures to 400s with a human message instead of 500s. **Two new walk-in schema fields** (`otherAddOnSelections` + `otherAddOnTotal` + `hasAnyOtherAddOns`) are advisory — the server rebuilds every entry from the catalog. **Verified:** `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c5-walkin-addons.test.ts` (16 guards) — 12 of them fail against the pre-fix code. **Explicitly NOT fixed here:** `handleAddRoomToReservation` also writes `breakfastSelections: {}` while inheriting the RESERVATION's `breakfastTotal` onto the new room's subtotal — a separate double-charge defect, tracked in `plan/project/AUDIT-OCTOBER-2026.md`; changing that billing behaviour is a separate decision. **Files:** `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts` (the walk-in validation + catalog rebuild + the booking-doc write + the 400 mapping); `guest-app/server/lib/breakfast-selections.ts` (the `staffCreated` flag); `shared/schemas/booking.ts` (the new walk-in fields); `guest-app/tests/api/oct-c5-walkin-addons.test.ts` (new).
| 282 | **The guest's Manage-Breakfast balance no longer double-counts the breakfast (OCT-H1, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `booking.totalPrice` ALREADY contains the breakfast (BR-01/BR-03 compute `subtotal = roomTotal + breakfastTotal + extraBedTotal`). The BR-04 manage path computed `balance = totalPrice + recomputedTotal - paidAmount`, which added the breakfast a SECOND time — a guest who booked and PAID for a ₱500 breakfast saw a ₱500 balance. The fix subtracts the breakfast already inside `totalPrice` before adding the current one: `(totalPrice − breakfastInTotalPrice + currentBreakfastTotal) − paidAmount`. **The anchor is `rateBreakdown.addOns[]`** (snapshotted at create, preserved verbatim by `rebuildRateBreakdown`) — NOT `booking.breakfastTotal`, which the manage path overwrites on every write. A booking with no rate breakdown has an UNKNOWN breakfast component, so the fallback is the CONSERVATIVE one: do not add the breakfast on top (never over-charge). New shared helper `sumBreakfastRateLines` + `isBreakfastRateLine` (`shared/utils/breakfastTotal.ts`); the shared folio was verified correct and is unchanged. **Verified behaviourally:** `shared/__tests__/oct-h1-breakfast-rate-lines.test.ts` (11 guards) + `guest-app/tests/api/oct-h1-guest-balance.test.ts` (6 source pins). **Files:** `shared/utils/breakfastTotal.ts`; `guest-app/server/lib/rate-breakdown.ts`; `guest-app/server/handlers/breakfast-manage.ts` (GET + update). |

VAT" figure on its own booking page. | 284 | **The "Booking & Holds" form re-syncs from the Firestore snapshot (OCT-H3, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the three inputs (payment hold window, in-flow hold minutes, maximum stay nights) were initialized with `useState`'s lazy initializer, which only runs on the FIRST render — BEFORE the snapshot lands. On a direct load of `/settings` the fields were stuck at the pre-snapshot defaults, and the next save of an UNRELATED field silently rewrote the stored policy: a hotel that had set the stay cap to "no limit" (stored `0` → resolved `Infinity`) had it replaced with `30`, with the field still DISPLAYING "30" so nothing looked wrong — and the guest site then refused 31+ night stays it had previously accepted. The same defect applied to the two hold-window fields, so the fix covers all three. **A re-sync effect refreshes each input from the snapshot, but ONLY when that field's SOURCE value actually changed since the last sync** — so a snapshot arriving mid-typing cannot clobber the admin's in-progress edit. `Infinity` is rendered back as the literal `0` the admin saved. **Verified:** `admin-app/src/__tests__/oct-h3-max-stay-sync.test.ts` (8 guards). **Files:** `admin-app/src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx`. |
| 285 | **The admin booking mapper hydrates the October add-on fields (OCT-H4, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the admin `Booking` type DECLARED `breakfastTotal` / `hasAnyBreakfast` / `revenueAllocation` / `otherAddOnSelections` / `otherAddOnTotal` / `hasAnyOtherAddOns` / `otherAddOnServed`, but the snapshot mapper never READ them — the same bug class the mapper's own `reservationId` comment (FOL-02, MRB-01) documents, reintroduced for the October fields. Consequences (read-side misreporting only; the write path is field-scoped so nothing was destroyed): the Reports "total breakfast revenue" showed ₱0 while "Top meals" showed real money (the report contradicted itself) and the CSV export carried ₱0; the folio could not see the stored `revenueAllocation` so the revenue-stream split fell back to the legacy heuristic with `breakfastGross = 0` and the room KPI silently absorbed the breakfast revenue; the whole BR-12.1 Add-Ons sub-tab showed ZERO for every booking (it filters on `hasAnyOtherAddOns === true`); and the drawer's Other Add-Ons tab showed "no add-ons" for a booking that had them. **The mapper now hydrates all of them and DERIVES the mirrors with the SERVER's own helpers** (`recomputeBreakfastTotal`, `recomputeOtherAddOnTotal`, `hasAnyOtherAddOns`) when the doc carries no explicit flag, so a booking written before the field existed still reads honestly. **Verified:** `admin-app/src/__tests__/oct-h4-admin-booking-mapper.test.ts` (9 guards). **Files:** `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx`. |
| 286 | **The audit entry can now carry a reason AND the cut-off flag; the override paths stamp both (OCT-H7, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `auditBreakfastOp` / `auditOtherAddOnOp` overloaded ONE parameter to carry EITHER a free-text reason (string) OR the cut-off flag (boolean). The kitchen list queries `collectionGroup("breakfastAudit").where("occurredAfterCutoff", "==", true)`, so the "Late changes" column was empty for exactly the case it exists for (a staff member overriding a locked date after the cut-off), and the add-on late-change counter was 0 for the same reason. **The parameter now also accepts an OBJECT carrying both fields**, and every override path uses it. | 287 | **A reschedule / cancel re-dates the add-on selections, and opens the refund liabilities the spec requires (OCT-H8, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `handleRescheduleBooking` (and the `mode: "extend"` wrapper) and `handleCancelBooking` contained ZERO references to `breakfastSelections` / `otherAddOnSelections` — the breakfast total carried forward and the selection keys kept the OLD dates. A guest who booked Oct 14–17 with three PAID breakfasts and rescheduled to Oct 20–23 was still charged for three breakfasts that were never served, and the kitchen list for Oct 20 showed nothing. **The fix** applies BREAKFAST.md §Edge cases 2 + 3: a selection whose service date falls outside the NEW window is auto-cancelled (marked `removedAt`, kept in the map for the audit trail — `assertSlotForAdd` only blocks LIVE slots, so the guest can re-order if the dates move back), a PAID removal opens a refund liability and an UNPAID removal simply comes off the bill. The BR-01 invariant `breakfastTotal === recomputeBreakfastTotal(selections)` is preserved. **The cancel path does NOT rewrite `totalPrice`** (the CRL-07 policy liability is computed from the COLLECTED amount), and the add-on liability is opened ONLY when the policy refunded NOTHING — the policy refund is a PERCENTAGE OF THE COLLECTED MONEY, which already includes the add-ons, so a second liability would refund the same meal twice. That guard is exactly the case the spec's rule exists for: a late cancel (0% policy) where the guest paid for breakfasts that will never happen. The pure logic lives in `shared/utils/bookingAddOnReschedule.ts`. **Verified behaviourally:** `shared/__tests__/oct-h8-addon-redate.test.ts` (26 guards) + `guest-app/tests/api/oct-h8-server-wiring.test.ts` (12 server pins). **Files:** `shared/utils/bookingAddOnReschedule.ts` (new); `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts`; `shared/index.ts`. |
| 288 | **A removed meal photo can no longer break every Dining settings save, and the photo upload is persisted immediately (OCT-H5 + OCT-H10, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** object spread copies own enumerable keys INCLUDING undefined-valued ones, so a `{ imageUrl: undefined }` patch left the meal object OWNING an `imageUrl` key with the value `undefined`. Zod's `.optional()` validates the VALUE, not the key's presence, so the bad payload passed schema validation and then threw `Unsupported field value: undefined` at the Firestore SDK boundary (the admin app never sets `ignoreUndefinedProperties`). Because the offending key sat inside one element of the `breakfastConfig.meals` array, EVERY subsequent write of that section failed — the admin could not save the cut-off time, the breakfast toggle, a meal name or a price until they reloaded. **Two layers of fix:** (1) `applyBreakfastMealPatch` is the ONE place a meal-row patch is applied — it drops undefined-valued keys and REMOVES a previously-set optional field when the patch clears it; (2) `updateSettings` strips undefined-valued keys from every payload (new shared helper `stripUndefinedValues`), a safety net for the whole settings surface. **The photo upload / removal now persists the `meals` array immediately** (through the same `BreakfastMealSchema` validation the Save button uses) instead of only mutating local state — the guest catalog no longer lags the admin's screen, and navigating away can no longer orphan the Storage object. The Storage object is deleted only AFTER the row no longer references it. The success toast moved to the caller: the upload alone is not the outcome the admin cares about — the catalog row must also be in Firestore. **Verified behaviourally:** `shared/__tests__/oct-h5-firestore-payload.test.ts` (12 guards) + `admin-app/src/__tests__/oct-h5-h10-meal-photo.test.ts` (10 guards). **Files:** `shared/utils/firestorePayload.ts` (new); `admin-app/src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx`; `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx`; `shared/index.ts`. |
| 289 | **The MBUX-18 edit-stay sheet traps focus, closes on Escape, restores focus and locks the page scroll (OCT-H11, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the sheet declared `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` with no focus trap, no Escape dismiss, no focus restore and no scroll lock — while its own backdrop comment pointed at a document keydown listener that did not exist in the file. A keyboard or screen-reader guest tabbed straight out of the sheet into a page that `aria-modal` had hidden from the AT tree (focus landed on an invisible target); Escape did nothing (the only dismiss paths were the small X and the backdrop); and on a phone, dragging the dimmed backdrop scrolled the page behind the sheet (the sheet's own `overscroll-contain` only contains the scroll WITHIN the sheet). The admin app already ships the correct primitive (`admin-app/src/utils/useFocusTrap.ts`, decision #107); the guest app gets the same contract as `guest-app/src/utils/useFocusTrap.ts` — deliberately NOT shared through `@spark-inn/shared`, because that package is imported by the server bundle (which has no React and no DOM). **Verified:** `guest-app/src/__tests__/oct-h11-edit-stay-sheet-a11y.test.ts` (12 guards). **Files:** `guest-app/src/utils/useFocusTrap.ts` (new); `guest-app/src/components/EditStayDetailsSheet.tsx`. |
