The catalog comes from the PUBLIC endpoint `GET /api/bookings/addons/config` — the guest app never reads the `addOns` collection directly (Firestore rules) — and the section renders NOTHING when the catalog is empty or the hotel has taken other add-ons off sale (OCT-M1). **(2) The union CTA.** The Step 2 CTA now covers breakfast AND other add-ons: the count is the breakfast slots + the other-add-on rows, the figure is `breakfastTotal + otherAddOnTotal`. The BR-10 "without add-ons" branch therefore means the union. **(3) The create path.** `createBookingSchema` accepts `otherAddOnSelections` (advisory); the create transaction REBUILDS every entry from the live catalog inside the transaction (the OCT-C2 price authority — a client-supplied price is never persisted), refuses an add-on that is not on sale (400), clamps the quantity to the guest count, and writes `otherAddOnSelections` + `otherAddOnTotal` + `hasAnyOtherAddOns` on the booking doc. **The other add-ons are NOT folded into `totalPrice`** — `plan/features/OTHER-ADD-ONS.md` decision 2 pins them as additive fields "added on top of it for the balance", the same shape the walk-in (OCT-C5) and the post-booking guest path write; the Step 2 grid says so in one line so the guest is not surprised. **New shared helper:** `shared/utils/otherAddOnDrafts.ts` (`buildOtherAddOnDraftSelections`, `countOtherAddOnDraftSelections`, `clampOtherAddOnQuantity`, `otherAddOnDraftKey`) — pure, so the grid's per-day × per-add-on rules are assertable behaviourally. **Files:** `shared/utils/otherAddOnDrafts.ts` (new), `guest-app/src/hooks/useOtherAddOnsCatalog.ts` (new), `guest-app/src/components/OtherAddOnsStep2Section.tsx` (new), `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx`, `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts`. |
| 298 | **The BR-12 other add-ons are part of the folio BALANCE (OCT-FOL-01, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the other add-ons are NOT inside `booking.totalPrice` — per `plan/features/OTHER-ADD-ONS.md` decision 2 they are "added on top of it for the balance" — but the folio ignored them entirely. A booking with a ₱2,000 airport transfer the guest had not paid for showed a **₱0 balance**: the hotel was owed the money and every balance surface said the guest was settled. The guest's own post-booking Manage page had ALWAYS computed it correctly (`totalPrice + otherAddOnTotal - paidAmount`), so the guest and the desk disagreed about the same booking. **The fix:** `computeBookingFolio` + `computeServerFolioTotals` take an optional `otherAddOnTotal` and add it to the grand total; the server's folio snapshot reads it from the booking (legacy path) or SUMS the children's (the reservation path — the reservation header's `totalPrice` IS the sum of the children's, so the add-ons must be summed the same way); the admin's folio call does the same for the selected booking. `otherAddOnTotal` is optional + defaulted to 0, so a pre-BR-12 booking and every existing caller behave exactly as before. **What this changes in practice:** the drawer folio, the receipt PDF, the verify-payment + confirm-with-balance + checkout paths, the admin table's payment-state filter and the Reports → Receivables + Collections balances now all include the add-ons. Two consequences were handled explicitly so the numbers cannot contradict each other: (1) the payment-state filter now uses the FOLIO's denominator (`folio.grandTotal`) instead of `booking.totalPrice` — the two already disagreed about store + incidental charges, and the filter was calling a booking with an unpaid transfer "paid" while the drawer showed a balance; (2) the folio breakdown and the receipt PDF now ITEMISE the "Other add-ons" line, so the lines add up to the folio total (and the receipt's "Folio Charges" section prints for a booking that has only other add-ons). The drawer's summary strip's "Total" metric now reads the folio's grand total, so Total / Paid / Balance share one denominator. **Deliberately NOT changed:** the list-level surfaces that show the ROOM BILL by design (the bookings table's "Total" column, the reservation "due" pill — MRB-12 decision #179 pins the reservation row's balance to `header.totalPrice − paidAmount` on purpose, to avoid the filtered-children bug), the `fullyPaid` transition in the payment handlers (that settles the ROOM bill, consistently with the store + incidental charges), and the guest booking email's "Total Amount Due" (the amount payable ONLINE — the other add-ons are settled at the property, per BR-12 decision 2). **Files:** `shared/utils/bookingFolio.ts`, `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts`, `admin-app/src/pages/BookingsPage.tsx`, `admin-app/src/components/BookingDrawerWorkspace.tsx`, `admin-app/src/pages/ReportsPage.tsx`, `shared/__tests__/booking-folio.test.ts`, `guest-app/tests/api/oct-fol-01-other-addons-in-balance.test.ts`. |
| 299 | **The mobile cart tray lives in the sticky footer's ACTION AREA, not in the page body (OCT-L1, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the tray was the last element of the Step 1 flow while the footer is `fixed` — so the tray scrolled away and the footer stayed pinned. On a phone with 4+ room types the guest tapped "+" and got no aggregate feedback near the thumb: the MBUX-01 scroll regression re-introduced on Step 1. Both the call-site comment and the component's own docstring described a fixed placement the code never implemented. `BookingStickyFooter` now has an `aboveAction` slot that renders **inside** the fixed bar, above the summary/action row, and the tray moved there — MBUX-03 allows "above the footer **or as part of the action area**", and this IS the action area, so the tray is now always reachable. **Two consequences handled explicitly:** the expanded lines are capped at `40vh` with an internal scroll (inside a fixed bar an uncapped list grows the footer until it swallows the screen), and Step 1's `<main>` bottom reserve grew from `8rem` to `13rem` so the taller footer never covers the last room card. **Files:** `guest-app/src/components/BookingStickyFooter.tsx`, `guest-app/src/components/RoomCartTray.tsx`, `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx`. |
| 300 | **The router write never happens inside a React state updater (OCT-L2, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `setSearchParams` was called from inside a `setRoomCart` updater in both `updateRoomQuantity` and `updateExtraBedCount`. A state updater runs during React's RENDER phase, so dispatching a router update from one is a render-phase side effect — React 19 warns about it, and the app runs `<React.StrictMode>`, which double-invokes the updater in dev. Nothing corrupted (the URL write is idempotent), but it sat on the hot quantity-change path. Both handlers now derive the next cart from the current `roomCart` value and write the cart and the URL from the event handler, in that order. **Files:** `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx`. |
| 301 | **One shared money-rounding rule: the other-add-on path stores 2dp like every other money path (OCT-L3, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump — owner-approved):** `recomputeOtherAddOnTotal` rounded to WHOLE PESOS (`Math.round(total)`) while breakfast used 2dp and the refund-liability amount for the SAME selection was computed at full precision — so a ₱499.99 airport transfer billed ₱500 but refunded ₱499.99: the hotel asked for a peso it never charged and the guest's bill and refund never reconciled. `shared/utils/money.ts` now owns the rule as `roundToTwoDecimals` (exported from `shared/index.ts`), and it is applied to the total, the per-date breakdown subtotal, the guest + staff refund amounts, the reschedule auto-cancel refund (`shared/utils/bookingAddOnReschedule.ts`) and the refund-liability writer — so the bill and the refund can no longer disagree. **The display does not change:** both apps' `formatPrice` uses `maximumFractionDigits: 0`, so a ₱499.99 total has always rendered as "₱500" on screen. The repo rule is **store at 2dp, display at 0dp**, and breakfast already worked that way — this makes the other add-ons behave the same way. **Why a shared module and not an inline `Math.round(x * 100) / 100`:** the 2dp pattern was already inlined in three places in `breakfastTotal.ts` and once in `bookingFolio.ts`, and that duplication is exactly the shape that let the other-add-on path drift in the first place. **The one test that pinned the old behaviour** (`shared/__tests__/br-12-total-helper.test.ts`, "rounds to integer") was re-pointed with the inversion spelled out in the test name, so a future agent cannot restore `Math.round(total)` by "fixing" the assertion. **Files:** `shared/utils/money.ts` (new), `shared/utils/otherAddOnTotal.ts`, `shared/utils/bookingAddOnReschedule.ts`, `shared/index.ts`, `guest-app/server/lib/otherAddOn-selections.ts`, `guest-app/server/handlers/addons-manage-save.ts`, `guest-app/server/handlers/addons-staff-update.ts`. |
| 302 | **A guest remove-op on a missing add-on answers 400, not 500 (OCT-L4, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `assertSelectionForChange` throws `SELECTION_NOT_ORDERED` when the selection is missing or already removed. In the guest save handler that call was UNWRAPPED, so the throw escaped the transaction and the outer catch returned a generic 500 "Failed to save add-on changes." — a client error dressed up as a server fault, and a dead end for the guest (retrying could never succeed). The staff handler already wrapped the same call and answered 400; the guest path now agrees. **The outer catch stays a 500 for genuine server faults** — the fix does not launder real faults into client errors, and the test pins that too. **Files:** `guest-app/server/handlers/addons-manage-save.ts`. |
| 303 | **The guest's `breakfastConfig` read path is TYPED and NORMALIZED (OCT-L5, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the booking page declared its own inline shape for the local `breakfastConfig` state, and that shape did NOT include `imageUrl` — the MBUX-04 meal thumbnail that `BreakfastMenuStrip` renders. The field survived only because the Firestore snapshot was read with `bSnap.data() as any`: one type edit away from silently dropping every meal photo on the booking flow, and the compiler could not see it. `guest-app/src/utils/breakfastConfig.ts` now owns the shape (`BookingBreakfastConfig` / `BookingBreakfastMeal`, with `imageUrl` declared) and a `normalizeBreakfastConfig` reader, and the page normalizes instead of casting. **The normalizer is behaviour-preserving against the old raw read** — `isEnabled` keeps truthiness semantics, `cutoffsEnabled` keeps its `!== false` default, the cut-off literals (`20:00` / `Asia/Manila`) are unchanged — with ONE deliberate tightening: `price` is coerced to a finite number, so an absent price can no longer render `₱NaN`. **Why not reuse `shared/types`' `BreakfastConfig` directly:** the shared type marks the cut-off fields REQUIRED because the admin write path always writes them, while the guest read path must tolerate a doc that predates BR-10. **Files:** `guest-app/src/utils/breakfastConfig.ts` (new), `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx`. |
| 304 | **Every date-accepting endpoint validates the calendar, not just the string shape (OCT-L7, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `new Date("2026-02-31T00:00:00Z")` **rolls over to Mar 3** rather than producing an Invalid Date, so an `isNaN(getTime())` guard NEVER fires for a calendar-impossible date — the booking is stored for a day the caller never sent. The guest CREATE endpoint was never affected (it validates with the shared `isValidIsoDate`), but the **walk-in create**, the **reschedule** and the **corporate inquiry→booking conversion** all used the raw `new Date` + `isNaN` pattern, so the front desk could save a booking for a day nobody entered. All three now validate with `isValidIsoDate` before the date arithmetic. **Why the helper and not a bigger regex:** the schemas validate the SHAPE only (`z.string().regex(/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/)` accepts "2026-02-31"), and `isValidIsoDate` (`shared/utils/stayRange.ts`) adds the calendar check on top of the shape — one helper, already used by the create path, so the rule cannot drift per endpoint again. **Files:** `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts` (walk-in create + reschedule + a redundant re-check on the create path), `guest-app/server/handlers/corporate-inquiries.ts`. |
| 305 | **The committed API bundle's one-VERSION lag is the ESTABLISHED COMMIT PATTERN, not a defect (OCT-L6, 2026-10-20 — no code change):** the committed `guest-app/api/[...route].js` carries `VERSION2 = "2.18.0"` while `shared/VERSION.ts` is `"2.18.1"`. The October audit filed this as a LOW ("the artifact should be regenerated"), but the cause is the hook ORDER: git runs `pre-commit` (`check:api-bundle`) **before** `prepare-commit-msg` (`bump-version.mjs`), so the bundle is validated against the pre-bump version and the bump then commits a version the artifact cannot match. Rebuilding and diffing the artifact confirms the constant is the ONLY difference. **The impact is nil:** `guest-app/server/` never imports the `VERSION` constant (the only `VERSION*` identifiers in the bundle are firebase-admin's own), so `VERSION2` is dead code inside the artifact and the real UI version comes from `shared/VERSION.ts` at build time in both frontends. This is already documented as a standing condition in `plan/project/ROADMAP.md` (the MBUX-08 entry), and a self-amending hook was prototyped and REVERTED during MBUX-08 — its `run()` helper silently dropped the `env` argument, so `HUSKY=0` never reached the amend and the hook could recurse. **Decision: nothing changed.** "Fixing" it would mean reordering the Husky hooks for a nil-impact difference. The one real operational consequence is unchanged and was honoured by this batch: any commit that stages a `guest-app/server/` or `shared/` file must rebuild and stage the artifact (`npm run build:api -w guest-app`). |
| 306 | **The guest's intercom thread and call signalling move to CAPABILITY-SCOPED session paths (PR-AUDIT-B3/B4/B5/B6, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `intercoms/{roomId}/messages` was world-readable and `calls/{roomId}/{document=**}` was world-readable **and** world-writable, so any anonymous caller could read a guest's chat, read a call document, inject an SDP offer pointing the guest's peer connection at an attacker, or flip a thread to `resolved`. The root constraint: **walk-in guests are not signed in**, so Firestore rules can only authorize against `request.auth` — there is no way to keep a guest's realtime read on a room-scoped collection and make it non-public. **Decision: put the credential IN THE PATH.** The guest's thread is `intercomSessions/{sessionToken}/messages` and signalling is `callSessions/{sessionToken}`, where the token is 32 hex characters of `crypto.randomBytes(16)` (128 bits) issued by `POST /api/intercom/verify-guest`, re-used for the stay and regenerated when the stay changes — the same "unguessable share link" model the booking-lookup token already uses. **Dedicated top-level collections, not nested under `intercoms`/`calls`**, so the legacy blocks' path shapes cannot match the new ones. **The server, not the client, is the identity authority:** `send-message` re-verifies the stay on every send (room match + `checked-in` status + token match) and derives `guestName` from the booking rather than trusting the request body. The rules are `allow read: if true` (capability) but `allow create: if false` on guest messages — the guest can read the thread and mark the desk's messages read, but only the server writes. The legacy room-scoped paths stay staff-only and are retained for the desk's history. **The honest limit, recorded rather than glossed:** under a capability path, *any* constructible path is readable — the per-stay binding is enforced by the server, not by the rules, and the emulator test states it as a capability rather than as a per-booking authorization. The admin inbox reads both threads with ONE unordered `collectionGroup(db, "messages")` query (no `orderBy`, so no collection-group index is needed; sorting happens in JS), and the desk's delicate second-call-wins logic (decision #94) is preserved — only the query and the room mapping changed. |
| 307 | **An audit-stamp pairing cannot be expressed with `hasOnly()` and needs an explicit rule (PR-AUDIT-R4, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `guests/{userId}` enforced the `audioRouting` contract (`plan/features/INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING.md` §D&L 46 — "sibling `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` audit stamp on every write") with `request.resource.data.diff(resource.data).affectedKeys().hasOnly([...])`. **`hasOnly()` is a SUBSET check:** it restricts WHICH keys a write may touch, it does not require that a listed key is *present*. Omitting `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` therefore passed the gate, and the stamp the spec called mandatory was never actually enforced — the rule claimed a guarantee it did not provide. **Decision: express the pairing explicitly** with a dedicated `audioRoutingStamped()` rule function — changing `audioRouting` requires changing `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` in the same write (`!changed.hasAny(["audioRouting"]) || changed.hasAll(["audioRoutingUpdatedAt"])`); a write that leaves `audioRouting` alone (e.g. the member editing their own `fullName`) is unaffected. The app always writes both fields together (`useAudioRouting.ts` writes `audioRouting` + `audioRoutingUpdatedAt` + `updatedAt` on both the update and the reset path), so nothing breaks. **The generalizable lesson:** a field *allowlist* and a field *requirement* are different rules, and `hasOnly()` only expresses the first. Where a spec says "X and Y together", the rule must say so. |
| 308 | **A GATE THAT CANNOT RUN IS A DEFECT — the Firestore rules suite now resolves its own JDK and fails loudly (PR-AUDIT, 2026-10-20; `fix:` PATCH bump):** `npm run test:rules` had **never been executed in this repo's lifetime** — the dev machine had no Java runtime (macOS ships only the `/usr/bin/java` stub), so the Firestore emulator could not start and the entire rules suite was silently unrunnable. Every rule was unverified, and the audit's own "the rules are correct by construction" claim was untested. Installing a JDK and running the suite for the first time surfaced four defects no other gate could see: **(R1)** every emulator file shared one emulator, one port and one `projectId` and each called `clearFirestore()` in `beforeEach`, so parallel files wiped each other's seeds (`fileParallelism: false`); **(R2)** a duplicate `const liability` in one scope made esbuild fail to transform the file, so its 11 tests never ran; **(R3)** that file's `adminCtx()` was `unauthenticatedContext()` with a comment claiming it bypasses rules like the Admin SDK — it does not, `rules-unit-testing` has no Admin context, so every seed write was `PERMISSION_DENIED` (replaced with a real rules-bypassing client, `connectFirestoreEmulator(..., { mockUserToken: "owner" })`); **(R4)** the `hasOnly()` subset defect above. **Decision: `scripts/resolve-jdk.mjs` wraps the rules suite** — it locates a JDK (JAVA_HOME → macOS `java_home` → `~/.local/jdk/*` → the standard `JavaVirtualMachines` locations → `java` on PATH) and, if none is found, **fails with an actionable message instead of letting the gate be skipped**. A missing JVM must never look like a passing gate. The repo has no CI, so this machine IS the gate. Suite status: **74 tests, 74 passed, 8 files** (from 40 / 31 passed / 9 failed / 1 unloadable file), including a new `pr-audit-rules-hardening.emulator.test.ts` (23 tests) that evaluates the audit's rule changes against the real rules text. |
| 309 | **`create` and `update` are DISTINCT operations — a blanket `allow create: if false` silently breaks a legitimate writer, and `write` ⊇ `create`+`update`+`delete` makes a sibling `allow delete:` DEAD CODE (2026-10-21; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the 2.18.5 release shipped the capability-session rules to production with `intercomSessions/{token}/messages` set to `allow create: if false`. The desk's reply is a client-side `addDoc` into that collection, so **every desk reply came back PERMISSION_DENIED** — the guest's message arrived (the server writes it), the desk saw it in the inbox, and the reply was rejected. Two rule-language facts caused it and both are now load-bearing knowledge: **(a)** `create` and `update` are separate operations, so `allow update: if isStaff()` does not admit a NEW reply document — denying `create` outright denies the reply; **(b)** `write` is shorthand for `create` + `update` + `delete` and matching `allow` statements are OR'd, so an unconditional `allow write: if true` grants delete REGARDLESS of a neighbouring `allow delete: if isStaff()` — that line was dead code, and the real intent (the guest deletes its own call session on hang-up) was the opposite of what it said. **Decisions:** (1) staff create is allowed on the session thread — the guest path is unaffected because the server writes through the Admin SDK, which bypasses rules entirely; (2) the call-session blocks now spell out `allow read, create, update, delete: if true` so the stated intent matches the behaviour. **The generalisable lesson: a rule is a contract with a WRITER, not a shape.** Every writer path needs a test that exercises the actual client call, not just the collection's shape — the suite had 74 assertions and none of them created a message the way the desk does. New file `firebase/tests/intercom-session-write-paths.emulator.test.ts` (5 tests) pins the desk's create, the guest's `isRead`-only update, the guest's hang-up delete and the ICE-candidate writes. **Suite: 79 tests, 79 passed, 9 files.** |

| 310 | **The MRB-11 revenue allocation's total IS the booking's total — the write-boundary guard asserts it, every post-create money path re-snapshots it, and the corporate path writes one at all (P1 revenue-reporting fix, 2026-10-22; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the guard `assertBookingRevenueAllocationInvariant` (per #177/#178, shipped 2026-08-03) asserted only that the four streams add up to the allocation's OWN `totalNet` — it never asserted that `totalNet` was the booking's persisted `totalPrice`. A STALE allocation therefore passed: the create-time snapshot kept the pre-discount gross while a discount applied / removed / rejected AFTER the create write moved `booking.totalPrice`. Reports reads `revenueAllocation.totalNet` (per-booking, `AdminContext.tsx`), so the reported revenue no longer matched the bill. **The fix has four parts.** **(1) The guard now requires BOTH identities** — the per-stream sum AND `allocation.totalNet == round2(totalPrice)` (the module's existing `round2`, 0.05 tolerance for 2dp noise). It runs FIRST so the more actionable message wins. The `totalPrice` argument MUST be the number the caller persists as the booking's `totalPrice`; two call sites were passing a different figure and are now fixed (below). **Pre-MRB-11 bookings are unaffected**: a booking with NO allocation never reaches the guard — the read path falls back to the legacy proportional split tagged `allocation: "legacy-heuristic"` in `getBookingRevenueStreams` — so legacy docs stay readable and the fallback stays reachable. **(2) The corporate write path now writes an allocation.** `handleConvertInquiryToBooking` wrote `totalPrice` with no `revenueAllocation` at all, so a corporate booking fell back to the legacy heuristic and was mis-attributed to the room stream. It now uses the SHARED `computeBookingRevenueAllocation` (never a hand-rolled computation). **(3) Every post-create money path re-snapshots the allocation.** `handleApplyBookingDiscount` / `handleApplyReservationDiscount` (per child) / `handleRemoveVoucher` / `handleRejectDiscount` all moved `totalPrice` and left the create-time snapshot untouched — the exact production shape the 2026-10-21 QA pass found (e.g. `A3SD05Walkin0001`: allocation 4,800 vs bill 3,840 after a 20% senior discount applied from the desk; `A3SD04Walkin0001`: a rejected government discount). They now call the NEW shared `rebuildBookingRevenueAllocation` helper, which reuses the GROSS per-stream values (a discount change moves only the deduction) and re-derives the deduction as the difference between the gross streams and the new bill, so BOTH MRB-11 identities hold by construction. **(4) The scanner can now SEE it.** `scripts/finance-integrity-scan.ts`'s `scanBookingRecord` gained three findings following the file's existing `{entityPath}:{suffix}` id scheme + the existing cohort / proposed-action enums: `allocation-total-mismatch` (an allocation whose `totalNet` ≠ `totalPrice`), `corporate-missing-allocation` (a corporate-source booking with NO allocation — a legacy non-corporate booking is NOT flagged, its missing allocation is legitimate), and `other-addon-outside-room-bill` (a BR-12 review reminder — see the BR-12 note below). **Also in this entry:** `getReservationFolioSummary` gained the `otherAddOnTotal` term (per OCT-FOL-01, decision #298 the other add-ons are NOT inside `totalPrice` — they sit ON TOP of the room bill; `computeBookingFolio` + `computeServerFolioTotals` both took the term, this helper was the one surface that did not). **BR-12 note — the add-ons were NOT moved inside `totalPrice`:** the brief proposed putting the other add-ons inside `totalPrice` and inside `addOnNet`. That CONTRADICTS decision #298 (2026-10-20) and the BR-12 formula (`folio grand total = booking.totalPrice + otherAddOnTotal + storeTotal + chargesTotal`): the add-ons are a BALANCE term, not part of the room bill, and `totalNet == totalPrice` is the MRB-11 identity this entry tightens. Putting them inside `totalPrice` would double-count them in every folio surface. The add-on revenue is NOT lost — the Reports "Add-Ons" sub-tab sums `otherAddOnTotal` directly and every folio surface adds the term. **Also fixed:** the staff breakfast update path computed `totalPrice + newBreakfastTotal − paidAmount` — the pre-OCT-H1 formula, which charges the guest for the breakfast TWICE because `totalPrice` already contains it (per BR-01 / BR-03). The arithmetic is now the shared `computeBreakfastAdjustedTotalPrice` helper (the guest Manage path routes through the same one, so the guest and the desk can never disagree). **Deliberately NOT changed:** the staff breakfast / add-on paths do NOT move `totalPrice` — per BR-01 / BR-03 + OCT-H1 (decision #282) `totalPrice` is the AS-BOOKED room bill and a breakfast change is a balance delta; moving it would change the field's meaning, desynchronise the reservation header's `totalPrice` (MRB-12 decision #179 pins the reservation balance to it) and break the MRB-11 identity. **NO production data was backfilled** — the eight violating rows are QA fixtures the owner asked to leave in place; the data-level backfill is a separate owner decision. **Verified:** `shared/__tests__/mrb-11-revenue-streams.test.ts` (3 new guard pins + the legacy-fallback reachability pin), `shared/__tests__/mrb-11-revenue-allocation-rebuild.test.ts` (7), `shared/__tests__/breakfast-adjusted-total-price.test.ts` (5), `guest-app/tests/api/finance-integrity-scan.test.ts` (5 scanner pins), `guest-app/tests/api/mrb-11-corporate-allocation.test.ts` (5), `guest-app/tests/api/mrb-11-post-create-allocation.test.ts` (8), `guest-app/tests/api/oct-h1-staff-breakfast-balance.test.ts` (5) — every new test demonstrated failing against the pre-fix source. **Files:** `shared/utils/bookingFolio.ts` (the guard + `rebuildBookingRevenueAllocation` + the folio summary's add-on term); `shared/utils/breakfastTotal.ts` (`computeBreakfastAdjustedTotalPrice`); `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts` (2 call sites + the 4 post-create paths); `guest-app/server/handlers/corporate-inquiries.ts`; `guest-app/server/handlers/breakfast-staff-update.ts`; `guest-app/server/handlers/breakfast-manage.ts`; `scripts/finance-integrity-scan.ts`.
| 311 | **A DENORMALIZED FLAG IS NOT A FILTER — the kitchen list's authoritative signal is the map it was denormalized FROM (BR-07-P0/P1/P2, 2026-10-22; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the chef's operational artifact — `GET /api/bookings/breakfast/kitchen-list?date=<D>` — returned `{"finalized":[],"lateChanges":[],"summary":{"totalMeals":0,"byMeal":{}}}` for **every** service date in production, while 4 live bookings carried real breakfast selections. **Three defects, one root cause each.** **(P0) The sheet was empty because the handler's ONLY gate was `where("hasAnyBreakfast","==",true)` — and the walk-in write path never wrote that flag.** The guest-create path and the reschedule path wrote it; the walk-in path (`guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts`) wrote `breakfastSelections` + `breakfastTotal` and nothing else. Every production booking was a walk-in, so the query matched **zero** documents and the sheet was blank on every service day. The handler's own comment called the flag a "best-effort filter" — it was not best-effort, it was the whole filter. **Decisions:** **(1) The kitchen list does NOT read `hasAnyBreakfast` at all.** It scans the `bookings` collection and filters in memory on the authoritative signal — a slot whose service date matches and whose per-slot `removedAt` is null. Firestore cannot OR two fields in one query, and a `!=` query does **not** match documents where the field is ABSENT (verified against production: `where("hasAnyBreakfast","!=",false)` returns 0 rows for the 49 flag-absent bookings), so no cheap server-side query can express "every booking with a non-empty `breakfastSelections` map". The collection is small (69 docs as of 2026-10-22) and the admin app already streams all of it. **(2) The flag is still WRITTEN by every write path** (the walk-in path now writes it, matching the create + reschedule paths) so the denormalization stops drifting for NEW bookings — but no read path may depend on it. **(3) The per-slot `removedAt` is the only cancellation gate.** A cancelled booking (`hasAnyBreakfast: false`, `breakfastTotal: 0`, every slot `removedAt`-stamped) contributes ZERO meals; a booking whose flag says `false` but whose slot was never removed still contributes a meal. **(P1) The guest app and the admin app used DIFFERENT index bases for the same slot.** The guest app wrote `<date>-guest-0` (0-based); EVERY admin read path looked up `<date>-guest-1` (1-based) — the booking drawer, the registration-form pre-fill + PDF, the daily kitchen-prep report, and the dashboard widget. For a 2-guest booking the drawer showed guest 2's meal on guest 1's row, guest 1's row read "no meal", and guest 2's meal was never shown to staff; staff also WROTE to `-guest-2`, a key the guest page's universe does not contain, so **a staff-added meal for the last guest was invisible to the guest.** **Decision: the canonical base is 0-BASED** (`<date>-guest-<n>`, `n` = the index into `booking.guests[]`; "Guest 1" is the display label `n + 1`). Four independent signals agree: the guest app already wrote 0-based; the server's own guest-name resolver already indexed `guests[]` 0-based; the legacy bare `<date>-N` form in production is provably 0-based (a ONE-guest booking stores `-0`); and the original BR-01 spec itself defined `BreakfastSlotKey = ${string}-${number}`. The admin app conforms. **(4) A shared normaliser in `shared/utils/breakfastSlotKey.ts` is the single resolver** — `buildBreakfastSlotKey` / `buildBreakfastSlotId` + `normalizeBreakfastSelections` — used by the guest app, the admin app, and the server, so the two apps cannot drift apart again. **(5) The legacy key forms are folded in, not rewritten.** The bare `<date>-N` form is ALWAYS 0-based, so it maps onto the canonical key with a PREFIX CHANGE AND NO INDEX SHIFT. The legacy 1-based `<date>-guest-N` form (written by the pre-fix admin app; one live production booking) is detected **per booking** — a `guest-n` index reaching `numGuests` cannot be a 0-based space — and shifted down. **(6) The two forms do not collide:** a booking mixing `<date>-0` with `<date>-guest-0` resolves to ONE slot per guest (the canonical form wins), so the kitchen never cooks the same guest twice. **(7) A write lands in the booking's OWN index space** (`buildBreakfastSlotKeyForBase`), so a legacy booking never ends up holding two index spaces at once. **(P2) The kitchen list hard-coded the DEFAULT cut-off time + timezone** while the guest view and the staff view read the CONFIGURED ones — a Settings change moved the cut-off for the guest + the staff but not for the kitchen list. It now reads the same `settings/breakfastConfig` doc; the built-in defaults survive only as the missing/malformed-doc fallback. **The generalisable lesson: a denormalization is a cache, and a cache you gate on is a correctness dependency you have not earned.** Where the authoritative fact is cheap to read, read it — and if you must denormalize, every writer must write it, and no reader may depend on it. The test gap that let this ship is the second lesson: the existing kitchen-list fixture carried `hasAnyBreakfast: true`, **a shape the server's own walk-in path never produces**, so all 56 guest-api tests passed against a completely broken feature. Fixtures must be built the way the WRITER writes them, not the way the reader wants them to look. **Verification (read-only, against production `spark-inn-prod`):** before — `where("hasAnyBreakfast","==",true)` matches **0** bookings → 0 meals on every date; after — the fixed computation returns **11 meals across 6 service dates** (2026-10-21: 1 · 2026-11-03: 2 · 2026-11-04: 2 · 2026-11-05: 2 · 2026-11-06: 2 · 2026-11-12: 2), and the three cancelled production bookings contribute **0** on their dates. New regression nets: `guest-app/tests/api/br-07-p0-kitchen-list-live-data.test.ts` (12 tests — walk-in-shaped fixture, cancelled-booking non-resurrection, all three key forms, configured cut-off time + timezone), `shared/__tests__/breakfast-slot-key.test.ts` (13 tests), `admin-app/src/__tests__/br-07-p1-admin-slot-key-space.test.ts` (7 tests) — all verified to FAIL against the pre-fix code. The existing `br-07-kitchen-list-routes.test.ts` fixture was rebuilt the way the walk-in path ACTUALLY writes it, and its fake Firestore now really filters on `where(...)` so a flag-gated handler cannot pass it. Suites: shared 883/883 · guest-api 2743/2743 · admin 2013/2013 · infra 6/6 · rules 83/83 · typecheck clean · server `tsc` unchanged at its 22-error baseline. **MD sync:** `BREAKFAST.md` (the canonical slot key + the read query + the cut-off + the index note), this decision entry, `ROADMAP.md`. |
| 312 | **THE ADMIN APP AND THE GUEST APP ARE TWO DIFFERENT ORIGINS — every staff API call resolves its base through `getApiBaseUrl()`, never through `window.location.origin` and never through a relative `/api` (P0-ADMIN-API-BASE, 2026-10-22; `fix:` PATCH bump):** the admin app is a SEPARATE Vercel project from the guest app that serves `/api/*`. The admin project's `vercel.json` ends with `{ "source": "/(.*)", "destination": "/index.html" }`, so **every** unmatched path on the admin origin returns the SPA shell with HTTP 200 and `content-type: text/html` — a 200 that looks like success until you try to parse it. **Five call sites built their API URL from the admin origin**, so each one fetched the HTML shell, `res.json()` threw, and the feature silently died: the booking drawer's **Breakfast tab** (the gate `if (!config || !config.isEnabled)` rendered "Breakfast is not enabled" — staff could not edit a single meal, even though the sibling SAVE endpoint worked), the booking drawer's **Add-Ons tab** (`if (!catalog) return out;` — no add-on rows at all), the **walk-in breakfast grid** ("Breakfast not enabled" — no breakfasts at walk-in creation), the **walk-in other-add-ons grid** (no add-on rows), and the **refund "Mark as paid" POST** (which ALSO sent no staff token, so it would have 401'd even with the right URL). **The sixth site was a red herring worth recording**: `KitchenListPage.tsx` looked identical in a grep but was CORRECT — it resolves `base` from `getApiBaseUrl()` a few lines ABOVE the `fetch(`, which is why a naive "does the fetch expression contain the helper" scan flags it. The contract is therefore **per file**, not per call site: any file that talks to the guest API must resolve its base through the shared helper. **Decisions:** **(1) `getApiBaseUrl()` is the ONLY sanctioned base.** It already existed and already resolved correctly (production admin host → `https://www.sparkinnbohol.com`; staging admin host → `https://stg.sparkinnbohol.com`; localhost → `http://localhost:3000`) — the defect was that five files bypassed it. **(2) `window.__API_BASE_URL` is deleted, not repaired.** It is injected NOWHERE in the repo, so it always fell back to the relative `"/api"` and the "override" was a no-op that read as a deliberate configuration. A global that nothing writes is worse than no global: it looks like the escape hatch and silently routes every call to the wrong origin. **(3) No credentialed cookies.** The guest API echoes `access-control-allow-origin: https://admin.sparkinnbohol.com` with `vary: Origin` but sends NO `access-control-allow-credentials`, so a cross-origin request with `credentials: "include"` would have its RESPONSE blocked by the browser even after the URL was fixed. The staff **Bearer token** is the credential — the same shape AdminContext already uses for every other staff call. **(4) The generalisable lesson: a 200 is not a response.** The SPA rewrite answers every unknown path with `200 OK` + HTML, so a client that only checks `res.ok` cannot detect it. The failure was silent because the code had a `catch { /* silent — keep null */ }` that swallowed the parse error and left the feature in its empty state, which the UI then rendered as a *business* message ("Breakfast is not enabled") rather than an error. **A feature that renders an empty state must distinguish "the server said no" from "I never reached the server"** — a gate that fires on `!config` cannot tell those apart, and in this case it told the front desk that breakfast was switched off when it was switched ON. The test gap is the second lesson: the five sites had NO test at all, and the surfaces they fed (breakfast, add-ons, refunds) each had thorough tests that never exercised the URL, because the existing tests called the handler directly. **Tests must assert the URL the browser will actually request, not just the behaviour of the function behind it.** **Verification:** the pre-fix live repro is unambiguous — `GET https://admin.sparkinnbohol.com/api/bookings/breakfast/config` returns `HTTP 200, content-type: text/html, 1021 bytes` (the SPA shell) while `GET https://www.sparkinnbohol.com/api/bookings/breakfast/config` returns `{"success":true,"data":{"isEnabled":true,…,"meals":[…6 meals…]}}`; the drawer rendered "Breakfast is not enabled" in the live admin app for a booking whose config was enabled. New regression net: `admin-app/src/__tests__/p0-admin-api-base-url.test.ts` (13 tests — **9 fail against the pre-fix code**): no file derives an API URL from `window.location.origin`, no file reads `__API_BASE_URL`, every file that calls a guest-API path resolves its base through `getApiBaseUrl()`, each of the five dead sites is pinned individually, the refund POST must send the staff Bearer token and must NOT use credentialed cookies, and the resolver must never return a relative path for a non-localhost admin host. Suite: admin **2026/2026** (was 2013) · typecheck clean. **MD sync:** this decision entry, `ROADMAP.md`, `QA-REPORT-2026-10-21.md`. |
